It leaves nothing the new type's build, numbering its own phase 1 and tasks 1.n, can meet — each repository's quick-fix branch set aside, renamed to `moved/fix/{wu}` where it holds commits past the branch it lands on, a name no landing reads as the unit's, its commits unlanded, else deleted, every phase the quick-fix wrote taken out of the tracker for good, its fixes' and its review's fix phases, their tasks with them, and then the pull request of each repository the quick-fix touches, as the landing reads them (**`land`**), where one is open, closed unmerged — one the person closed by hand read as none open, another's of the same name, a fork's or one opened before the unit, never closed — its branch on the host left as it stands — and sets the old type's own files aside in the topic's folder, under `topics/{wu}/earlier/`: a quick-fix's `build/` whole, its `summary.md` and its review's `review/` whole, each round's report read at the topic's first opening, a bugfix's investigation log as `investigation-log.md`, so the discussion keeps a log of its own; the carry's `files` names each a carried line cites or links, so the line follows it, and an absorb takes them with the topic. It writes the brief at `topics/{wu}/brief.md`, and, last, re-types the unit in place under its name in its manifest and its registration in one save — the description given, a quick-fix's `fixes` and its finish checklist, `checklists.finish` — its close's, closed where a review was asked — and a bugfix's investigation item dropped, so the new type starts as one before its spec and works its own finish, and its one topic added as its first phase's item, `{"status": "unstarted"}`, which the first entry starts. Its seeds, discovery logs and assets stay where they are. It re-indexes the unit, its chunks carrying the type (warn-don't-block), removes the draft and the staged payload, and commits, `workflow({wu}): move {from} to {to}` — the unit, the project manifest, the tracker's store where the fixes began and, where lines were carried, the ledger. Response: `{"ok": true, "work_unit", "work_type", "from", "brief", "fixes_closed": [N.n…], "branches": [moved/fix/{wu}[ in {repo}]…]|[], "pull_request_closed": bool, "checkout": branch|null, "kept_aside"?: [{"task", "repo", "ref", "why", "see", "recover"}], "committed", "next"}` — `fixes_closed`, `branches`, `pull_request_closed` and `checkout` what this call did, the fixes it closed, the branches it kept, whether it closed a pull request and the branch it took the project's checkout to, so a second yes names none a first made; `kept_aside`, where any fix's park or this move set work aside, each such fix's work the move kept — never dropped, as its commits are kept under moved/ (R26) — a line for each repository holding it, its ref, `why` as its commit says it was set aside, `when a question parked it` or `as the quick-fix moved`, `see` the `git diff` that shows it, as **`task`**'s `start` names it, and `recover` the `git cherry-pick --no-commit` that brings it into the checkout where it now stands, `git -C {folder}` in a nested one — each named too on start this work's screen before the yes, at the topic's first opening and on a cancel's receipt (R26); `next` the handoff into the new type's first phase, `leeway-discussion-process` or `leeway-investigation-process` with `[type, wu]`. Known edges: a failure between the save and the commit leaves the move made and uncommitted, the unit of its new type, so the yes refuses to run again; a quick-fix named like a waiting roadmap idea moves beside it, its name kept; a line another clone writes at the quick-fix's implementation after the move is no line the carry lists: merged in, it stands at the phase it was made at, read as the new type's own.

```bash
engine workunit create <work-unit> <work-type> --description <text> --session-log-file <path> [--seed <path> …]
engine workunit cancel <work-unit>
engine workunit reactivate <work-unit>
engine workunit pivot <work-unit>
engine workunit absorb <feature> --into <epic> --topic <name>
engine workunit move <work-unit> --to feature|bugfix --file <payload.json>   # stages the move on start this work's screen — its yes is start-this-work's
engine workunit position <work-unit> auto|step   # the build's position switched — see **`ready-to-build`**
```

**`agent`** — the background-agent lifecycle store, colocated with the topic's content files at `.leeway/.cache/{wu}/{phase}/{topic}/state.json` (engine-owned, cache-resident; deleting a topic's cache directory — restart — or the work unit's cache — close — removes state and content together; agent content files stay markdown and carry no state — frontmatter is never written or read). `dispatch <wu> <phase> <topic> --kind <kind> [--label <slug> …] [--set <NNN>] [--final]` allocates the next per-kind number (`review-001`; `deep-dive-002-auth` with a label; numbering skips past any legacy files in the cache dir), records the row(s) `in-flight`, each naming the Claude process and conversation that dispatched it as a presence record names its owner (`pid`, `pid_start`, `session_id`, null where Claude Code handed none), and answers with the content-file path(s) the sub-agent(s) must write — no skeleton is created; a file's later non-empty existence IS that agent's completion signal. A discussion review refuses while the topic's mailbox holds messages (a waiting message is a pending change to the document the review would read), and while unarmed (9.8.14) — the first review is free; review n+1 needs `min(n, 3)` of the topic's moves on the ledger since the anchor (`domain/ledger-moves.cjs`): each question asked on the topic but a review's finding (`by: review`) and one Claude's own call answers, each decision made on it — a replaced answer among them, the person's or Claude's, "your call" and a finding's answer included — and each question moved, counted on the topic it left and the topic it went to; a finding, a rewording, a confirmation, a keep and a merge move nothing. The anchor is the latest report-backed review or closing-pass row's `ledger_mark`, the id of the ledger's last line when it was dispatched, null on an empty ledger, which every discussion review and closing-pass row is stamped with (a killed dispatch with no report never anchors; a closing pass does, so after a reopen the moves it read arm nothing); an investigation's validation, `dispatch <wu> investigation <wu> --kind root-cause-validation|fix-validation`, is stamped with it too, and counts as taken in for the entry it validates once its report is in and its row incorporated, dispatched after the entry was made (**`investigation`**); `--final` bypasses the movement gate, still stamping the mark — a review the person asked for; refused on any other kind or phase. A closing pass (`closing-pass`) waits on no arming. A `perspective` dispatch is one polarity pair (`domain/lenses.cjs`): two `--label`s, each a lens and the other's counter-lens, any other lens refused. Repeating `--label` dispatches one SET: every row shares the allocated number (that shared number, echoed as `set` on every row, is the set identity a perspective pair and its synthesis are joined by), and the response carries `set` plus per-label `agents: [{id, file}…]`. A synthesis always dispatches `--set` (required; no `--label`), joining an existing perspective set as `synthesis-{NNN}` — refused when the set is unknown, when any member is still in flight (synthesis reads the whole council), when a live synthesis already exists for the set (an incorporated dead one permits a recovery re-dispatch), or when a legacy file already occupies the name. Kinds: `review`, `deep-dive`, `perspective`, `synthesis`, `root-cause-validation`, `fix-validation`, `closing-pass`, `spec-review`. A unit's own dispatches — the spec's, which is the unit's — keep their store at the unit's spec, `dispatch <wu> specification <wu>`, the place `enter specification` reads for the agents it was left with; a spec review's row is bound, as its brief is issued, to the land of the spec it reviews (**`brief`**), `land` on its row. A spec review's report is taken in only as it reads, by one grammar (`domain/spec-report.cjs`): a line inside a fenced block is text, whatever it holds, and a fence never closed does not read; its `## Findings` section runs to the report's end and reads `None identified.` alone, or holds its findings, each opening `### F{n}: {label}`, numbered F1, F2, … in order, its first line its lane, `**Lane:** rewrite`, `gap` or `fact`, the bare word; a finding's heading before the section, or any other line within it that markdown may read as a heading, does not read. A pass counts as reported once its report is taken in, the findings it was taken in with kept on its row, `report_findings`, which every later read takes in place of the report; a report that does not read never is. `scan <wu> <phase> <topic>` promotes finished rows to `pending` and answers the decision-ready snapshot — `in_flight`, `pending`, `acknowledged`, and `incorporated` rows (every row public-shaped, carrying `set` and `created` — freshness and earlier-session judgments read `created`; conclusion gates read `in_flight`), plus `review_arming` on discussion topics — `{armed, cycles, moves_seen, moves_needed, reason}`, the same verdict dispatch enforces, so the session's dispatch check reads drained state and arming from one call. A spec review whose report does not read is closed instead of promoted, `refused` on its row with what does not read, and answered under `refused`, `[{id, refusal}]`, each refusal ending in its way on: another pass dispatched, its brief carrying the refusal. Which row and which finding to take next is the reading flow's judgment, not the store's. `ack … <id> (--findings F1,F2,… | --clean)` records the finding ids the model read from the content file; a clean report incorporates immediately. A spec review is acknowledged with exactly the findings its report was taken in with, in any order: other ids, or `--clean` while it holds any, are refused, naming the findings it holds. `surface … <id> <finding>[,<finding>…]` records one unsurfaced finding, or a comma batch of them when a whole lane lands at once — every id validated before any is recorded, so a bad entry fails the batch whole; surfacing the last one auto-incorporates the row. `--replaces <decision> --reason <reason>` surfaces one finding, raised as that decision's replacement: kept on the row, `raised`, with the decision by its full id, the reason — one a replacement gives — and the ledger's mark, refused for a batch, another reason, or an id no entry or several begin; no question stands for such a gap on the ledger, so the spec's loop holds the writer on it while the decision is current and kept by no answer since (**`brief`**'s `spec-to`). `--question <question>` surfaces one finding, asked as that question: kept on the row, `asked`, `{"finding", "entry"}` with the question by its full id — the link the board follows from the finding to its answer (**`board`**) — refused for a batch, beside `--replaces` (`a finding is raised as a replacement with --replaces, or asked as a question with --question, never both`), an id no entry or several begin, and an entry that is no question asked where the row's agent ran — at the unit's spec for the spec's store, which is the unit's, else at the store's own place (`--question names a question asked at {wu}.specification, and {id} is none`, or `… at {wu}.{phase}.{topic}, …`). `incorporate` is the skip-all exit — remaining ids stay unsurfaced, an honest record of what was offered and declined; from in flight it closes the row unread, so a report landing after is never taken in, since only a row in flight is. A spec review whose report is in and reads — pending, or in flight and not yet scanned — is refused until it is acknowledged, so a finding is declined only where its pass was acknowledged with it and then incorporated; one in flight whose report does not read is refused as a scan refuses it, `refused` on its row, and the refusal answered under `refusal`. A row closed already — by `incorporate`, by `ack --clean`, or by its last finding surfaced — is answered as that close left it, nothing written, so a caller closes every row with `incorporate` whatever closed it first. `ack` refuses never-surfaced kinds (a perspective is a synthesis input). Off-path calls refuse loudly naming the row's actual status. Responses carry the public row: `status`, `set`, `created`, `findings`, `surfaced`, `remaining`; a spec review's rewrite findings, handed to the writer whole and never surfaced, are `for_writer`, never among `remaining`, so its row stays `acknowledged` while it holds any, and `incorporate` closes it.

**`discovery-session`** — discovery's session lifecycle (`domain/discovery-session.cjs`), at either of its places: an epic's, its logs at `{wu}/discovery/session-NNN.md` and its marker `phases.discovery.active_session` on the unit's manifest; and `product`, the product's conversation, its logs at `.product/sessions/session-NNN.md` and its marker `product_session` on the project manifest. `enter discovery` opens one where its home has none open: the next session number from the place's logs on disk, the log made empty for the conversation to write, and the marker set, committed with the entry (**`enter`**).

`close` ends the session the marker names, as one transaction: delete the marker (the next entry sees a closed session) and commit the place's scope with the caller's message — an epic's `commit --discovery` scope, the product's `commit --product` one — never a peer session's half-written document. Nothing is indexed: a log never is. The message rides on `-m` because it varies by caller. The log's content is model-authored **before** the call — the engine never writes prose. Refuses loudly, manifest untouched, when no marker is set (`no discovery session is open for "{place}" — nothing to close`) or when the marker names a session with no log on disk. Response: `{"ok": true, "session": "NNN", "session_log": …, "committed": "<short-sha>", "warnings": []}` (`committed: null` plus a note naming the place's scope when nothing was committed).

```bash
engine discovery-session close <work-unit|product> -m "<message>"
```

**`discovery-log`** — the three lists of an epic's discovery logs the engine writes, which the board reads (`domain/discovery-log.cjs`, 20.11.1, 20.11.3): each call puts one entry, a line, in the log of the epic's open session, `{wu}/discovery/session-{NNN}.md`, under its fixed heading — `## Not taken`, `- {direction} — {why}`; `## Views of the product`, `- {view}`; `## Spikes`, `- {spike} · {tool} — {what it showed}`, the tool `[{tool}]({live link})` where `--link` names one — after the last entry under the last heading of its name outside a fenced block and the lines indented beneath it, else as the first under it, or under the heading added at the log's end where the log has none. The rest of the log is the conversation's; nothing is committed, the log riding with the next state commit. Response `{"ok": true, "log", "heading", "line"}`, the log from the state root. Refuses, writing nothing: an entry of no kind, positionals or flags other than its usage's, a unit the project lacks or that is no epic, an epic with no discovery session open, a value missing, empty or past one line, a direction or tool holding ` — `, a tool holding `[` or `]`, a `--link` that is no web address, and a spike the epic does not hold — named as `asset spike` answered it, its `spike`, never a path.

```bash
engine discovery-log not-taken <epic> --direction "<direction>" --why "<why>"
engine discovery-log view <epic> --text "<view>"
engine discovery-log spike <epic> <spike> --tool "<tool>" --showed "<what it showed>" [--link <live link>]
```

**`investigation-log`** — the hunt a bugfix's investigation keeps under fixed headings in its topic's log, `{wu}/topics/{wu}/log.md`, which the investigation's gates read (`domain/investigation-log.cjs`, 19.4.1–19.4.3, P1): each call puts one entry, a line, under its heading, as `discovery-log` does (`domain/log-lists.cjs`); an experiment, the root cause, the fix direction and an intended hypothesis rest on a ledger entry, named by its full id, a symptom, a validation and any other hypothesis on none — `## Symptoms`, `- {symptom}`, in the person's words; `## Hypotheses`, `- H{n} · {title} · {state}`, its state suspected, confirmed, ruled out, or intended on the person's ruling, `intended [{decision}]`; `## Experiments`, `- E{n} · {title} — {what it showed} [{finding}]`, the investigation's own checks; `## Root cause`, `- {the decision's words} [{decision}]`; `## Validation`, `- {root cause|fix} · {what it found} — {what Claude did with it}`; `## Fix direction`, `- {the question's words} [{question}]`. A hypothesis is numbered as it is first logged, and a later line for it, `hypothesis <bugfix> H{n}` with its `--title`, its `--state` or both, says it as it is now, what it does not give carried, standing over the earlier; an experiment is numbered as it is logged. `--body <file>` puts the file's lines beneath the entry, each indented four spaces, for the board's pages (20.12.2). The fix direction's body is what the step back found, copied from this conversation's latest raise down the question's chain — one answering it, or one replacing a decision that did or replaced one that did — whose seal holds `stepped_back`; where none does, `--body`, one finding a line as the seal's are — a `--body` given besides is passed over, said among the warnings. None of the headings is `## Investigation`, under which the reshape's migration puts an old investigation, its own headings one level deeper. Claude's own prose goes under headings of its own, never under these. Nothing is committed, the log riding with the next state commit. Response `{"ok": true, "log", "heading", "line", "warnings"?}`, the log from the state root, in `warnings` every line of the log the read passed over and what the ledger's read said on the way. Refuses, writing nothing: an entry of no kind, positionals or flags other than its usage's; a place whose investigation cannot take work, as `enter investigation` refuses it, or has not started (`{wu}'s investigation has not started — engine enter investigation {wu} starts it`); a value missing, empty or past one line, a title holding ` · ` or an experiment's ` — `, a validation's finding holding ` — `; a state of none of the four, an intended hypothesis with no `--by` and any other with one, a hypothesis the log does not hold or logged again reading as it already does; an entry named that is not one of the unit's current entries of its kind — `--entry` a finding for an experiment and a decision for the root cause, `--by` a decision of the person's, `--question` a question — naming what it is instead: of another kind, no longer current, standing in another unit, or a call of Claude's; a fix direction while the root cause points at no current entry, none logged or its decision replaced since (19.4.1); and a body that cannot be read, holds nothing, or holds a blank line, which would end it.

```bash
engine investigation-log symptom <bugfix> --text "<the person's words>" [--body <file>]
engine investigation-log hypothesis <bugfix> --title "<title>" [--state suspected|confirmed|ruled-out|intended] [--by <decision>] [--body <file>]
engine investigation-log hypothesis <bugfix> H<n> [--title "<title>"] [--state suspected|confirmed|ruled-out|intended] [--by <decision>] [--body <file>]
engine investigation-log experiment <bugfix> --title "<title>" --showed "<what it showed>" --entry <finding> [--body <file>]
engine investigation-log root-cause <bugfix> --entry <decision> [--body <file>]
engine investigation-log validation <bugfix> root-cause|fix --found "<what it found>" --did "<what Claude did with it>" [--body <file>]
engine investigation-log fix-direction <bugfix> --question <question> [--body <file>]
```

**`investigation`** — a bugfix investigation's close (`domain/investigation-conclude.cjs`, 19.4.23, 24.1.2): `conclude` is the yes of its conclude gate, `render conclude` (**`render`**). It stands once the investigation can close, and refuses, writing nothing, each refusal naming the way on: positionals or flags other than its usage's, answering the usage, `Usage: engine investigation conclude <bugfix>`; and, each opening `investigation conclude: `, a place whose investigation cannot take work or has not started, as `investigation-log` refuses it; the log's root cause or its fix resting on no current entry — none logged, its entry replaced since, the fix direction's question with no answer yet, the fix being that answer; either validation not taken in since what it validates was made — the root cause's since the root cause, the fix's since the fix and the root cause it is checked against (26.4.10) — so a root cause or a fix replaced after its validation is validated again, and so is a fix kept over a root cause replaced: where none was dispatched since, `the {root cause|fix} {id} has not been validated since {it|it, or the root cause it rests on,} was logged — both validations always run: …` (19.4.7); where one was and is not yet taken in, the latest, by where it stands, never dispatched again — `the {root cause|fix} {id} is being validated — ` then `{agent} is still running — the conclude waits for its report`, `{agent} was lost with the process that dispatched it — engine agent incorporate … abandons it, and another is dispatched`, `{agent}'s report is in — ` with its scan where it is not yet read in and its `agent ack`, or `{agent}'s findings are acknowledged — once each is acted on, engine agent incorporate … takes it in`; a validation of the other kind, or one closed unread, never counts; a question of the person's open at the place, a row of its map standing open, each named; mail waiting in its mailbox; nothing staged by the gate, or what is staged drawn before the root cause or the fix changed (`the conclude was drawn before the root cause or the fix changed — it is staged again with engine render conclude {wu} --file <recap.json>`); a verdict still to ask (`a verdict is still to ask — engine render conclude {wu} asks each before the yes`); and what the bug is in, staged with the recap, refused again as the gate refuses it should it no longer hold, each such refusal ending `— engine render conclude {wu} --file <recap.json> stages it again`. **What the bug is in** feeds the bugs found after review (17.1.6, 17.1.9; **`measures`**), and is the recap's alone, drawn on no line of the screen (17.2.2): `fixes`, the unit whose code the bug is in, left out where no unit built that code — refused where the project holds no such unit (`there is no work unit "{unit}" to fix — …`) and where it is this unit; and `area`, the area of the verify setup's feature map it sits in, Claude's reading of the map, which is the product's in its own words and never checked against it — refused past one line of words and where no verify setup is recorded (`no verify setup is recorded, so no feature map names an area — leave the area out`). Then, under the writer lock, in one manifest save, it completes the investigation's item and stamps its close, `concluded_at`, the instant — the field surface takes only an instant for it — with what the bug is in on the item as staged, none kept from an earlier close — `fixes` and `area` where named and, with `fixes`, `after_review`, whether that unit's review phase had ended, a round of its passed or settled, judged at the first conclude naming that unit, the moment the bug was found — a later conclude naming the same unit keeps it, one naming another unit judges it afresh, and one naming none drops it with `fixes` — commits the unit's manifest, the log riding along (`investigation({wu}): conclude`), drops the staged recap, and releases the place's presence, as a conclusion does. Response `{"ok": true, "unit", "concluded_at", "committed", "next"}`, `next` the handoff into the spec, `{"kind": "handoff", "skill": "leeway-specification-process", "args": ["bugfix", "{wu}"]}`, with `warnings` and a pending note where the commit failed.

```bash
engine investigation conclude <bugfix>
```

**`topic`** — a topic's mail, its map, and its unit's lifecycle. A topic's phase item is started by `enter` (**`enter`**), never by a topic verb, and closes through its phase's own door — a discussion on its decided checklist (**`checklist`**), an investigation at `investigation conclude`. The start creates the item `in-progress` when absent, or sets an existing non-terminal one back to `in-progress` — an `unstarted` stub included, that status's one exit — the entry's commit taking the manifest change. It refuses a completed item, which reopens through a message sent to it (`send`), a cancelled one, which comes back through `reactivate`, a postponed one and a promoted one; a specification item on every unit, the one spec keeping no items (P7: `{wu}'s spec keeps no items — it is entered at the work unit: engine enter specification {wu}`); an implementation or review item, which stands by the build's cut (`{phase} stands by the build's cut — it is entered by `engine enter {phase} <work-unit>`, never written by a topic verb`); and on an epic, read under the writer lock, the birth of a discussion the map's next action does not name, or of a topic off the map (an in-progress item resumes regardless). `send <work-unit> <topic> --from <sender> --content <file> --slug <kebab>` (`domain/mail.cjs`) lands a message in the topic's one mailbox — the topic named by its key or by the title a rename gave it (8.8.17), the response's `topic` its key — `.leeway/{wu}/topics/{topic}/mail/NNN-{slug}.md`, its path naming no phase (10.1.3): the content is a scratch file under `.leeway/.cache/`, consumed once the message lands, its first line the message's title (leading `#` marks dropped); under the title the engine writes who sent it and when, as the waiting mail heads it (10.4.2) — `From {sender} · {16 Oct}`, the sender titled as the screen titles it. `--from` is a name, never text: a topic of the unit (`Commercialization`) or an item of one of its phases — `{unit}.discovery` for the epic's own discovery (`Discovery`), whose input reaches a started topic as a message (8.7.6), `{unit}.specification` for its spec (`Specification`), whose sweep's messages `sweep take` sends, reopening the topics they reach and drawing no send report (8.10.5), `{unit}.{topic}` for another unit's topic (`First Run, on Fumi`, a single-topic unit by its name alone), or an experiment of the unit — `E{n}[.{m}]` for one in the target topic's series (`Experiment E1`), `{topic}/E{n}[.{m}]` for one in another topic's, where the person moved the question it measures (`Experiment E1, on Commercialization`); the number is the next in that mailbox, taken under the writer lock, so a send racing another lands after it. It refuses, writing nothing and keeping the scratch: a flag missing, a sender that is none of those names, a slug not kebab-case, a scratch outside the cache, missing, or with a blank first line, a topic named across folders, a unit the project lacks (`there is no work unit "{unit}"`), a quick-fix, which talks through no conversation, and any other target the topic's `enter` refuses, by its own rule (`domain/topic-standing.cjs`) and in its words — a unit completed, a unit past its `spec.md` (from the spec on, a change is settled where it is found, 10.2.2), a topic the unit does not hold (a single-topic unit's topic is the unit), and a topic promoted out of the unit; a postponed or decided topic takes the message, as follows. The topic's conversation — its discussion, a bugfix's investigation — is parked as a landing parks it: absent, created `unstarted` (a parked message never reads as started work); `unstarted` or `in-progress`, untouched; `completed` — decided — set back to `in-progress` (10.2.7), so the topic shows mail waiting and its next decided needs a fresh close (**`checklist`**) and the gate's yes. A decided close the topic holds, taken or not, is voided by the delivery (`voided`), so a message answered before the yes still has its decision read by a fresh close. A postponed topic is left as it stands, the message waiting in its mailbox for its return (10.1.8). A message to a cancelled topic — its map row or its conversation cancelled — to a topic removed from the map, one removed while the send waited on the writer lock among them (8.8.16), or to a cancelled unit lands in the inbox instead, as an idea, `.leeway/.inbox/ideas/{YYYY-MM-DD}--{slug}.md`, its first line the title as an H1, the line naming its sender under it, then the rest (10.1.6); an idea of that name already there refuses. A landing flags nothing downstream: a topic's own phases — discussion, investigation — have no neighbour to flag, the one spec keeping no items (P7). A send writes no ledger entry: the topic it reaches writes the question the message raises (5.3.8). It commits what it wrote — the message and the unit's manifest, as `mail({wu}): send {NNN-slug}.md to {topic}`, or the idea, as `mail({wu}): {slug} to the inbox — {topic or unit} is cancelled` (`— {topic} was removed` for a removed topic), naming whichever is; a commit git refuses leaves the landing with `committed: null` and a `note` naming the retry — for a mailbox landing `commit {wu} --topic {phase}/{topic} --sweep`, the topic's scope as a sweep, since the session sending is not working the topic; for the inbox `commit --inbox`. Response: `{"ok": true, "unit", "topic", "message_path", "reopened"?: true, "committed", "warnings"?, "note"?}`, or for the inbox `{"ok": true, "unit", "topic", "inbox_path", "committed", "warnings"?, "note"?}`; the send report is `render send-report`'s. `mailbox <work-unit> <topic>` lists what waits, in the order it landed, taking no lock — the loop's read, so a message landing while the topic is in session is raised from it (10.1.5): `{"ok": true, "unit", "topic", "count", "files": [absolute paths]}`, a folder or a file not `.md` no message; it refuses a unit the project lacks. `answered <work-unit> <topic> <NNN-slug> --entry <id>` removes the message named `NNN-slug` — named whole, since a number comes back once the highest is answered — once `--entry` — a prefix of an id, resolved as a ledger link's is — names the question it raised: one raised on this topic wherever it stands since, moved to another topic or later (5.3.8), or one any live move of the unit's ever took to this topic, wherever it stands since — the message that carried it (9.S17): under the writer lock, refusing, writing nothing, a name of another shape (the number alone, or the file's name), a unit not in progress, a name no message holds, an id no entry begins, an entry that is no question, and a question neither first raised on this topic nor taken to it by any move of the unit's. The removal is committed alone, `mail({wu}): {topic} answered {NNN-slug}.md`, and git keeps the file; a commit git refuses is retried as a send's is, in the topic's scope as a sweep. Response: `{"ok": true, "unit", "topic", "answered": "NNN-slug.md", "entry": "<short id>", "remaining", "committed"}`.

Derived phases (`DERIVED_PHASES` in the shared schema — currently the experiment phase) sit outside the hand lifecycle: the item is derived bookkeeping the phase's own verbs maintain, so a start refuses it outright (the spawn creates and reopens the series; the last record's terminal transition closes it) — see `experiment`.

`cancel` and `reactivate` are the epic transactions, topic-level over the Discovery unit — the phase argument is `discovery`, never a phase item: the map row with everything under its name (the discussion and the experiment series); any other phase is refused (there is no phase-level cancel). Each is one transaction: the manifest write and a commit of the work-unit manifest alone — plus the project manifest when a roadmap join reverted (`workflow({wu}): cancel {topic} (discovery)` / `… reactivate …`); a cancel additionally releases the calling session's own heartbeat on each item it took, a peer's never (see `presence`), and closes the agents still running on each item it took — each row in flight whose report is not on disk marked incorporated, so a report landing after is never taken in, a landed report's row left for the topic's return, the ids answered as `closed_agents` (a store that does not read is a warning, the cancel standing) — and a reactivate releases nothing. Every commit takes the logs changed beside it, the topic's among them (**`commit`**). A cancel answers its `next` from the calling conversation's position (`domain/topic-leave.cjs`): sitting in the topic it took, at its discussion or experiment, the handoff to the epic's menu, `leeway-continue-epic {wu} {phase} cancelled`; from anywhere else `continue`. Both run beside sessions holding the unit's topics — from the epic menu, and a cancel from inside the conversation it closes — so they take nothing but the state they wrote, and a commit failure degrades to the generic `commit pending` note (the manifest alone is narrower than any state scope).

Past the unit's spec, written or concluded, its map is closed (18.4.19): a discovery cancel is refused (`{wu} has its spec — a decision that must change is settled in the phase the epic is in, and abandoning the work is the unit's cancel, through manage on the start menu`, 12.15.4, 18.3.4), and a discovery reactivate too (`{wu} has its spec — a topic exists only before it, and a change is settled in the spec from here`); `postpone` stays open until ready to build's yes (P3). A **discovery** cancel is refused when the name is on neither the map nor a discussion item, when the unit already reads cancelled, when it would take nothing — no live item under the name and no map row to mark (`"{topic}" has nothing to cancel — no live item under its name and no map row`). In one locked write, in this order: every evidence wait the topic's discussion holds is released and every open record on the series ends `abandoned` with `reason: "topic cancelled"`, the series settling over what remains; the non-terminal discussion item of the name — an `unstarted` stub included — takes `previous_status`/`status: cancelled`; and the map row, when one exists, takes `cancelled: true` with its `order` stashed as `previous_order` — a never-started topic needs nothing else, and a legacy topic with no map row reads cancelled through its items. Under the same lock, each message waiting in the topic's mailbox moves to the inbox as an idea, as `topic remove` moves it (10.1.6), the ideas and the emptied mailbox riding the commit. After the lock: the caller's own heartbeat released on each cancelled item, the roadmap's cancel-revert hop (any item bound to the topic returns to waiting, `roadmap_reverted` on the response, the project manifest riding the commit — see `roadmap`), the commit. Response: `{"ok": true, "topic": "…", "phase": "discovery", "status": "cancelled", "cancelled": [{phase, previous_status}…], "abandoned": [id…], "released_waits": [{phase, released, remaining}…], "roadmap_reverted": [name…], "inbox": [path…], "closed_agents": [id…], "committed": "<short-sha>", "warnings": [], "next"}`, `inbox` each waiting message's idea, absolute.

`reactivate` requires the unit to read cancelled. A **discovery** reactivate clears the map marker, restores the cancelled discussion item — a stash returns as the status; an item cancelled with none returns to never-attempted, its `status` deleted and `null` in `restored` — and returns the map `order` from `previous_order` only while no live row holds the number — the stash drops either way, a taken number leaving the row unordered so the sequencing flag repairs it. No roadmap re-join — the revert is one-way. Response: `{"ok": true, "topic": "…", "phase": "discovery", "status": "reactivated", "restored": [{phase, status}…], "committed": "<short-sha>", "warnings": []}` (`committed: null` plus a note when nothing was staged).

`postpone` is the Discovery unit's other exit, epic-only (a non-epic work unit is refused naming its type — its topic is the work unit, and a pulled unit's "not now" is the work-unit cancel's revert). Cancel says "not doing this"; postpone says "doing this later": the topic leaves whole, nothing on disk moves, and the pull is the way back. `--horizon` is required — the roadmap bucket the topic waits in. The stage is implied: `postpone` addresses the Discovery unit and no other. `postponePlan` is the one reading the gate, the menu row, and the verb share; its locks, in refusal order: the name is on neither the map nor a discussion item; the unit already reads postponed (`"{topic}" is already postponed — it waits on the roadmap`) or cancelled (`"{topic}" is cancelled — reactivate it from the epic menu first`); the unit's build begun — `build` on its manifest, ready to build's yes having closed the map for good (18.4.19), where a decided topic still goes while the spec is written, for a split (11.10.3, P3) (`postponing "{topic}" is refused — {wu}'s build has begun, so its map is closed: a decision that must change is settled in the build, and abandoning the work is the unit's cancel, through manage on the start menu`); a non-terminal record sits on its experiment series (named as the gates name them, counted top-level — a laboratory cannot run under a topic that has left, and abandoning it is the destructive act this exit avoids); the horizon is not a legal name (`"{horizon}" is not a legal horizon name — dots and slashes break manifest addressing` — the roadmap could not name it, so the postpone refuses before the epic moves); and a roadmap item of the topic's name that is not this topic's, waiting or archived (`postponing "{topic}" is refused — a roadmap item named "{name}" (horizon "{h}") is not this topic's: rename it on the roadmap, or rename the topic, first`, `(archived)` for an archived one). In one locked write, the project lock nested inside the work unit's: the non-terminal discussion item of the name takes `previous_status`/`status: postponed`, the map row takes `postponed: true` with its `order` stashed as `previous_order` — the experiment series is left exactly as it stands — and the roadmap side lands **before** the epic manifest is saved, so a refusal there (the clash re-read under the project lock) leaves the topic where it was: an item joined to `{work_unit, topic}` **re-waits** (the join deleted, the chosen horizon taken, its own name and origin left as they were), or one is **born** under the name the topic goes by, its title where a rename gave it one (`summary` the map row's, falling back to the topic titlecased; `origin: postpone:{work_unit}`), the node and the horizon created just-in-time. Either way the item records `postponed_from: {work_unit, topic}` and gains the topic's brief and log as `sources` where they exist (relative to the state). After the lock: the caller's own heartbeat released on each item taken, and the agents still running on each item taken closed, as a cancel closes them. One confined commit covers both manifests (`workflow({wu}): postpone {topic} → {horizon}`). Response: `{"ok": true, "topic": "…", "status": "postponed", "postponed": [{phase, previous_status}…], "roadmap": {name, horizon, born_map, born_horizon, reverted_join}, "closed_agents": [id…], "committed": "<short-sha>", "warnings": [], "next"}` — `next` as a cancel's, its outcome `postponed`.

After the postpone the roadmap owns the topic (see `roadmap`): `topic cancel` and `topic reactivate` refuse a postponed row naming the pull, `roadmap archive` cancels the epic's row in the same transaction, and `roadmap pull-forward` back into the same epic restores it. A start refuses a postponed phase item the same way, and the field surface refuses a `status` write onto one and any `delete` of it or its fields (`{phase} item "{topic}" is postponed — pull it forward from the roadmap instead`). `topic send` **accepts** a postponed row: a message for a topic that waits is mail that waits with it. `postponed` is a terminal status (`TERMINAL_STATUSES`, and the discussion vocabulary alone), so aggregation, epic completion, and the gap analysis's input set all pass a postponed unit by exactly as they pass a cancelled one; the map row's lifecycle reads `postponed` (tier `⊖`) from the marker, the cancel outranking it where both sit.

**The map's verbs** — `add`, `add-batch`, `edit`, `rename`, `remove`, `order` and `brief` (`domain/discovery-map.cjs`) — change an epic's map at the person's word (8.8.6): each does what was asked, commits it, and answers its JSON line and then its one-line report, a `DISPLAY: confirmation` section (8.8.7); none asks a confirm. A map topic is `{source, summary[, description][, title][, prior]}` at `phases.discovery.items`, never a status — its lifecycle is computed at render time — and a topic is unstarted while it reads fresh. **`add <work-unit> <name> --summary <text> --brief <draft> [--description <text>] [--from-roadmap <item>] [--re-add] [--source <tag>]`** lands the topic and installs its brief at `{wu}/topics/{name}/brief.md` from Claude's draft, a file under `.leeway/.cache/` it consumes — the one brief write, shared with `brief` and both gates' yeses, start this work's and approve the map's (8.7.6); `--source` defaults to `discovery`. It refuses, writing nothing: first, an epic past its spec, written or concluded, whose map is closed, new work belonging in the product's conversation as an epic's discovery refuses it (`topic add: {wu} has its spec, so its map is closed — talk about it belongs in the product's conversation, where what comes of it lands as roadmap items: engine enter discovery product`, 18.4.19) — as every landing on the map refuses it, the decided gate's candidates and approve the map among them, and `roadmap pull-forward`, `workunit absorb` and `pivot` each with its own way on; a name that is not a lowercase slug of letters, digits and hyphens, since it names the topic's folder (8.8.18); a name on the map, or one another topic goes by as its title; a name struck from the map, unless `--re-add` says the person asked for it back (8.10.11) — the name then leaves `dismissed`; a draft missing or empty; and a name a waiting roadmap idea carries, unless `--from-roadmap` takes that item in (8.1.6) — the item then joins the topic and leaves the roadmap, the topic's `source` records `roadmap:{item}`, and an item another epic postponed gives the topic its `prior`; an item that is not a waiting idea is refused. **`add-batch <work-unit> --file <topics.json>`** lands several as one transaction, entries `{name, summary, brief, description?, from_roadmap?, re_add?}`, each checked before any is written. **`edit <work-unit> <name> [--summary <text>] [--description <text>]`** sets either or both, at any lifecycle. **`rename <work-unit> <old> <new>`** moves an unstarted topic the ledger names not whole — its key, its folder with its brief and mail, its place in the order, and a roadmap item it took in (`roadmap_reaimed`) — dropping any title it took while set aside; any other topic takes the new name as the `title` every screen shows (8.8.17), its key, folder, mail and ledger entries keeping the name it started under. A rename to the key drops the title, at any lifecycle. A name struck from the map leaves `dismissed`, the person having asked for it (`re_added`, 8.10.11). It refuses a name that is not a slug, the name the topic goes by already, a name another topic goes by, a name a waiting roadmap item carries — in add's words, the item taken in only through `add --from-roadmap` (8.1.6) — and a move onto a folder that stands. **`remove <work-unit> <name>`** takes an unstarted topic the ledger names not off the map: each message waiting in its mailbox moves to the inbox as an idea (`inbox`, 8.8.16), a roadmap item it took in goes back on its horizon (`roadmap_returned`, 18.8.20) — on none where that horizon was removed meanwhile, its `horizon` null there and the item named with the horizon it came from in `roadmap_horizon_gone`, and the report says so, for Claude to ask the person which (18.8.2), its stub and its folder go, and its name joins `dismissed` (8.10.11). A topic started, cancelled or postponed, or one the ledger names, is refused naming the way on — cancel from the epic menu (8.8.8), reactivate, or pull forward. **`order <work-unit> <topic>=<n> …`** sets the topics' order (8.6.2); a closed topic takes none (`"{topic}" takes no order — {why}`), its order stashed as `previous_order` for its return. **`brief <work-unit> <name> --brief <draft>`** rewrites an unstarted topic's brief; a started one is refused, `started — what bears on it goes as a message`. `rename` and `remove` take the writer lock first and read the manifest, the ledger and the mailbox under it, so no entry or message lands between the check and the write. A commit git refuses leaves the change with `committed: null` and a `note` naming the retry, `order`'s `engine commit {wu} --discovery`. Responses: `{"ok": true, "work_unit", "op", "name", "title", "lifecycle", "map_total", "committed"}` — `brief` without `lifecycle` and `map_total` — with each op's own fields: add's `source`, `summary`, `description`, `brief`, `from_roadmap`, `prior` and `re_added`; edit's values written; rename's `renamed_from`, `renamed_to`, `was`, `moved` and `re_added`; remove's `inbox`, `roadmap_returned` and `roadmap_horizon_gone`; brief's `brief`. `add-batch` answers `{"ok": true, "work_unit", "op", "added": [{name, title, from_roadmap?}…], "readded", "map_total", "committed"}`, and `order` `{"ok": true, "work_unit", "op", "ordered": {"{topic}": N, …}, "titles", "committed"}`.

Across the family, the knowledge base is a derived index — its failures land in `warnings`, never block.

```bash
engine topic send <work-unit> <topic> --from <sender> --content <file> --slug <kebab>   # lands the message in the topic's mailbox under the line naming its sender, parking or reopening its conversation — a cancelled or removed topic's in the inbox as an idea — and commits it
engine topic mailbox <work-unit> <topic>   # what waits in the topic's mailbox — {"count": N, "files": [paths…]}; no lock
engine topic answered <work-unit> <topic> <NNN-slug> --entry <id>   # the message removed once the topic holds the question it raised
engine topic cancel <work-unit> discovery <topic>   # the Discovery unit — the map row with its discussion and experiments
engine topic reactivate <work-unit> discovery <topic>   # the unit the cancel took; never re-joins the roadmap
engine topic postpone <work-unit> <topic> --horizon "<horizon>"   # epic-only, the Discovery unit: the map row and its conversations into the postponed hold, the roadmap item born or re-waited under the horizon, one commit over both manifests; the pull is the way back
engine topic add <work-unit> <name> --summary <text> --brief <draft> [--description <text>] [--from-roadmap <item>] [--re-add] [--source <tag>]   # the topic and its brief, one commit
engine topic add-batch <work-unit> --file <topics.json>   # several, one transaction; entries {name, summary, brief, description?, from_roadmap?, re_add?}
engine topic edit <work-unit> <name> [--summary <text>] [--description <text>]   # any lifecycle
engine topic rename <work-unit> <old> <new>   # unstarted: moves whole; started: the new name its title
engine topic remove <work-unit> <name>   # unstarted only; its mail to the inbox, its roadmap item back, its name dismissed
engine topic order <work-unit> <topic>=<n> [<topic>=<n> …]   # the map's order
engine topic brief <work-unit> <name> --brief <draft>   # before the topic starts
```

**`experiment`** — the experiment series: the ordered run of experiments a topic's discussion spawned, recorded at `phases.experiment.items.{topic}.experiments.{id}` with each record's documents on disk in the topic's folder, `.leeway/{wu}/topics/{topic}/experiments/{id}-{slug}/` (`domain/experiment-paths.cjs`). Every document is model-authored — the engine records the lifecycle, never writes prose. Each command is load → apply → save under the work-unit lock plus one decision-ready JSON line. The spawn commits what it wrote; every other command leaves its commit to the session's cadence (`commit --topic experiment/{topic}`, the record directory plus the manifest).

`create` is the spawn — the phase's one door. With `--from discussion` it allocates `E{n}` (per-topic numbering, highest existing plus one), records it `conceived`, installs the problem statement, opens the experiment item (creating it, or setting a completed series back to `in-progress`), and locks the spawning item: `awaiting_experiments` gains the id on the in-progress discussion the question came from — a locked conversation cannot complete until the wait releases; a spawn from a discussion past its unit's spec is refused (`{wu} has its spec — a topic exists only before it, and a change is settled in the spec from here`). `--question <id>` is required on a spawn — every experiment measures a point on the map (9.1.11), and its report reaches the topic through it: it names the question on the topic's map the experiment measures, by a prefix of its ledger id resolved as a ledger link is: a current question standing open on that topic's map, refused otherwise (`domain/experiment-question.cjs`). The record keeps its full id as `question`, where `ledger map` reads it: the question shows waiting on the experiment until the discussion writes the result (see **`ledger`**); the response names it by its short id. `--problem <file>` is required on a spawn: the spawning session writes the problem statement to a cache scratch (`.leeway/.cache/…` — enforced) and the create moves it in as `{dir}/problem.md`, scratch consumed — the file lands before the manifest names the record, so no conceived record ever exists without its problem statement. The spawn then commits the problem statement and the unit's manifest on the state branch (`experiment({wu}/{topic}): spawn {id} {slug}`), with any log changed beside them, the spawning topic's among them (**`commit`**): the response adds `committed`, and a commit git refused leaves the spawn saved, its `note` prescribing the retry, `commit {wu} --topic experiment/{topic} --sweep`. The response's `dir` is where every later document is authored. With `--parent <E{n}>` it is the split: a **running** experiment discovering its question decomposes takes sub-experiments `E{n}.{m}`, each walking the same lifecycle in miniature, records nested inside the parent's directory — no lock moves (the wait stays on the parent and releases once, when the parent as a whole ends), and `--problem` and `--question` are refused: a split carries no spawn-side problem statement — the sub-record's design frames its question, and the parent measures the map's. A split never splits again.

The record lifecycle is `conceived → designed → approved → running → concluded | abandoned`. `advance` walks the mechanical steps (conceived → designed when the design is written; approved → running when measurement begins); `approve` records the briefing gate's freeze (designed → approved), holding the day of it on the record as `approved` (`YYYY-MM-DD`), and nothing else — the one transition requiring the user's confirm, never a step a loop drifts past. `conclude` takes a **running** record and its one-line `--verdict` (the decision rule's mechanical outcome); a parent refuses while any sub-experiment is live — its verdict synthesises them; and one whose report is owed as a message past its unit's spec refuses before it writes anything, so no report is lost (`experiment {id} ("{topic}") owes its report to "{to}" as a message, and {wu} has its spec — a topic exists only before it: abandon the experiment, and settle what it found in the spec`). `abandon` is the first-class terminal from any pre-terminal status, `--reason` kept on the row. `runs --file <runs.json>` records a **running** record's tally of its runs from a cache scratch, `[{"condition", "of", "values", "unit"?}]` — each condition the design runs, the runs it gives it, every value measured so far and the unit they are in: kept on the record as `runs: [{condition, of, done, median, unit?}]`, the values' count and median in place of the values — the middle one, else the mean of the two middles, to twelve significant figures — the whole tally standing over any before; refused before anything is written for a unit, topic or experiment the project lacks, a record not running, a file outside the cache, a tally that is no list or empty or names a condition twice, and a condition whose keys are other than those, whose name is not one line, whose `of` is no whole number above nought, a value no number, or more values than its `of`. Like `advance`, it leaves its commit to the session's cadence. A top-level conclude or abandon releases the spawning item's wait on that id, and flags nothing: the result reaches the discussion on its map, the question it measured waiting there until the discussion writes the result, or as the report's message where that question closed first. A sub-experiment's terminal transitions release nothing. After every terminal transition the item's derived status settles: `completed` when every record is terminal, `in-progress` otherwise — nothing above the records ever concludes by hand. **A report after its question left the topic:** a top-level conclusion whose record measures a question no longer open on the spawning topic's map — the person closed or moved it while the experiment ran — sends `{dir}/report.md` through `topic send` to the topic the question stands on now: the topic of its unit it was moved to, while live, else the spawning topic — so one moved to the roadmap as later goes to the spawning topic. The copy is the report as written, each relative link (inline, image or reference definition; none naming a scheme, an absolute path or only an anchor, and none in fenced code) rebased to resolve from the topic's mailbox, under the line naming the experiment — `--from {id}` where the report goes to the spawning topic, `--from {topic}/{id}` where it goes to another; the send reopens a decided topic and commits the delivery as any send's. With no report written the conclusion is refused, nothing recorded. A question still open takes the report in the discussion, which writes the result. Responses carry the record (`id`, `slug`, `status`, `dir`) plus `previous` (advance/approve), `runs` (the tally recorded), `verdict`/`reason`, `awaiting` and `question` (create — the lock after the spawn, the question measured), `released_waits`, `report_sent` (`{topic, message_path, reopened, committed}`, the report sent as a message and the topic it went to), `item_status` (terminal transitions), and `warnings`.

The register, the briefing confirm, the record picker, the return leg's next-or-menu gate, and the conversation's two pauses — the post-spawn now-or-later choice and the blocked-conclusion gate — are render surfaces (`experiment-register`, `experiment-approval-gate`, `experiment-pick`, `experiment-next-gate`, `experiment-spawn-gate`, and the decided checklist — an open question an experiment still measures holds the topic's close); the epic menu carries one row per topic whose series holds a live top-level record, ranked above every other recommendation, appearing at the first spawn and retiring when no live record remains — which experiment to work resolves inside the phase.

```bash
engine experiment create <work-unit> <topic> --slug <kebab> (--from discussion --problem <file> --question <id> | --parent <E{n}>)
engine experiment advance <work-unit> <topic> <id>       # conceived → designed, approved → running
engine experiment approve <work-unit> <topic> <id>       # the freeze — designed → approved, user-confirmed
engine experiment runs <work-unit> <topic> <id> --file <runs.json>  # the tally of a running record's runs, by condition
engine experiment conclude <work-unit> <topic> <id> --verdict "<one line>"
engine experiment abandon <work-unit> <topic> <id> --reason "<one line>"
```

**`presence`** — the per-topic session heartbeat, cache-resident (`.leeway/.cache/{wu}/{phase}/{topic}/presence`, gitignored). Awareness, never mutual exclusion. Every phase a session sits in carries one — discovery, experiment, discussion, investigation, specification, implementation, review — discovery's at an epic, the epic itself its topic, or at the product's conversation, `product/discovery/product`, which has no work-unit directory.

**Beats are mechanical, and no prose ever issues one.** The engine stamps the heartbeat as a side effect of the verbs a session already runs, and only where the verb is structurally self-referential — the session acting on its own topic. A beat writes *this* process's identity, so a beat from a verb acting on another topic would manufacture a false hold there. The read verbs (`topic mailbox`, `agent scan`) are reachable for any topic — a session legitimately checks a foreign mailbox — so they **refresh** instead: re-stamp a heartbeat this session already owns, never create one, never overwrite a peer's.

| Verb | Beat |
|---|---|
| `commit --topic {phase}/{topic}` | beats — the session-cadence commit on a live item, so "last active" means "time since the last real write" |
| `commit --topic …` on a **terminal item** (`completed`, `cancelled`, `promoted`) | **clears** — the topic is finished, so every commit that follows its close (the conclusion) releases the slot rather than re-taking it until the process dies. An item that does not exist neither beats nor clears |
| `commit --topic … --sweep` | suppressed, **outranking both** — the commit is on a topic this session is not working (the conclude sweep's leavings, a foreign-topic delivery's retry, a resolution landed in another phase's document); stamping it would manufacture a hold, and clearing it would destroy a peer's |
| `render phase-note` | beats — only ever rendered where a phase starts, for its own topic, so announcing the start is the same act as claiming it |
| `topic mailbox` | refreshes the topic's row in the phase it is talked through — the loop checks its own topic's mailbox every turn, so a turn with no writes still registers; a read of another topic's mailbox stamps nothing |
| `enter` | beats the place it enters; a level move (`--move`) **clears** the calling session's own row where it leaves, a topic's or a discovery home's |
| `topic answered` | beats — taking an answered message out of the session's own mailbox, in the phase the topic is talked through |
| `experiment create --from` | beats the **spawning** discussion — the conversation recording the spawn on its own item |
| `experiment create --parent` · `advance` · `approve` | beat the experiment topic — the laboratory session working its own record |
| `experiment conclude` · `abandon` (top-level id) | **clear** — the last record's close settles the item, so the record's close is the release; a cadence commit while records remain re-claims (that is the session still in the laboratory); a sub-experiment's beats instead (the parent still runs) |
| `agent dispatch` · `ack` · `surface` · `incorporate` | beat — the session's own background agents |
| `agent scan` | refreshes — a read of the store, so it re-stamps an owned hold and creates nothing |
| `topic send` | never — delivery acts on the **target** topic from the origin's session |
| `manifest set` · `apply` | never — a three-segment `set` looks self-referential and frequently is not: the gap analysis's `source` stamp and a brief's `brief_incorporated` mark both write a discovery item from another phase's session. The session's cadence commit is its heartbeat |
| `topic cancel` · `topic postpone` | never beats, and **clears the calling session's own row** on each item it cancels — the cancel is reachable from inside the conversation it closes, where a hold left behind would read held to every peer until the process died. A row this session does not own is left standing: a peer still sitting in the topic releases at its own exit |
| `topic reactivate` | never — analysis and navigation actors reaching across topics |
| every other verb — `manifest push`/`pull`/`delete`, the task verbs, every read, render and projection | never |

A beat is best-effort: it never throws, never changes a verb's response, and no-ops on a phase outside the set or a work unit with no directory, the product's conversation aside.

`beat` and `clear` remain as explicit verbs for tests and repair; a beat with no `CLAUDE_PID` in the environment refuses rather than write a record nothing can hold. `scan` reads every heartbeat with one verdict per row: `held` — the owning process still runs (pid + start time verified, so a dead or recycled pid reads unheld instantly; a record beaten where the start time could not be read falls back to bare aliveness, and so does a start time that cannot be read at the scan — `ps` failing to spawn, answering nothing, or not answering within ten seconds proves nothing, so a pid that is there counts as alive; a record with no identity cannot be verified and is never held), however long it has idled. `age_seconds` is the time since the row's last write — rendered as "last active {age} ago" wherever a hold is named, never a verdict. With a work unit: `{"work_unit": "…", "held": N, "held_sources": N, "sessions": [{phase, topic, age_seconds, held, session_id, pid, checkout}…]}`, plus a `DISPLAY: presence deferral` section after the JSON line when a **source** session is held — emitted verbatim per its marker at the same call, wherever an analysis defers. `held_sources` counts the discussion rows alone: the analyses read the settled source record — a laboratory session never touches those corpora — so a held experiment or code session never holds one up, and the caller's own rows are never counted — a session does not defer to itself; the callout names only the rows that do, each with its last-active age, and names `presence clear` for a holder that is wedged but alive. `checkout` is the work tree the owning session works in — presence lives in the one state every checkout shares, and the code gate reads only the rows of the caller's own checkout. With the argument omitted it walks the whole cache root, the read the code gate filters: the same row shape with `work_unit` on each row and `"scope": "project"` in place of `work_unit`, the `held` total but no `held_sources`, and no deferral section (a deferral always names the work unit whose sources it waits on). An *empty* work unit refuses loudly — that is an unset shell variable, not a request to widen the scope. `cleanup` is the exit sweep — the target of the settings-level SessionEnd hook the engine installs in the project's `.claude/settings.json` (a SessionEnd hook declared in skill frontmatter never fires), with the hook contract: session id from the argument or the hook's stdin JSON, never errors, and with no id at all it touches nothing. It drops every heartbeat the named session owns, across all work units, on the exits that keep the process alive (`/clear`, `/logout`) — rows that would otherwise read held until the process exits; a dead process's row reads unheld the moment it dies regardless, gitignored cache the next beat overwrites. Response `{"ok": true, "session_id": "…", "cleared": [{work_unit, phase, topic}…]}`. Consumers: the epic view marks held topics (menu strike-through, recommendation skip, in-session confirm gate); the topic-discovery dispatch defers while a source session is held; the conclude sweep leaves a held row's dirt alone and commits a dead session's leavings action-scoped.

```bash
engine presence beat <work-unit|product> <phase> <topic>
engine presence clear <work-unit> <phase> <topic>
engine presence scan [work-unit]                    # argument omitted: the whole project (an empty one refuses)
engine presence cleanup [session-id]                # the SessionEnd hook in .claude/settings.json; reads stdin JSON when no argument
```

**`session`** — tmux session labels, the opt-in "where am I" rename. Every place labels itself on arrival, so leaving a place is never an event: `label <name> <phase> <topic>` renames the user's tmux session to `{original} · {work-unit} · {phase} · {topic}` (topic collapsed when it equals the work unit), called by every process skill at Step 0 — a phase entered through `enter` labels through it instead (**`enter`**) — and by discovery at its run step; `label <name>` alone applies `{original} · {name}` — the navigation skills and the bridge on arrival at a work unit, the roadmap and the product's conversation with their own identity (`roadmap`, `product`: project-level places with no work-unit directory and no phase); leeway puts the original back through boot's `repair`. The rename is a display courtesy, never state: with the feature off, outside tmux, on any tmux error, or on a failed stash write it renames nothing and answers so (`{"ok": true, "labelled": false, "reason": "disabled"|"no-tmux"|"tmux-error"|"stash-error"}`), never blocking a flow, and the position is recorded all the same (see `conversation`) — only a bad argument fails loudly, an authoring bug: a phase without its topic is a usage error, and an unknown phase, an illegal name, or a name that is neither a work-unit directory nor a project identity refuses too — each whatever the opt-in, before anything is labelled or recorded. The original name is stashed in the state's cache (`.leeway/.cache/.session-labels/`, keyed by tmux socket + session id) and written before the rename, with the owning Claude process's identity (pid + start time). The stash key is not stable — tmux ids renumber when the server restarts, and name-restoring setups carry a still-labelled name across under a new id — so the original always resolves by exact applied-name match across the socket's records, chained (a record written against a stranded label carries that label inside its own `original`) and consuming the spent links: phase hops and restart-stranded labels both recompose from the true original and never compound suffixes. Only a name matching no record is adopted as the user's own — a manual rename becomes the new original at the next label. `cleanup` restores the original name — one command of the settings-level SessionEnd hook the engine installs in the project's committed `.claude/settings.json` (`node "$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR/.claude/skills/leeway-engine/scripts/engine.cjs" session cleanup`, beside `presence cleanup` and `conversation end`; a SessionEnd hook declared in skill frontmatter never fires, so no skill declares one), with the hook contract: session id from the argument or the hook's stdin JSON, never errors, and with no id at all it touches nothing (an id-less sweep could take a live peer's label). It sweeps stashes the named session owns (an ownerless stash counts) plus any whose owning process is dead; a session is renamed only when its current name is exactly one we applied — found by the stash's id or, after a renumbering, by exact name among the socket's live sessions — and always to the chain-resolved true original. A manual rename is never clobbered; a restored or inapplicable stash is dropped, one whose rename failed is kept for the next sweep, as is a link a live session's name still chains through. Response `{"ok": true, "restored": bool}`. Every `label` call also records the conversation's **position**, labels on or off (see `conversation`), and it outlives the session: `cleanup` restores the name and leaves the position. `resume` brings the label back on `claude --resume` — one command of the settings-level SessionStart hook (`{"matcher": "resume", "hooks": [{"type": "command", "command": "… session resume"}]}`, present while labels are on) — under the same hook contract (session id from the argument or the hook's stdin JSON, never errors, nothing without an id) and `label`'s own gates: with a position for that id it re-applies it through `label`'s path (the stash records the resuming process's identity; a name already worn costs no rename); a position naming a work unit or phase this project does not have — resumed in another clone, or the unit gone — labels nothing and is kept, its lifetime its folder's; and no position, disabled, or outside tmux answers nothing. Response `{"ok": true, "resumed": bool}` **on stderr with stdout empty** — a SessionStart hook's stdout is injected into the resumed conversation as context. `repair` is boot's pass over the current terminal — leeway is the place whose label is the original name, and no position: first, labels on or off, it drops the calling conversation's own position (a dead owner's stays for its resume); then, when the session's name is a label this session applied (its session id, or its pid for a later conversation in the same process — presence's ownership rule) or one whose owner is dead — a session that never restored, a restart that carried the label across — it puts the chain-resolved original back, then prunes dead-owner records nothing live still needs (a record on an unreachable server is kept: an unverifiable name proves nothing). No position ages out: one goes with its conversation's folder. The label pass is gated exactly like `label` — disabled, no-tmux, and tmux-error all no-op — and a peer's label whose owning process still runs is live, never stripped. Response `{"ok": true, "repaired": bool}`. `label-config` records the opt-in as the project manifest's `defaults.tmux_labels` (sibling defaults preserved, under the project lock; a manifest that does not parse refuses loudly, never replaced), syncs the session hooks in `.claude/settings.json` — SessionEnd's `session cleanup` and SessionStart's `session resume` present iff `true`, SessionEnd's `presence cleanup` and `conversation end` and the three PreToolUse guards (`layout guard`, `brief guard`, `shell guard`) always; each event reconciled on its own, merged beside whatever the file already holds (permissions, other events, sibling groups and their matchers) and keyed on the hooks' identity, so a second sync never adds a twin, never rewrites a recognised hook, and a removal takes ours alone. It makes two commits under one message, `chore: record session-label choice`: the project manifest, a state commit on the state branch; and the settings file on the branch checked out, as boot commits its own change to it — only where the workflows' change is the file's whole difference from the branch, so a change an earlier call left uncommitted is committed now, one beside the person's own edits in the file is held back with a warning, and an ignored file is never committed (answering `no` on a project whose boot already installed the workflows' own hooks commits the manifest alone). The hooks are not written while git holds a merge, cherry-pick, revert, rebase or bisect open in the code tree (`session hooks not written: {operation} is in progress in the code tree — the next boot writes them`). Response `{"ok": true, "tmux_labels": bool}`; a settings file that does not parse leaves the hooks untouched (`session hooks not synced: …`), and a commit git refuses leaves the files on disk — the choice is recorded either way and the response gains a `warnings` array. An absent value means never asked — no system config holds it and no migration carries it, so every project re-asks. Boot reports the state as `tmux_labels`, runs `repair` (`label_repaired`), and re-syncs the hooks every start (`session_hooks_installed` when the file changed) — `repair`'s stranded arm stays the backstop for the first session after opt-in (a settings hook takes effect from the next session, since Claude Code snapshots hooks at startup), for crashes, and for a tmux server dying.

```bash
engine session label <name> [<phase> <topic>]       # the name alone on arrival — a work unit, or roadmap|product; with phase + topic inside a phase
engine session label-config <true|false>            # records defaults.tmux_labels on the project manifest; syncs the session hooks
engine session repair                               # boot's pass over the current terminal — this session's own label, or a stranded one, comes off
engine session cleanup [session-id]                 # the SessionEnd hook in .claude/settings.json; reads stdin JSON when no argument
engine session resume [session-id]                  # the SessionStart hook (matcher resume); reads stdin JSON when no argument; answers on stderr
```

**`conversation`** — the conversation's folder, `{config}/conversations/{session-id}/` beside the system config (`LEEWAY_CONFIG_DIR`, else `~/.config/leeway`): what belongs to one Claude Code conversation rather than to a work unit or a project, found by the session id Claude Code hands every command (`CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`) from wherever the command runs, the folder named by the id's safe characters alone. Each concern writes a file of its own: `workflow`, the mark that the conversation runs the workflows, which the gate mod reads to set the workflow harness, to say the phase in its spinner, to answer a compaction and to record each task's builder and reviewers — written once by every engine invocation that carries a session id (after the command, whatever its exit) and every gateway script (`runGateway`), only where the command runs in a workflows project (its repository holds the state, a code worktree's included), and never by the commands Claude Code's own hooks run (`presence cleanup`, `session cleanup`, `session resume`, `conversation end`, and the three guards, `layout guard`, `brief guard` and `shell guard`), which run for every conversation in the project; `transcript`, written by `end`; `position.json`, where the conversation is working, written under `position.json.lock` beside it (below); `left.json`, the place boot left when it dropped the position, where that names a phase, which the next position recorded takes away and `board` reads for a unit's close (below, **`board`**); `gate-shown.json`, the gate it was last shown, which `gate` answers against (**`gate`**); `raise-shown.json`, the raises it was drawn, each sealed before the person answers (**`raise`**); `trust-pending.jsonl`, the trust-log events waiting to be appended — one an append failed on, or one a write keeps ahead of its ledger entry — which the conversation's next record and every boot append (**`gate`**, **`ledger`**); `briefs/`, the briefs it issued, each `{id}.md` beside `{id}.json` naming whose it is (**`brief`**); `gate.json`, `sent.json` and `pressed.json`, the gate mod's own, `pressed.json` naming the gate and the row a press answered, and when; `compacted.json`, the gate mod's record of every note it appended to a compaction, which the rows mod reads; `discussing.json`, the gate mod's discussion queue, `{"discussing", "queue"}` by question number — the question under discussion and those after it — which `raise` reads for the next its receipt names (**`raise`**); `rows.json`, the rows mod's own. `end` is the SessionEnd hook's target — one command of the settings-level SessionEnd hook boot installs in every project beside `presence cleanup` (`node "$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR/.claude/skills/leeway-engine/scripts/engine.cjs" conversation end`): it reads `session_id` and `transcript_path` from the hook's stdin JSON and writes the path to the folder's `transcript`, only where the folder exists — a conversation that never ran the workflows gets nothing — under the hook contract: it never errors, and an id or a path missing, empty or not a string records nothing. Response `{"ok": true, "recorded": bool}`. Every project's boot tidies every folder. The path is recorded as the hook hands it, and it does not say where the transcript is — a resume from another directory hands the hook one under that directory's project key while Claude Code goes on writing where the conversation began — so the tidy reads it only for the directory Claude Code keeps its projects in (`<projects>/<project-key>/<id>.jsonl`, a leading `~` the home directory) and looks for the transcript by its file name in every project folder there, reading each projects directory once. A folder goes once none holds it and no trust-log event waits in it — Claude Code has deleted the conversation, so its retention setting is the retention these records keep — and a projects directory that is gone holds none; one that cannot be read, a relative path, or no transcript at all keeps the folder.

**The position** (`domain/position.cjs`). `position.json` holds where the conversation is working — `{"name", "phase", "topic", "tasks"}`: a work unit or a project identity, its phase and topic, and inside a unit's build — its implementation, or its review phase, whose fix tasks the same loop builds (14.3.2) — every task in flight there (4.11.14, 24.2.4), each `{"task", "builder", "reviewers", "worktree", "stopped"}` — its `{phase}.{n}`, the agent id of its builder (null until one is recorded), those of its reviewers, the pair its later rounds go back to (13.3.3), the absolute path of the worktree it is built in, once `task worktree` laid it out (left out until then) — Claude Code makes each beside the others, so none holds another — and when its builder stopped, unable to build past a problem, once `task stopped` held it, until a builder's or fix round's brief for the task or a raise parking it takes it off (left out otherwise); `tasks` is left out where none is in flight. It is read through `domain/conversation.cjs` alone (`readPosition`; `tasksInFlight` for the commit door, the hook before Bash and **`verify`**'s attribution; and `taskAt`, the task in flight whose worktree holds the folder a call is made in), a task in flight, an agent id or a worktree that does not read as one left out. The engine is its one writer, only a place the project has and only for a conversation that runs the workflows — recording it marks the conversation first, as any engine call in a workflows project does — each write reading it and writing it whole under `position.json.lock`, so two recorded at once both stand; a write that fails never fails the verb it rides. Every `session label` and `enter` call records it, labels on or off — a re-label of the same place keeps every task in flight, a move between a unit's implementation and its review phase carries them, and any other place leaves none; the tracker's `task start` adds its task, with the unit as the topic, at the unit's implementation or its review phase where the conversation is at either, else at its review phase for a task of one of its fix phases and at its implementation for any other — re-run for a task already in flight, it keeps it as it stands, its agents among it — its `task worktree` records the worktree it laid out against it, and its `task done` and `task close` take it off, its agents and worktree with it, once the worktree's child repositories are taken apart; the start menu is no position, so boot drops the calling conversation's own before its migrations run — a boot that fails leaves none — keeping the place it left where that names a phase, which the next position recorded takes away, and `session repair` drops it too.
