The tmux label's `session resume` re-applies it, and the gate mod's spinner reads it. `builder <session-id> <agent-id> --brief <id>` is the gate mod's alone, run as the conversation's main loop dispatches `leeway-builder` or `leeway-reviewer`, the dispatch's pointer naming the brief: it records the agent id the dispatch answered with on that conversation's position, against the task the brief was issued for (**`brief`**) — a builder's brief, the fix round's among them, as that task's builder, replacing any before it; a reviewer's beside the reviewers already there; only where that task is in flight there, so anything else records nothing. An agent id of anything but letters, digits, `-` and `_`, or a brief id of anything but letters and digits, is a usage error. Response `{"ok": true, "recorded": bool}`. `position [session-id]` — the calling conversation's `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID` when no id is given, refused when neither is — answers what that conversation re-reads to carry on from its position, and writes nothing: the skill that runs the work there — a phase skill (`.claude/skills/leeway-{phase}-process/SKILL.md`), or `leeway-discovery`, `leeway-roadmap` — then the topic's documents: investigation its topic's log, `topics/{topic}/log.md`; discussion its log, `topics/{topic}/log.md`; implementation, once its build has begun, the build's log, `{unit}/build/log.md`; an experiment each live record's `problem.md`, `design.md` and `report.md`, in register order; discovery and the roadmap the open session's log — the product's conversation's for `product` and `roadmap`, at `.product/sessions/` — (discovery and `product` the latest on disk where none is open). It names only what the project has, each file by its absolute path under the project root the call runs in — with nothing else in context, a relative path invites a guess at the root — and never the start skill, a navigation skill or the bridge: a work unit's menu, like no position at all, answers that there is nothing to carry on from, and so does a place no longer open — the work unit missing from the state or not in progress, its spec concluded at the spec's place (`its spec is concluded`), a build's place whose build is not to be entered, in the entry's refusal (P12) — a quick-fix's review among them, until the person asks for one — the place's item closed (`cancelled`, `superseded`, `promoted` or `postponed`), or, in an experiment or a discussion, its topic cancelled or postponed on the map — and a place whose skill this project lacks. Two sections: `DATA`, the note the gate mod appends to the conversation's compaction — that the conversation was just compacted, where it is working (the work unit by its work type; in implementation each task in flight with its internal id, and its builder's and reviewers' agent ids where recorded — `on task 2.3 (internal id …), its builder agent …, its reviewer agents … and …` for one, `on 3 tasks: …; …; …` for several), then to follow the skill's "Resuming After Context Refresh" steps before anything else, re-reading the skill and its framework in full, then the documents in full; a discussion's note, discovery's, specification's, a build's and the review phase's carry their recovery whole instead, their skills keeping no steps for it (24.2.6, 24.2.8): re-read, before anything else, the skill and its framework, then a discussion's topic's record by running its `ledger record` call, or at an epic's discovery its map by running its `render discovery-map` call, the product's conversation running none, or at the spec — the unit's, whatever topic its place names — what its entry reads to carry it on: each live topic's `ledger record` call, the unit's own and, once cut, the `manifest get {unit} cut` call, then each topic's log, each file the unit's current entries link as canonical and, once written, `spec.md`, and while the person's review page is open, a line naming its address and the file its marks are read back to, the spec keeping no log of its own (**`enter`**), or in a build, what its entry reads to carry it on: the `ledger affected {unit}` and `ledger record {unit}` calls, the build's log, then where the build stands, a line each, as the entry's `DATA` gives them — or, where that cannot be read, one line saying why (**`enter`**, 12.1.6, 24.2.2) — a quick-fix's, what its arm reads: the `ledger record {unit}` call, the build's log, then its change and its fixes, a line each (**`enter`**) — or in the review phase, what its entry reads to carry it on: the `ledger record {unit}` call, each round's report, the spec and the build's log, then where the review stands, a line each, as the entry's `DATA` gives them, its fix tasks in flight those the position holds — or, where that cannot be read, one line saying why (**`enter`**); and the log where it is written — discovery's the open session's, else the latest on disk; catch the log up from the conversation's transcript from its last write onward, the transcript named where Claude Code keeps it, or, where it cannot be found, say so in one line and carry on from the log; where the log does not yet name the conversation, the line its first write opens with, and where another conversation has written since, the line its next write opens with again (**`enter`**); then carry on as if the break never happened — and `POSITION` (`json for the gate mod — never display`), one line of `{"session_id", "position", "place", "skill", "files", "text"}` for the gate mod: `place` the position as one line (`fumi › discussion › management-window`, the topic left out where it is the work unit; null with no position), `text` the note unchanged; `skill` null with `files` empty is the empty answer, nothing to carry on from.

```bash
engine conversation end                               # the SessionEnd hook in .claude/settings.json; reads the hook's stdin JSON
engine conversation position [session-id]             # what the conversation re-reads to carry on from its position — DATA + POSITION sections
engine conversation builder <session-id> <agent-id> --brief <id>  # the gate mod, as a builder or a reviewer is dispatched — records it against its task in flight
```

**`enter`** — the one call a phase is entered by (`domain/enter.cjs`), generic over phases: each phase that enters through it has a row of its own — its level, at a topic or at the work unit; how it stands; whether and how it starts; the questions it raises first; what it reads; and the log its conversation keeps, where it keeps one — discussion's and a bugfix's investigation's at a topic, discovery's at the work unit or the product, and specification's at the work unit; the other phases keep their own entry calls until each moves onto it. A unit-level row takes no topic and is entered after the spec as before it; its place — its position, label and presence — is the unit itself, as a single-topic unit's topic is, and its response carries no `topic`. **It refuses** what cannot be entered, each refusal naming the way on, before anything is written: a phase with no row, no unit, an empty topic, a flag but `--take` and `--move`, a flag twice, `--move` at a row that takes no move, or an extra argument is a usage error; a unit this project lacks (`there is no work unit "{unit}" — the start menu lists the work in progress`), one cancelled (`… reactivating it comes first`) or completed (`… new work starts from the start menu`); a phase the unit's work type has no place for, naming its phases — every phase, discovery among them, for a migrated unit of a type the reshape retires, which waits on the migration's cut (`{unit} is a {type} unit, a type the reshape retires — it waits on the reshape's 29.17 migration, and nothing is done in it until then`); and a topic named at a unit-level row (`{phase} is entered at the work unit — name no topic: engine enter {phase} {unit}`). A topic-level row refuses an epic named without a topic; an illegal topic name and a topic the unit lacks, each naming where its topics are — the epic's map, or a feature's topic the unit itself; and any topic once the unit is past its spec — its `spec.md` written, or its build begun (`{unit} has its spec — a topic exists only before it, and a change is settled in the spec from here`), the one rule every door the spec closes reads (`domain/spec-standing.cjs`). Discussion's row refuses a topic cancelled — reactivating it goes through the epic menu's discovery row, or manage on the start menu — postponed — pulling it forward goes through the same row while its roadmap item waits, and once another epic has taken the item it stays postponed with no way back — promoted (`… it carries on there`, naming where), or decided (`… it reopens through a message sent to it, or through` that row). A feature's topic is the unit, named or not. Investigation's row is entered at a bugfix's one topic, the unit, as a feature's discussion is (19.4.3), any other type refused as having no investigation; it refuses the unit past its spec, as every topic-level row does, and an investigation concluded, the unit's work going on where its menu leads (`the topic "{unit}" is concluded — its work goes on in {phase}, from the start menu, and a message sent to it reopens it`). Discovery's row is entered at an epic or at `product`, the product's conversation, which has no manifest of its own and takes no topic (`discovery is entered at the product — name no topic: engine enter discovery product`), its position and label the project place `product` and its presence `product/discovery/product`; it refuses a unit of any other type (`{unit} is a {type} — its discovery ended at start this work, and reopening its decision goes through manage on the start menu`) and an epic past its spec (`{unit} has its spec, so its map is closed — talk about it belongs in the product's conversation, where what comes of it lands as roadmap items: engine enter discovery product`). Specification's row is entered at the unit, whose one spec it is (`domain/spec-entry.cjs`; the spec's standing and its refusal `domain/spec-standing.cjs`), once its topics are settled — a live topic decided and every live topic decided, a cancelled or postponed topic being no longer live, a feature's topic once it has closed into its spec — decided's checklist closed, neither voided nor taken since (**`decided`**'s `stage`) — and a bugfix's one topic once its investigation has concluded (11.2.1, 11.8.5, 18.5.1); it refuses an epic with a topic still open, naming each by the name it goes by (`{unit}'s spec waits until every topic is decided, cancelled or postponed — still open: "{topic}", …; work them from the epic's menu, or postpone them to the roadmap`), an epic with no live topic, its map not yet shaped or every topic set aside (`{unit}'s spec waits until a topic is decided — none on its map is live: shape the map in its discovery, or pull a topic forward from the roadmap`), a feature whose topic has not closed (`{unit}'s spec waits until its discussion closes, nothing of the person's open and its closing pass run — its discussion carries on: engine enter discussion {unit}`), and a bugfix whose investigation has not concluded (`{unit}'s spec waits until its investigation concludes — the investigation carries on, from the start menu`); a quick-fix's pipeline holds no specification. Implementation's row is entered at the unit whose build it is (`domain/build-entry.cjs`) once ready to build's yes has begun the build, a quick-fix's by an arm of its own (below); it refuses a unit built under the old plan, its old spec's, planning or implementation items standing on its manifest — those a quick-fix's old scoping made among them (`{unit} was built under the old plan, and its build is entered once the reshape's 29.17 migration has cut it`) — and a build not begun (`{unit}'s build has not begun — ready to build's yes begins it: render ready-to-build {unit}`). The review phase's row is entered at the unit as the build's is (`domain/review-entry.cjs`), refusing what it refuses, a quick-fix, whose review runs only once the person asks for one at its close until `review ask` records the request (`{unit} is a quick-fix, whose review runs only once the person asks for one at its close — render finish {unit} draws it`), and a build not finished, naming each phase of the cut not closed (`{unit}'s review phase starts once every phase of its cut has closed — phase {N} of {M} has not: enter implementation {unit}`, `phases {N}, {N} of {M} have not` for several) — `buildFinished`, the one rule (14.6.1) — and, in step, a phase of the cut whose stack waits at its merge gate, naming the lowest (`{unit} builds in step, and its review phase is entered once every phase of its cut has merged — phase {N} waits at its gate: render phase-merge-gate {unit} {N}`, 7.2.4); the review phase's verbs and `inbox file` refuse as it does. **It asks first** where another live session holds the place — its presence row held by a running process that is not the caller's (18.4.21): the answer is the question alone, `MENU: held topic gate`, naming when that session was last active, what proceeding means and how to release a wedged session's hold, as the epic menu's in-session gate words it; nothing is started, recorded or beaten, and the response is `{"ok": true, "phase", "unit", "topic"?, "entered": false}`. Its rows: `n/no`, recommended, leaves it to that session — its `next` the handoff to the epic's menu, or for a single-topic unit or the product's conversation, which have no menu, `continue`, the conversation's turn ending there — and `y/yes` proceeds, its `next` the entry again with `--take`, a move kept a move, which enters without asking, its presence beat taking the hold over. Implementation's and the review phase's ask before that where another session holds the checkout's code — a live implementation or review presence row in this checkout not the caller's, whatever unit or topic it sits in (12.7.8): the answer is the code gate alone, `DISPLAY: code gate` and `MENU: code gate`, naming each such session and when it was last active, nothing written and the response as above; `b/back`, recommended, leaves it to that session — its `next` as `n/no`'s — and `y/yes` is the entry again with `--take`. **It starts what the row starts** where it is not yet started — discussion's topic, and a bugfix's investigation, by the start **`topic`** names; discovery's session where its home has none open, its next log made empty and its marker set; specification's starts nothing and keeps no log, writing nothing but the place, and implementation's the same, the build's log being Claude's to append, a log of the layout's that every state commit carries — and commits the start (`{phase}({unit}): start {topic}`; discussion's entry at a feature's topic closed into its spec voids the close as well, the discussion going on, and commits the manifest (`discussion({unit}): carry on {topic}`) — an epic's topic re-entered between its close and its gate keeps the close, its gate reading what was opened since from the ledger; or `{phase}({unit}): start` at the unit or the product), holding the writer lock around the manifest write — the product's, the project manifest — and the commit alone; it writes nothing ahead of the opening — the log is the conversation's to write, discovery's opening on the conversation's line (below). A commit git refuses leaves the start on disk with `committed: null` and a `note` naming the retry (`engine commit {unit} --state`, or `engine commit --product`). **It moves between levels** with `--move`, at discovery's row (8.2.11, 8.2.12, 24.1.8): the conversation carries on, in the same session and with no handoff, from where its position says it is working — the product's conversation, an epic's discovery, or a topic's discussion moving up to its own epic's discovery or to the product's conversation. Leaving a discovery home appends the pointer to the log entered at the end of the home's open log, `→ Carried on in {home}: [session-NNN.md]({path from the log})`; leaving a topic takes its log, the sitting, into the move's commit; and the calling session's own presence row where it leaves is cleared. The move is committed with any start, under the writer lock (`discovery({unit}): carried on from {place}`, the place as one line, `fumi › discussion › licensing`). It refuses, writing nothing, a conversation with no position (`a level move carries this conversation on from where it is working, and it holds no place — enter without --move: engine enter discovery {unit}`), one in the home entered already (`this conversation is in {home} already — carry on there`), a topic moving to another epic's discovery (`a topic's talk moves up to its own epic's discovery or the product's conversation — "{topic}" is a topic of "{unit}"`), and one working anywhere but discovery or a topic's discussion (`a level move carries the conversation on from discovery or a topic's discussion — this one is working in {place}; enter without --move`). **It records** where the conversation is working — the position, the tmux label and the place's presence, as `session label` does (**`conversation`**, **`session`**, **`presence`**). Everything else takes no lock: the refusals, the question and the reads run while another writer holds it. Response `{"ok": true, "phase", "unit", "topic"?, "entered": true, "started", "committed"?, "note"?, "warnings"?}`, `started` true where this entry started it, `committed` only then or on a move. **It answers in sections**, read before the first question: any question owed first, each in the section it is drawn in (2.10.3) — discussion's, `DISPLAY: waiting mail` while messages wait in the topic's mailbox, each by its title over the rest, raised at every entry until `topic answered` clears it — then a `DATA` of `key: value` lines naming what to read, as absolute paths and engine calls, never their contents. Discussion's, in order: `mail`, each message waiting in the topic's mailbox (`{unit}/topics/{topic}/mail/NNN-{slug}.md`); `brief`, the topic's discovery brief where one is written; for a unit's one topic, `discovery log`, each of discovery's session logs, and `seed`, each of the unit's seeds; `decided`, the `ledger record` call of every other live topic of the unit decided so far — one promoted among them, its entries staying under the unit — never its log; `product`, the `ledger product {unit} {topic}` call — the product record, and what discovery found of the product and of the unit, which no topic's record shows, and of the topic, which a first opening reads no record for; `spike`, each of the unit's spikes; at a first opening, where the topic was postponed in another epic and pulled here — the map row's `prior` for an epic's topic, the `postponed_from` of the roadmap item joined to a one-topic unit — the record it left there, read as input and never changed: `earlier brief`, `earlier record` (its `ledger record` call), `earlier log` and `earlier mail`, each message in its mailbox, the files where they exist; and at a first opening of a unit's topic a move carried work to (**`workunit`**'s `move`), what it carried, read as input and never changed, each where the move carried it: `earlier record`, the topic's own `ledger record` call, where lines were carried to it; `earlier log`, a bugfix's investigation log set aside; `earlier build log`, a quick-fix's build log set aside; `earlier summary`, its fixes' short records, `summary.md`, set aside; `kept branch`, the branch a moved quick-fix's commits were kept on, one in each repository that kept one; and `kept work`, each fix's work its park set aside and the move kept, a line for each repository holding it, naming its ref and the calls that show it and bring it into the checkout; on resume, `record`, the topic's own `ledger record` call, `map`, its `ledger map` call, and `affected`, its `ledger affected` call — the entries a replacement changed under it, each confirmed or replaced in the conversation; and of the background agents the topic was left with untaken — each dispatch row names the process and conversation that dispatched it (**`agent`**), and a live peer's are its own — `landed`, each whose report is on disk, by the report's path, and `lost`, each whose report never landed and whose dispatching process no longer runs — a conversation resumed after a crash keeps its session id, never its process — by its agent id. The engine never re-dispatches one: the skill closes a `lost` row through `agent incorporate`, or it is answered again at every entry. Investigation's asks about waiting mail first, as discussion's does, and reads, in order (8.7.7): `mail`, `brief`, `discovery log`, `seed` and `product` as discussion's for a unit's one topic; on resume, `record` and `map`; `landed` and `lost`, the agents the investigation was left with; then `log`, the topic's log, `{unit}/topics/{unit}/log.md`, the investigation itself (19.4.3), and `log header`. Discovery's asks nothing first (8.8.12) and reads the home as it stood before anything was written. Specification's asks nothing first: `record`, the `ledger record {unit} {topic}` call of each live topic of the unit — a single-topic unit's own — then the unit's own record's, `ledger record {unit}`, the gaps asked at the spec and their answers; `topic log`, each one's log on disk, a bugfix's its investigation, its topic's log (`{unit}/topics/{unit}/log.md`); `asset`, each file the unit's current entries link as canonical (9.10.5, 11.6.3), once, by its path in the state — a reference link, or one made where it is set aside, names none; once the spec is written, `spec`, `{unit}/spec.md`; once the manifest holds a cut, `cut`, the `manifest get {unit} cut` call; while the person's review page is open (**`spec`**, `published`), `review page`, its address, and `read back`, the file Claude reads its marks back to, `.cache/{unit}/specification/read-back.json`; while a candidate of the sweep's waits on the person (**`sweep`**), `approve the map`, the `render approve-map {unit}` call, which reads back the map after the sweep staged — Keep shaping's among it — or with `--sweep` where none is staged; `landed` and `lost`, the agents the spec was left with, as discussion's; and `running`, each review pass still running on a land, its report not yet on disk, by its id (**`agent`**). Before it reads, it abandons each review pass another conversation left in flight — a row whose session id is not the caller's — whose report is not on disk, as `agent incorporate` abandons one in flight, so a report landing after is never taken in and the review dispatches afresh; a row with no session id is judged by its process, as before. What its read of the ledger said on the way rides the response as `warnings`. Implementation's asks nothing first and is the build's recovery note too (**`conversation`**'s `position`, 12.1.6, 24.2.2). Before it reads, it puts in flight on the calling conversation's position, at the unit's implementation, every task the tracker holds in progress in a phase the build has written — the cut's, and the review's fix phases past it — as `task start` records one: a task already there kept as it stands; one another conversation left comes with no builder and no pair, which go afresh on its branch, that conversation's pair abandoned and its own record left as it stands; one at its step gate (below) waits on the person's go there, its builder never sent afresh, and one behind the task in flight waits for that task to be done, no builder going to it until then (12.7.9). Its `DATA`, where the build stands (`buildStanding`, below): `position`, `auto` or `step`; `phase`, a line for each phase of the cut, in order — `phase {N} of {M}, "{name}": {state}` — then for each fix phase the review phase has written past it, `phase {N}, "the review's fixes, round {k}": {state}`, each then, where the tracker holds its tasks, ` — tasks ` and each task by its id and where it stands, `not started`, `in progress`, `behind the task in flight`, `at its gate`, `done` or `closed`, or `parked on Q{n}, …` while a question waiting parks it; `fixes owed`, after a phase's line, a line for each fix its failed review owes, as the phase close's `phase-review-passed` reads it and in its words, which `land phase`'s refusal gives too — `phase {N}'s review failed, and no settlement answers it — write its fixes as tasks of the phase, then settle it, naming them: task settle {unit} --phase {N} --file <why> [--fixes <task>,…]`, or `phase {N}'s review failed, and task {N}.{n}, a fix of it, is {status}`, one closed adding ` — settle it again, naming the fixes that stand: task settle …`; `merge waiting`, in step, while a phase of the cut is closed and its stack waits at its merge gate — the lowest such phase — `phase {N} waits at its gate: render phase-merge-gate {unit} {N}` (7.2.4); else `finished`, once every phase of the cut has closed, `every phase of the cut has closed — the review phase is next: enter review {unit}`; `in flight`, each of the conversation's tasks in flight — `task {N}.{n}`, then its builder agent and its reviewer agents by their ids where recorded, else `no builder dispatched here — ` and where it goes: where a round its pair was briefed for is not reported whole, `round {r} of its review is not reported whole — reviewer {letter} is reading it here|is briefed again for it|'s report goes back to it, …: engine brief reviewer {unit} {task} --letter <letter>; its builder goes afresh once the round is read`, its pair's to read and never its builder's, the brief named only where a reviewer is to be briefed again; where its last round failed, `round {r} of its review failed — a new builder goes with its brief, then its fix brief, engine brief fix {unit} {task}`; where its last round passed, by how far it stands towards landing: its account taken, `round {r} of its review passed, its account taken — show its landing, render task-landed {unit} {task}, then land it, engine land open {unit} {task}` — a quick-fix's `push it to {unit}'s pull request, engine land open {unit} {task}, then end it, engine task done {unit} {task}`; one written that reads and not taken, `round {r} of its review passed, its account written and not taken — take it, engine task account {unit} {task}`; none, `round {r} of its review passed, its account not taken — ` and where its reports are read for their notes for later and the nits the task's commits do not yet fix: a quick-fix's in the project's checkout, `read its reports, {paths}, for their notes for later and the nits the task's commits do not yet fix, and fix those in the project's checkout, on fix/{unit}, through the door; then send its builder its brief for its account, engine brief builder {unit} {task}`; else in the worktree git's worktree list has the task's branch checked out in, `read its reports, {paths}, for their notes for later and the nits the task's commits do not yet fix, and fix those in {path}, where its branch is checked out; then send its builder its brief for its account, engine brief builder {unit} {task}`, or, where none has, `no worktree has its branch checked out, so send its builder its brief first, engine brief builder {unit} {task}, which lays one out and asks for its account; read its reports, {paths}, for their notes for later and the nits the task's commits do not yet fix, and fix those in that worktree before its account is taken`; else `its builder and pair go afresh on its branch`; one a question waiting parks, its agents where recorded, then `parked on Q{n}, … — it waits on the person's answer, and no builder goes to it until then`; one whose builder stopped, its agents where recorded, then `its builder stopped, unable to build past a problem — send its builder back in with brief builder {unit} {N}.{n}, or raise what stopped it to the person`; one behind the task in flight, its agents where recorded, then `behind the task in flight — it goes on once that task is done, and no builder goes to it until then` — a quick-fix's `once task {N}.{n} is done or closed, or a question parks it`; a bugfix's reproducing task that would stand at its gate while its bug is not proven at its tip, in progress, its agents where recorded, then `its account taken, its bug not yet proven — {why}`, `land open`'s refusal naming the series to run or the rework (**`land`**); one at its step gate, its agents where recorded, then `at its gate — it waits on the person's go there, the gate drawn again with render step-gate {unit} {N}.{n}, and its builder is never sent afresh`; `waiting`, each question waiting on the person, `Q{n} — {what it asks} — parks {its tasks}`, as `raise waiting` answers them; `affected`, the `ledger affected {unit}` call — the entries a replacement changed under the build since the spec concluded (11.9.1); `record`, the `ledger record {unit}` call; `build log`, `{unit}/build/log.md`, read beside the ledger (12.2.2); and `engine`, the engine the orchestrator runs, `node {path}` by its absolute path, for a call it makes from a task's worktree. What its read of the tracker and the ledger said rides the response as `warnings`. **A quick-fix's** implementation is entered by an arm of its own (`quickFixBuild`, `domain/derivations.cjs`), with no cut, no ready to build and no position (19.5.1, 19.5.4): it asks the code gate alone, as the build's does; its start is the fixes begun: their one phase, `phase 1, "the fixes"`, written where the tracker lacks it, as `task phase` writes one and in that commit (`task({unit}): write phase 1`), each fix its own task, then their beginning recorded on the manifest, `fixes`: `{"began"}`, the time, which the entry commits (`implementation({unit}): start`, answered as `committed`, the tracker's commit's warnings among the response's) and which the implementation's status reads — in progress once begun, completed with the unit (`fixesStatus`), the board's phase row with it; it puts in flight every task of the phase the tracker holds in progress, as the build's does; and its `DATA`: `change`, the unit's description; `seed`, each of its seeds; `tasks`, each fix by its id, its title and where it stands, as the build's phases name them (`1.{n} "{title}" {state}`, comma-separated), `none yet` before the first; `summary`, `{unit}/summary.md`, once written; `record`, the `ledger record {unit}` call; `build log`; and `engine`, as the build's says it. Then, as the build's lines say them, each fix `in flight` with its builder and its pair, or the question its builder parked it on, and each question `waiting` on the person — a question Claude raises in the quick-fix's conversation is drawn there, parking nothing, so it waits only where the person set it aside (19.5.25); the review's fix phases, asked for at its close, number past its one (P14). It is the quick-fix's recovery note too, in the quick-fix's own skill: the `ledger record {unit}` call, the build's log, then its `change`, `seed`, `tasks`, `summary`, `in flight` and `waiting` lines (**`conversation`**'s `position`). **`buildStanding(cwd, unit, inFlight, warnings?)`** is where a build stands for one conversation, worked out at each read and never kept — `inFlight` that conversation's tasks in flight in the build, `[{task, builder, reviewers, worktree?}]` as its position holds them, which every caller names, the standing reading no session of its own; `warnings`, where given, gathering what its reads said — only the position stored, as the manifest's `build`: `{unit, position, phases: [{phase, name, round, relies_on, state, tasks: [{task, title, status, parked, after}], fixes_owed}], in_flight: [{task, builder, reviewers}], waiting, finished}` — `round` null for a phase of the cut; each phase of the cut `not yet written`, the tracker holding none of it (7.4.1), `written`, its start checklist not closed, `running`, `in review`, every task of it not closed done, `closed`, its close checklist closed, or `merged`, closed with its stack merged as `stack-merged` reads it (**`checklist`**); `in_flight` the tasks in flight it is given, of the conversation it is read for — the entry's the calling conversation's, the recovery note's those the position of the conversation it is asked for holds (`conversation position <session-id>`), never the calling process's; `status` the tracker's — `open`, `in-progress`, `done` or `closed` — or, in step, `behind`, a task going on beside the task in flight and held behind it until that one is done (`task start`'s one task at a time, **`task`**), or `at-gate`, a task at its step gate: in step, in progress, parked by no question, its pair passed and its account taken (`task account`) with nothing added to it since — its figures' `additions` the count the task still holds — until the person's go there and `task done` (12.7.9, 12.14.3); an addition after the account, whoever made it, the person's Comment at the gate among them, put onto the task before its builder goes again (13.11.3), or one of its calls decided against its lean, means the task changed under its account: it stands in progress while it is reworked, and back at its gate once its pair passes again and a new account counts it; `parked` each question waiting that parks the task, by its number; `after`, for a task not started, each task not yet done it is linked after (12.6.4), read whole only for a phase holding a task not started; `fixes_owed` the fixes the phase's review owes, each saying its way on — `phase-review-passed`'s own faults wherever every report of the count is in and read, one failed and the item reads open, none where the review is not complete or passed (**`task`**'s `settle --phase`); and `finished` once every phase of the cut has closed, so nothing ready with a phase not yet written never reads finished (7.4.3, 12.6.7) — the rule `phaseStatus` and `computeNextPhase` read off the manifest of a unit whose build runs by its cut, its implementation in progress until then — a unit built under the old plan read by its items alone (P12). Past the cut, each fix phase the review phase has written (P14) — round {k}'s numbered {k} past it, read to one past the rounds recorded, as a round's fix phase is written before it is recorded — follows, `name` `the review's fixes, round {k}`, `round` {k}, relying on nothing and owing no fixes: `written`, no task of it started, `running`, `closed`, every task of it not closed done, or `merged`, each landed; one not yet written is not listed, and `finished` never counts them. The review phase's asks nothing first; as the conversation arrives it puts in flight on its position, at the unit's review phase, every task the tracker holds in progress in a phase the build has written, as implementation's does — a fix task another conversation left among them — and at round 1 it snapshots the working tree outside `.leeway/` for that round — the round's number, each repository's HEAD, and each path its status lists with the fingerprint of what it holds — in `.cache/{unit}/review/tree.json`, where none is held; a later round's is taken as `review reads` opens it. The snapshot belongs to its round: what `review round` holds the tree to and drops once the round is recorded; it is never taken again while held — entering again takes none, so a tree changed before a `/clear` still stops the round — and only `review accept-tree` takes it again (14.1.5). Its `DATA` (`domain/review-phase.cjs`): `round`, `round {N}`, the round under review, or `the review phase {passed|was settled} in round {N} — finish is next`, `— its fix tasks are built, then finish` while that round's fix tasks wait, `— the notes its fix tasks' builders left are answered, then finish` once they are done and a builder's note stands; `area`, each of the spec's areas, `{n}-{slug} — §{n} {title}`, never merged (14.2.3); `reads`, while the review goes on, the reads the round takes by name, the test surface as `the test surface (tests)` — every area and the test surface at round 1, then those `review reads` named (14.3.3), or `not yet named — once round {N-1}'s fix tasks are done, review reads {unit} names them from what those changed, among them {reads}`, in step `fix tasks are done and their phase has merged`; `report in`, once the round's reads are named, each of its reports already written and reading as a reviewer's, by its path, so a return to the round briefs only the readers whose reports are not; `fix phase`, the phase a failing round's fixes are under way in, numbered past the cut as `review round` numbers it (P14) — while the last round's fix tasks wait, `phase {M+k}, where round {k}'s fix tasks are built`; else, while the round under review has its reads, `phase {M+N}, where round {N}'s fix tasks are written`; none while its reads are not yet named, nor once the review phase has ended and its fix tasks are done; `fix task waiting`, each task of the last round's fix phase neither done nor closed — its findings' fixes, and any task written there beyond them, as the tracker holds the phase — `{N}.{n} {not started|in progress|not in the tracker}`, which what comes next waits on, finish's `review-passed` among it; `merge waiting`, in step, once none waits, the lowest phase up to theirs whose stack waits at its merge gate — their phase while a task of it not closed has not landed, or one below it a switch from auto left unmerged, which merges first — `phase {N} waits at its gate: render phase-merge-gate {unit} {N}` (14.6.2, 7.2.6); `builder note`, each builder's note standing, `notes#{k}` (**`review`**), answered by the round under review, or once the round that ended the review has its fix tasks done by `review notes`, and `notes`, the notes' file, `build/notes.md`, where any stands; `set aside`, while the unit's set-aside holds anything, `{folder} — {copy}, …, kept there until the person removes them`, as **`review`** `restore-tree` sets them aside; for a bugfix (15.5.6), `unfixed series`, the build's series of its reproducing test on the unfixed code (**`verify`**'s `run … --unfixed`), `{scenario}, \`{command}\` — {failures} of its {n} runs failing on the unfixed code, the project at {commit}, {repo} at {commit}, …`, or `none — the build recorded no run of the reproducing test on the unfixed code`, then `unfixed run`, each of its runs' files, and, while round 1 is under review, `rerun`, its run on the unfixed code again — `not yet — round 1 is recorded once the test runs on the unfixed code again, where it must fail: \`{engine} verify run …\``, or `{n} since the review began, every one failing`, or `…, one passing — unfixed#1, a finding round 1 carries, so it fails`; `base`, `{branch} — the branch the unit's work lands on, the --base of each reader's brief`; `in flight`, each of the conversation's tasks in flight, and `waiting`, each question waiting on the person, as implementation's says them; `round report`, each round's report recorded; `spec`, the unit's `spec.md`; `record`, the `ledger record {unit}` call; `build log`; and `engine`, as implementation's says it. The product's conversation's: `session log`, each of its two latest logs; `roadmap`, the `roadmap state` call; `product`, the `ledger product` call — the product record, with what was found in the product's code; and `research owed`, `yes` while the project manifest's `brownfield_offer` is `accepted` (8.12.13), else `no`. An epic's: `older log`, each log before the two latest, by its path and its closing line — its last written line beyond the fixed lists `discovery-log` writes that is neither a conversation's line nor a pointer; `session log`, each of the two latest; `shaped by`, each place in the product's sessions its first log points at on a line opening `Shaped by: `, a link's target as an absolute path, its anchor kept; `map`, the map's `render discovery-map {unit}` call; and `product`, the `ledger product {unit}` call. Either, carrying on a log another conversation wrote last: `catch up`, that conversation's transcript to read from the log's last write onward — the path its line names, else where Claude Code keeps it — or, where it cannot be found, that it is gone, to say so in one line and carry on from the log (8.14.9); none where the log ends on a level move's pointer, the talk after it in the log moved to. Every entry's `DATA` but specification's, implementation's and the review phase's ends with `log`, the topic's log (`{unit}/topics/{topic}/log.md`) or discovery's open one, then, where the log's last conversation line is not the calling conversation's — it does not name it yet, or another conversation has written since — `log header`, the line its next write opens with: `## {date} · session {id} · transcript {path}` — the transcript where Claude Code keeps it, left out where it cannot be found — and none without a session id. Discovery's row writes that line into the log itself, at the end of what is there, so its `DATA` carries no `log header` (8.14.8).

```bash
engine enter <discussion|discovery|specification|implementation|review|investigation> <work-unit|product> [<topic>] [--take] [--move]   # refuses with the way on; asks first where a live peer holds the place, or for implementation and review the checkout's code (--take enters all the same); starts it where unstarted, records the place; --move carries the conversation on between discovery's levels; question sections, then DATA
```

**`layout`** — the layout on disk (`domain/layout.cjs`) and its one verb, `guard`, Claude Code's hook before Write, Edit and NotebookEdit (`domain/layout-hook.cjs`), its JSON on stdin, taking no lock. Boot installs it in every project's `.claude/settings.json` (**`boot`**), so it holds with or without the gate mod. **The layout** is one table of every place the workflows keep in the state, `.leeway/`, each named from the state root and one of four kinds: a file; a free folder, its contents named by whoever fills it — `.cache/`, `.knowledge/`, a unit's `assets/` and `seeds/`, a spike, an experiment's `data/`, the product's `spikes/` and `assets/`; a file only the engine writes — the project manifest and each unit's, the ledger, a unit's `spec-review.json` and `sweep-taken.json`, a quick-fix's `summary.md`, which only `quick-fix record` writes, its notes for later, each task's `figures.json` and the settlement of its failed round, and a verification run, `{unit}/build/runs/{scenario}/run-NNN.json`, which only `verify` writes; and a log — the product's and discovery's sessions, a topic's `log.md` and the build's. A reviewer's report is named by its letter, one per reviewer of the count (`kernel/reviewers.cjs`), and an area's report by the area's number and slug too, `{n}-{slug}-{letter}.md`, so no area's report is named as the tests' (`tests-{letter}.md`) or a phase review's (`review-{letter}.md`). The tracker's store is the task driver's to name: the files it keeps on the state branch. Beside those sit today's places, the paths the engine and the skills still write until each phase's rewrite moves its writer — a unit's `.state/`, the roadmap's notes and `.state/environment-setup.md` — each naming the phase that retires it. **The hook** reads the target where Claude Code's tool would write it — its `..` from the text, its links followed, a code worktree's path into the main checkout's state among them — against the state of the project the target is in, its names in lower case where the filesystem ignores case. A file place's `.txt` beside it is its staging and passes as the place — an agent's two-step write, the `.txt` then renamed to the `.md`, since the harness blocks a report-shaped `.md` a subagent writes; a `.txt` beside no file place has none. It refuses on stderr with exit 2 a path the layout has no place for (`.leeway/{path} is outside the workflows' layout — the state holds only the places the workflows keep; scratch goes in .leeway/.cache/`), and a file only the engine writes, naming the verb that writes it where one does (`the ledger (.ledger.jsonl) changes only through the engine's commands — engine ledger ask|find|decide|replace|hold|move|merge`; the manifests `manifest set|push|pull|delete`, a unit's with `apply`; a run `verify`); and, once a unit's spec is concluded — `build` on its manifest — a write to its `spec.md` (`.leeway/{unit}/spec.md is {unit}'s concluded spec, never corrected — a later change replaces its entry on the ledger`). At user level it refuses, in the system config directory, every file whose bytes reach the trust log, a conversation's gate shown and the gate mod's last press, its sealed raises, every file in its `briefs/`, the brief hook's refusals, the settings file and the record of the person; and in Claude Code's projects directory, a conversation's transcript and a subagent's transcript and record, which the trust log reads the model from. Everything else passes, the hook saying nothing and writing nothing; input naming no file passes, and a stray argument is a usage error.

```bash
engine layout guard   # the hook before Write, Edit and NotebookEdit in .claude/settings.json; reads the hook's stdin JSON
```

**`ledger`** — the project's stream of questions, findings and decisions, `.leeway/.ledger.jsonl`: one event a line, written by the engine alone and never edited (`domain/ledger.cjs`, the events and their schemas `domain/ledger-events.cjs`). Every line opens with its schema version, its id, the event's name, its tags — the unit, the topic (none from the spec on, where everything is the unit's), the phase and the session — and the time the engine wrote it; a session's line says who made it, `by` (`person` or `claude`; a question also `review`), and `who_moved_whom` (`none`, `claude-moved-person`, `person-moved-claude`). The schema is at version 1. An entry's `task` is the task it was made for in the build (12.12.5), as screens show it, `{phase}.{n}`, null where it was made for none, so a task's calls are counted and listed from the ledger. A `parked` line — `{question, replaces, reason, cause, relies_on, task, number, call, rows_judged, why_asking, label}` — is a question the build needs the person for, its task parked (**`raise`**): what it is about, the question it answers or the decision a replacement would replace with its reason and cause, one and not both; what its answer relies on; the task it parks, null for a call made in no task, which parks none; its number in its unit, `Q{number}`, or, for a task joining a question already waiting, that question's; and the call sealed — Claude's call as the raise took it, the rows the one-decision check judged one rule, the decision point's reason, and the labeller's label, null where it gave none; a fact only the person holds in the same shape, as the ledger holds a fact's answer, with no lean — its call's `lean`, `why` and `sort` null, its rows any number, its label null, answering a question and never a replacement. It names no maker, is no entry and is never replaced. An `unparked` line — `{task, parks}` — is a task closed in the build, taken off the questions of its unit that parked it (**`task`**): the parks naming it it is taken off, by full id, those alone, so the task reopened and parked again parks again; tagged with the build's phase the first of them was made in; it names no maker and is no entry. A `rehomed` line — `{lines, files}` — is an absorb's carry, or a moved unit's (**`workunit`**): the lines made at the places a unit gave up, listed by full id, never none, carried to the place the line is made at, and each file those lines cite or link that the move took elsewhere, `[from, to]`, each named once; every read — each fold, each line it shows, and the files every commit of the ledger takes — takes a line it lists as made in that unit and on that topic — at the carry's phase where the carry is made in the line's own unit, a move re-typing it, and at its own where it takes it into another, an absorb — live while that place is, however the unit it came from stands, and each file it names where the move took it; a line carried twice — a quick-fix moved to a feature, then absorbed — is read where the later carry took it, each file it names followed through every carry's moves in the ledger's order; it names no maker and is no entry. An `asset` line — `{entry, path, status, into}` — links a file, by its path from the state root, to an entry: it gives the file a `status`, `canonical` or `reference`, records a lift into the project at `into`, a path from the project's root, or both, never neither, and the first link of an entry and a path gives its status. It names no maker, is no entry and is never replaced: for an entry and a path the latest live status stands, and apart from it the latest live lift, which no status after it undoes. Every path a line holds is written plain — relative, no part of it empty, `.` or `..` — so one file is one path. **Every read and write is checked first**: the ledger on disk must start, byte for byte, with the one the state branch last committed, and is read a line at a time, each line validated against the shape its version names; a line that is no entry, or repeats an id, stops every read and write, naming it, and one a newer release wrote stops the engine until it is updated — each such stop kept for its screen, `render ledger-stop`. **The write verbs** — `ask`, `find`, `decide`, `replace`, `hold`, `move`, `merge` (`domain/ledger-write.cjs`) — each append one event and commit the ledger on the state branch, pushed: the place as the engine's dot-path, `<work-unit>.<phase>.<topic>`, or `<work-unit>.<phase>` for an entry at the unit's level (`move` always names a topic); the links as flags naming entries by a prefix of their id, resolved as git resolves a short hash; for `ask`, `find`, `decide` and `replace`, `--task <phase>.<n>`, the task the entry is made for, taken at the build's places alone, `<work-unit>.implementation` and `<work-unit>.review` — elsewhere refused, and the entry's `task` null — while `decide` on a question waiting in the build, in any of its wordings, `move` and `merge` of it, to any destination, and `replace` of the decision a replacement waiting there would replace, or a `merge` naming that decision its `--duplicate`, are refused under the lock, naming its raise, `raise draw <work-unit> Q{n}`: the person answers it there alone (**`raise`**); and the content as one JSON object on stdin — `ask` `{"text", "by", "who_moved_whom", "cites"}`, `find` adding `"how"` in words, `"known"` (`measured`, `exploratory` — measured in the conversation with no experiment — `documented`, `spike` or `read`), `"command"` and its `"result"`, and `"product_wide"`, `decide` `{"text", "why", "rules_out", "product_wide", "sealed", "alternatives", "evidence", "given", "cites", "by", "who_moved_whom"}` — `given` `own`, `your-call` (the person handed it to Claude), `dont-care` (the builder's) or `carried` (carried over by the migration, who decided it unrecorded) — `replace` the content of the kind it replaces with what it `drops`, and `hold`, `move` and `merge` `{"by", "who_moved_whom"}`; `cites` is a list of `{"path", "section"}`, a file's path from the state root and a heading in it. Content never carries a link, nor anything the engine gives, and `rules_out`, `product_wide`, `sealed`, `alternatives`, `evidence`, `given` (`own`), `command`, `result` and `cites` may be left out. The person's decision is their own, never given otherwise, and a decision is given `carried` only while the migration runs (**`commit`**) — at any other time it would hide a call of Claude's from the trust log; a `measured` or `exploratory` finding records its `command` and `result`, on any finding the two come together, and `known` is never null on a finding written now. The unit must exist and be live, the phase discovery or one of its work type's; a topic exists only before the spec and must hold an item in the phase — past it a write at a topic is refused, `{wu} has its spec — a topic exists only before it, and a change the person asks for is settled at {wu}.specification` — and an entry of the unit is written only from the topic it stands on. A topic decided at its gate takes no question, asked there or moved onto it (`{said}: the topic "{topic}" is decided — a question reaches it as a message sent to it, which reopens it: topic send`, a move's ending `topic send, then the move again`, 10.2.7). A question asked on a topic, or moved onto one, while the topic's decided close stands voids the close (**`checklist`**), the unit's manifest riding the write's commit. What is found of the product as a whole, which no unit holds, goes at the product's place, `product.discovery` (`product` is no work unit's name): it has no manifest, takes no other phase and no topic, and is always live. Discovery, at the product's place or a unit's, takes `find` alone: every other write there — a raise's among them — is refused, `nothing is decided in discovery — carry it as input`, before anything is read, and a finding there is never product-wide, the product record taking a line decided in the first topic it bears on; the session is the conversation's own, `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`, and a write without one is refused. A decision answers a current question of its own unit; an entry is replaced, held or relied on only while current; a replacement's cause is never what it replaces, a `new-fact`'s being a finding and an `other-topic`'s a decision standing on another topic; only a question moves (to another topic of its unit, or to the roadmap as later, `--to roadmap:{item}`, an item the roadmap holds, which closes it where it stands) or merges (into a current decision, `--duplicate` naming another current decision that already answered it); `hold` confirms an entry still holds after the latest live replacement on the way of one it is held against; and `who_moved_whom` is held to the departure rule (`domain/ledger-verdicts.cjs`). A question an experiment of its unit still running measures is closed by the person alone, their own answer or one they hand Claude: Claude's own decision, move or merge of it is refused, naming the experiment. The engine sets a replacement's kind and the question a replaced decision answers, and, where the content leaves them out, the replaced entry's product-wide mark and who moved whom where the departure rule settles it; and a decision's `kind` and `hard_to_undo`, never content, from the labeller's label of its question (**The labeller**). **The one-decision check** (`domain/ledger-one-decision.cjs`): a decision `decide` writes, and one `replace` writes in place of a decision, is judged by a separate Claude call before the writer lock — its answer, why and what it rules out, nothing else. Several rules refuses the write, nothing appended, naming each rule and the question, so the writer splits it; a call that cannot run lands the entry `unchecked`, for the late check. **The labeller** (`domain/labeller.cjs`, 16.11) labels the decision's question beside the check: its call, a separate raw `claude -p` on Sonnet at effort high from an empty folder of its own, is started before the check, waited on once the check answers and cancelled where it refuses, so both verbs stay synchronous and a refusal never waits on it (`kernel/claude-call.cjs` `startClaude`); every call the engine puts runs in a process group of its own that ends at its limit, and leaves no folder, whatever ends the engine. It reads Claude's words alone: Claude's own call gives the question's wording, its answer and what it rules out; the person's decision, or one carried over, the wording alone. Its label — `{"labeller", "answers"}`, the model that answered by its full id and its twelve answers, true or false, in the order of `content/trust/questions.json` — gives the entry its `kind`, composed in code (16.11.3), and `hard_to_undo`, its tenth answer; a call that fails in any way leaves both null. **Every run of the check is logged** — here, at a raise's draw, a line for each row, and in the late check — one line appended to `{config}/decision-checks.jsonl`, across the person's projects: `{"v": 1, "at", "project_id", "project", "unit", "verb", "rules", "outcome", "model"}`, `verb` the one whose check it was (`decide`, `replace`, `raise` or `late-check`), `outcome` `one`, `several` or `unchecked` where the call could not judge, `rules` how many it found and `model` the full id of the model that answered (each null where unchecked); never the entry's text. An append that fails is a warning, and the check stands. The layout hook refuses Claude's writes to the log. **What the trust log is told**: Claude's decision, or replacement of a decision, logs a call, but one carried over — its `kind` and `label` the labeller's, its `sort` null; the person's replacement standing over a lean the entry it replaces held logs a reversal; any other write logs nothing — the event kept waiting in the conversation before the append and landed after it, so a kill between them neither loses nor doubles it. While the migration runs (**`commit`**) it is told nothing, and nothing is kept waiting. Everything the request alone settles is refused before the writer lock, so a malformed write never waits on a peer; under the lock the ledger is folded, the place's liveness and every link checked again, the entry appended — copied beside the ledger with the line added and renamed over, so a read meets it whole or not at all — and committed (`ledger({unit}): {event} {id}[ in {topic}]`). Every commit of the ledger — each write's, the late check's, an asset link's, an absorb's and boot's catch-up — takes the files the ledger's `asset` lines name with it, as additions and edits alone, never a removal; a ledger with no asset line commits the ledger alone. Then, the lock released, the knowledge base's copies of the ledger are rendered and indexed, a failure a warning. Response `{"ok": true, "id", "committed", "note"?, "warnings"?}` — `id` the entry's short id; a commit git refused is `commit pending`, the entry standing in the ledger, and the next ledger write's commit, or boot's, takes the ledger whole; a commit held while the migration runs is noted `held for the migration's commit` (**`commit`**). **The read verbs** (`domain/ledger-read.cjs`) answer a projection of the ledger as JSON, worked out when read and never kept, current entries only, every id short: `record <work-unit> [<topic>]` the entries standing in a unit or on a topic, each with the live entries it replaced folded beneath it, its confirmations and the person's keeps, and the files linked from it (`assets`, each `{path, status, into}`, its latest live status and lift, either null where none gives one), the moves and merges made there, and the entries standing elsewhere that were made there, that it relies on, or that answer its questions (`{unit, topic, live, questions, findings, decisions, moved, merged, other_topics}`); `map <work-unit> <topic>` a topic's questions as they stand — `open`, `answered`, `merged`, `later` (moved to the roadmap, `moved_to` naming it), `moved-away`, `elsewhere` or `question-set-aside` — each with its current answers, whose and how given, and an open one an experiment of the unit measures marked `waiting_on` that experiment, `{experiment, running}`, whose record names the question's full id as `question` (`experiment create --question`) — running, or concluded with `running` false, until the discussion writes the result; an abandoned one leaves it open (`{unit, topic, live, questions}`); `product [<work-unit> [<topic>]]` the product-wide findings and decisions, and apart from them, as `discovery`, what discovery found: the current entries standing at the product's place, given a unit at that unit's own level, and given a topic of it on that topic, their chain begun in discovery and none product-wide — a later replacement of one among them, shown where it stands (`{findings, decisions, discovery}`); `affected <work-unit> [<topic>]` the current entries a replacement they never saw changed under them, waiting there until confirmed or replaced (`domain/ledger-affected.cjs`; `{unit, topic, live, waiting, affected}`); `since <work-unit> [<topic>]` Claude's current decisions the place shows that no look on this machine has covered, none carried over, each with the files linked from it (`assets`, as `record` shows them) — what Claude spiked or read to make the call (`domain/ledger-since.cjs`); and `shown <work-unit> [<topic>]`, answering as `since` does and adding each call it showed to the unit's last-looked mark, `.leeway/.cache/.marks/{unit}.json`, off the state branch, under the mark's own lock. A read takes no lock of its own but `shown`, which takes the mark's; a read whose ledger check fails makes it again under the writer lock, once a landing a kill cut short is finished, before it stops; a decision the late check judged shows `late_check`. It refuses a unit the project lacks and a topic the unit lacks. **`late-check`** is boot's detached job (`domain/ledger-late-check.cjs`): each decision that landed unchecked, with no `checked` event naming it, is judged, then under the writer lock a `checked` event naming it and its rules, `one` or `several`, is appended and committed, one decision at a time; one the call still cannot judge stays owed for the next start. One check works at a time — a second finds the first's claim in the state's cache and answers `{"ran": false}`; else `{"ran": true, "checked", "several", "owed", "cannot"?, "warnings"?}`. A decision found holding several rules shows flagged wherever its record is read. **`carry <work-unit>.<phase>.<topic>`** is the migration's (`domain/ledger-carry.cjs`; the records directive, 079): a topic's whole record appended in one write, so a session that dies leaves the topic with all its entries or none, and refused while the migration does not run — the state branch has its first commit. The topic holds an item in the phase — discovery, discussion or investigation, the phases 079 carries a topic of, frozen with it — live or not, so a cancelled unit's topics are carried too, their entries not current; a topic an epic promoted to a cross-cutting unit is that unit's to carry. The record is read where it is composed in the state, `.leeway/.cache/migrate/records/{unit}/{topic}.json`, whichever checkout the session runs in: `{"entries": […]}`, each entry an `asked`, `found` or `decided` in the content its write takes, `who_moved_whom` left to the engine (`none`), with `ref`, a name unique in the record; its links by ref to an entry before it — a question's `part_of`, a decision's `answers`, a question of the record no other decision answers, and `relies_on`; a question's or decision's `map_item`, the old map's subtopic it carries; a question's `thread`, its research's thread it carries — each a name, or a list of names where one entry carries several; an open question's `raise`, `true`, a point no source settles, for the raises at the end; and `assets`, each `{path, status}`, a file the state holds, linked after the entry as `reference` or `canonical`. A decision lands `unchecked`, for the late check. The record is held to the directive's rules before a line is written — its check's own, so a record carried passes the check: every entry citing a heading its file holds once, in a file of the topic's own — its log, its brief, an experiment's record — or the unit's assets or `spec.md`, every subtopic of the old map and thread of the research still open carried, every import from the topic whose file the state holds linked — in a unit of one topic that is no epic, those from its discovery too — and an entry at least where the log holds anything — and a record breaking one is refused, naming each thing it breaks. Before the append, what it carried — the record as given, and each entry's ref with the id it was given — is kept at `.leeway/.cache/migrate/carried/{unit}/{topic}.json`. Both are frozen with 079: the directive's check rests on the carried file's record and on each of its ids standing as a line's `id` in the ledger, and on no other field of a line; a topic the ledger holds an entry on already is refused, its record carried once. Once carried, the verifier's findings beside the record and any refusal of it are cleared. The commit is held for the migration's. It refuses, carrying nothing, no session; argv of another shape; a place naming no topic, a phase 079 carries no topic of, a unit the project lacks, a topic with no item in the phase, a topic promoted. What the record's composer must answer is refused too, the refusal written beside the record, `.leeway/.cache/migrate/records/{unit}/{topic}.refused.md`, for the next per-topic agent's brief: no record composed, or a file holding none; an entry with no ref or one given twice, of another event, giving `who_moved_whom`, a link naming no earlier entry of the kind it needs, a question answered twice, a point to raise a decision of the record answers or `raise` given otherwise, a map item on a finding, a thread on any entry but a question, either of another shape, content its write refuses, an asset of another shape or that the state holds no file at, and a record breaking the directive's rules. Response `{"ok": true, "carried", "ids": {ref: short id}, "committed": null, "note"?, "warnings"?}`. The layout hook and the hook before Bash refuse every write to the ledger but the engine's; a raise lands its answer through these same writes (**`raise`**).

```bash
engine ledger ask <work-unit>.<phase>[.<topic>] [--part-of <id>] [--relies-on <id> …] [--task <phase>.<n>]   # a question; content on stdin; --task in the build alone, as on find, decide and replace
engine ledger find <work-unit>.<phase>[.<topic>] [--relies-on <id> …]                           # a finding
engine ledger find product.discovery [--relies-on <id> …]                                       # a finding of the product as a whole
engine ledger decide <work-unit>.<phase>[.<topic>] --answers <id> [--relies-on <id> …]          # a decision, the one-decision check first
engine ledger replace <work-unit>.<phase>[.<topic>] --replaces <id> --reason <new-fact|other-topic|changed-mind|was-wrong> [--cause <id>] [--relies-on <id> …]
engine ledger hold <work-unit>.<phase>[.<topic>] --entry <id> --after <id>                      # the entry still holds after that replacement
engine ledger move <work-unit>.<phase>.<topic> --question <id> --to <topic|roadmap:<item>>
engine ledger merge <work-unit>.<phase>[.<topic>] --question <id> --into <id> [--duplicate <id>]
engine ledger record <work-unit> [<topic>]
engine ledger map <work-unit> <topic>
engine ledger product [<work-unit> [<topic>]]  # the product record, and what discovery found of the product, the unit and the topic
engine ledger affected <work-unit> [<topic>]
engine ledger since <work-unit> [<topic>]
engine ledger shown <work-unit> [<topic>]      # as since, and the calls it shows marked looked at on this machine
engine ledger late-check                       # boot's detached job; never called by a flow
engine ledger carry <work-unit>.<phase>.<topic>   # the migration's: a topic's whole record in one write
```

**`asset`** — spikes, and the files that inform a decision linked from the ledger's entries (`domain/asset.cjs`). **The verb never moves a file**: Claude writes it where it lives, and the verb records where that is. **`spike <work-unit|product> <slug>`** starts a spike: it numbers the unit's next one, or the product's, in the order spikes start, one past the highest of its `spikes/NNN-{slug}/` (`{wu}/spikes/` or `.product/spikes/`), makes its folder and its twin at the same path under the gitignored cache — where the spike's dependencies, builds and run output go — and answers both, `{"ok": true, "spike", "path", "folder", "cache"}`: `spike` the folder's name (`001-frame`), `path` its path from the state root, the one a link names its files by, and `folder` and `cache` absolute. A design spike's rounds go in `round-N/` inside it. Nothing is committed — the folder holds nothing yet — and the writer lock is held around taking the number alone. It refuses a slug that is no kebab-case slug, a slug one of its spikes has already (`… carry on in it, or name the new one apart`), a unit the project lacks, and one cancelled or completed. **`import <work-unit|product> <path> …`** lands the files the person shared in the unit's `assets/`, or the product's `.product/assets/`, each named by the import landing's filename rules (`domain/import-landing.cjs`: the stem lowercased with runs of other characters one hyphen, markdown-ish sources landing `.md` and any other extension kept and lowercased, a name taken suffixed `-2`, `-3` … against the folder and the call, each copy `0644`, a dotfile skipped), under the writer lock so two imports never take one name, and answers `{"ok": true, "imports": [...], "skipped_imports": [...]}` — each landed file's path from the state root, for the log or an entry to link, and each source the rules skipped, as given. Nothing is committed: a file enters the state with what links it. A path naming no file, or a folder, refuses the whole call before anything lands, naming each (`missing_imports`, the re-prompt's payload); so does a call whose every file the rules skip, and a unit the project lacks, cancelled or completed. **A link** is the ledger's `asset` event (**`ledger`**) from an entry to a file, by its path from the state root, written plain. Each names a current entry made in the unit, `--entry` a prefix of its id; is tagged as that entry is — its unit, topic and phase — and carries the conversation's session, `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`, a link without one refused; and commits the ledger on the state branch (`ledger({unit}): asset {id} …[ in {topic}]`), as every ledger commit does — with every file the ledger's links name (**`ledger`**). So a file enters the state when a link first names it, and every ledger commit from then on carries its edits and a linked folder's new files; a link whose commit was refused is caught up with the next ledger write's, or boot's; while the migration runs the files go with the line, held for its commit (**`commit`**). No ledger commit removes a linked file: a lift after the file moved into the product commits no removal. A file in `assets/` or a spike that no entry links is not taken by the ledger's commits — it stays untracked until a link names it, or a log that links it is committed (**`commit`**), and two clones can start the same spike number for spikes nothing links yet — but `engine commit <work-unit> -m`, which takes the whole unit folder, and `--workflows` and `--migrations`, which take the whole state, commit it as they find it. A path is stored as the file system spells what of it exists, so one file is one path where the file system ignores case, and a folder's without a trailing slash. A link belongs to its entry: a replacement carries none forward, so a file the replaced entry linked shows on no current entry until `add` links it from the new one — and `status` on a file only replaced entries link refuses, naming `add`. A link to a question is tagged at the topic the question was written on, so it goes dead where that topic is set aside after the question moved. **`add <work-unit> <path> --entry <id> [--status <canonical|reference>]`** is the first link of an entry and a file, and gives its status: the path must name a file or folder below one of the unit's homes — its `assets/`, or one of its spikes — or the product's, `.product/assets/` or `.product/spikes/NNN-{slug}/`, or a spike's folder itself (`{unit}/spikes/NNN-{slug}`), never the bare `assets/` or `spikes/`; one anywhere else (`… is not where an asset lives`, naming the homes), not written plain, or naming nothing on disk (`{path} names no file — save it there first, or correct the path`) is refused. A file in a spike is `reference` unless the status is said, as draft code is; anywhere else a first link without one is refused. An entry already linking the file is refused, naming its status — the status changes through `status`. **`status <work-unit> <path> <canonical|reference> [--entry <id>]`** changes the status — every current entry of the unit linking the file, or the one named, which must link it already; a file no current entry of the unit links is refused. **`lift <work-unit> <path> --into <path-in-project> --entry <id>`** records a linked file lifted into the project, at a path from the project's root that is there — read from the call's folder, so a call from a subfolder of the project reads it from there, and stored as the file system spells it: a link giving no status, so a later status leaves the lift standing; the entry must link the file already. An entry a link would give nothing new is passed over, and a call giving none anything writes and commits nothing (`committed: null`, `note: "nothing written — every entry named already gives the file that"`). The argv, the unit and the paths are refused before the writer lock, so a malformed call never waits on a peer; under it the ledger is folded, the entry resolved and checked, the links appended and the ledger committed, and then, the lock released, the knowledge base's copies — which show each entry's files — rendered and indexed, a failure a warning. Response `{"ok": true, "path", "links", "committed", "note"?, "warnings"?}` — `links` each link written, `{id, entry}`, both short; a commit git refused is `commit pending`, the link standing in the ledger for the next ledger commit, and one held while the migration runs is noted `held for the migration's commit`.

```bash
engine asset spike <work-unit|product> <slug>                                                  # the next spike's folder and its cache twin
engine asset import <work-unit|product> <path> …                                               # land shared files in its assets/ — nothing committed
engine asset add <work-unit> <path> --entry <id> [--status <canonical|reference>]             # a file's first link; a spike's file reference unless said
engine asset status <work-unit> <path> <canonical|reference> [--entry <id>]                   # every current entry linking it, or the one named
engine asset lift <work-unit> <path> --into <path-in-project> --entry <id>                    # a linked file lifted into the project
```

**`gate`** — the person's answer at a gate (`domain/gate.cjs`), logged to the trust log by the gate's name, answered with what the row leads to. **The gate shown.** Every MENU's gate is collected whether or not the gate mod announced itself (its GATE block is still emitted only where it did), and a command's output carries one MENU at most, whose gate is recorded for the calling conversation as `gate-shown.json` in its folder — the gate's name, its question, each row by its key with its word, its head, whether the engine recommended it and its `next`, each typed row by its key (its label's words, lowercase, joined by hyphens: `comment`, `1-4`) with its label and its `next`, and when it was shown. The engine's dispatch and every gateway script (`runGateway`) write it once the command has answered, never as the MENU is built, and never for a command that failed; nothing is written without `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`, a menu composed inside an illustration is never recorded, a command whose MENU states no gate leaves the last one standing, and a record that cannot be written never fails the command. It is written for a conversation not yet marked too, so a gate before the project has state is answered like any other, its event naming no project. **The name** is the one the MENU declares, up to its first ` — `: one gate is one name, however many entries share it (`in-session gate — 3` is `in-session gate`, and `gate` takes either), and no name is two menus' — a gateway names each of its menus from its own table of gates (`GATES`, `menuBlock(name, body)`); a name the trust log cannot key refuses the answer rather than being made to fit. **The answer.** `--pick <key>` a row; `--typed <key>` a typed row, such as a Comment; `--typed` alone free text naming no row. No typed text reaches the engine: it stays in the conversation, and the event says only that the answer was free text, its reading null until the separate call reads it. The name may be given quoted or as bare words. **How it came:** `pressed` only where the gate mod announced itself to the command and its `pressed.json` names this gate and this row, sent after the gate was shown; otherwise `typed` — with the mod off, always. The recommended row is the engine's, from the record. It refuses, logging nothing, with no session id, no gate shown, another gate's name, a key the gate lacks, and a gate already answered — the record marks the answer, and only the gate shown again takes another. **`next`** is what the row's composer declared — `{"kind": "engine", "argv": […]}`, `{"kind": "handoff", "skill", "args": […]}` or `{"kind": "continue"}` — null where it declared none and for free text; `gate` performs nothing of it. It takes no writer lock: it writes the trust log under that log's own lock, and the conversation's folder. While the migration runs in the project (**`commit`**), the answer reaches no trust log. Response `{"ok": true, "gate", "picked", "recommended", "via", "next"}`, `warnings` where the trust log could not be appended to and the event waits in the conversation.

```bash
engine gate "<name>" --pick <key>       # a row the person picked, pressed or typed
engine gate "<name>" --typed [<key>]    # a typed row, or free text naming none
```

**`raise`** — a call Claude has a lean on, answering a question or replacing a decision, put through the decision point and, where it asks, drawn for the person; or a fact only the person holds, answering a question, always drawn for them; and the person's answer to either, recorded by one command in the ledger and, for a call, the trust log (`domain/raise.cjs`, the screen `domain/projections/raise.cjs`, the decision point `domain/decision-point.cjs`). **The draw**, `raise <work-unit>.<phase>[.<topic>] --answers <question> [--relies-on <id> …]`, takes the place and the links as `ledger decide` does, since the answer lands as that decision, and Claude's call on stdin as one JSON object: `{"question", "rows": [{"key", "name", "text", "notice"}, …], "lean", "why", "sort", "mechanism", "product_wide"}` — the question as the person reads it, cold and product first; each row's key, a short name for what the person would notice, the answer's text and what they would notice; the lean, a row's key; why Claude leans so; `one` or `several`; the mechanism behind the screen's toggle; and whether the answer is product-wide (`true` or `false`). It may hold besides `"stepped_back"`, what Claude found stepping back from the problem before setting out its rows (19.4.13, 19.4.21) — a list of lines, one at least, drawn as WHAT I FOUND STEPPING BACK above `I lean:` and kept in the seal with the rest of the call; a raise in the build refuses it (`raise: a raise in the build takes no stepped_back — its call parks in the ledger, which keeps the call's own fields alone`), and so does a raise at an investigation's place, the fix direction's screen, while the root cause points at no current entry, as `investigation-log fix-direction` is refused; `investigation-log fix-direction` copies it from the seal as its line's body.
