Every other field is text on one line but the mechanism, which may hold several, drawn as written; no field beyond these is taken. **On a replacement**, `raise <work-unit>.<phase>[.<topic>] --replaces <entry> --reason <new-fact|other-topic|changed-mind|was-wrong> [--cause <id>] [--relies-on <id> …]` takes the place and the links as `ledger replace` does, since the answer lands as that replacement, and the same call: its rows Claude's proposed answers in place of the decision's, one at least, and its lean one of them. The screen adds a row of its own, `keep` — `Keep it as it stands`, its text the decision's answer as the draw found it, `nothing changes` — for the person to keep the decision. **It refuses**, drawing and sealing nothing: no `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`; no workflows state; a place or a link `ledger decide` refuses — `ledger replace`, on a replacement — checked by that code against the ledger as it stands and said in the raise's words; a question no longer open — answered, or merged into another's answer — whether or not the conversation holds its seal; an entry replaced that is no decision; stdin holding no JSON object, a field the call does not hold, lacks, or holds in another type; on a call, fewer than two rows — on a replacement, none; a key the trust log cannot key (letters, digits, `-` and `_`, up to 24), one two rows share, or one that is the screen's own word in any case (`t`, `e`, `technical`, `explain`, `comment`, and `keep` on every raise, so a raise event's `keep` always means a keep); on a call, a lean naming no row of Claude's; a proposal whose text is the decision's answer as it stands; a row holding several rules; and a seal that cannot be written. **The one-decision check** judges every row of Claude's at once, never `keep` — its answer, and Claude's other rows as what it rules out; the why comes with the person's answer — so the draw waits about one call; a row holding several rules refuses the raise, naming each such row and its rules (`payload.several`), and a row the call cannot judge is drawn all the same. The why is not judged with the rows, so every entry a raise lands lands `unchecked: true`, for the late check to judge whole at the next start: the seal's `rows_judged` records what the draw judged, and the answer makes no call. **The labeller** labels the call beside the checks, as `ledger decide` labels a decision, from its question and its rows, each its answer and what the person would notice; the label is sealed with the call — `label`, null where the call failed, and always for a fact — in the conversation's seal or on the park, so `raise show`, `raise draw` and the answer never label again, and a task joining a question waiting copies its first park's. **The decision point** answers `ask`, `proceed` or `blind`, with the reason the person is shown; until the ask rule is built every call asks, its reason the rule's own. A `proceed` lands Claude's call, drawing and sealing nothing: Claude's decision answering the question — the lean row's text, the other rows what it rules out, its why — through the writer lock and the write `ledger decide` takes, the question checked open under it — on a replacement, Claude's replacement of the decision through the write `ledger replace` takes, dropping the decision's answer as it stood — logged as a `call` event carrying the sort. A `blind` refuses, naming where it is built. **A fact**, `raise <work-unit>.<phase>[.<topic>] --answers <question> [--relies-on <id> …] --fact`, is a fact Claude cannot check and only the person holds — a deadline, an outside constraint, an account Claude cannot reach — taking the place and the links as a raise answering a question does, and on stdin `{"question", "rows": [{"key", "name", "text", "notice"}, …], "mechanism", "product_wide"}`: no lean, no why and no sort, each refused as a field the fact does not hold. Its rows may be none, one or more, each checked as a call's row is — its shape, a key two rows share, the screen's own words. Nothing in it is Claude's to judge or decide: the one-decision check judges no row, its seal's `rows_judged` empty, and the decision point is never reached, so a fact is always asked, whatever the decision point would answer, its reason its own, `Only you know this: it's a fact I can't check.` Its answer, a pick (`{"why"}`, who moved whom refused: it moves no one) or the person's own words (`{"text", "why"}`, with `"rules_out"` where Claude gives it), lands as the person's decision as any raise's does, but with `sealed` null and `who_moved_whom` `none`: no lean, so no departure, and nothing waits in `verdict queue` or shows in `ledger since`. A fact writes nothing to the trust log, neither its answer nor `--didnt-follow`, which still counts on the seal and leaves the raise open: it takes no label, adds no evidence and meets no threshold. `--fact` refuses given twice, and with `--replaces`: a fact that changes is the person's to say, and the decision it changes is replaced as any other. **The seal** is `raise-shown.json` in the conversation's folder, each raise by its id — `{"v", "id", "menu", "place": {"unit", "phase", "topic"}, "answers", "relies_on", "replaces", "call", "fact", "rows_judged", "label", "reason", "at", "answered", "didnt_follow", "parks"}`: the call as given, whether it is a fact, the question — null on a replacement — and the entries relied on by full id, the replacement — `{"entry", "reason", "cause", "keep"}`, the decision by full id, the reason, the cause and the `keep` row, so the answer needs nothing more; null on a question — the menu it is declared as, the keys of the rows whose answer and what it rules out the check judged one rule, the labeller's label (null for a fact, or where the call failed), the reason, when it was shown, its answer (null until given), how many times the person did not follow it, and the question waiting in the build it was drawn from — its first park, its number and the tasks it parks — null outside one (**`draw`**). The id is minted at the draw and is the id the raise's entry lands as. A raise of a question, or of a replacement of a decision, the conversation holds an open seal for — the same place, the same question or decision — draws that seal, whatever call it is given, so the answer is measured against the first lean, and the response says so (`redrawn`). A seal of another version is never matched and stays as written; a seals file that cannot be read refuses the raise. Every write to the seals — a raise sealed, answered, or not followed — takes a lock of their own beside the file, `raise-shown.json.lock`, reads them again under it and changes its own raise alone, so raise commands run at once in one conversation keep each other's; a draw finding an open seal of its question or replacement written while it waited draws that one, `redrawn`. The layout hook refuses Claude's own writes to the seal. **The screen** is one MENU, declared `raise — <id>`: where the question stands (`{Unit} · {Topic}`), the question, `I lean: …`, WHAT YOU'D NOTICE with a line per row, WHY I'M ASKING with the reason, THE MECHANISM where the toggle shows it, then `◆ Which way?` over the rows — on a replacement Claude's, then `keep` — the lean `(recommended)`, `t/technical` (show or hide the mechanism, leading to `raise show`), `e/explain` (I don't follow, leading to `raise answer <id> --didnt-follow`) and `Comment`; every line above the rows is the gate payload's statement. A fact's screen draws no `I lean:` line and no row `(recommended)`, WHAT YOU'D NOTICE only where it has rows, and asks `◆ What's the answer?`, its `Comment` row taking the answer in the person's own words. Claude's words are escaped, so they read as written, never as markup; each of the mechanism's lines is drawn from its first word. **A raise is no gate**: its menu is never recorded as the gate shown, `gate raise …` refuses, naming `raise answer`, and drawing one clears the conversation's gate shown. **`show <id> [--technical]`** draws a raise the conversation drew again from its seal alone — the id or a prefix of it — `--technical` showing the mechanism; it refuses a raise the conversation never drew — saying a raise is answered in the conversation that drew it, so after a `/clear`, or a resume under a new session, raise the question again — and no session id. Neither takes the writer lock: each writes the conversation's folder alone. **`answer <id> (--pick <key> | --own | --didnt-follow)`** records the person's answer to a raise the conversation drew, found by its seal — the id or a prefix of it. A pick takes `{"why"}` on stdin, with `"who_moved_whom": "claude-moved-person"` on a pick of the lean where the person came round to it; the person's own words take `{"text", "why"}`, with `"rules_out"`, a list, where Claude gives it; the why is Claude's account of the person's reason. Either lands as the person's decision at the id the draw minted, where the raise was drawn, answering its question and relying on what it relies on — `sealed` the lean row's text, `product_wide` the call's, `kind` and `hard_to_undo` the sealed label's, `unchecked: true` — a pick's text the row's and what it rules out the rows not picked, own words' text theirs and what they rule out Claude's list, else none. On a replacement either lands as the person's replacement of the decision instead, for the reason and the cause sealed, dropping its answer as it stood, a pick ruling out Claude's other proposals. **`--pick keep`**, on a replacement, takes `{"why"}` on stdin as a pick does: the person's `kept` line lands at the id the draw minted, where the raise was drawn — the decision kept, by full id, the reason and the cause sealed, `why` Claude's account of the person's reason, `sealed` the lean row's text, Claude's proposal — and changes nothing current: the decision stays current, nothing relying on it is affected, and `ledger record` shows the keep beside it, under `kept`. A live keep confirms the decision as `ledger hold` does against the replacement it names as its cause: where that replacement is the latest on a way the decision is held against, `ledger affected` no longer lists it for that way, until a newer replacement on it; a keep naming no cause, or a cause on no such way, confirms nothing. No `ledger` verb writes a keep. **Who moved whom is the engine's alone**: `person-moved-claude` for a pick other than the lean, for own words, and for a keep, which stands over Claude's proposal — a departure, waiting in `verdict queue` with the proposal as its lean; `none` for a pick of the lean, or `claude-moved-person` where stdin says so; any other value given refuses. Claude proposed the replacement, so no answer to it is a reversal. **The same command** logs the trust log's `raise` event naming the line it landed, an entry or a keep — the sort, the lean's key as `sealed` and the row's as `answer` (`keep` for a keep, null for own words), `via`, `with_text` (true for own words), `reading` and `blocked_build` null, `kind` and `label` the sealed label's, `didnt_follow` false — never a word of the question, the rows or the why: under the writer lock, the event kept, then the append, land and commit `ledger decide` takes, then the seal marked answered, `{"at", "answer"}`, with the row the ledger's entry holds. An answer was `pressed` where the gate mod announced itself and `pressed.json` names this raise's menu and the row, pressed after the raise was drawn; anything else was `typed`. **Once**: under the lock, a ledger holding the raise's id means it is answered — answered again, after a kill past the append or a compaction, it writes no second entry and no second event, finishes what a kill left (the event landed, the ledger committed), and says the raise was answered already, with the row the line holds — `keep` for a keep, else the row whose text the entry holds, null for own words — which marks the seal where it is not; the seal keeps its first answer. Killed before the append, the rerun lands its own answer, the event the killed run kept let go. Else the question must still be open. **`--didnt-follow`** takes no stdin and writes no ledger line: a raise event in the trust log alone — no entry, no answer, no reading, `didnt_follow` true — and the seal's `didnt_follow` counted; the raise stays open, for `show` and an answer after. On a raise the ledger holds the answer to — an old screen's press — it logs and counts nothing, and answers `{"ok": true, "raise": "<id>", "already": true, "answer"}`, the row the line holds, marking the seal where it is not. **It refuses**, recording nothing: no session id; a raise the conversation never drew, or a prefix several begin — the first saying the way on, as `show` does; argv of another shape; a key that is no row; stdin holding no JSON object, lacking the why — for a pick, own words and a keep alike — (and the text, for own words), or holding a field its form does not take; who moved whom given where it is the engine's alone; a question no longer open, answered since the draw; and on a replacement a decision no longer current — the refusal names the decision, what replaced or merged it since the draw, and what is current now to raise again on — the person's answer stays in the conversation. The person's answer to Claude's lean is recorded here alone: the person's `ledger decide` or `ledger replace` carrying `sealed` refuses, naming `raise answer`, and the person's `ledger decide` moves no one — `who_moved_whom` `none`, set where it is left out, any other refused, naming `raise answer` — nor does their `ledger replace`, for any reason, of a decision on a chain Claude never leaned on, no entry down it Claude's own or carrying a sealed answer: no departure, no reversal. Response: the draw `{"ok": true, "decision", "reason", "raise": "<id>", "redrawn": bool}` — `redrawn` true where the conversation's open seal of the question or the replacement was drawn and the call given set aside — `warnings` where the ledger's read gave any; `show` `{"ok": true, "raise": "<id>"}`; each followed by the MENU. A proceed `{"ok": true, "decision": "proceed", "reason", "id", "committed"}`, no MENU; `answer` `{"ok": true, "raise": "<id>", "id", "committed"}` — each the line's short id and the state commit, as `ledger decide` answers, `committed` null where an answer given again commits nothing, `note` where the commit is pending; an answer given again adds `"already": true` and `"answer"`, the row's key the ledger holds — `keep` for a keep — or null — or, not followed, `{"ok": true, "raise": "<id>", "open": true}`; `warnings` with any where the ledger's read or an append to the trust log gave any. **In the build** (12.13; `domain/questions.cjs`), a raise at `<work-unit>.implementation` or `<work-unit>.review` names the task it is for, `--parks <phase>.<n>`, where it is made in one; a call made in no task — the task writer's gap, the consolidation agent's, the phase review's (12.5.5) — names none; and a raise anywhere else takes none; at a quick-fix's build places, its implementation or its review, a question a task's builder asks parks its task, as in the build, and one Claude raises in the quick-fix's conversation, naming none, is drawn there as anywhere else, parking nothing (19.5.25): refused elsewhere with one, given twice, as a task not as screens show it, naming a task the tracker lacks or one done or closed (`domain/task-parks.cjs`), and a raise given `--task` anywhere, its task named by what it parks. A fact (`--fact`) at a build place, with `--parks` or without, parks as a call that asks does: it always asks and never meets the decision point, and is answered as a fact, with no lean and nothing in the trust log. A proceed lands Claude's call with `task` the one named, or null where none is. One that asks draws nothing and seals nothing in the conversation: it parks the task, or with none waits in the build's queue parking none (12.8.4) — one `parked` line (**`ledger`**), checked as the answer it waits for is, its number minted under the writer lock, the next after the highest its unit's parks hold, live or not, so none is reused — and answers `{"ok": true, "decision": "ask", "reason", "parked": "Q{n}", "task", "id", "committed", "line"}`, `task` null where it parks none, `line` the one line Claude says (12.13.3): `Task {task} parks on Q{n}, waiting for you: {the question} The other tasks carry on.`, a quick-fix's park that set work aside naming each path before its last sentence, `What the checkout held not committed is set aside until it starts again: {paths}.`, or, for a raise naming no task, `Q{n} waits for you, parking {no task|task 6.7|task 6.7 and {N} more}: {the question} The {build|review} carries on.`, as the raise's place is the build's or the review's, the tasks the question parks, the question as it is worded now. The task stays in progress, held; what depends on it is what the tracker's links hold back. At a quick-fix, the park of the fix holding its one checkout first sets aside what the checkout holds not committed, as **`task`**'s `start` says, answering its paths, from the project, as `set_aside`; the fix's next start puts it back. Parked, the task is raised to the person, so a stop its builder made comes off it on the calling conversation's position (**`task`** `stopped`). Raised again while a park of the same question, in any wording, or the same decision, parking the same task, still waits, or with no task while any does — a rerun after a kill among them — it parks nothing and judges no row, answering that question with `committed` null and `"already": true`; another task meeting it joins it, asking nothing and judging no row: one more `parked` line, under its number, minting none, holding the call as first sealed, answered as a park is. A question coming to wait while a raise's rows are judged is met under the writer lock as it lands: the park joins it, its number and its first park's call as sealed copied there. A **question** is a park and each park after it holding its number about the same thing, and parks every task they name but where a close took the task off that park; one parking none waits as any other. A question **waits** while its park is live and what it is about stands open: its question live, in some wording, and open, or the decision current with no live keep of it since the park. While it waits its answer comes through its raise alone — `ledger decide` on its question, `ledger move` and `ledger merge` of it, `ledger replace` of its decision and a `ledger merge` naming that decision its `--duplicate` are refused (**`ledger`**), as is any raise without `--parks` on either, asking or proceeding alike, naming `raise draw <work-unit> Q{n}`: before a row is judged, again under the writer lock as a proceed or an answer lands, and at `--didnt-follow`, for a park that came after the raise was drawn — so the wait ends with the raise's answer, a keep among them, or the unit, its place or its question set aside; a question set aside with its topic waits again once the topic is back. A merged or moved line a clone's merge brings in, made where the park had not yet arrived, ends it too, as the fold reads any line. **`draw <work-unit> <question>`** raises a question waiting, by its number, `Q{n}`, or a prefix of a park's id, once the person says they are ready (12.13.10): sealed in the conversation from its first park alone — the call, the rows judged, the reason, the subject and its links, and `parks`, `{"park", "number", "tasks"}` — never from Claude's words, so after a `/clear` it draws as it was sealed; its question as it is worded now, which it seals, and shows in place of the call's where it was reworded since. A seal of that question, on that wording, the conversation holds open is drawn again, `redrawn`, holding the tasks it parks now, a seal matched to its question by its park alone. It answers `{"ok": true, "raise", "question": "Q{n}", "parks": [tasks], "redrawn"}`; its screen is headed `Q{n} · {Unit} · parks task {task}`, or `parks task {task} and {N} more` (12.12.5), or `Q{n} · {Unit}` where it parks none; a replacement's adds `keep`, the decision's answer as it stands. It refuses a unit that does not exist, a number the unit's parks hold none of — naming those waiting — a number two clones each minted before either pushed, naming each by its id, a question no longer waiting, no session id, and argv of another shape. It is answered as any raise is — a fact's as a fact, with no lean and nothing in the trust log: the answer carries the first task it parks, a call's `raise` event in the trust log `blocked_build: true`, as `--didnt-follow`'s does; and before anything lands, under the lock, a question no longer waiting refuses the answer and `--didnt-follow` alike, recording nothing (20.6.10). An answer away from the lean of a question parking tasks marks each changed, as an addition does (**`task`**'s `add`), so a task whose last round passed is read again by its pair before its account (12.1.4); a fact, with no lean, marks none. **The receipt** (12.13.16, 20.6.9), one line Claude shows as it is, is added to the answer's response as `receipt`: `{Q1} {answered|set aside|still open, explained again} · {task 6.7 builds again|their tasks build again|task 6.7 waits on Q2|no task waited on it|still waiting} · {N waiting on you|nothing waiting on you}{ · next: Q2}` — the tasks the answer freed, one or each of theirs: those of the parks that waited before it and wait no longer, that no park still waiting holds — a lone one its pair is reading a round of building again ` once its pair has read round {r}`, its builder waiting for that round's reports (12.1.4); in step, building again ` once task {N}.{n} is done` where another is in flight — in a quick-fix's one checkout ` once task {N}.{n} is done or closed, or a question parks it` — or `, task {N}.{n} first` where several are freed and none other is, one task at a time (12.7.9); where none is, the task a question still waiting holds, waiting on it; where it parks none, that no task waited on it; how many of the unit's questions still wait; and the next, the first of the conversation's discussion queue, `discussing.json` in its folder — `{"discussing", "queue"}`, by number, which the gate mod keeps — still waiting and not just recorded; none with no queue or none such, and none for an explained-again question, still under discussion. A queue that cannot be read names none, saying so in `warnings`. **`aside <work-unit> <question> …`** sets each question named aside, by number or id (12.13.17): it stays waiting, nothing is written, and the response is `{"ok": true, "receipt"}`, one receipt naming each in the order given; any named not waiting refuses the whole call. **`waiting <work-unit>`** is the projection every surface showing the questions waiting reads (12.13.6, 12.13.7), never a file: `{"ok": true, "unit", "waiting": [{"question": "Q{n}", "id", "asks", "about", "parks": [tasks], "since", "waited_minutes"}]}`, oldest first — its number, its first park's short id, what it asks, its question as worded now, the short id of what it is about — that question, or the decision it would replace — the tasks it parks, none where it parks none, when it parked, and the whole minutes it has waited. Neither writes, nor takes the writer lock.

```bash
engine raise <work-unit>.<phase>[.<topic>] --answers <id> [--relies-on <id> …]   # Claude's call on stdin; ask, proceed or blind, and the screen
engine raise <work-unit>.<phase>[.<topic>] --replaces <id> --reason <new-fact|other-topic|changed-mind|was-wrong> [--cause <id>] [--relies-on <id> …]  # the same, proposing a decision's replacement; the screen adds keep
engine raise <work-unit>.<phase>[.<topic>] --answers <id> [--relies-on <id> …] --fact   # a fact only the person holds on stdin, no lean; always asked, nothing to the trust log
engine raise show <id> [--technical]                                            # the raise drawn again from its seal
engine raise answer <id> (--pick <key> | --own | --didnt-follow)                # the person's answer on stdin; the ledger and the trust log, or the trust log alone
engine raise <work-unit>.implementation --answers <id> [--parks <phase>.<n>]    # in the build (or .review, or --replaces …): proceed for the task, or park it; with no task, wait parking none
engine raise draw <work-unit> <Q{n}|park-id>                                    # a question waiting, raised in full from its park
engine raise aside <work-unit> <Q{n}|park-id> …                                 # set aside, still waiting; the receipt
engine raise waiting <work-unit>                                                # the questions waiting, oldest first
```

**`verdict`** — the verdicts on departures (`domain/verdict.cjs`; the departure rule and the queue's projection are `domain/ledger-verdicts.cjs`, the departures and verdicts this machine logged `domain/trust-evidence.cjs`). A departure is the person's decision whose answer stood over Claude's lean, or their keep of a decision Claude proposed to replace, standing over the proposal — who moved whom `person-moved-claude` on its ledger line — that this machine's trust log holds as one: a raise answered with another row than the lean — `keep` among them — or in the person's own words, or the person's reversal of an answer that held the lean. The trust log is the machine's, so a departure is judged on the machine where it was made: a departure the ledger brought from another machine is no departure here, nor is the person's replacement of their own departure still against the lean, which logs nothing and is owed no verdict; an event naming an entry the ledger lacks is skipped; and an event under another project's id is that project's. The trust log's events are read landed, waiting in a conversation's folder after an append that failed, or kept there ahead of an entry the ledger holds, by whichever of the project's clones on the machine. `queue` answers every such departure made in a live place, current or replaced since, that has no verdict — in the trust log, or waiting in any conversation's folder for it — in the ledger's order: each its line as a ledger read shows it, with `standing`, where it stands, and `lean`, Claude's lean beside it — the sealed answer of the nearest entry down its chain that holds one, or the answer of the nearest that held the lean; beside a keep, the proposal it seals, the keep's row carrying `text`, the answer it kept, as a decision's carries its own. Response `{"ok": true, "departures": […]}`; a read, holding no lock, refusing any argument. `give <entry> <nothing|fix|loss|skip> --at <point>` logs a `verdict` event in the trust log (`{config}/trust.jsonl`, beside the conversations' folders): the departure by its full id, the verdict, and the point it was given at — `decided` (a topic's decided screen), `ready-to-build`, `conclusion` (an investigation's), `finish` (the finish gate), `close` (a quick-fix's) or `queue`. The entry is a prefix of its id, resolved as a ledger link's is. It refuses an entry the ledger holds as no departure; a departure this machine logged none for — made on another machine, whose verdict is given there, or the person's replacement of their own departure; a departure made in a place no longer live, which the queue leaves out; and a departure whose latest verdict, landed or waiting in any conversation's folder, is no skip: a skip leaves the departure waiting in the queue for the verdict that follows it, and a verdict given is corrected as a correction, never given again. It runs under the writer lock, so a peer's verdict on the same departure never lands between its check and its append, and writes nothing the state holds. Response `{"ok": true, "entry": "<short id>", "verdict": …, "point": …}`; an append to the trust log that fails rides as a warning, the event waiting in the conversation's folder for its next record or the next boot.

```bash
engine verdict queue                                          # the departures waiting on a verdict
engine verdict give <entry> <nothing|fix|loss|skip> --at <point>  # a verdict on a departure, logged in the trust log
```

**`brief`** — a brief for one of the workflows' own agents, filled by the engine from the records and stored for the dispatch to carry (`domain/brief.cjs`, the records `domain/brief-records.cjs`, the spec's sections `domain/spec-sections.cjs`). **The roles**: each names its template, `content/briefs/{role}.md`, the agent it is dispatched as, and the records it draws on — `builder` (`leeway-builder`): the task, its spec sections, the decided calls, the linters, where it works, and where its account and the verify setup it builds or changes go — once the task's review has ended, its last round passed or settled, the settlement's text beneath, and no account written there covers it, that the task has landed and its account is owed now, and, beside that alone, where an account written there does not read, every fault, so a refused account goes back to its builder in its brief again — or, where the task changed after its last round passed (**`task`**'s `account`), that it changed in that round and nothing of its landing: the builder builds what changed, and its pair reads the task again before its account is written; `reviewer` (`leeway-reviewer`): the task, its spec sections, the decided calls, the diff's range, where its scratch worktree lays out each repository, the linters and where its report goes; `fix` (`leeway-builder`, sent to the builder by SendMessage): the task's last round's reports, one per reviewer, and nothing else — refused where no builder of the task was sent its own brief in the calling conversation, as after a `/clear`, `task {N}.{n} of {unit} has no builder briefed in this conversation, so none to send the round back to: send a new builder its brief first, engine brief builder {unit} {task}, which carries the task, the engine and where it builds, then this fix brief`; and where the task changed since a brief to its builder last went past it, `task {N}.{n} changed since its builder's last brief — … send its builder its brief again first, engine brief builder {unit} {task}, which carries the task and its calls as they stand, then this fix brief`; issued, it records what the builder's brief records, ending `task ready`'s `back` as that does; `product-researcher` (`leeway-product-researcher`): none; discussion's, each for a topic (9.8.13) — `deep-dive` (`leeway-deep-dive`): the question, the dive's kind, the topic's record, the product record and the report; `perspective` (`leeway-discussion-perspective`): the question, the lens, the topic's record, the product record and the report; `synthesis` (`leeway-discussion-synthesis`): its pair's question, the perspectives, the topic's record and the report; `discussion-reviewer` (`leeway-discussion-reviewer`): the topic's record, its log, the other records, the product record and the report; `closing-pass-reader` (`leeway-closing-pass-reader`): those and the gap review; `plain-search` (`leeway-plain-search`, 22.6.10): the conversation's last knowledge query alone; and the migration's (21.10.5) — `migration-topic` (`leeway-migration-topic`): the migrated topic, the verifier's findings, the carry's refusal and the directive; `migration-verifier` (`leeway-migration-verifier`): the migrated topic, its composed record and the directive; and the phase cut's, for a unit (11.5.6) — `phase-cut` (`leeway-phase-cut`): the spec's outline, its scenarios, the graph of what its parts rely on, the decisions made at the spec since the cut, the cut's refusal and where the cut goes; and the sweep's, for a unit (8.10.3) — `sweep` (`leeway-sweep`): the unit's records, the product record, what the person struck or removed, the sweep's refusal and where its report goes; and the spec's, each for a unit (11.1.5, 11.7.8) — `spec-writer` (`leeway-spec-writer`): where the spec goes, the unit's records, for a bugfix the root cause and the fix its investigation rests on and the rules the fix keeps — its investigation's other current decisions (19.4.15) — the topics' logs, a bugfix's its investigation's, the canonical assets, the builder's calls, the entries written since the spec last landed, the review's findings, the wording the person agreed, the numbers the open review page holds, the lines that do not trace and the land's refusal; `spec-reviewer` (`leeway-spec-reviewer`): the sections to review, the lines that do not trace, the measured findings, the product-wide entries the spec cites, the unit's records, the builder's calls, the reports refused and the report; and the build's, each for a phase of the unit's cut (12.5, 12.10.3, 12.11) — `task-writer` (`leeway-task-writer`): the phase, its sections, its scenarios, for a bugfix's phase 1 the test that reproduces its bug — its first task, 1.1, writing that test and nothing else, of type `test`, naming the command that runs it alone (15.5.1) — the notes held for it, its tasks written so far, the earlier phases' unfinished tasks, the calls made in the build, the entries a replacement affects and where its task files go; `consolidation` (`leeway-consolidation`): the notes the phase takes, its tasks, its range, where its report goes and where its task files go; `phase-review` (`leeway-reviewer`, the reviewer's second template, 6.5.2): the phase, its sections, its tasks' criteria, what it relies on, the decided calls, its range, where its scratch worktree lays out each repository, its scenarios' runs at the phase's tips, what survived the mutation tool, the linters and where its report goes; `review-area` (`leeway-reviewer`, the reviewer's third template, 6.5.2): the engine, an area of the spec, where it meets the others, the unit's calls whole, the change's commits, where its scratch worktree lays out each repository, the area's scenarios' runs at the change's tips, the linters, where its report goes, and what it holds the change against — the spec's intent for the area, never a task's criteria; a quick-fix's, its fixes' acceptance in their own words, read as one — a quick-fix's one area the change (`1-the-change`), never spec.md: the area's place opening `{unit} is a quick-fix, which has no spec: its fixes' acceptance, in their own words, stands for one.`, then the change asked for, its record, `summary.md`, whole, and each fix's acceptance criteria as its task file words them, the area meeting no other; `test-surface` (`leeway-test-surface`): the spec's scenarios — a quick-fix's, its fixes' acceptance in their own words, opening with that same line — the change's commits, where its scratch worktree lays out each repository, the linters and where its report goes; and a bugfix's validations (19.4.7), each for the unit, its report written into the investigation's agent store, `--agent` naming a dispatch there of its role's kind still in flight — `root-cause-validation` (`leeway-investigation-root-cause-validation`): the path of the investigation's log, its topic's record, the root cause it rests on and each repository of the project by its path; `fix-validation` (`leeway-investigation-fix-validation`): those and the fix it rests on, the current answer of the fix direction's question, with that question — each refused for a unit whose investigation cannot take work or has not started, as `investigation-log` refuses it, and where the root cause or the fix rests on no current entry, naming the way on (**`investigation`**). A role whose records include the task's takes `<unit> <task>`, the task as `{phase}.{n}`; one whose records include a phase's, and no task's, takes `<unit> <N>`, a phase of the unit's cut, refused for a unit the project lacks or one set aside, a unit whose build has not begun (`brief: {unit}'s build has not begun — a phase is briefed once ready to build's yes begins it`) and a phase its cut lacks (`brief: {unit}'s cut has no phase {N} — its phases are 1 to {M}`); one whose records include a topic's takes `<unit> <topic>`, refused for a unit the project lacks, a topic the unit lacks, and a topic set aside, cancelled or postponed (5.8.17); one whose records include a unit's takes `<unit>`, refused for a unit the project lacks or one set aside, cancelled (`brief: {unit} is cancelled — reactivating it comes first`); any other takes none. A role takes the flag each of its records names, every one but a read dive's `--path`, the writer's `--wording` and the build's `--base`, once, and no other: `reviewer`, `consolidation` and `phase-review` `--base <branch>`, where the range starts elsewhere than the engine reads it, `reviewer` and `phase-review` `--letter <a|b>` for the reviewer of the count (`kernel/reviewers.cjs`) whose report the brief places, `--question <id>` for the question, `--kind <survey|read|feasibility|landscape|verify>` for a dive's kind, and `--path <file>` for the file a read reads, `--wording <file>` for the wording the person agreed, and `--agent <id>` — the dispatch `agent dispatch` answered — for the question it is bound to, its pair's question, the report, the lens and the perspectives, and for a spec review's report and the land its pass is bound to; and the migration's `--topic <unit.phase.topic>` for the migrated topic, of a unit live or not, and `--directive <id>` for its directive. A flag carries a name, an id or a path, never text that reaches a brief: anything else an agent should know goes onto the task (`task add`) or into the ledger. **The template** is markdown: its own words, and a slot, `{{record}}` alone on its line, for each record its role draws on — refused, before anything is read, for a slot that is not alone on its line, one no record answers or its role does not draw on, or a record's slot missing or there twice. **The records**, each in one fixed form: `task` — the task's text whole as the tracker holds it, each of its headings a level down, then each addition in order under a heading of its own, `## Added by the orchestrator` or `## Added by the person`; `spec-sections` — for a quick-fix's task, which has no spec, the line `{unit} is a quick-fix, which has no spec: the task's acceptance scenarios, in their own words, stand for its sections.` (19.5.24); else each section the task's `Spec reference` cites, verbatim from the unit's `spec.md`, in the order cited, each once, a part whose own area is drawn left to the area: an area is a heading `## N. Title` and runs to the next heading at level two or above, holding its parts; a part is `### N.M Title` and runs to the next at level three or above; a heading inside a fenced block is text. A reference cites in runs: a run opens at `§` or `§§` and goes on over numbers joined by `,`, `and` or `&` (`§1.1, 1.2`, `§1.1 and 2.1`), and two numbers joined by `–`, `-` or `to` are a range, every section at their level from the first to the last in the spec's order (`§1.1–1.3`, `§1 to 3`); every number a run holds is cited; `decided-calls` — the unit's current decisions at its own level (topic null), the person's and Claude's, the builder's own calls among them (13.11.10), in the ledger's order, each one list item, `- {short id}, {the person's|Claude's}: {answer}` with its `Why:` beneath, every line after an item's first indented; `linters` — the project manifest's `environment.linters`, each `- {name}: {command}` — a quick-fix's, where the project records no tests and no linters, `None recorded: the project has no environment setup, so run the tests and linters you find in the repository, asking nothing.` (22.5.10); `diff-range` — a quick-fix's task's, with no `--base`, its own commits alone on the quick-fix's one branch, each stamped with it by the door, the other fixes' among them left out: the line `The task's change is its own commits alone, each stamped with it — fix/{unit} holds the other fixes' among them, which are not its: read each, `git show <sha>`, in its repository.`, then in each repository holding any, under ``## In {the project's repository|`{folder}`}: its commits on `fix/{unit}` ``, each commit a line, `` `{sha}` {subject}``, and their `git show --stat` beneath — refused where it holds none (`{task} has no commit of its own on fix/{unit} — its builder commits its change through the door first`); else in each repository holding the task's branch — the project's own first and a nested one only where the branch holds work there, as `land` finds them, its base, with no `--base`, where the task was laid out — the commit `task worktree` recorded there (12.7.10), or the tip of the work below it the task's branch holds since, merged back onto its chain — its own phase's tasks done and not landed for a task of the cut, every task of the unit done and not landed to its phase for a fix phase's — whichever descends from every other, a commit shown by its short form, else the branch the repository lands on as `land` picks it — and with one, the `--base` branch where the repository has it as a local branch, else the repository's default branch as `land` picks it, a heading ``## In {the project's repository|`{folder}`}: `{base}...{branch}` `` and beneath it the range's `git diff --stat` fenced, every path whole and the graph at 40 columns, or `Nothing changed in the range.` — never the diff's text; `scratch-layout` — where the reviewer's scratch worktree lays out every repository of the project, a line each, the project's own first and each before those nested in it, as `task worktree` laid them all out for the builder: `` - {the project's repository|`{folder}`}: `{where}` — {why} ``, one holding work on the task's branch at that branch (`the range's tip there`), a submodule otherwise at the commit its holder as laid out points it at, and a clone otherwise at the task's branch, or the branch it lands on where the task's branch is not made there; then the two calls, by the engine's absolute path, that lay it out and take it apart — `scratch lay {name} {commit} [{folder}={commit} …]`, every repository at the commit its line names, and `scratch remove {name}` (**`scratch`**) — its name `{unit}-{task}-{letter}` for a task's reviewer, `{unit}-phase-{N}-{letter}` for the phase review's, `{unit}-area-{n}-{letter}` for an area's reader and `{unit}-tests` for the test surface's; `reports` — each reviewer's report of the task's last review round, `build/tasks/{task}/round-{N}-{letter}.md`, verbatim, in the reviewers' order, each under `# Reviewer {A}'s report`, its letter upper-cased, a blank line between them — refused where the task has no round's reports, a round lacks a reviewer's report or holds one that does not read as a reviewer's report (**`task`**'s `account`), and where the last round passed, which sends nothing back to the builder; where the task changed while its pair read that round (**`task`**'s `add`), then `# What changed while they read it`, that it did — something added to it, or one of its calls decided against its lean — so the builder builds what changed beside the fixes, the task as it stands drawn as `task` draws it, and `# The decided calls now` as `decided-calls` draws them; `account-to` — the absolute path of the task's account, `build/tasks/{task}.md`, where the builder writes it as the task lands and `task account` reads it — and where its pair passed it while a question waiting parks it, that it waits on that question and writes no account until the answer decides whether it lands as passed or changes — refused while a round its pair was briefed for is not reported whole, `round {r} of task {N}.{n} of {unit} is not yet reported whole — {each reviewer's way on} — and its builder goes back once the round's reports are in`, each reviewer of the round whose report is not in or does not read named with its way on, `engine brief reviewer {unit} {task} --letter {letter}`: one the calling conversation briefed is waited for, or briefed again where it stopped without its report; one an earlier conversation briefed, or none, is briefed for the round; one whose report does not read goes back to it, every fault named; where the account there reads and is not yet taken, that it is written and not taken, `accounted` to report; issued, it records the freeing and the changes the builder was briefed past and the conversation issuing it (`.cache/{unit}/build/tasks/{task}/briefed.json`, `{"round", "readers", "builder", "change", "session"}`, beside the round its pair was briefed for and, by letter, the conversation that briefed each reviewer, the checkout's alone), which ends `task ready`'s `back`; `engine` — the engine the builder runs, `node {path}`, by the absolute path of the one filling the brief, in the checkout the orchestrator runs in, so a worktree started at the remote's default branch never runs one older or missing; `workplace` — where the builder builds, runs and commits: the worktree it was started in, laid out by `task worktree {unit} {task}`; a quick-fix's task, the project's own checkout, by its absolute path, on the quick-fix's one branch, `fix/{unit}`, which the task's start put it on, never a worktree of its own (19.5.15); `chain` — for a quick-fix's task, that its branch is the quick-fix's one, every task before it on it, with no chain to merge back; else whether the task's branch holds its phase's chain as it stands: where the work below it (`review-change.cjs`) has moved past the branch in a repository holding it, the merge of that work's tip back onto it in each such, children first, `git merge --no-ff --no-commit {tip}` or `git -C {folder} merge --no-ff --no-commit {tip}`, each committed through the commit door, which finishes it (**`commit`**), before the next starts, a child's pointer in the repository holding it staged at that child's merged tip (`git add {folder}`, or `git -C {holder} add {folder}`); the merge committed naming each file resolved, or where nothing conflicted any file it changed; then, while the task's review is not over, the task to go on with; once it is, where its account is not taken, the account written and `accounted` reported; where it is taken, `merged`; else that it holds it; `setup-to` — the absolute path of `.cache/{unit}/build/verify-setup.json`, where the builder writes the verify setup its task builds or changes for `verify setup --file`, outside the checkout, then `The verify setup recorded now, which the file starts from:` over the setup on the project manifest as it stands, fenced as JSON without its run, or `No verify setup is recorded yet.`; `round-report-to` — the absolute path of `build/tasks/{task}/round-{N}-{letter}.md`, where the reviewer `--letter` names writes its whole report of the round under review, its final message carrying only the verdict and the nits (13.5.9) — issued, it records the round its pair was briefed for (`briefed.json`), the round a change marks itself against (**`task`**'s `add`): N read from the task's reports on disk, never the conversation, so it holds across a `/clear` and for both reviewers of a round — the last round reported while a reviewer's report of it is still to be written or does not read, else the next, a settled round's among them — refused where its report is written already and reads, and where the task's last round passed (`round {N} of {task} passed — its review is over: {way}`, the way on the build's entry gives the task by how far it stands towards landing (**`enter`**) — its landing, its account's taking, or its reports read for their notes for later and nits and its builder's brief — none where a question parks it), but where the task changed after that round passed (**`task`**'s `account`), when its pair reads it again in the round after; a report there that does not read is named as the one the brief's replaces, with why it does not read, as every reader refusing it names briefing its reviewer again; and a phase's, each of the phase of the unit's cut the brief names, its tasks read from the tracker — every task of each phase to it, once — and never the whole spec (12.5.6): `cut-phase` — `Phase {N} of {unit}: {name}`, its goal, and `It relies on {phase K|the work unit {name}}, ….` or `It relies on nothing.`, a blank line between; `phase-sections` — the absolute path of `spec.md` in backticks, then each section the phase names, `- §{address}, first built in {this phase|phase {K}}` — the first phase naming it, or for a part its area, so an invariant in one first built here has its check in this phase (15.8.2) — then each verbatim, as `spec-sections` draws one, in the cut's order, a part whose area the phase names left to the area; `phase-scenarios` — each scenario the phase holds, `- {N.Sk}: {wording}`, its wording closing on the statements it draws on; `held-notes` — each note for later held for the phase (**`task`**'s `note`), `## Note {k}` over its text; `written-tasks` — each task of the phase the tracker holds, `- {N.n} {title}, {not started|in progress|done|closed}`, or `The phase is not written in the tracker yet.`; `earlier-tasks` — each task of an earlier phase not ended, likewise; `build-calls` — the unit's current decisions written at `{unit}.implementation` or `{unit}.review`, in the ledger's order, each `- {short id}, {the person's|Claude's}[, for task {N.n}][, replacing {short id}]: {answer}` with its `Why:` beneath; `affected-entries` — the affected entries `ledger affected <unit>` answers (11.9.1), fenced as JSON; `task-files-to` — the absolute path of `.cache/{unit}/build/phase-{N}/{role}`, where the agent writes its task files, the task writer's apart from the consolidation agent's; `phase-notes` — each note the phase has to take at its end, as `held-notes` draws them: those held for no phase that no other phase's consolidation report took; `phase-tasks` — each task of the phase, its text as `task` draws it, its title's heading `## {N.n} {title}`, then `It is {not started|in progress|done}.` or `It was closed: {reason}`, then its additions under `### Added by the {orchestrator|person}`; `phase-criteria` — each task of the phase not closed, `## {N.n} {title}` over its acceptance scenarios and its additions; `relied-on` — each phase it relies on, `## Phase {K}: {name}`, its goal, `Its sections: §…` and `Its tasks:` as `written-tasks` draws them, and each other unit, `## The work unit {name}` over `This phase relies on its delivery.`; `phase-diff-range` — in each repository holding work on the branch of a task of the phase not closed, as `diff-range` finds them, the range from its base — with no `--base`, the branch the repository lands on, where the phase's chain leaves it, so the range holds the phase's own work alone; with one, as `diff-range` picks it — to the stack's tip there — the branch every other the phase's tasks hold there descends from — drawn as `diff-range` draws a range; `phase-scratch-layout` — the phase review's scratch worktree, laid out as `scratch-layout` lays it out, one holding work at the stack's tip there and a clone holding none at the branch it lands on; `consolidation-to` — the absolute path of the phase's `build/phases/phase-{N}/consolidation.md`, where the agent writes its report; `recorded-runs` — each scenario the phase holds, `- {N.Sk}: {proven|not proven|named, not run}` as `verify`'s runs read it at the phase's tips — the stack's tip in each repository holding the phase's work, as `phase-diff-range` picks it (`domain/runs.cjs`, 15.3.5) — only runs at that code or before it counting, those nearest it deciding (**`verify`**) — then each run counted, `  - {verdict}, recorded {at}: `{the run's file}``; `mutants` — what survived the verify setup's mutation tool on the lines the phase changed, as `verify mutants` left it in the unit's cache run at the phase's tips (15.6.3) — the brief never runs the tool — each `- {path}:{line} — {mutant}`, or `None survived.`, or `The verify setup names no mutation tool.`, its reviewers then breaking the riskiest line themselves (15.6.5); `phase-report-to` — the absolute path of the phase's `build/phases/phase-{N}/review-{letter}.md`, where the reviewer `--letter` names writes its report, a report there that does not read named as the one it replaces; each refused for a phase not written where it draws the phase's tasks (`phase {N} of {unit} is not written — its tasks are written as it starts`), a section or a scenario the spec lacks (`phase {N} of the cut names §{address}, which the spec lacks`, `phase {N} of the cut holds {N.Sk}, which the spec lacks`), a task with no type, a base no repository holding the phase's work has, tasks that are no one stack in a repository (`phase {N} of {unit}'s tasks are no one stack in {repository} — …`) and no repository holding work on them, no verify setup recorded, a setup naming a mutation tool that has not run over the phase at its tips (`the mutation tool has not run over phase {N} of {unit} at its tips — the project's repository at {commit}, {repository} at {commit} — engine verify mutants {unit} {N} runs it`), a letter none of the reviewers' (`{letter} is no reviewer's letter — the reviewers are a and b`) and a report already written that reads (`{path} is written already — that reviewer's report of the round is in`); the review phase's, of the round under review (`domain/review-phase.cjs`) — `area-sections` — the spec's path, then the area `--area` names by its number, verbatim, its parts in it; `area-meets` — the spec's other areas, `- §{n} {title}`, then each pair of parts, one in the area and one outside it, where one relies on the other, as `relies-on-graph` draws them; `unit-calls` — every current decision of the unit's, at any of its places, in the ledger's order, `- {short}, {the person's|Claude's}[, handed over as {your call|don't care}], at {unit}.{phase}[.{topic}]: {answer}`, then `It answers: {question}` and `Why: {why}` beneath — the ledger whole, for calls fine alone and wrong in combination (16.17.8); `change-commits` — in each repository holding the change, under ``## In {the project's repository|`{folder}`}``, the unit's own work, never what others landed beside it: each of its landings, a commit on the base's first-parent line whose message names a task of the unit, oldest first, under ``### Landed: `{commit}` {subject}``, read against its first parent; then its work not landed, the stack its tasks done and not landed make there — a fix phase's, or a cut phase waiting at its gate in step — under ``### Not landed yet: `{from}..{branch}` ``, read from where it leaves the base; each with its `git diff --stat`, or `Nothing changed in it.`: the base the branch `--base` names where the repository has it, else the branch it lands on; the change's tip that stack's tip, else the base; a repository with neither holding none of the change (`domain/review-change.cjs`); `change-scratch-layout` — the reviewer's scratch worktree, laid out as `scratch-layout` lays it out, one holding the change at its tip there (`the change's tip there`) and a clone holding none at the branch it lands on; `area-runs` — each scenario of the area, `- {N.Sk}: {proven|not proven|named, not run}` read at the change's tips, its wording beneath, then each run counted as `recorded-runs` draws it; `area-report-to` — the absolute path of `review/round-{N}/{area}-{letter}.md`, where the reviewer `--letter` names writes its report of the area, as `phase-report-to` draws it; `area-authority` — the review's opening: every task and phase passed its own review, the whole change held against the area of the spec as one piece, the spec's intent and the product it describes the authority, never a task's criteria; a quick-fix's, every fix passed its own, the change held against what was asked of it, its fixes' acceptance in their own words, read as one, the authority; `tests-report-to` — the absolute path of `review/round-{N}/tests-a.md`, where the test surface's one reader writes its report, refused where the round does not read the test surface (`the test surface is not read in round {R}, which reads {reads}`); each refused where the round cannot be read, as **`review`** refuses it — a review phase `enter review` refuses, one a round ended, a round waiting on the round before's fix tasks, a later round whose reads are not named — an area the spec lacks (`the spec has no area {N} — its areas are §1, §2, …`) or the round does not read (`area {N} is not read in round {R}, which reads {reads}`), a base no repository has (`the base {branch} is no local branch in any repository of the project`), the unit's tasks not landed that are no one stack in a repository, and a change no repository holds (`no repository of the project holds {unit}'s change — nothing of it has landed, and no task of it is done`); and a topic's, never a word of the conversation (26.1.5): `topic-record` — the topic's record as `ledger record <unit> <topic>` answers it, each current entry with what it replaced (9.2.5), fenced as JSON; `topic-log` — the absolute path of the topic's `topics/{topic}/log.md`; `other-records` — the record of each other live topic of the unit that shows anything, in the order the manifest names them sorted, each under `## {topic}` and fenced as JSON, never a log, and none of a topic set aside; `product-record` — the product record, and what discovery found of the product, the unit and the topic, as `ledger product <unit> <topic>` answers it, fenced as JSON — for a role that takes no topic, the product record and what discovery found of the product and the unit, as `ledger product <unit>` answers it; `question` — the question `--question` names, by any wording or a prefix of one, in its current wording, `{short id}: {text}`, refused where it names no question, is no longer current, stands on another topic or is not open (answered, merged or later); once every record is drawn, the dispatch `--agent` names is bound to it, `question` on its row, by its first wording — refused where the dispatch was bound to another; `pair-question` — the question each perspective of that synthesis's set was bound to, in its current wording, refused where one was bound to none or they differ; `dive-kind` — the kind `--kind` names, or for a `read`, `read — `{absolute path}``, the file `--path` names, refused for a kind that is none, a read naming no file, and another kind naming one; `gap-review` — `A gap review has run since this topic's last decision: {id}.`, naming the latest report-backed review or closing pass whose `ledger_mark` the topic's last decision — a replaced answer among them — does not pass, else `No gap review has run since this topic's last decision.` (9.9.6); `report-to` — the absolute path of the report the dispatch `--agent` names writes, refused where the topic's discussion store lacks it, it is another kind than the role is briefed for (`review`, `deep-dive`, `perspective`, `synthesis`, `closing-pass`), or it is no longer in flight; `lens` — that perspective dispatch's label, a lens of the polarity pairs as `agent dispatch` holds it; `perspectives` — the report of each perspective in that synthesis's set, verbatim, in the order they were dispatched, refused for one with no report; the plain search's: `knowledge-query` — the conversation's last `knowledge query` as it kept it, `Terms:` and `Filters:` each flag as given, or `none`, then `It returned:` and its output fenced, `It failed: {why}`, or `It never finished.`, refused where the conversation has run none; and the migration's: `directive` — the directive `--directive` names, verbatim, each heading a level down; `migrated-topic` — the topic `--topic` names as the move left it: the unit, its work type and status, the phases of 079's the topic is in, the state's place and the project's folder in the main checkout, each file of `{unit}/topics/{topic}/` but its mail; as the directive's check reads them, its specs — each spec whose sources in the manifest the code branch's HEAD holds name the topic, read at `{unit}/assets/specs/{spec}.md`, or, where the unit's building started, at `.workflows/{unit}/specification/{spec}/specification.md` and cited as `{unit}/spec.md` — its old map's subtopics with their states, and its research's threads still open or digging; the unit's `imports[]` each as `{unit}/{path}` with where it came in from, marked the topic's to link where the check asks it of the topic, or gone, linked by none, where the state holds no file at it; and where its record is composed, the verifier's findings written and a carry's refusal kept, `.cache/migrate/records/{unit}/{topic}.json`, `.findings.md` and `.refused.md`, every path from the state root but one under `.workflows/`, from the project; `composed-record` — that record, verbatim, in a `json` fence; `verifier-findings` — those findings, verbatim; `carry-refusal` — what `ledger carry` refused that record for, verbatim; the brief takes both, each file cleared once drawn, so neither reaches a later brief; and the phase cut's, each read from the unit's `spec.md` by **`spec`**'s grammar and from the unit's current entries: `spec-outline` — the absolute path of `spec.md` in backticks, a blank line, then each area `- §{N} {title}` with each of its parts beneath it, `  - §{N.M} {title}`, in the spec's order; `spec-scenarios` — each scenario `- {N.Sk}, of §{N} {area's title}: {wording}`, its wording closing on the statements it draws on; `relies-on-graph` — each pair of parts where one relies on the other, `- §{A} relies on §{B}: {line} cites {id}, which relies on {id}, cited by {line}`, every link between them joined by `; `, in the spec's order of A and then of B: part A relies on part B where a statement or a screen of A cites one of the unit's entries whose `relies_on`, followed in current forms through the unit's entries no line cites, reaches an entry a statement or a screen of B cites, the chain shown, `{line} cites {id}, which relies on {id}, which relies on {id}, cited by {line}` — never within a part; a part citing nothing has no edges, nor a cite naming none of the unit's entries, so a link to another unit is left out; `cut-requests` — the unit's current decisions made at its own level at the spec after the ledger's mark the last cut's land committed — every one where this checkout keeps none — in the ledger's order, each in `changed-entries`' form, below: what the person asked of the cut among them; once every record is drawn, the brief keeps its pending take, the ledger's last line as it was issued, written whole over the last at `.cache/{unit}/specification/cut-taking.json`, which the next `cut` commits; `cut-refusal` — what `cut` refuses of the cut staged at `.cache/{unit}/specification/cut.json`, its error verbatim, checked without writing, or `None recorded.` where nothing is staged or the cut would land; `cut-to` — the absolute path of that staged cut, where the agent writes it and which `cut <unit>` lands; and the sweep's: `unit-records` — the unit's current entries (**`ledger`**), under a heading `## {title} ({topic})` for each live topic, sorted by name, then `## The unit's own` for those at the unit's level, then `## Made here, standing on {unit}/{topic}` for an entry made in this unit on another's place, each entry `- {short id} {kind}: {text}`, a "don't care" decision's kind `decision, don't care` (P2), every line after its first indented, a heading with none reading `None recorded.`; `sweep-refusal` — what `sweep take` refuses of the sweep's report at `.cache/{unit}/specification/sweep.json`, its error verbatim, checked without writing, or `None recorded.` where there is no report or the take would take it in; `sweep-to` — the absolute path of that report, where the agent writes it; and the spec's, each of the unit, with `unit-records`, above, from its current entries (`domain/unit-entries.cjs`), its `spec.md` and its review passes (`domain/spec-passes.cjs`): `topic-logs` — the absolute path of each live topic's log on disk, a bugfix's its investigation, each `` - `{path}` ``; `canonical-assets` — the absolute path of each file the unit's current entries link as canonical, likewise; `builder-calls` — the calls the sweep left to the builder, as the sweep taken records them (**`sweep`**), each `- {id}: {call}` then `  Why: {why}`; `changed-entries` and `review-findings` — what waits for the writer, read as `review-settled` reads it (**`checklist`**): every current entry made at the unit's own level at the spec after the ledger's mark the last committed take kept — after the first land's, before any take, none before either — and every pass of the spec's review whose report a scan took in (**`agent`**), holding a `rewrite` finding that take did not carry; `changed-entries` — each such entry, in the ledger's order, `- {short id} {kind}, {where it stands: its topic's heading, the unit's own, or made here, standing on {unit}[/{topic}]}[, replacing {short id}]: {text}`; `review-findings` — each such pass's report, verbatim, in the order dispatched — once every record is drawn, the brief keeps its pending take, written whole over the last at `.cache/{unit}/specification/taking.json`: `{"ledger_mark": "<id>"|null, "passes": ["<id>", …], "wording"?: {"file", "print"}}`, the ledger's last line as it was issued and every pass whose report was in by then, which the next `spec land` commits (**`spec`**); a brief issued again before a land carries all the last committed take left; `wording-changes` — the file `--wording` names, absolute or from the working folder, verbatim: the wording the person agreed on the review page, one change a line, each naming its line, written by the skill in its own scratch; `None recorded.` where none is named — the file and its print kept in the brief's pending take, and the land committing the take removes the file where it is as the brief read it, one written to since staying — refused where it names no file (`brief: --wording names no file — {path}`) or an empty one (`brief: --wording names an empty file — {path}`); `land-refusal` — what `spec land` refuses of `spec.md` as written, its error verbatim, checked without writing, or `None recorded.` where none is written or it would land; `spec-to` — the absolute path of the unit's `spec.md`, which the writer writes, refused, opening `brief: `, for a unit `spec land` refuses before reading it — a concluded spec, or one `enter specification` refuses — and while a gap waits on the person, a question asked at the unit's own level at the spec still open, naming it and the move that puts it to them again from any conversation, `engine raise {wu}.specification --answers {id}` — or a review's gap surfaced as the replacement of a decision still current and kept by no answer since (**`agent`**), `engine raise {wu}.specification --replaces {id} --reason {reason}`: its rewrite waits for the answer; `changed-sections` — `` The spec: `{path}` ``, then `Every section — the review's first pass:` or `The sections changed since {id}, the last pass reported:`, then each such section's own text verbatim, as **`spec`**'s land reads a section (its print) — in the spec's order, then `§{N.M} is no longer in the spec.` for each that pass held and the spec does not; a section is changed where its print differs from the land the last pass whose report is in was bound to, a pass whose report no scan has taken in passed over; refused where this checkout holds no land of the spec — a land is kept in the checkout's own cache, as every agent store is, so a fresh clone or a cleaned cache lands it again — or `spec.md`'s print is not the land's (`brief: this checkout holds no land of {wu}'s spec — …`, `brief: {wu}'s spec.md has changed since it landed — …`, each naming `engine spec land {wu}`), and where no section has changed since the latest pass whose report is in (`brief: {wu}'s spec has not changed since pass {id}, whose report is in, so a pass has nothing to read — {the way on}`, the way on the gaps waiting on the person, each with the raise that puts it to them again; else each pass in flight, the `gap` and `fact` findings waiting to be surfaced, a pass not yet acknowledged named to be acknowledged first, and what waits for the writer; else `its review has settled`); once every record is drawn, the dispatch `--agent` names is bound to the land it reviews, `land` on its row — refused where it was bound to a land of another print; a brief refused closes its dispatch where it is bound to no land, so no reader of the store lists it in flight; `invented-lines` — each statement and screen of `spec.md` that does not trace, `- {number}: {why}`, as `spec land` answers them, none where no `spec.md` is written or its grammar refuses it; `measured-findings` — each of the unit's current findings recording a command, `- {short id}: {text}`, then `Its result:` and `Its command:` with the command fenced; `product-cites` — each current entry of the unit marked product-wide that a statement or a screen cites, in the unit's records' form, then `Cited by {numbers}`; `report-refusal` — each spec review refused since the last whose report was taken in, in dispatch order, `- {id}: {what does not read}`; `unit-report-to` — the absolute path of the report the dispatch `--agent` names writes, refused where the unit's own store lacks it — a validation's, its investigation's — it is another kind than the role is briefed for (`spec-review`, `root-cause-validation`, `fix-validation`), or it is no longer in flight; `investigation` — for a bugfix, `- The root cause, {short id}: {text}` and `- The fix, {short id}: {text}` where its investigation rests on each, then each other current decision standing on its investigation's topic, `- A rule it keeps, {short id}: {text}` — a permission for the hunt, asked at the investigation's own level, `{wu}.investigation`, never among them (19.4.20) — none recorded for any other type; a bugfix's validations': `hunt-log` — the absolute path of its investigation's log; `hunt-record` — its topic's record as `ledger record {wu} {wu}` answers it, fenced as JSON; `hunt-root-cause` — `{short id}: {text}` of the root cause it rests on; `hunt-fix` — the fix likewise, then `It answers {short id}: {text}`, its question; `code` — each repository of the project, `` - `{path}` ``. A decided-calls, linters, topic-log, other-records, topic-record, product-record, verifier-findings, carry-refusal, spec-scenarios, relies-on-graph, cut-requests, topic-logs, canonical-assets, builder-calls, changed-entries, review-findings, invented-lines, measured-findings, product-cites, report-refusal, phase-scenarios, held-notes, written-tasks, earlier-tasks, build-calls, affected-entries, phase-notes, phase-tasks, phase-criteria or relied-on record the project holds none of reads `None recorded.` **The brief** is stored at `{config}/conversations/{session-id}/briefs/{id}.md`, exactly what the agent is handed, its id a new one; beside it, `{id}.json` records whose it is, `{"role", "agent", "unit", "task", "topic"}` — the task (`{phase}.{n}`) and the topic each null for a role that takes none, the unit null for a role that takes no unit — and a read of the review phase's round, a `review-area` or `test-surface` brief, its `"round"` too, a `review-area` brief's its `"area"` with it, which the board's clock reads (**`board`**). A `builder` or `fix` brief sends its task's builder back in, so it takes off the stop the calling conversation's position holds against the task (**`task`** `stopped`). Each is written beside and linked in place, the record first, so neither is seen half written, neither is stored over a file, and a brief always has its record; both go with the conversation's folder. Its first line names it — `Brief {id} — the builder's, for {unit} task {N.M}`, likewise `the reviewer's` and `the fix round's`; `Brief {id} — the deep dive's, for {unit} topic {topic}`, likewise `the perspective's`, `the synthesis's`, `the discussion reviewer's` and `the closing-pass reader's`; `Brief {id} — the phase cut's, for {unit}`, likewise `the sweep's`, `the spec writer's` and `the spec reviewer's`; `Brief {id} — the task writer's, for {unit} phase {N}`, likewise `the consolidation agent's` and `the phase review's`; or `Brief {id} — the product researcher's`, likewise `the plain search's`, `the migration's, for one topic` and `the migration verifier's` — then a blank line and the filled template. The dispatch carries the pointer, `brief:{id}`, alone, as the Agent prompt or the SendMessage message. **It refuses**, storing neither file: no `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`; a role it does not know, and arguments of another shape — a flag its role takes missing, twice, with no value or with its value joined by `=`; a flag its role does not take; a unit or a task the project lacks; a task done or closed; in step, a task waiting behind another in flight, until that one is done, as `render step-gate` refuses it (12.7.9), and in a quick-fix's one checkout (R27) a builder's or fix round's brief for any fix but the one holding it: a fix a question parks, naming each question's raise, as `task done` refuses it (`brief: task {N}.{n} of {unit} waits on Q{n}, parked for the person — they answer it through its raise: raise draw {unit} Q{n}`), one waiting behind the holder, until that one is done or closed or a question parks it (`brief: {unit} builds in its one checkout, one task at a time — task {N}.{n} is in flight, and task {N}.{n} goes on once task {N}.{n} is done or closed, or a question parks it`), one not started (`brief: task {N}.{n} of {unit} holds no part of {unit}'s one checkout — start it first, engine task start {unit} {N}.{n}, which puts the checkout on it`), and the holder whose work a question's park set aside, until its start puts it back (`brief: task {N}.{n} of {unit}'s work, set aside when a question parked it, is not back in the checkout — start it again first, engine task start {unit} {N}.{n}, which puts it back`) — its pair's brief never, the pair reading commits, not the checkout; a unit with no `spec.md`; a spec reference that cites no section, a number deeper than a part, a range whose ends differ in level or run backwards, or a section the spec lacks, each naming the reference; a spec numbering a section twice or a part under another area or none; an `environment.linters` that is not a list of `{name, command}`; a task with no type, whose branch is named from it, a task's branch no repository holds work on, and a base that no repository holding it has as a local branch; a fix round's reports refused as `reports` says; a topic's or a unit's record it cannot read, as each record above says; a directive the migrations lack; a migrated topic its unit lacks; a composed record that is no file or is empty; and for the phase cut, a unit with no `spec.md`, or one its grammar refuses (`brief: {unit}'s spec.md does not read —` and every fault, one a line). It runs whole under the writer lock, since its read of the task or the phases may commit the tracker's set-up. Response `{"ok": true, "id", "role", "agent", "pointer"}`, with `committed` where the task's or the phases' read committed the tracker's set-up, and `warnings` where a read gave any. **`brief guard`** is the hook before Agent and SendMessage (`domain/brief-guard.cjs`), its JSON on stdin, taking no lock. It acts on a call that reaches one of the workflows' agents, those named `leeway-*`: Agent's `subagent_type`, or the type a SendMessage's `to` was dispatched as, from its record `{session_id}/subagents/agent-{to}.meta.json` beside the conversation's transcript, in the folder of `transcript_path`. Any other call — Claude's own subagents, a message to one, a recipient with no record, input it cannot read — it lets through, saying nothing and recording nothing. A call that reaches one carries the pointer alone in every field that holds its text, Agent's `prompt` and SendMessage's `message` and `content`. The hook finds the brief in the folder of the conversation stdin's `session_id` names, prints `{"hookSpecificOutput": {"hookEventName": "PreToolUse", "updatedInput": {…}}}` and nothing else — the tool's input whole, the stored brief in place of each pointer, and no permission decision, so the person's own rules hold — and exits 0. It refuses on stderr with exit 2, appending one line to `{config}/brief-refusals.jsonl`, `{"v": 1, "at", "project_id", "project", "session", "tool", "agent", "brief", "reason", "detail"}`, the reason one of `no-template` (an agent none of the roles is dispatched as), `no-brief` (no pointer), `never-issued` (a brief the conversation did not issue, its `{id}.md` or its `{id}.json` missing), `altered` (a field holding anything beside the pointer), `other-agent` (a brief its `{id}.json` says was issued for another agent) and `error` (any failure checking one of the workflows' agents). The layout hook refuses Claude's writes to a conversation's briefs and to the refusals' file.

```bash
engine brief builder <unit> <task>                                   # the builder's brief for a task not yet ended
engine brief reviewer <unit> <task> [--base <branch>] --letter <a|b>  # the reviewer's, the range from where the task is built on to its branch, its report's file for the round under review
engine brief fix <unit> <task>                                       # the fix round's, the last round's reports from the task's build folder
engine brief product-researcher                                      # the product researcher's brief, no unit needed
engine brief deep-dive <unit> <topic> --question <id> --agent <id> --kind <kind> [--path <file>]  # the deep dive's, on an open question of the topic
engine brief perspective <unit> <topic> --question <id> --agent <id> # one lens of a pair, on a question of the topic
engine brief synthesis <unit> <topic> --agent <id>                  # the synthesis of a pair's perspectives, on the question they argued
engine brief discussion-reviewer <unit> <topic> --agent <id>         # the background review's
engine brief closing-pass-reader <unit> <topic> --agent <id>         # the closing pass's fresh reader, with the gap review
engine brief plain-search                                            # the plain-search helper's, from the conversation's last knowledge query
engine brief migration-topic --topic <unit.phase.topic> --directive <id>     # the migration's per-topic agent's, for one topic
engine brief migration-verifier --topic <unit.phase.topic> --directive <id>  # the migration verifier's, on that topic's composed record
engine brief phase-cut <unit>                                        # the phase cut's, from the unit's spec, the entries its lines cite and what was decided since the cut
engine brief sweep <unit>                                            # the sweep's, from the unit's records, the product record and what was struck
engine brief spec-writer <unit> [--wording <file>]                   # the spec writer's, from the unit's records, what waits for it and the wording the person agreed
engine brief spec-reviewer <unit> --agent <id>                       # a pass of the spec's review, on the sections changed since the last
engine brief task-writer <unit> <N>                                  # the task writer's, for phase N of the cut as it starts
engine brief consolidation <unit> <N> [--base <branch>]              # the consolidation agent's, the phase's notes and its range
engine brief phase-review <unit> <N> [--base <branch>] --letter <a|b>  # a phase review's, its runs and the mutation tool's survivors at the phase's tips
engine brief review-area <unit> --area <N> --base <branch> --letter <a|b>  # an area review's, of the round under review, the unit's calls whole and its area's runs at the change's tips
engine brief test-surface <unit> --base <branch>                     # the test surface's, of the round under review, every scenario of the spec
```
