```bash
engine brief root-cause-validation <unit> --agent <id>               # the root cause's validation, from the investigation's log, its record and the code
engine brief fix-validation <unit> --agent <id>                      # the fix's validation, against the root cause it settles
engine brief guard                                                   # the hook before Agent and SendMessage, its JSON on stdin
```

**`cut`** — the phase cut, written onto the unit's manifest as `cut` (`domain/cut.cjs`; the spec's sections and scenarios `domain/spec-sections.cjs`), the one writer of it: whatever shows or follows the cut reads it there. The file, absolute or from the working folder — where none is given, the cut the corridor staged, `.cache/{wu}/specification/cut.json`, where the cut's agent writes it (**`brief`**'s `cut-to`) — holds the cut as the manifest keeps it, `{"phases": [{"name", "goal", "scenarios", "sections", "relies_on"}, …]}`: the phases in build order, numbered from 1 by it; `name` and `goal` text; `scenarios`, the scenarios the phase proves by id, `N.Sk`; `sections`, the spec's sections it draws on by address, an area `N` or a part `N.M`; `relies_on`, what it relies on, an earlier phase by its number or another work unit, whole, by its name. Each list names an item once, and may be empty. A scenario is one of the lines of the unit's `spec.md`, as **`spec`** reads them; a section is an area's heading `## N. Title` or a part's `### N.M Title`, as `brief` reads them. **It refuses**, every refusal opening `cut: `, naming every fault and writing nothing: a unit the project lacks, or one with no `spec.md`; no file given and nothing staged (`nothing is staged for {wu} — the cut's agent writes it at {path}`); a file that is not JSON or holds no cut, a phase whose fields are missing, extra or of another kind, and a list naming one item twice; a scenario or a section `spec.md` lacks; a scenario of `spec.md` placed in two phases or in none; a phase relying on itself, on one after it, on its own unit, or on a work unit the project lacks; a spec its lines refuse (**`spec`**), naming every fault — a scenario given twice, naming both its lines, a section numbered twice or a part under another area or none among them. **A second cut** replaces the whole cut until a phase has started — the start's checklist closed on the unit's manifest, `checklists.phase-{N}-start` — and once one has, it keeps every phase to the last started exactly as it stands, refusing a cut that changes or drops one, and changes only the phases after. It runs under the writer lock, and commits the unit's manifest alone (`cut({wu}): {N} phases`), pushed. A cut landed commits the pending take of the cut's brief, the ledger's mark as the brief was issued, to `.cache/{wu}/specification/cut-landed.json`, `{"ledger_mark": "<id>"|null}` — the ledger's last line at the land where no brief left one, a cut from a file of the person's: the cut's next brief draws, and ready to build's `phases-complete` waits on, the decisions made at the spec since (**`brief`**'s `cut-requests`). Response `{"ok": true, "unit", "phases": N, "committed": "<short-sha>"}`, with `warnings` and a pending note where the commit failed.

```bash
engine cut <work-unit> [--file <cut.json>]   # the phase cut onto the unit's manifest, checked against its spec.md — the staged cut where no file is given
```

**`spec`** — the unit's spec, `{wu}/spec.md` (`domain/spec-land.cjs`; its lines `domain/spec-sections.cjs`, the trace `domain/spec-trace.cjs`, the unit's entries `domain/unit-entries.cjs`). **`land`** is the one route by which the spec reaches the state branch: the spec's writer writes the file in its place, open to Claude until the spec is concluded (**`layout`**), and the verb reads it, checks it and commits it. **Its lines** are read as the person's review page reads them, so the page, the trace and the cut agree on each. The spec opens with its title, `# Title`, and what stands before its first area, `## Contents` among it, is its introduction; the rest is read in blocks split on blank lines, a fenced block — opened and closed by a line opening ``` — kept whole. A block opening `N.M.K ` starts a statement, in its part `### N.M`; one opening `N.Sk ` a scenario, in its area's block, `## N-S. Scenarios for §N`, after the area; and one opening `Screen…:` after a line a screen of it, `<line>-scr<k>` for the k-th. A line owns every block after it up to the next line, screen or heading: paragraphs, lists, fenced blocks. A statement's or a screen's source bracket is the trailing `[...]` of its last block that is not fenced — `[Ab12Cd, Ef34Gh]`, the ledger entries it cites by short id; `[builder: Ab12Cd]`, the builder's, citing what made it so — the person's "don't care" decision it rests on, or the sweep's call that left it to the builder, by its id, `[builder: call-1]`; or `[builder]` alone, citing nothing — and a scenario's last block that is not fenced closes on the statements it draws on in parentheses, `(1.1.1, 1.2.3)`. The spec is read with `\r\n` or `\r` taken for a newline, as the page reads it. **The trace**: a statement or a screen traces where each cite resolves — a prefix, as git resolves a short hash — to a current decision or finding placed in the unit, standing on a live topic or at the unit's level or made there of another unit's entry, and a builder's cite to a "don't care" decision or a call the unit's sweep taken records (**`sweep`**) — a unit that took no sweep has none; `[builder]` alone cites no entry; any other is invented. **It refuses**, every refusal opening `spec land: `, writing and committing nothing, `spec.md` left as written: a unit the project lacks (`there is no work unit "{wu}"`); a concluded spec, `build` on the unit's manifest (`{wu}'s spec is concluded, and a concluded spec is never corrected — a later change replaces its entry on the ledger`); a unit `enter specification` refuses, in its words — one not in progress, a topic not yet settled, a quick-fix, a unit of a retired type; an epic of more than one live topic whose sweep is not taken (`{wu}'s sweep is not taken — it runs before the spec lands: engine brief sweep {wu}, then engine sweep take {wu}`, **`sweep`**), and a unit whose sweep's candidates wait on the person (`{wu}'s sweep found calls waiting on the person — {names} — taken or struck at approve the map: engine render approve-map {wu} --sweep`); a unit with no `spec.md`; a spec its grammar cannot read, naming every fault, one per line: no title, no area or no statement; a section numbered twice or a part under another area; a heading not standing alone, or a second title; a heading at level two neither an area nor its scenarios; scenarios after another area; a statement outside its part or a scenario outside its area's block; a line numbered twice; a note, a block after a heading opening no line; a screen straight after a heading; a block that reads as a line but opens with a number the grammar does not read, as `1.1.1.` or `- 1.1.1` — one ending in a source bracket, or for a scenario's number in its statements; a line opening straight after another's source, a blank line left out; a bracket of none of the three forms, or one closing an earlier block than the line's last; a bracket on a scenario, or a scenario closing on no statements; a scenario drawing on a statement the spec lacks; a fence left open; while the person's review page is open — its publishing recorded by `spec published`, `.cache/{wu}/specification/published.json` — a number the page holds changed (11.11.3), one fault a line, the numbers held being those of the spec last landed and of the marks, with every number each land since the page opened gave, kept with its last wording in `.cache/{wu}/specification/held.json`: a line whose wording another number held, which no longer holds it (`{N.M.K} now holds the wording {N.M.J} held — a number never changes while the review page is open: …`); a number removed since the page opened given to another line (`{N.M.K} was removed while the review page is open, and its number is not given to another line — a line added takes the next free number, {N.M.J}`), a line brought back with its last wording being that line again; or an added number not past every number held in its part, its area's scenarios or its line's screens (`{N.M.K} is added while the review page is open — a line added takes the next free number, {N.M.J}`); and a commit git refuses. An invented line refuses nothing: the land lists it, and ready to build's `statements-traced` stays open (**`checklist`**). It runs under the writer lock and commits `spec.md` (`spec({wu}): landed`) with each file it links that is on disk in a unit's or the product's assets or spikes, pushed. Once committed it keeps the land's record, written whole over the last, at `.cache/{wu}/specification/landed.json`: `{"digest": "<sha256>", "sections": {"introduction"|"<N|N.M>": "<sha256>"}, "first_mark": "<id>"|null}` — the spec's print: the SHA-256 of each section's own text — its title and introduction, every row before its first area, as `introduction`; a part's text; an area's from its heading to its first part, with its block of scenarios — and of them all in no order, identifying the land, the blank lines between sections and their order no section's; and the ledger's last line at the unit's first land, kept by each land after — read by the spec's review passes and the writer's next brief (**`brief`**); and it commits the writer's pending take, `taking.json` becoming `taken.json`, a land of the spec unchanged among them, removing the wording file the take carried where it is as the brief read it; a refused land keeps the last of each. Response `{"ok": true, "unit", "statements": N, "invented": [{"number": "N.M.K"|"N.M.K-scrK", "why"}], "changed": ["introduction", "N", "N.M", …], "committed": "<short-sha>"}`: `why` that it cites no entry, or for each cite that fails, the prefix naming none, several or too short to name one, naming no entry, the entry replaced, merged away or on a topic or unit set aside, belonging to another unit, a question, whose answer is what the line comes from, or for a builder's cite being no "don't care" decision; `changed` each section whose print differs from the spec the state branch held, or that it lacked, in the spec's order, then each it held that the spec no longer does; every one at the first land; with `warnings`, and `"note": "nothing to commit"` where nothing changed.

**`marks`** stores the marks Claude read back from the person's review page (`domain/spec-review.cjs`; the page's model `domain/spec-page-model.cjs`) at `.cache/{wu}/specification/marks.json`, answering the comments waiting for Claude. The file, absolute or from the working folder, holds the page's `marks` collection by document, each `{"m": {"<lineId>": {"s", "h", "t", "at"}}}` as the page writes it, or saved by the artifact store under `data`: a line's id is its number with its dots as dashes (`3-2-1`, `3-S4`, `3-2-1-scr1`); `s` is `tick`, `comment` (an open conversation) or empty; `h` the fingerprint of the wording ticked or unticked, the first 12 hex digits of the SHA-256 of the line's wording, its source bracket left out; `t` the conversation, each message keyed `p<ms>` (the person) or `c<ms>` (Claude) and its time in epoch milliseconds, `{"by", "x"}`. A comment waits for Claude where its line's conversation is open — `s` `comment`, or a tick on a wording since changed — and its last message is the person's. **It refuses**, every refusal opening `spec marks: `, storing nothing: a unit the project lacks; a unit with no `spec.md`; a concluded spec (`{wu}'s spec is concluded — its review ended when the build began`); a page not published (`{wu}'s review page is not published — publish it, record it with spec published {wu} <url>, then read its marks back`); a file it cannot read as the marks, naming every fault, one per line — a document other than `s<N>`, a mark on an id that is no line's, a status, fingerprint, time or message of another shape; and a spec its grammar cannot read. It writes nothing to the state branch and commits nothing. Response `{"ok": true, "unit", "waiting": [{"number", "id", "doc": "s<N>", "wording", "conversation": [{"by": "person"|"claude", "at", "text"}]}]}`, `doc` the marks' document Claude answers the line in. Nothing on the page reaches Claude by itself, so the comments waiting and the record are only as fresh as the marks last stored: ready to build's yes takes the page's marks while it is open, storing them as `marks` does before it reads the comments waiting and writes the record (`settleReview`). The review's record, `{wu}/spec-review.json`, is written by that yes, never by Claude, and only where the page was open: `{"lines": {"<number>": {"state", "conversation"}}}`, `state` one of `approved`, `approved-after-comment`, `claudes-tick`, `open`, `changed`, `passed` and `removed` — `claudes-tick` a line carrying Claude's tick that the person neither ticked on its wording nor ever unticked, whatever wording the untick held.

**`page`** writes the person's review page and the first writes to its store at `.cache/{wu}/specification/page/` (`domain/spec-page.cjs`), and a page built is not yet open: it opens once Claude publishes it and records that with **`published`**. The page is one fixed page in every project, `content/spec-page/page.html`, filled with the spec's title and, as its data, the store's `spec` collection; every line, its flags and Claude's tick come from the page's model, never from a second reading of `spec.md`. Claude publishes `page.html` as a private artifact with the icon and capabilities the answer gives — `db` alone, each of the engine's collections read by viewers and written by the page's admins; nothing that brings another Claude in — then sends each batch file with ArtifactData's batch writes, in order. The store's collections, beside the page's own `marks`: `spec`, `index` `{"title", "h1", "intro", "sections": [{"num", "title", "count"}], "total", "c", "f"}` (`c` the last change the spec holds, 0 before any) and `s<N>` an area `{"items", "f"}` — a part's heading `{"k": "h3", "t"}`, the area's scenarios' `{"k": "h2s", "t"}`, a line `{"k": "line", "id", "num", "kind", "after"?, "parts", "src", "flags", "h"}` (`flags` any of `claude`, `builder` and `screen`), and a line the spec no longer holds `{"k": "removed", "id", "num", "kind", "parts", "src"?, "c"}` kept where it stood; `history`, `s<N>` `{"lines": {"<id>": [{"h", "at", "parts", "c"}]}}`, each line's earlier wordings, oldest first, `c` the change that replaced each; `changes`, `c<n>` `{"n", "at", "why", "from", "added", "reworded", "removed"}`; `assess`, `s<N>` `{"lines": {"<id>": {"v": "soft"|"open", "why", "h", "at"}}}`, Claude's tick on each line and the wording it holds for; and `live`, `status` `{"x", "at"}`. A document's `f` is the first 12 hex digits of the SHA-256 of its JSON without `f`, keys sorted. The first writes are the areas, the index, Claude's ticks and an empty status line; each batch file lists at most 40 writes and 900 KiB, `[{"op": "set", "collection", "doc_id", "file_path"}]`. **It refuses**, every refusal opening `spec page: `, writing nothing: a unit the project lacks; one with no `spec.md`; a concluded spec (`{wu}'s spec is concluded — its review ended when the build began`); a spec its grammar cannot read; and a document over the store's limit of 245,760 bytes (`… — split the section`). It commits nothing. Response `{"ok": true, "unit", "page": "<path>", "title", "icon": "checklist", "capabilities": {"db": {"rules": [{"path", "read": "view", "write": "admin"}, …]}}, "lines": N, "batches": ["<path>", …]}`, `title` the spec's `# Title`.

**`published`** records that Claude published the unit's page, `spec published <wu> <url>`, the artifact's address: it writes `.cache/{wu}/specification/published.json`, `{"url", "at"}`, beside the page and outside it, so building the page again never closes it. That opens the page: from then on `spec land` holds its numbers, `spec marks` reads its marks back, ready to build's yes takes them, and `spec push` plans its changes. **It refuses**, every refusal opening `spec published: `, writing nothing: a unit the project lacks; one with no `spec.md`; a concluded spec; a unit with no page built (`{wu} has no review page built — build it with spec page {wu}`); and an address that is no URL (`{url} is no page's address — give the artifact's URL`). It commits nothing. Response `{"ok": true, "unit", "url", "at", "read_back"}`, `read_back` the file Claude reads the page's marks back to, `.cache/{wu}/specification/read-back.json`, absolute — named by the spec's entry and its recovery note too, while the page is open (**`enter`**).

**`push`** plans, after a re-land, the writes that bring the change to the open page without republishing it (11.11.17), into the page's next `push-<k>/`. The engine cannot read the store, so `--store` names a folder holding what it holds, as ArtifactData's list saves the `spec`, `history`, `changes` and `assess` collections with `out_dir`: `<dir>/<collection>/<doc>.json`. It records one change, numbered past the store's last: `--reason`, its one line, needed only where a line changed; `--from`, the lines whose conversations led to it, by number or id, comma-separated; and the lines it added, reworded and removed, which the engine computes. A reworded line's last wording joins its history; a removed line stays where it stood, and an area that loses every line stays as its removed lines alone, counted 0 in the index, its lines added again when it comes back; a line whose number comes back is that line again, the wording removed joining its history; a change to a line's sources alone, or to how it wraps, changes no line and records no change. It writes only the documents that differ from the store's, the history first, then the changes, the areas, the index last, then Claude's ticks redrawn, each keeping when it was made where it holds the same: until the index lands, the store's newest change is numbered past the index's `c`, so a push that stopped part way, planned again from a fresh listing, finishes that change rather than counting another. A write to a document the store already holds takes the version its listing gave as `if_version`: `existing` names each. **It refuses**, every refusal opening `spec push: `, writing nothing: what `page` refuses; a unit with no page built (`{wu} has no review page built — build it with spec page {wu}`); a page not published (`{wu}'s review page is not published — publish it, then record it with spec published {wu} <url>`); a listing that is no folder; a line in `--from` the spec and the store both lack (`--from names lines the spec does not have: …`); and lines changed with no reason (`this push changes lines (…): say why with --reason "<one line>"`). It commits nothing. Response `{"ok": true, "unit", "url", "change": {"n", "at", "why", "from", "added", "reworded", "removed"}|null, "resumed": bool, "batches": ["<path>", …], "existing": ["<collection>/<doc>", …]}`, `url` the page's address as published, `resumed` where it finished a push that stopped; with `"note": "the store already holds this spec: nothing to write"` and no batches where nothing differs.

```bash
engine spec land <work-unit>                     # the unit's spec.md checked, traced against the ledger and committed
engine spec marks <work-unit> --file <marks.json>   # the review page's marks stored, answering the comments waiting for Claude
engine spec page <work-unit>                     # the review page and its store's first writes, for Claude to publish
engine spec published <work-unit> <url>          # the page's publishing recorded, which opens it
engine spec push <work-unit> --store <dir> [--reason <line>] [--from <ids>]   # a re-land's change planned for the open page
```

**`shell`** — `shell guard`, the hook before Bash (`domain/shell-guard.cjs`, its reader `kernel/shell-reader.cjs`), its JSON on stdin, taking no lock. Boot installs it in every project, matcher `Bash`, so it holds with or without the mod. It reads `tool_input.command` as the shell would run it: simple commands split at `;`, `&&`, `||`, `|`, `&`, a newline and a subshell's parentheses; a quoted word, a heredoc's body and a comment as data, a command substitution's own commands read as commands; the assignments, the reserved words a list opens with, and `command` and `env` with their options passed over before a command's name; a `cd` or `pushd` followed within its subshell, and a `popd` back to the folder its `pushd` left; and git's global options (`-C`, `-c`, `--git-dir`, `--work-tree`, `--no-pager`) read before its subcommand. It refuses, on stderr with exit 2 — the code Claude Code blocks the call on — the first command that meets one of these, each refusal with the way on: `git worktree unlock|remove|move` of a path ending in `.leeway`; `rm -r` of `.leeway`, `.leeway/`, `.leeway/.` or `.leeway/*`, a file under it allowed; `git branch -d|-D|--delete leeway-state` and `git update-ref -d refs/heads/leeway-state`; `git push --delete|-d leeway-state`, or a refspec `:leeway-state`; `git push` with `-f` or `--force…` and a refspec naming `leeway-state`, a `+` refspec naming it, `--mirror`, `--all` or `--branches` with force, and, from the state worktree, a refspec naming `HEAD` or `@` — the branch checked out there — or a forced push naming no refspec; a raw `git commit` in the state worktree — the folder it runs in, moved by each `-C`, or a `--git-dir` or `--work-tree`, with `.leeway` in its path — at any time; a raw `git commit` wherever it runs while the conversation stdin's `session_id` names has a task in flight on its position (a subagent's call carries the conversation's), its way on `engine commit --paths <file>… --task <unit> <N.M> -m "<message>"` for each task in flight, and, as long, every other git command that makes a commit past the door where the commit would land on a task's branch — one run where the checkout of its folder has out, by git's own files, a branch of a unit with a task in flight, a rebase's stopped on one among them, or where the text cannot say which folder it runs in; anywhere else it passes, the engine's own landing remedies on a phase's branch or the branch the project lands on among them — a `git merge` but with both `--no-ff` and `--no-commit`, or with `--abort` or `--quit`; a `git pull`; a `git rebase` but with `--abort` or `--quit`; a `git cherry-pick` or `git revert` but with `--no-commit`, `-n`, `--abort` or `--quit`; a `git am` but with `--abort` or `--quit` — its way on the command's way to make no commit, then the same door, which finishes a merge: for a merge, a pull (fetched first) and a rebase, a merge with `--no-ff --no-commit`, what conflicts resolved, and for a `git merge --continue`, each file resolved named in the door's call; and a command any of whose words or redirections' targets names, by its path from the command's folder (a leading `~` the home folder), a file at user level the layout hook refuses Claude's writes to — a conversation's briefs, the folder or a file in it, the brief hook's refusals, the trust log and its kin, Claude Code's transcripts — a read among them, since the text cannot tell one from a write, its way on the Read and Grep tools; and a command writing to a file in the state only the engine writes — the manifests, the ledger, `spec-review.json` — for the layout hook's reason, naming the verb that writes it: an output redirection onto it (`>`, `>>`, `>|`, `&>`, `<>`, `>&` a file, each with a descriptor), `tee` onto it, an in-place `sed` (`-i`, `-I`, `--in-place`) or `perl -i` naming it, `mv` from or onto it, `cp`, `install` or `ln` onto it — the last word, or the file in it a source would land as — and `rm`, `unlink`, `truncate`, `touch` or `dd of=` of it, the file read from the command's folder, the part of its path after the first `.leeway` read by the layout as the layout hook reads Claude's own write; reading one passes. It decides from the text, with one read of the position for a commit alone, and for a write into the state alone the layout and whether the state's filesystem ignores case, and never asks git. A pass says nothing and writes nothing, and so does a call it cannot read. A word that expands at run time — a glob among them — an alias, a script, and a command handed to another as text (`sh -c`, `eval`, `xargs`) are not seen. Where the text cannot say which folder a command runs in — after a `cd` to such a word, or `cd -` — it is not taken for the state worktree, and a relative path there names no file at user level or in the state; but an `rm -r` or a `git worktree` of a relative `.leeway` is refused there, its name enough. Like the other guards, it is answered at the shell door before the engine loads any module but its own (`guards.cjs`).

```bash
engine shell guard                                                   # the hook before Bash, its JSON on stdin
```

**`checklist`** — the checklist of a closing point, held by the engine (`domain/checklist.cjs`): Claude works its items in any order, each ending done or skipped with a reason, and closes it once none is open. **The points**, each named by what follows it: `decided <work-unit> <topic>`, a topic's close; `ready-to-build <work-unit>`; `phase-start <work-unit> <N>` and `phase-close <work-unit> <N>`, for a phase the unit's cut holds; `finish <work-unit>`; and `directive <id>`, a directive the migrations hold, by its id, `NNN-{slug}`. **The items**, by key, in order, each shown with its text, the spec's words: decided — `questions-closed` (the engine's), `closing-pass`, `decisions-entered`, `replacements-checked`, `conflicts-read`, `findings-rerun`, `mailbox-empty` (the engine's), `product-lines-shown`; ready to build — `topics-settled` (the engine's), `sweep-run` (the engine's, only where the unit has more than one live topic), `replacements-checked`, `statements-traced` (the engine's), `review-settled` (the engine's), `phases-complete` (the engine's), `invariants-checked`, `sections-drawn` (the engine's), `assets-linked` (the engine's); a phase's start — `tasks-written` (the engine's), `design-settled`, `relies-on-done` (the engine's); a phase's close — `tasks-passed`, `phase-review-passed`, `scenarios-reported`, `notes-consolidated`, `stack-merged`, each the engine's; finish — `review-passed` (the engine's, only where the unit has a review phase: every type the build takes, and a quick-fix only once the person asked for its review at its close, the request recorded as `fixes.review_asked` on its manifest), `questions-answered` (the engine's), `notes-answered` (the engine's, only for a quick-fix whose review was not asked for: each note for later answered at its close, **`quick-fix`** `notes`), `inbox-closed` (the engine's), `build-log-closed`, `records-indexed` (the engine's, only where the checkout's knowledge base is set up and reads, as **`knowledge`** `check` reads it — one set up that does not read takes the item off with a warning saying so, which finish's screen and yes carry); a directive — `check` (the engine's). **The engine's items** are worked out at every call and never kept: `questions-closed`, no question open on the topic's map (`ledger map`); `mailbox-empty`, no `.md` file in `{wu}/topics/{topic}/mail/`; `topics-settled`, the unit's topics settled as `enter specification` reads them (**`enter`**); `sweep-run`, the unit's sweep taken, its record `{wu}/sweep-taken.json` standing (**`sweep`**) — one sweep for the spec, and a cleared cache leaves it standing — and none of its candidates waiting on the person; `phases-complete`, the unit's cut holding phases, each naming a scenario and a section, what it relies on maybe nothing, and the cut holding against `spec.md` as it stands as **`cut`** checks one, read without writing — every scenario in one phase, no scenario or section the spec lacks, what each relies on — so a land since that adds or drops one opens it — and no decision made at the spec since the brief whose cut last landed waiting for it (**`brief`**'s `cut-requests`); `statements-traced`, every statement and screen of `spec.md` tracing (**`spec`**), a spec with no `spec.md` or one its grammar cannot read reading open; `review-settled`, the review's last pass changing nothing: the latest spec review whose report is in — as the reviewer's brief reads it, a pass whose report no scan has taken in passed over — with no section of `spec.md` changed since its land, no gap waiting on the person — a question asked at the unit's own level at the spec still open, or a review's gap surfaced as the replacement of a decision still current and unkept (**`agent`**) — and nothing waiting — for the writer (**`brief`**'s `changed-entries` and `review-findings`); to be surfaced: each `gap` or `fact` finding of a pass whose report was taken in, surfaced (`agent surface`) or declined, its pass acknowledged with it and then incorporated; or in flight: each pass bound to a land and not yet taken in, its report on disk for `agent scan` to take in, its agent running — where it returned without writing its report, `agent incorporate` abandons it and another pass is dispatched — or lost with the process that dispatched it, for `agent incorporate` to abandon; a pass abandoned in flight is ignored whole, its findings neither waiting nor dealt with, and never the latest pass reported; `sections-drawn`, every part of `spec.md` drawn on by a phase of the cut, itself or through its area, and every area with no parts itself; `assets-linked`, every file or folder the unit's current entries link as canonical linked from `spec.md` — a markdown link read from the spec's folder, then from the state root, to it on disk, a folder's to it or to what it holds (11.6.3) — its text naming the way out for one gone or no longer needed, `asset status <work-unit> <path> reference`; a phase's (`domain/phase-checks.cjs`), each read once a call — the phase's tasks under the state's lock, as every read of the tracker takes it, and what landed — where a task of the phase is one of it not closed: `tasks-written`, each of the phase's scenarios an acceptance scenario of exactly one task in the tracker (12.4.3), so a phase not written there has none; `relies-on-done`, each earlier phase the phase relies on with its close recorded and its stack merged, and each other unit completed — a phase relying on nothing unfinished starts beside one in its review (12.7.5); `tasks-passed`, every task, one at least, passed by its pair — its last round passed by every reviewer of the count, or settled (**`task`**'s `settle`) — rounds that do not read failing it, said as a warning; `phase-review-passed`, a report of the phase review from every reviewer of the count, `{wu}/build/phases/phase-{N}/review-{letter}.md` — or each area's, `{area}-{letter}.md`, where the review was split — each reading as a reviewer's report, passing; or, where one failed, the orchestrator's settlement answering those reports as they stand, every fix task it names done (13.10.1, **`task`**'s `settle --phase`), one closed naming the settlement given again as its way on — every task of the phase done answering nothing — its open line naming what stands in the way, the settlement its way on until there is one; `scenarios-reported`, each of the phase's scenarios proven by its runs at the phase's stack's tips (**`verify`**), or else named, in its standing's words — `not proven`, `named, not run` — by a finding of every report of the phase review, none missing or unread, a stack whose tips cannot be read proving nothing, said as a warning — its open line naming each scenario neither, in its standing's words, with the ways on: a run from a worktree at the tips (**`verify`** `run`), or the phase review's reports naming it; `notes-consolidated`, no note for later left for the phase to take, or its consolidation report, `{wu}/build/phases/phase-{N}/consolidation.md`, taking each of them once (**`task`**'s `note`); `stack-merged`, every task, one at least, landed — a commit on the default branch of a repository of the project naming it on a `Task:` line, as every landing driver lands one (**`land`**) — or the build in step, its stack waiting at its gate holding every task of the phase not closed, one at least: each opened into the phase's stack wherever its change touches — a repository whose branch for it holds nothing past the default branch passed over — as the landing driver reads its stack (`holds`), fetching and writing nothing, each repository's stacks read once a call; a task opened nowhere not, nor one whose landing cannot be read, said as a warning (7.2.4); finish's (`domain/finish-checks.cjs`), each read once a call: `review-passed`, the review phase over as **`review`** reads it — a round passed or settled, its report written, and every fix task that round made done or closed — its open line naming what stands in the way, the review's entry refusal (**`enter`**) where it cannot be entered yet; `questions-answered`, no question of the unit waiting on the person, as **`raise`**'s `waiting` reads them — wherever it was raised, the review phase's among them — its open line naming each, the question and what it asks, with the build's stop, `render build-stop {wu}`, that puts them to the person — and, at a quick-fix, no question asked at its places still open with no park holding it, each named with the raise that asks it again (`{id}, {question}, asked in the conversation and still open — raise it again for the person to answer: raise {wu}.{phase} --answers {id}`, 19.5.25); `notes-answered`, no note for later of a quick-fix's standing — banked by **`task`**'s `note`, answered by no round and not at its close (**`quick-fix`** `notes`) — its open line naming each and the verb (`notes#{n}, … — each note for later is filed to the inbox or dropped before the close: quick-fix notes {wu} --file <notes.json>`); `inbox-closed`, no item the unit's build log, `{wu}/build/log.md`, claims resolved still live in the inbox — the claim a line of its own, `Resolved: {path}`, the path read from `.inbox/` on, wherever it is rooted, and a mention anywhere else in the log no claim — its open line naming each, the close, and the claim taken back (`the build's log claims {paths} resolved, still in the inbox — close each the work resolved: inbox close {wu} <path> …; where the work did not resolve one after all, take its Resolved: line out of the log`); a unit whose log claims none owes nothing; `records-indexed`, every record of the unit's in `.leeway/.knowledge/records/` held by the store as it stands, none never indexed or changed since, its open line naming each and the way on, `knowledge index --work-unit {wu}`; `check`, the directive's check passing (**`migrate`**). Claude marks the rest. **The record**, on the manifest of the work it closes, under `checklists`: the unit's `decided.{topic}`, `ready-to-build`, `phase-{N}-start`, `phase-{N}-close` and `finish`, and the project's `directive.{id}`, each `{"items": {"<key>": {"state": "done"|"skipped", "reason"?, "at"}}, "closed"?: "<at>"}` — Claude's marks alone, each skip with its reason, and the last close; decided's also keeps `"ledger_at": <n>`, the ledger's last line as it closed, `"voided"?: "<at>"`, when the topic's discussion went on while the close stood — a message or an experiment's report delivered (**`topic`**), a feature's discussion entered again (**`enter`**), a question asked on it or moved onto it (**`ledger`**) — so the manifest alone says whether a close stands, and `"taken"?: "<at>"`, when the decided gate's yes took the close (**`decided`**); ready to build's keeps `"spec_hash"` and `"cut_hash"`, the first 12 hex digits of the SHA-256 of the landed `spec.md`, as the state branch's head holds it, and of the cut's JSON as it closed — its close refused while `spec.md` on disk is not the landed one (`{wu}'s spec has changes that haven't landed — land them: spec land {wu}`, or `{wu}'s spec has not landed — land it: spec land {wu}`) — and its gate stands only on a close neither has changed since — a land or a cut after it voids it (E12) — every engine item holding still (**`ready-to-build`**). A mark counts only once made after the last close, so a checklist closed again — a decided topic reopened by mail — is worked afresh. A decided close shows no decided screen once something new has come to the topic since it — a message delivered, or a question opened on the topic, asked there or moved to it, a line of the ledger past `ledger_at` — nor once the yes has taken it, until the checklist is closed afresh; a decision changed since voids nothing. **`show`** answers `{"ok": true, "unit"?, "checklist": "<name>", "closed": "<at>"|null, "items": [{"key", "text", "by": "engine"|"claude", "state": "done"|"skipped"|"open", "reason"?, "at"?, "way"?}], "skips": [{"checklist", "item", "text", "reason", "at"}]}`, with `warnings` where its read of the ledger gave any; `way`, on an open engine item that names one — `phase-review-passed`, `scenarios-reported`, `review-passed`, `questions-answered`, `notes-answered`, `inbox-closed` and `records-indexed` — what stands in the way of it, each fault saying its way on; `skips` are those its gate shows: decided's and ready to build's their own, finish's every phase start's and close's as their records keep them, in the cut's order, then its own, and none for a phase's start or close or a directive. It takes no lock. **`done`** and **`skip`** record Claude's mark of an item, refusing one the checklist lacks and one the engine checks, and `skip` a blank reason; each answers `{"ok": true, "unit"?, "checklist", "item", "state", "reason"?, "at", "committed"}`. **`close`** refuses while any item is open, naming each, and phase 1's close until the verify setup is recorded (`phase 1 of {wu} closes once the verify setup is recorded — the first phase gets the product running and drivable: verify setup {wu} --file <setup.json> --scenario <scenario>`, 12.3.3), a recorded setup that does not read said as a warning — a refusal beyond the items, which list the spec's alone; a decided close while an experiment of its topic is still out — conceived, designed, approved or running — its report owed (`{topic}'s experiment {id} is still out — the topic closes once each has concluded or been abandoned, and its report is read; one no one will run is abandoned: engine experiment abandon {wu} {topic} {id} --reason "<one line>"`, 11.2.1); and otherwise records the close — a directive's close records the directive in the migrations record too, `.state/migrations`, so the next boot runs past it — answering `{"ok": true, "unit"?, "checklist", "closed", "committed"}`. Each write runs under the writer lock and commits the manifest it wrote alone, `checklist({wu}): {checklist} {item} done|skipped`, `checklist({wu}): {checklist} closed`, or `checklist: directive.{id} …` for the project's, pushed — a phase's close, beside it, every record its folder `{wu}/build/phases/phase-{N}/` holds, its review's reports, its consolidation report and its settlement, so a second clone reads the phase as this one did — with `warnings` and a pending note where the commit failed, or the held note while the migration runs (**`commit`**). **It refuses**, every refusal opening `checklist {verb}: `, writing nothing: a unit, topic or phase the project lacks, a unit with no cut for a phase's point, a phase's start before the unit's build has begun (`{wu}'s build has not begun, so no phase starts — ready to build's yes begins it: render ready-to-build {wu}`, 11.8.3), and a directive's id of another shape or one the migrations lack; arguments of another shape answer the usage.

```bash
engine checklist show  <checklist>                              # each item done, skipped or open, and the skips its gate shows
engine checklist done  <checklist> <item>                       # Claude's item done
engine checklist skip  <checklist> <item> --reason "<text>"     # Claude's item skipped, its reason recorded
engine checklist close <checklist>                              # the close, refused while any item is open
```

**`decided`** — a topic's close, the person's yes at the decided gate (`domain/decided.cjs`, the screens `domain/projections/decided.cjs` over `domain/projections/closing.cjs`). A feature's topic has no decided gate: it closes into its spec, staged with `stage`, and its close is shown and taken at ready to build (**`ready-to-build`**, 11.2.1, 11.8.5). **The screen**, `render decided <wu>.<topic> [--file <payload.json>] [--calls]`, stands on decided's checklist closed, nothing new come to the topic since, and the close not yet taken (**`checklist`**), a discussion in progress, and the unit in progress; a feature is refused, as its yes is (`{wu} is a feature, whose topic closes into its spec with no gate, its decided shown with ready to build — once decided's checklist is closed, decided stage {wu} {topic} --file <payload.json> stages Claude's lines and candidates and hands off into the spec`). Its payload is Claude's, exactly `{"lines": [{"text", "why", "widens"}], "withdraws": [{"line", "why"}], "candidates": [{"name", "reason", "brief"}]}` for an epic, each candidate `{"name", "reason", "horizon"}` for a unit with one topic, `withdraws` optional — the product-record lines, each widening a decision on the topic's map, by id, into a rule for the product; the lines of the record it withdraws, each by id a current product-wide decision widening a decision of this topic, with why; and the work no topic owns. An epic's candidates are new topics, each with its brief — `brief` its draft under `.leeway/.cache/` — and an optional `from_roadmap` taking in the waiting roadmap item of its name, every one checked by every rule `topic add-batch` checks, the roadmap's twin among them; a unit with one topic parks its candidates on the roadmap, `horizon` the person's — where they named none, `render horizon-pick --park <name>` asks them — each refused where it is on the roadmap already or the person struck its name before — a struck name the person roadmaps themselves, `roadmap add` taking it; a candidate missing its `brief` or `horizon`, or carrying the other's, is refused naming the key; `--file` stages it at `.cache/{wu}/discussion/{topic}/decided.json`, stamped with the close it was staged for, and the screen read again draws from what is staged. A line is refused where a product-wide decision standing in the record relies on the decision it widens already — a line landed for it at an earlier yes, or by a yes killed after it landed — naming that entry; a replacement of the decision is another decision, which a line may widen, and where a line of the record widens a decision the widened one replaced, the new line replaces it. A payload widening one decision twice is refused (`line N widens X, as line M does`), as is one withdrawing a line twice, or withdrawing a line a staged line replaces (`withdrawal N withdraws X, which line M replaces — a record line is replaced or withdrawn`); nothing reaches the ledger or the map before the yes. A restage mints its lines afresh and keeps the strike of every candidate it still names, whatever order it names them in, so a strike survives a comment that changes a line. The display: what the person decided — where nothing was, what every question was instead: a call of Claude's, carried over or moved on, or that no question was asked; the rows carried over, in a block of their own, never among the person's decisions or Claude's calls; Claude's calls counted by kind, most first, and those the labeller gave no label counted last, listed with `--calls`, a call the person handed over tagged so beside its kind (`your call`, `don't care`); the lines, one replacing a line of the record shown with `↳ Replaces:` and the text it replaces, then each withdrawal by its why with `↳ Withdraws:` and the text it withdraws, the block counting both (`FOR THE PRODUCT RECORD (1 line · 1 withdrawn)`); the candidates, each with its reason, a struck one marked — a one-topic unit's under `TO THE ROADMAP`, each tagged with its horizon, the strike keeping it off the roadmap; every skip on the checklist, with its reason. Then, while a departure on the topic logged by this machine has had no verdict asked of it, the verdict gate over the first, counted among the departures this close asks — one given its verdict before the close is not among them — `MENU: verdict`, whose rows run `verdict give <entry> <verdict> --at decided`, the block titled by the question the departure answers — the ledger holds no short title, so the screen carries no `Decides:` line — above Claude's answer and the person's; `verdict give` answers no `next`, so the screen is drawn again after it (`render decided <wu>.<topic>`), and asks the next departure or shows the gate; once none is, `MENU: decided`, an authority gate that always stops, its yes saying what it covers, the counts up to nine in words (`with the two product-record lines, the withdrawal and the candidate`): `y/yes` runs `decided yes`, `c/calls` lists Claude's calls where they are not listed, `s/strike` strikes a lone standing candidate (`decided strike`) or opens the pick among several (`render decided-strike <wu>.<topic>`, `MENU: decided strike`, its rows the standing candidates by number and `b/back`), and a comment changes the payload. **`yes <wu> <topic>`** first checks everything it writes against the ledger and the map as they stand — each line by the checks its landing makes, each candidate not struck by the rules its landing makes — so a refused yes writes nothing. It then lands each staged line as an entry of its own at the unit's level, off the topic's map (`ledger`, said `decided yes`): a question in the wording of the one the widened decision answers, relying on that decision, by Claude; and the person's product-wide decision answering it, relying on the decision too — so the topic's map and its screen are as they were. Then each withdrawal lands as the person's replacement of the line (`replaced`, `reason: changed-mind`, at the unit's level and not product-wide, its text the decision the widened one now stands as, relying on it, dropping the line's text), so the line leaves `ledger product` and its trail stays. Each line and withdrawal, the person's decision, takes its `kind` and `hard_to_undo` from the labeller's label of its question, read from the wording alone, every label put at once before the first lands (**`ledger`**, **The labeller**). Each lands as the ids minted when it was staged, and a raise the ledger holds already passes the check and lands nothing, so a yes run again after a kill lands none twice. Then, in one save of the unit's manifest, it adds each of an epic's candidates not struck to the discovery map (`source: topic:{topic}`, its reason the summary, a `from_roadmap` one joining its roadmap item), or parks each of a one-topic unit's on the roadmap under its horizon (`origin: topic:{wu}/{topic}`, its reason the summary, its one source the topic's log); records each struck name among the unit's dismissed names, so the map's add refuses it without `--re-add` and the closing-pass reader sees it; marks the discussion completed and stamps the close taken; installs each landed candidate's brief from its draft; commits `decided({wu}): {topic}` — the briefs and, where an item parked or joined, the project manifest riding it — removes the staged payload, and answers `{"ok": true, "unit", "topic", "decided": "<at>", "lines": [<id>…], "withdrawn": [<id>…], "candidates": [<name>…], "struck": [<name>…], "committed", "next"}`, `next` an epic's spec, at the unit (`leeway-specification-process epic {unit}`), once its every live topic is decided, read over the manifest as the yes saved it — a topic its own candidates landed counting, one set aside meanwhile not — else the epic's menu, where the person picks the next topic (24.1.2, 8.8.13). A staged line that replaces one lands as the person's replacement of it (`replaced`, `reason: changed-mind`, product-wide, relying on the decision it widens), never as a new question and answer. It refuses while a verdict is still to ask, while a line of the product record widens a decision of the topic replaced since and nothing staged replaces or withdraws it (`the product record's line {id}, "{text}", widens a decision of {topic} replaced since, and nothing staged replaces or withdraws it — a line widening the replacement, or a withdrawal of the line, is staged with --file`), without a conversation to write in, and where a line or withdrawal cannot land — the decision it widens or the line it replaces no longer current since it was staged — or the map or the roadmap would refuse a candidate; either is staged again with `--file`. **`strike <wu> <topic> <n> [--page]`** strikes the n-th staged candidate, answering `{"ok": true, "unit", "topic", "struck", "next"}`, `next` the screen again — a feature's ready to build, `--page` carried to it — which a gate's answer runs, as `verdict give` does not. **`unstrike <wu> <topic> <n> [--page]`** undoes the n-th candidate's strike — the route a comment keeping a struck candidate takes — answering `"unstruck"` in place of `"struck"`. Each refuses a candidate the payload lacks, a strike made already, and an unstrike of a candidate not struck. **`stage <wu> <topic> --file <payload.json>`** is a feature's close into its spec: standing as the screen stands, it stages the payload as `--file` does, refused as it is, and answers `{"ok": true, "unit", "topic", "lines": N, "withdrawals": N, "candidates": [<name>…], "next"}`, `next` the handoff into the spec (`leeway-specification-process feature {wu}`) while the spec is not written, else `continue`, a restage at ready to build; another unit's topic is refused (`{wu} is an epic, whose topic closes at its decided screen — render decided {wu}.{topic} --file <payload.json> stages Claude's lines and candidates`). **It refuses**, every refusal opening on its command and writing nothing: a unit not in progress, a topic with no discussion, a topic decided already or not in progress, a checklist open, taken, voided by a message delivered or a question opened since its close, or with items open since it, nothing staged or a payload staged for an earlier close, each naming the call that stages it, and a payload of another shape. A message to a decided topic sets it back in progress (**`topic`**); its close is worked afresh before the screen shows again.

**`start-this-work`** — discovery's authority gate at the work-type commit, the person's yes creating the unit (`domain/start-this-work.cjs`, the screen `domain/projections/discovery-gates.cjs`). **The screen**, `render start-this-work [--file <payload.json>]`, takes no address: the unit is the payload's to name. Its payload is Claude's, exactly `{"type", "name", "description", "reason", "shaped_by": [<place>…], "seeds"?: [<inbox path>…], "roadmap"?: [<item>…], "brief"?: <draft>}` — the type, a feature, an epic, a bugfix or a quick-fix; the name Claude derived; the description; the one-line reason for the type; the places in the product's sessions that shaped the work, each `.product/sessions/session-NNN.md` with a section's `#anchor` where one is named, at least one and each on disk; the inbox items and the waiting roadmap items it starts from; and a feature's or a bugfix's one topic's brief, a draft in the cache, which any other type is refused (`an epic's topics get their briefs as the map is approved — drop the brief`, `a quick-fix has no topic to brief — drop the brief`). `--file` stages it, checked, at `.cache/product/discovery/product/start-this-work.json`, and the screen read again draws from what is staged; nothing is created before the yes. It is refused for a type it does not create — any but a feature, an epic, a bugfix or a quick-fix (`the type is one of feature, epic, bugfix, quick-fix — got "{type}"`), a name a unit has (`a work unit is named "{name}" already — derive a more specific name from the work, never a number`) and a taken name with a number appended (`"{name}" numbers the taken name "{base}" — …`), a seed `workunit create` would refuse, a roadmap item the roadmap lacks, one joined already or one named twice, a missing brief for a feature or a bugfix, and a feature or a bugfix named like a waiting roadmap idea it does not start from (`"{name}" would stand beside the waiting roadmap item of its name — take it in with "roadmap": ["{name}"], or name the topic apart`). The display: the work's title and type, its description, and `↳` its reason; then `MENU: start this work`, an authority gate that always stops, its lead saying what the yes creates and where the work goes next: `y/yes` runs `start-this-work yes`, `Keep shaping` takes the person's word. **`yes`** checks the staged payload against the project as it stands, so a refused yes writes nothing, then as one transaction writes the unit as `workunit create` does — its manifest, its seeds moved out of the inbox, the note of each roadmap item it starts from copied into `seeds/` beside them, recorded with `source: roadmap:{item}`, and its first discovery log, `discovery/session-001.md`, holding a line opening `Shaped by: ` for each place, a link from the log's folder — the one topic's brief at `topics/{unit}/brief.md`, and then, in one write of the project manifest, the unit's registration, each roadmap item joined to it (`pulled_to: {work_unit}`) and the product's open session closed. A failure part-way puts the unit back and leaves the inbox and the project manifest as they were. It removes the draft and the staged payload, commits `discovery({name}): start this work ({type})` — the unit, its seeds' inbox paths, the project manifest and, where a session closed, the product's sessions — and answers `{"ok": true, "work_unit", "work_type", "seeds", "roadmap", "session_log", "brief"?, "product_session_closed", "committed", "next"}`, `next` for an epic `{"kind": "engine", "argv": ["enter", "discovery", "{name}", "--move"]}` — the conversation carried on into its own discovery — for a quick-fix its skill invoked in this same conversation, nothing cleared and no handoff for the gate mod to carry (19.5.1, 24.1.5), `{"kind": "invoke", "skill": "leeway-quick-fix-process", "args": ["quick-fix", "{name}"]}`; and for a feature or a bugfix the handoff to its first phase, `leeway-discussion-process` or `leeway-investigation-process` with `[type, name]`. Every refusal opens on its command; nothing staged answers `nothing is staged — render start-this-work --file <payload.json> stages the work`. A unit's move stages here too, through `workunit move` (**`workunit`**): the screen, read again, draws it, and its yes moves the unit rather than creating one. What is staged last is what the yes lands.

**`approve-map`** — the authority gate at the end of an epic's shaping, the person's yes landing the map (`domain/approve-map.cjs`, the screen `domain/projections/discovery-gates.cjs`). **The screen**, `render approve-map <wu> [--file <payload.json>]`, stands on an epic in progress whose map is open (the refusals `enter discovery` makes). Its payload is Claude's, exactly `{"topics": [<topic>…], "parks"?: [<park>…], "binds"?: [{"item", "topic"}…]}`: every live topic of the map in its order, foundations first — one on the map `{"name"}` alone, by its key or its title, a new one `{"name", "summary", "brief", "description"?, "from_roadmap"?}`, its brief a draft in the cache and `from_roadmap` the waiting roadmap item it takes in; each park `{"name", "summary", "horizon", "sources", "kind"?}`, a roadmap item on the horizon the person gave it, `sources` the logs that shaped it, at least one and each on disk; and each bind, a roadmap item the epic was started from, not yet bound, which becomes a topic, bound to a staged topic. `--file` stages it, checked, at `.cache/{wu}/discovery/{wu}/approve-map.json`, and the screen read again draws from what is staged; nothing reaches the map or the roadmap before the yes. It is refused for a park with no horizon (`park "{name}": it has no horizon — the horizon is the person's: ask with render horizon-pick --park {name}, before the screen`), a topic on the map given more than its name (`… so it is named alone — its summary and brief change at the person's word: topic edit, topic brief`), a topic cancelled or postponed, a live topic left out (`the map's "{name}" is left out — every live topic stands in the order, and a topic leaves the map at the person's word alone: topic remove`), a name struck from the map (`… it comes back only at the person's ask, through topic add --re-add`), a new topic beside a waiting roadmap item of its name no topic takes in (`"{name}" would stand beside the waiting roadmap item of its name — take it in with "from_roadmap": "{name}", or name the topic apart`), a topic named like a roadmap item the epic was started from that the payload does not bind to it (`"{name}" would stand beside the roadmap item {wu} was started from, unbound — bind it with {"item": "{name}", "topic": "{name}"} in binds, or name the topic apart`), a take-in `topic add` would refuse, a name or item standing twice, a park named as a topic or as a roadmap item already, and a bind of another item or to a topic not staged. The display: `THE MAP (N topics, foundations first)`, each topic by its title — and, on a re-shape, the map approved before, its topics holding their `order`, a topic it did not order tagged `[new]` and an ordered one off the longest run kept in its approved order `[moved]`, so a topic moved is the one tagged; a first map is drawn untagged, a topic the person added while it was shaped among it; and `TO THE ROADMAP (N, with the map)`, each park by its summary tagged with its horizon; then `MENU: approve the map`, an authority gate that always stops: `y/yes` runs `approve-map yes <wu>`, `Keep shaping` takes the person's word. **`yes <wu>`** checks the staged payload against the map and the roadmap as they stand, so a refused yes writes nothing, then as one transaction writes each new topic's brief at `topics/{topic}/brief.md`, the project manifest — each item taken in or bound joined to its topic (`pulled_to: {work_unit, topic}`), each park on its horizon with `origin: park:{wu}` and its sources — and the epic's manifest: the new topics as `topic add-batch` adds them, every topic's `order` from 1, the epic's open discovery session closed, so the menu marks the first topic next, and when the map was approved, `phases.discovery.approved_at`, which each yes writes again and the board reads as the epic past its discovery. A failure part-way puts the briefs and the project manifest back. It removes the drafts and the staged payload, commits `discovery({wu}): approve the map — {N} topics, {k} to the roadmap`, its topics and its parks counted, and answers `{"ok": true, "unit", "topics": [<name>…], "added": [{"name", "brief"}…], "parked": [{"name", "horizon"}…], "bound": [{"item", "topic"}…], "session_closed", "committed", "next"}`, `next` the handoff to `leeway-continue-epic` with `[wu]`. Every refusal opens on its command; nothing staged answers `nothing is staged for {wu} — render approve-map {wu} --file <payload.json> stages the map`. **After the sweep** before the spec (8.8.4, 8.10.9), `render approve-map <wu> --sweep` stages the map from the sweep taken (**`sweep`**), never with `--file`: every live topic in its order, as the map stands, and the candidates the take recorded, `{"name", "reason", "brief", "from_roadmap"?}`, the brief its text whole; the sweep's messages and its calls left to the builder reach no screen. It is refused where the sweep is not taken (`{wu}'s sweep is not taken — engine sweep take {wu} takes its report in`), where it found no candidate, there being nothing to approve, and where a candidate standing would be refused as the yes refuses it. A restage keeps the strike of every candidate it still names, and Claude's map staged with `--file` once candidates are staged — the person's Keep shaping — carries them over with their strikes, a topic of the map named as a candidate refused (`"{name}" is staged as a candidate of the sweep's — name the topic apart`). The display: `THE MAP (N topics, every one decided)` — `K decided` where a topic is not — each topic numbered in its order by its title, ✓ decided and ◐ not; `FOUND BY THE SWEEP (N candidates, yours to take or strike)`, each candidate by its title with its reason beneath, a struck one tagged `struck`; then `MENU: approve the map`: `y/yes` runs `approve-map yes <wu>`, its label saying what it takes, `s/strike` strikes a lone standing candidate (`approve-map strike <wu> <n>`) or opens the pick among several (`render approve-map-strike <wu>`, `MENU: approve the map strike`, its rows the standing candidates by number and `b/back`), and `Keep shaping` takes the person's word. **`strike <wu> <n>`** strikes the n-th staged candidate, answering `{"ok": true, "unit", "struck", "next"}`, `next` the screen again; **`unstrike <wu> <n>`** undoes it, answering `"unstruck"` in its place; each refuses nothing staged (`nothing is staged for {wu} — engine sweep take {wu} stages the sweep's candidates`), a payload with no candidate staged, a candidate the payload lacks, a strike made already, and an unstrike of a candidate not struck. The yes checks the candidates not struck as their landing does, so a refused yes writes nothing, and in the same transaction adds each to the map as `topic add-batch` does (its reason the summary, `source: sweep`, or `sweep,roadmap:{item}` for a `from_roadmap` one, which joins its roadmap item), ordered after the map's topics, installs its brief, and records each struck name among the unit's dismissed names, a struck candidate's roadmap item left waiting; its response adds `"candidates": [<name>…]` and `"struck": [<name>…]`, and its `next` is the epic's menu where a topic is no longer decided — a candidate taken, or a topic one of the sweep's messages reopened — else `continue`, the spec carrying on.

**`sweep`** — the sweep's report taken in, once, before the spec (`domain/sweep-take.cjs`; the report and the record `domain/sweep.cjs`). **`sweep take <wu>`** reads the report the sweep's agent wrote (`.cache/{wu}/specification/sweep.json`, the `sweep` brief's `sweep-to`), exactly `{"candidates": [{"name", "reason", "brief", "from_roadmap"?}], "messages": [{"topic", "text"}], "builder_calls": [{"call", "why"}]}`, and checks it whole against the unit before anything goes: each message not yet sent goes to a live topic of the unit, by its key or its title, and each candidate lands as `topic add-batch` would land it, a name struck from the map the person's answer, never proposed again (8.10.11). It then sends each message not yet sent from `{wu}.specification` through `topic send`'s own landing — its slug from its title, a decided topic it reaches set back in progress (10.2.7), no send report drawn — marking each in the report as `"sent": "<path>"` once it lands, so a take killed part-way and run again sends none twice; writes the sweep taken at `{wu}/sweep-taken.json`, `{"candidates": [<candidate>…], "builder_calls": [{"id", "call", "why"}…]}`, each builder's call given its id, `call-N` in the report's order — the cite by which a spec line left to the builder names it, briefed to the spec's writer — and commits it alone (`sweep({wu}): taken`); and stages the candidates for approve the map, where there are any. Response `{"ok": true, "unit", "sent": [{"topic", "message"}…], "reopened": [<topic>…], "candidates": [<name>…], "committed", "next"}`, `next` approve the map (`render approve-map {wu} --sweep`) where it staged a candidate, else the handoff to `leeway-continue-epic` with `[wu]` where a topic is no longer decided, else `continue`, the spec written next. It refuses, opening `sweep take: ` and sending nothing: a unit not in progress or no epic; one past its spec (`{wu} has its spec, so its map is closed — the sweep runs before the spec is written, and what it would find now is settled in the spec`, P3); a sweep taken already (`{wu}'s sweep is taken already — one sweep for the spec (8.10.3)`); live topics not every one decided; one live topic alone, which owes no sweep; no report, or one that does not read (`the sweep's report, {file}, does not read —` and every fault, one a line); a message to a topic not live (`the sweep's messages do not hold —` and each); and a candidate refused. The record is what the sweep run is, never the cache. A candidate waits on the person until approve the map answers it, taken onto the map or struck from it, each read from the unit's manifest; the sweep's step is done once it is taken and none waits: ready to build's sweep-run, `spec land` and the writer's brief hold to it, and the sweep's brief is refused once the sweep is taken (`brief: {wu}'s sweep is taken already — one sweep for the spec (8.10.3)`, then `; ` and approve the map's refusal where a candidate waits).

```bash
engine start-this-work yes          # creates the unit from the staged payload — see **`start-this-work`**
engine approve-map yes <work-unit>  # lands the staged map — see **`approve-map`**
engine approve-map strike|unstrike <work-unit> <n>  # strikes the n-th of the sweep's candidates, or undoes it — see **`approve-map`**
engine sweep take <work-unit>       # takes the sweep's report in, once, before the spec — see **`sweep`**
engine ready-to-build yes <work-unit> --position auto|step [--marks <file>]  # concludes the spec and begins the build — see **`ready-to-build`**
```

**`ready-to-build`** — the spec's gate (11.8), raised once per spec (`domain/ready-to-build.cjs`, the screen `domain/projections/ready-to-build.cjs`). **The screen**, `render ready-to-build <wu> [--page] [--calls]`, stands on the landed spec alone, and on ready to build's checklist closed and nothing landed or cut since, every engine item holding still (**`checklist`**). It draws the title, `{Title} — ready to build`; Claude's calls at the spec — the current decisions of Claude's made at the spec, carried-over ones aside — counted by kind, listed under their questions with `--calls`; THE OUTLINE, the landed spec's areas; THE PHASE CUT, read from the manifest, each phase with its scenarios counted and the sections it draws on; and each item skipped, with its reason. A feature's carries its topic's close with them, stood on as `render decided` stands on it, over what `decided stage` staged (3.2.4, 9.7.6, 11.8.2): titled `{Title} — decided, and ready to build`, what the person decided, Claude's calls on the topic counted with the spec's, FOR THE PRODUCT RECORD and TO THE ROADMAP as the decided screen draws them, then the outline and the cut, decided's skips before ready to build's; it refuses a staged line or withdrawal that could no longer land — the decision it widens changed at the spec since — as the yes would, naming the restage (`… decided stage {wu} {wu} --file <payload.json> stages it again`), so the person never picks against a stale payload; its verdicts are decided's first, `--at decided`, then the spec's, counted across the whole close; `x/strike` strikes a candidate as the decided gate's `s/strike` does, `--page` carried; its Comment asks for a change to what the person decided, a product-record line, the outline or the phase cut; and its foot opens `Your pick marks {Title} decided, with …,`. Each departure among the spec's gap answers — the person's answer made at the spec standing over Claude's lean — that this machine logged is asked its verdict first, one at a time, its rows `verdict give <entry> … --at ready-to-build` (16.10.11); then the gate, `ready to build`: `a/auto`, first and recommended, nothing preselected, and `s/step`, each running the yes with its position; `r/review` with `--page` alone, where the session can publish a page (11.11.11) — `spec page {wu}` while no page is open, `spec marks {wu} --file <read-back>` once one is; `c/calls`, where calls stand and are not listed; and `Comment`. Its foot: `Your pick approves the outline and the phase cut, and starts the build in that position.` — while the review page is open (`spec published`), counting by the marks last stored the lines the person ticked, those carrying Claude's tick they let stand, and those passing without review (11.11.7), and its picks taking as `--marks` the file Claude reads the page's marks back to, `.cache/{wu}/specification/read-back.json`, absolute. **It refuses**, opening `render ready-to-build: `: a unit `enter specification` refuses, in its words — one the project lacks, one not in progress, a quick-fix, a unit of a retired type, topics not settled; `spec.md` on disk not the landed one, as the close refuses it; a unit keeping the old spec's items (`{wu} keeps its spec in the old form, which never comes to ready to build — the migration's cut for built units carries it across (29.17)`); a unit whose build has begun (`ready to build has passed for {wu} — its build began in {position}, and its position switches through workunit position {wu} auto|step`); the checklist open (`ready-to-build is open — its items are worked, then it is closed: checklist close ready-to-build {wu}`); the landed spec changed since the close (`ready-to-build was closed before {wu}'s spec changed — a land after the close voids it, so its items are worked afresh and it is closed again`) or the cut (`… before {wu}'s spec was cut again — a cut after the close voids it, …`); and an engine item failing since the close (`ready-to-build has items open since its close: {items} — they are worked, and it is closed again`), a decided topic postponed for a split among it, the lines citing it then invented (11.10.3).

**`yes <wu> --position auto|step [--marks <file>]`** is the person's pick: it stands on the screen as drawn, refusing as the screen refuses and while a verdict is still to ask (`a verdict is still to ask — render ready-to-build asks each before the yes`). While the review page is open it takes the page's marks, read back fresh — nothing on the page reaches Claude by itself (11.11.5) — and settles the review on them (**`spec`**, `marks`): it stores them first, then refuses while a comment waits for Claude, listing each by its number and the person's last word, the marks staying stored (`a comment on the review page waits for Claude, so the build does not start — Claude answers each beneath its line, then shows ready to build again:` then `  {N.M.K}: "{text}"` a line each, 11.11.8); else it writes `{wu}/spec-review.json`. It refuses an open page with no marks given (`{wu}'s review page is open, so the yes takes its marks — read them back from the page and give them as a file`), and marks given while no page is open (`{wu}'s review page is not open, so the yes takes no marks — the build starts with no review recorded`): a spec the person did not review has no record (11.S22). Then it writes `"build": {"position": "auto"|"step"}` on the unit's manifest — the first pick, whose record is the gate's answer, never a switch (E14) — which concludes the spec (`spec land` refuses from then on), lets a phase start, and closes the map for good (`topic postpone` refuses); and commits the manifest with the record, if any (`ready-to-build({wu}): {position}`), under the writer lock, pushed — a failed commit's pending note naming the unit's own commit, `engine commit {wu}`, which takes both. A feature's yes takes its topic's close as `decided yes` takes it (9.7.6): it refuses as that yes refuses, every line and candidate checked before anything is written; once the review has settled it lands the lines, then in the same save as the position parks the candidates, records the struck, marks the discussion completed and stamps the close taken, the project manifest riding the one commit, the staged payload removed, and its response adds `"decided", "lines", "withdrawn", "candidates", "struck", "parked"`. Refusals open `ready-to-build yes: ` and write nothing but the marks named; arguments of another shape, a position missing or none of the build's, answer the usage. Response `{"ok": true, "unit", "position", "record": "<wu>/spec-review.json"|null, "committed", "next"}`, `next` a handoff into the build for every type (24.1.2), `{"kind": "handoff", "skill": "leeway-implementation-process", "args": [work_type, wu]}`. **`workunit position <wu> auto|step`** switches the build's position once it has begun, the person's stated intent (3.3.6, 3.3.7): it writes `build.position`, logs a `position-switch` event in the trust log (`{unit, to}`), and commits the manifest alone (`build({wu}): position {position}`). It refuses, opening `workunit position: `: a unit the project lacks or not in progress; a quick-fix, which has no position (`{wu} is a quick-fix, which has no position: its fixes and its review's fix tasks run as in auto, its close its one gate (19.5.23)`); one whose build has not begun (`{wu}'s build has not begun — its position is chosen at ready to build`); and the position the build is in (`{wu}'s build is in {position} already`); a position none of the build's answers the usage. Response `{"ok": true, "unit", "position", "committed"}`, an append to the trust log that fails riding as a warning.

**`verify`** — the proof by running the product (`domain/verify.cjs`; the runs `domain/runs.cjs`, the setup `domain/environment.cjs`). Each verb runs the verify setup's parts, each a command in the product's code, by `sh -c` in the code root of the calling checkout — a builder's worktree, where a builder calls it — with no timeout of the engine's own and holding no lock while they run, so a part may run for minutes and may run the engine; give the Bash tool's call a timeout to match. Every part is handed `LEEWAY_RUN`, a fresh folder outside the project, removed once the verb answers, which the parts write into, and in a scenario's run `LEEWAY_SCENARIO`, the scenario's id. **A scenario's run** calls `launch`; then `health`, which answers only once the product does; then `drive` and `evidence`; then `cleanup` whatever came before. The drive's script checks what happened and writes its verdict to `$LEEWAY_RUN/verdict`, a file: `pass`, `fail`, or `skipped` where the criterion's tests were named and not run; its exit code is no verdict. Text evidence — a captured screen, a response — goes in `$LEEWAY_RUN/evidence/`, 64 KB a file at most, and recordings and images in `$LEEWAY_RUN/recordings/`, a file each. A run proves committed code: a run, and a setup's trial, refuse a checkout whose repositories hold any change not committed — tracked, or untracked and not ignored — naming each file, and once the cleanup has run, refuse again where the parts left any such file behind, so a setup that leaves files is caught where it is built. The way on, in both refusals: ignore a file a run leaves behind, have the cleanup remove it, or write it under `$LEEWAY_RUN`; commit one through the door (**`commit`**) only where it belongs to the product. **`run <wu> <scenario>`** takes a scenario of the unit's cut, `N.Sk` — a quick-fix's, which has no cut, one its fix owns, `{n}.Sk` of fix `1.{n}`, not closed, among that fix's acceptance scenarios (F2) — runs it, and records it as `{wu}/build/runs/{scenario}/run-NNN.json`, numbered past the highest the scenario's folder holds, a file only `verify` writes (**`layout`**): `{"verdict", "unfixed": false, "at", "checkout", "commits": [{"repo", "commit"}], "task", "evidence": [{"name", "text"}], "recordings": [{"name", "sha256"}]}` — the verdict as the drive wrote it, never Claude's; `unfixed`, whether it ran on the unfixed code, the bug-proven run's; the checkout's code root, and each of its repositories — `.` the project's own, a nested one by its folder — with the HEAD it ran against, null before its first commit; the task it ran for, `{phase}.{n}`, the task of the unit in flight in the calling conversation whose worktree the run is in (`task worktree`), else the one task of the unit in flight there whose acceptance scenarios hold the scenario, by its id (**`conversation`**), null where there is none or several — so a run the orchestrator makes of an earlier phase's scenario while a task is in flight is no task's; each text file whole; and each recording by its name and SHA-256, the file itself copied to the unit's cache at `.cache/{wu}/build/runs/{scenario}/{sha256}/{name}`, never committed. A run never holds its own number — a second clone's merge renumbers this machine's runs (**`state-merge`**) — so a recording is found by its hash, and every reader reads a scenario's runs from its folder whatever their numbers, ordered by `at`, a tie by number (`runsOf(cwd, unit, scenario, head?)`). Given a head — the code to judge against, `[{"repo", "commit"}]` as a run names its own: the commits a run just recorded, or the phase's tip in each repository holding its work, as the phase review reads them — only the runs at that code or before it count, since builders side by side run one scenario at different code in whichever repository the work is: a run counts where, in every repository both name, its commit is the head's or an ancestor of it there, a repository only one of them names deciding nothing, and a null commit behind only a null (`behindHead`, the one place the rule is spelt). Among those not skipped the runs nearest the head decide: fewest commits between them, summed over the repositories both name, then the latest by `at`, then by number. Without a head every run of the change counts and the latest not skipped decides, a standing tied to no code. The run that decides makes it proven where it passed and not proven where it failed, so a regression after a pass reads not proven; named, not run where every run counted was skipped; not proven with none. It commits the run alone (`verify({wu}): {scenario} {verdict}`), pushed, under the writer lock. Response `{"ok": true, "unit", "scenario", "verdict", "run": "<state-relative path>", "standing": "proven"|"not proven"|"named, not run", "committed"}` — `standing` read against the commits the run recorded, the checkout's HEAD in each of its repositories, with `warnings` and a pending note where the commit failed. **`run <wu> <scenario> --unfixed --test "<command>"`** is the bug-proven run, a bugfix's alone (`domain/bug-proven.cjs`, 15.5): the test that reproduces the bug — task 1.1's, phase 1's lowest-numbered task, which writes that test and nothing else — run on the unfixed code by its own command, needing no verify setup. The scenario is one of phase 1 of the cut and one of task 1.1's acceptance scenarios. The engine lays the project out once a call in a scratch worktree of its own, as `mutants` lays its own, at `.claude/worktrees/leeway-unfixed-{unit}`: in the build, at the tip of task 1.1's branch in each repository holding it; in the review phase, once every phase of the cut has closed, at the commits the build's series ran at, or at task 1.1's tip where the build recorded none. It runs the command there by `sh -c` in the project's folder up to five times, stopping at the first pass, and takes the worktree apart whatever happened. Each run is recorded as a scenario's run is, `unfixed: true` with `"command"` beside it: the verdict `pass` where the command exited 0, else `fail`; the evidence one text, `output`, the command's output and errors, at most their last 64 KB, from the first line that starts among them where that leaves something, else from the first character; no recordings; the task `1.1` in the build, null in the review phase. `runsOf` never reads them.
