A series is consecutive runs at the same commits: a call at the commits the last ran at carries its series on, a pass in it standing, and a new commit on task 1.1 begins another. The build's series proves the bug where no run of it passed, five failures. It commits the runs alone (`verify({wu}): {scenario} on the unfixed code, five failures`, or `…, passed in run {k}`). Response `{"ok": true, "unit", "scenario", "command", "runs": [{"run", "verdict"}], "proven", "committed", "way"?}` — `proven`, in the build, where the series proves the bug, and in the review phase where no run since the review began passed; `way`, where it does not, what comes next: in the build, the test reworked, a new commit on task 1.1, then a new series; in the review phase, that round 1 carries the pass as a finding, `unfixed#1`, and fails. **`setup <wu> --file <setup.json> --scenario <scenario>`** records the verify setup on the project manifest as `environment.verify`. The file lies outside the checkout — the state's cache, `.leeway/.cache/`, or anywhere else — since the trial refuses a checkout holding a file not committed, and holds `{"place", "feature_map", "launch", "health", "drive", "evidence", "cleanup", "mutation"?}` — the setup's folder, below the code root, never the root itself, and the feature map beside it, each a path from the code root inside the code; the five parts, each a command; the mutation tool's command, where the setup names one. The verdict comes only from the product's committed, reviewed code: the drive's command is the path of an executable file under `place` the checkout holds, as its first word, then plain arguments only, holding no shell operator or expansion — `;`, `&`, `|`, `<`, `>`, `$`, a backtick, `(`, `)` or a newline — so a tool is wrapped in a script there, such as `verify/drive.sh` running `npm run drive`; launch, health, evidence and cleanup are free. It runs all five end to end for the scenario, a scenario of the cut, or of a quick-fix's fix as `run` takes one, and records the setup only where the drive wrote a verdict, whatever it says, and the evidence reads as a run's does, with what it ran, `"ran": {"unit", "scenario", "verdict", "commits", "at"}`; the trial is no run, and a setup recorded is replaced whole. It commits the project manifest alone (`verify({wu}): the verify setup recorded`) and draws nothing. Response `{"ok": true, "unit", "setup": {…}, "committed"}`. **`mutants <wu> <N>`** runs the setup's mutation tool over the lines phase N of the cut changed, at the phase's tips wherever it is called from: the engine lays the project out in a scratch worktree of its own where Claude Code lays out the builders' isolation worktrees, `.claude/worktrees/leeway-mutants-{unit}-phase-{N}` at the top of the main checkout of the project's repository, so the tool runs where the setup's trial ran, below no folder a test runner leaves out — each repository holding the phase's work at its stack's tip there, as `phase-diff-range` picks it, a submodule holding none at the commit its holder points it at, a clone holding none at the tip of the branch it lands on (**`land`**), each in its folder from the project's, and the project's own repository's worktree there at its top, so a project nested in a larger repository sits at its folder of it, as a task's worktree lays it out (`domain/project-layout.cjs`) — prepares it as Claude Code prepares an isolation worktree — in each repository, the files its checkout's `.worktreeinclude` names that git ignores copied, and each directory the project's `.claude/settings.json` and `.claude/settings.local.json` name in `worktree.symlinkDirectories` linked where the checkout has it, nothing there replaced and a name leaving the repository passed over — runs the tool in the project's folder there, and takes it apart again whatever the tool did, a laying out that fails among them, a linked directory's link alone. In each repository there, the commits whose `Leeway-Task` trailer names a task of the phase (**`commit`**), and the change from the parent of the earliest to the tip, confined to the files they touched, each file's lines as they stand at the tip, a submodule's pointer never one of them. The tool is handed them as `LEEWAY_CHANGED`, a JSON file, `[{"path", "lines": [[from, to], …]}]`, each path from the project's folder, and writes what survived to `$LEEWAY_RUN/survivors.json`, `[{"path", "line", "mutant"}]` — `[]` where nothing did — read whatever its exit code, since many tools exit failing where a mutant survives. A survivor's path is the project's, from its root, as `LEEWAY_CHANGED` names each file: it is posix-normalised, and an absolute one made relative to the project's root. It writes nothing of the state or the code: what survived is left in the unit's cache, `.cache/{unit}/build/mutants/phase-{N}/{key}.json`, `{"commits", "survivors"}`, `commits` each repository with the commit it ran at, as a run names them, `key` their hash, so a run at the same code replaces the last; the phase review's brief reads the one run at the phase's tip in each repository holding its work — so the tool runs once a phase review, holding no lock, and never inside a brief. Response `{"ok": true, "unit", "phase": N, "survivors": [{"path", "line", "mutant"}]}` — those on a line the phase changed, each path the project's. **Each refuses**, opening on its command — `verify run: `, `verify setup: `, `verify mutants: ` — and recording nothing: a unit the project lacks; no setup recorded, for a run and the mutants, naming `verify setup`; a scenario that is none of the cut's, or a phase beyond it, and a quick-fix's its fix does not own (`{scenario} is no acceptance scenario of a fix of {wu} — fix 1.n names its own, n.S1 and on: engine task show {wu} 1.<n>`); a setup file that holds no setup, naming every fault, a place under `.leeway/`, which no pull request carries, one the checkout lacks, a place that is the code root, and a drive that is no script under the place, saying how to wrap a tool; for a run and a setup, a checkout holding a change not committed, or a file the parts left behind; a part that fails — the drive aside — naming it, its exit code and its output's last lines, the cleanup's failure beside any other; what the parts wrote that a run cannot hold — no verdict, a folder in its place among it, with the drive's exit code and output, or another word; among the evidence a file that is not text or is over 64 KB, a folder or a symbolic link; among the recordings a folder or a symbolic link; a setup naming no mutation tool, where each reviewer breaks a line by hand instead; the phase's tasks in no one stack, or holding no work in any repository; no commit of the phase at its tips; survivors the tool did not write, with its exit code and output, or not of their shape; and a survivor naming none of the files the phase changed, naming each. A run on the unfixed code refuses besides, each with the way on: a unit but a bugfix (`verify run: {unit} is a {type}, and a run on the unfixed code is a bugfix's, proving its bug (15.5.5)`); a scenario of no phase 1 of the cut, or none of task 1.1's acceptance scenarios; no task 1.1 in the tracker; no repository holding work on task 1.1's branch — in the project's own repository, a commit beyond the branch it lands on — naming the commit of its test through the door (**`commit`**); and in the review phase a command but the series' own, naming its call, and a repository of the checkout the series names no commit for. `--unfixed` without `--test`, or `--test` without it, answers the usage. A run or a setup takes the writer lock around its record and its commit alone.

```bash
engine verify run <work-unit> <scenario>                                    # the scenario run through the verify setup from this checkout, its run recorded
engine verify run <work-unit> <scenario> --unfixed --test "<command>"       # a bugfix's reproducing test on the unfixed code, up to five runs, each recorded
engine verify setup <work-unit> --file <setup.json> --scenario <scenario>   # the verify setup recorded once all five parts run end to end for the scenario
engine verify mutants <work-unit> <N>                                       # the setup's mutation tool over the lines phase N changed: what survived
```

**`scratch`** — a reviewer's scratch worktree (`domain/scratch.cjs`), where the build's reviewers and the review phase's readers run the tests, make a break and run the product, never in a checkout of the work; each brief dispatching one names its two calls (**`brief`**'s `scratch-layout`). **`lay <name> <commit> [<folder>=<commit> …]`** lays the project out afresh in a worktree of its own beside the builders' isolation worktrees, `.claude/worktrees/leeway-scratch-{name}` in the project's main checkout, whatever was laid out under that name taken apart first: the project's own repository at the first commit, each child repository at the commit given for its folder, every one detached and nested as the project lays them out, each prepared as Claude Code prepares an isolation worktree — the `.worktreeinclude` files copied and the `worktree.symlinkDirectories` linked — so the project's tests and its verify setup run there as in its checkout; it answers `{"ok": true, "name", "path"}`, `path` the project's folder there. **`remove <name>`** takes that worktree apart, each repository's registration of it with it, one not there passing, answering `{"ok": true, "name", "removed": true}`. **They refuse** a name but lowercase words, digits, dots and dashes; a child given as anything but `{folder}={commit}`, a folder none of the project's child repositories', or one given twice; a repository of the project given no commit (`no commit is given for {folder} — every repository of the project is laid out, each <folder>=<commit>`); and a commit a repository lacks (``{the project's repository|`{folder}`} has no commit {commit}``) — each `scratch {verb}: …`, before anything is laid out. Neither writes the state, and neither takes the writer's lock. Each reader's layout is named for the reader, as its brief names it: a task's reviewer `{unit}-{task}-{letter}`, a phase review's `{unit}-phase-{N}-{letter}`, an area's reader `{unit}-area-{n}-{letter}` and the test surface's `{unit}-tests`. A reader that dies leaves its layout, so the engine takes apart each one left once its readers are done: a task's at its `task account`, a phase review's at `task settle --phase` and at `land phase`, a round's readers' at `review round`, and every one of the unit's at finish's yes. The engine's own layouts, every `.claude/worktrees/leeway-…` in the project's main checkout, the mutation run's among them, are never the person's change: where the project does not ignore that folder, `code status`, the commit door's `left_dirty` and the review's hold on the working tree (**`review`**) pass over each.

```bash
engine scratch lay <name> <commit> [<folder>=<commit> …]   # the project laid out afresh at those commits in scratch worktree <name>, prepared as its checkout is
engine scratch remove <name>                               # scratch worktree <name> taken apart
```

**`migrate`** — a directive's check (`domain/directives.cjs`), for the session carrying out the directive boot stopped at (**`boot`**). `check <id>` loads the check beside the directive in the installed migrations, `leeway-migrate/scripts/migrations/{id}.check.cjs` — exporting `{ counts, done, check({ projectDir, stateDir }) }`, `counts` what one of its pieces is called and many (`{ one: 'topic', many: 'topics' }`) and `done` what a piece done has (`have their records`), as the stop's screen counts them — runs it against the project's code root and its state, and answers `{"ok": true, "directive", "passed", "done", "of", "counts": {"one", "many"}, "unfinished": [{"name", "problems": […]}]}`: `passed` where no piece has a problem, `done` of `of` the pieces with none, and each piece not done with what it lacks. A directive whose own steps end in raises exports them beside the check, `raises({ projectDir, stateDir })`, each `{place, answers, text}`; once it passes, the answer adds `"raise": [{"place", "answers", "text"}]`, each point whose question is still open and that no raise of the calling conversation has drawn, answered or left open, `answers` its short id — so a session compacted part-way reads them back. It writes nothing and takes no lock. It refuses, opening `migrate check: `, an id that is not a directive's file name, `NNN-{slug}`, a directive the migrations lack, and a check that will not load, exports another shape, fails, or answers anything but its pieces, or its points to raise as anything but such a list; arguments of another shape answer the usage.

```bash
engine migrate check <id>    # the directive's check: its pieces done of all of them, and each not done with what it lacks
```

**`task`** — two sets of verbs. **The tracker's verbs** — `phase`, `create`, `add`, `type`, `link`, `ready`, `start`, `done`, `close`, `list`, `show` (`domain/task-verbs.cjs`) — are the only way anything in the workflows touches a tracker, through the project's task driver (`domain/drivers.cjs`): a folder the workflows ship, `scripts/drivers/task/{name}/driver.cjs`, named by the project manifest's `drivers.task`, which the first verb to need it writes as the default driver where it is absent; a setting naming a driver the workflows don't ship is refused, naming those they do. Before anything reaches the driver, the state's `.gitignore` takes the files its tool keeps local — refused where that file is gone — and its store is made in the state worktree. A verb names a task `{phase}.{n}` beside its unit; its internal id, `{unit}-{phase}-{n}` (`domain/task-id.cjs`), is the lasting one, in commit subjects, branch names and trailers. `phase <unit> <N> --title <title>` writes a phase into the tracker as it starts, the unit's parent made on first use — refused where it is written already, or the title runs past one line. `create <unit> <N> --file <path>` makes a task of a written phase from the task template in a file, numbered after the highest the phase holds: a level-one title, then level-two `Type`, `Problem`, `Solution`, `Outcome`, `Acceptance scenarios` and `Spec reference` (`domain/task-file.cjs`), the type one of `feat`, `fix`, `refactor`, `perf`, `test`, `docs`, `build`, `ci` and `chore` — a file missing a field, holding one twice or empty, holding a heading the template lacks, or titled over more than one line is refused, every fault named at once; a quick-fix's task, its unit having no spec, references none, its `Spec reference` exactly `none`, any other refused (`{unit} is a quick-fix, which has no spec — its task's Spec reference is none, not "{reference}"`, 19.5.5); and, there being no spec to number them, its fix `1.{n}` names its scenarios `{n}.S1`, `{n}.S2` and on, so each fix owns its runs — one naming another number refused (`task 1.{n} of {unit}, a quick-fix's fix, names its scenarios {n}.S1, {n}.S2 and on — not {ids}`). `add <unit> <task> --file <path> --by <orchestrator|person>` puts an addition, its text from the file, onto a task not yet ended — where its pair has been briefed for a round, marking the task changed under the round it was briefed for last (`.cache/{unit}/build/tasks/{task}/changed.json`, `{"round", "n"}`, `n` the changes made since the first round was briefed, the checkout's alone, beside the record of the task's briefs it is read against, the engine's alone, keyed on the brief records, never on the last report written), so a change made while the pair reads a round counts against that round: where it passes, its pair reads the task again before its account, and where it fails, its builder is briefed again, past the change, before the fix round (12.1.4); before any round is briefed it marks nothing, the first round reading the task as it stands; `type <unit> <task> <type>` gives a task not yet ended another type; `link <unit> <task> --after <task>` holds a task not yet ended after an unfinished one of its own phase or an earlier one. `ready <unit> [<N>]` answers every task waiting with nothing before it and no question waiting parking it (**`raise`**), in the phase or the whole unit, and, in step or in a quick-fix's one checkout, `back`, the task in flight where an answer freed it, in progress already, its turn come (12.7.9) — named until a brief to its builder, its own or a fix round's, is next issued after the freeing, and never while a reviewer the calling conversation briefed for a round of it has yet to report, its builder waiting for the round's reports (12.1.4). `start <unit> <task>` puts the task in progress, where it is not already, and in flight on the calling conversation's position beside any others there — at the unit's review phase while the conversation is there, or holds no place in the unit's build and the task is of one of its fix phases (14.3.2), else at its implementation (see **`conversation`**) — a task already in flight kept as it stands — refused, for a phase the unit's cut holds, before the phase's start checklist closes (`task start: phase {N} of {unit} has not started — its tasks start once its start checklist closes: checklist close phase-start {unit} {N}`, 12.S7), a fix phase past the cut never, nor a quick-fix's fixes — and, while the build is in step, while another of the unit's tasks is in flight or at its gate, which a task reaches before it is done: one in progress in any phase the unit has written — the cut's, and the review's fixes past it — in flight here or in another conversation, that no question waiting parks, a parked task holding nothing new to test; where more than one is — an answer freeing one while another went on, or two left running in auto — the task in flight is the one that went back in flight first: one never parked first, then by the ledger's line of the answer, merge, move, replacement or keep that last freed each, the tracker's order between those alike; those behind it wait until it is done (`task start: {unit} builds in step, one task at a time — task {N}.{n} is in flight or at its gate, and the next starts once the person's go there is given and it is done`, 12.7.9). A quick-fix builds one task at a time too, every task of its build in its one checkout on its one branch (19.5.23): its checkout has one holder (R27): the fix in flight whose turn it is, in progress with no question parking it, never one a question parks. A fix a question parks has its start refused as `done` refuses it (`task start: task {N}.{n} of {unit} waits on Q{n}, parked for the person — they answer it through its raise: raise draw {unit} Q{n}`); any other but the holder while one holds the checkout — a fix not started, or one an answer freed while another holds it, which waits behind it — is refused (`task start: {unit} builds one task at a time, in the project's one checkout on its one branch — task {N}.{n} is in flight, and task {N}.{n} starts once it is done or closed, or a question parks it`), and `ready` names a freed fix `back` once the checkout is free. The holder leaves the checkout clean as it stops holding it: a park sets its work aside, and its `done` and `close` are refused while the checkout holds changes not committed (below), so any change there is the holder's. A question that parks the fix holding the checkout sets aside what the checkout holds not committed (**`raise`**), in each repository holding any, on a ref of the unit's own, `refs/leeway/aside/{unit}/{id}` — never on the stash — the checkout left clean at the branch's tip; the fix's next start puts it back, merged onto the tip as it then stands and left uncommitted, its ref dropped, and answers its paths as `put_back` — refused, nothing moved, where it conflicts there (`task start: task {N}.{n} of {unit}'s work, set aside when a question parked it, does not go back onto fix/{unit} as it stands — it conflicts at {paths} — so nothing moved: it is kept at {ref}; see it, `git diff {ref}^ {ref}`, drop it, `git update-ref -d {ref}`, start the task again, `engine task start {unit} {N}.{n}`, then make its change again by hand and commit it through the door`, a nested repository's calls `git -C {folder}`). The set-aside reads each repository's paths from its checkout's top, a project that is a folder of a larger repository among them, and is all or nothing: a step that fails undoes every part — each path back in the checkout as it was, not staged, each ref it wrote dropped — and the raise is refused, nothing parked (`raise: task {N}.{n} of {unit}'s work could not be set aside, so nothing was: the checkout holds it as before, none of it staged, and no question parked the fix — {why}`). Nothing set aside is lost: a fix closed with `task close` keeps what it set aside, its answer naming it as `kept_aside`, a line for each repository holding it, `{task, repo, ref, why, see, recover}` as the move's (**`workunit`**), and a move and a cancel keep it too (R26). A quick-fix's task is built in the project's own checkout (12.7.8, 19.5.18; `domain/fix-branch.cjs`), every task of its build — its fixes, and the fix tasks of a review asked for at its close — on its one branch, `fix/{unit}`, in each repository it touches (7.2.24), as the landing reads them (**`land`**) — a nested one's made at the first commit of a task's there (**`commit`**): its start puts that checkout on it once every repository's branch is readied, each nested checkout whose branch the readying made — its branch gone here, or a second clone of it — on it too, and answers it as `branch`. A fix's own work is its own commits on the branch, each stamped with it by the door, which its pair's range and its figures read (**`brief`**'s `diff-range`, `account`) — the other fixes' between them never its. The host's copy of the branch is the truth for what the pull request holds, refused first, nothing moved, where a repository's host holds a branch by its name that is another's (`land: {the project's host|{repo}'s host} holds a branch fix/{unit} that is not {unit}'s — none of its commits is a fix of {unit}'s or was made since {unit} was — and nothing moved: delete it there, or rename it, then try again`; **`land`**): where a repository the quick-fix touches lacks the branch, it is taken from the host's copy — made at the first start from the branch the repository lands on as the host has it, fetched on a host, only where the host has none either; the host's head is then brought in where it holds a commit not landed — never a pull request's head its merge by hand left behind — fast-forwarded to where the branch is behind it, else merged with it where it holds a change the branch lacks, a review's suggestion, the host's update of the branch and the person's own commits kept — refused, nothing moved, where the two conflict, naming the merge to make (`land: fix/{unit} and its pull request's head, {sha}, each hold commits the other lacks, conflicting at {paths}, so nothing moved — merge {sha} into fix/{unit} there, settling the conflict, and try again`), and, while a task of the unit's is in flight in the conversation, the merge the hook before Bash passes, finished through the door (`… — merge it into fix/{unit} there with \`git merge --no-ff --no-commit {sha}\`, settle the conflict, then commit the merge through the door, naming each file you settled, which finishes it: \`engine commit --paths <file>… --task {unit} 1.{n} -m "<message>"\`, and try again`, `git -C {repo}` in a nested one). Where a merge of its pull request by hand, by any method, left the branch holding work landed, the start cuts it again from the branch it lands on as the host has it, each of its commits not landed replayed onto it in order under its own message — a task's its stamp kept, one stamped with no task the person's, kept where it holds a change that branch lacks — and answers `cut_again`, a list of `{repo, from, to, replayed, left_off?}`, each repository cut again with the tasks replayed and those left off — `land open` and the landing ready the branches alike; a task's commit that does not apply there is left off with every later commit of its task's, warning of each (`task 1.{n}'s commit {sha} does not apply on {into} as it stands, at {paths}, so fix/{unit} was cut again without it and its commits after it — make its change again there through the door: `git cherry-pick --no-commit {sha}`, settling any conflict and ending the pick with `git cherry-pick --quit`, which keeps its change, then `engine commit --paths <file>… --task {unit} 1.{n} -m "<message>"``), the door taking it though the task is done, and one of the person's left off alone (`the commit {sha} "{subject}", stamped with no task, does not apply on {into} as it stands, at {paths}, so fix/{unit} was cut again without it — the engine never wrote it, so it is the person's to make again there`), a warning of a nested repository naming it, nothing in the conversation rewriting history; refused before the tracker moves while the checkout holds changes not committed outside the state, naming each (`task start: task 1.{n} of {unit} is built in the project's own checkout, which holds changes not committed — {paths} — so it was not put on {unit}'s branch: commit them or set them aside, then start the task again`) — the person's, for a start that makes the fix the holder, none holding it, and for a start of a fix an answer freed since its last start, the freeing each quick-fix start was made past recorded at `.cache/{unit}/build/tasks/{task}/started.json`, `{"freed"}`, the checkout's alone; any other start of the holder takes them as its own; taking the checkout back at the close is the close's. `done` ends it done, refused while a question waiting parks it, naming each question's `raise draw <unit> Q{n}` (13.4.6), for the fix holding a quick-fix's checkout while the checkout holds changes not committed, naming each — a fix leaves it clean as it stops holding it (R27; `task done: task {N}.{n} of {unit} holds {unit}'s one checkout, which holds changes not committed — {paths} — and a fix leaves it clean as it stops holding it: commit what is the fix's through the door, remove what is not, as a scenario's run leaves, then run it again`, a close's ending `… : a fix closed gives its work up with it, so remove them, then run it again`) — where no account is taken of its review as it stands, as `land` refuses it — a quick-fix's task as any task's, passed by its pair (19.5.22), once its end below stands, which it is refused on first — and for a bugfix's reproducing task, 1.1, while the build's series of its test on the unfixed code does not prove the bug at its tip, as `land open` refuses it (`domain/bug-proven.cjs`, 15.5.3) — `task done: {unit} is a bugfix, so task 1.1 of {unit}, its reproducing task, is done once the test it wrote proves the bug, failing five times on the unfixed code at its tip (15.5.3): {why}`, the why naming the series to run (**`verify`**'s `run … --unfixed`), a new one where its branch moved past the last, or the test reworked where a run passed; and, for a quick-fix's task (`domain/fix-end.cjs`) — its own work the commits the door stamped with its task (**`commit`**'s `Leeway-Task`), on the quick-fix's branch or wherever the repository keeps them, on any ref or reflog; its landing read against the branch it lands on as the host has it, fetched on a host, whether or not the quick-fix's branch is left, as a merge of its pull request by hand by any method leaves it (`quickFixLanding`, `domain/landing.cjs`, as the close reads it, **`finish`**): a commit of its own landed where that branch holds it, a pull request of the quick-fix's merged holds it, or that branch holds its change, under its task's name or as a copy of it; the task landed where its newest commit of its own has, or, where the repository holds none of its own, where a commit on that branch names it — it is done committed and in the quick-fix's one pull request, or landed, and refused while it holds no commit of its own, naming its close as met (`task done: task 1.{n} of {unit} holds no commit of its own — a fix met already, or with nothing to change, is closed as met: task close {unit} 1.{n} --reason <why>`); while its newest commit is not on the quick-fix's branch, as a branch cut again leaves one that did not apply there, naming its change made again there through the door (`task done: task 1.{n} of {unit}'s commit {sha} is not on fix/{unit} — make its change again there through the door: `git cherry-pick --no-commit {sha}`, settling any conflict and ending the pick with `git cherry-pick --quit`, which keeps its change, then `engine commit --paths <file>… --task {unit} 1.{n} -m "<message>"``); and while its newest commit is not in the pull request, never opened or committed again since, naming `land open`, which pushes it (`task done: task 1.{n} of {unit}'s newest commit is not in {unit}'s pull request — a task is done once it is committed and in it: land open {unit} 1.{n} pushes it`), done's rule kept in one place (`undoneRefusal`); a refusal the landing makes as these are read — the pull request closed by hand among them — opens on the verb, as every refusal here does (`task done: {unit}'s pull request … — …`); `close --reason <reason>` ends it without it being done, its reason kept, whatever waits on it — refused for the fix holding a quick-fix's checkout while the checkout holds changes not committed, as `done` is, and for a bugfix's reproducing task, 1.1, whose test proving the bug is what a bugfix is (`domain/bug-proven.cjs`, 15.5.5), naming its test's rework and the unit's cancel (`task close: {unit} is a bugfix, so task 1.1 of {unit}, its reproducing task, is never closed: …`), and for a quick-fix's task holding work of its own — a commit of its own through the door, landed or not, read as its done reads it — naming the reversal: its change lands as it is and is given up as a landed change is, by a fix of its own whose commit through the door reverts it (`task close: task {N}.{n} of {unit} holds work of its own — it lands as it is with {unit}'s pull request, done once it is in it, and its change is given up as a landed change is, by a fix of its own whose commit through the door reverts it: task create {unit} {N} --file <task.md>`; one landed `… — it has landed, so it is done, and its change is given up …`; one its pair failed in its last round, not landed, `… — its pair failed it in round {r}, so it lands once its review is settled, task settle {unit} {N}.{n} --file <why.md>, then done once it is in {unit}'s pull request, and its change is given up …`), the landing's own refusals as it is read opening on the verb — a task holding none, met already or given up before its commit, always closing, and a fix recorded having its record taken out of `summary.md`, the file gone with its last record, and out of `fixes.recorded`, both committed with the tracker's store, the knowledge base then brought in line where the checkout's is set up, a move's close the one to meet one, since a fix holding work never closes (**`quick-fix`**): before the tracker moves it is taken off every question parking it, one `unparked` line (**`ledger`**) naming the parks, under the conversation's session, committed — refused, before anything moves, with no session where a question parks it — and a question it leaves parking none still waits; each frees what waits on it and takes it off the position; its `next`, where it is the last of its phase of the cut to end and another of the phase is done, is the phase's mutation run, as `land chain` names it (**`land`**). `list <unit> <N>` answers the phase's tasks in order, `show <unit> <task>` the task whole, its additions and what it is linked after among it. `worktree <unit> <task>` (`domain/task-worktree.cjs`) is the builder's, run in the worktree Claude Code's isolation made for it (12.7.10): it makes the task's branch there, named as the landing names it (**`land`**), where the task is built on, never where the isolation started — Claude Code's starts at the remote's default branch, whatever the checkout holds — and checks it out, the worktree's files moving to it, the isolation's own branch left as it stands: at the tip of the work below it where the project's repository holds that work, else at the tip of the branch it lands on (**`land`**): a task of the cut's, its own phase's chain alone — its tasks done and not landed — never another phase's work not landed, as a phase relying on another starts once that one has merged and one relying on nothing unfinished may start beside one still open; a task of one of the review's fix phases', every task of the unit done and not landed to its phase, so each fix phase is built on the one before (7.2.6) — or, where the branch is made already and no worktree has it out, switches to it where the worktree is clean; then lays each child repository of the project out at its folder there, as the project's own checkout has them, nested in its folder at any depth: a worktree of that repository on a branch of the same name, each before the repositories nested in it, made at the commit the holder's laid-out commit points it at for a submodule — its own checkout's commit where that points it nowhere — and at the tip of the branch it lands on for a clone (**`land`**), whatever its own checkout is on — in a repository holding the work below the task, either, at that work's tip — or, where the branch is made already and no worktree has it out, checked out as it stands; a worktree of that repository already at its folder, on another branch, takes the branch as the project's own does. Nothing merges: a child repository the task never changes holds the branch with nothing on it, which the landing passes over, and merging a parallel task back is Claude's. It records the worktree against the task on the calling conversation's position (see **`conversation`**), so the commit door and `verify` find the task by the folder they run in, and the task's commits are made there alone; and the reads of the change made there — the commit door's paths and checks, `code status`, `code diff` — read each child repository laid out there. It records the commit each repository's branch was made at, `.cache/{unit}/build/tasks/{task}/laid-out.json`, `{"{dir}": "<sha>"}` by the repository's folder from the project's root, `""` the project's own, the checkout's alone, where its pair's range starts (**`brief`**'s `diff-range`). Run again where it is laid out already, it makes nothing. **A task started again** — after a `/clear`, in a new conversation and a new isolation worktree — finds its branch out in the worktree it was built in before, which Claude Code keeps for its commits. Where the project's own repository has the branch out in a linked worktree that is not a repository's own checkout, whose folder is there, that is the worktree of no task in flight in the calling conversation, and that holds no change not committed, nor does any child repository's worktree in it, `worktree` takes the branch over: it removes each child repository's worktree there, deepest first, as `done` does, detaches that worktree at its own commit, so no file there moves, and lays the task out here on the branch as it stands. It refuses, before anything is made: no conversation, or the task not in flight in it — start it with `task start`; the call made in the project's own checkout, or in the worktree of another task in flight; a task the tracker holds with no type, which its branch is named from — give it one with `task type`; its branch out in another worktree of any of the project's repositories that it does not take over, naming it and why — where it is the worktree of a task in flight in this conversation — a builder working there still — to stop and report it, so the orchestrator sends that task's builder back in to carry on there, naming the task, its unit and the worktree; where it or a child repository's worktree in it holds changes not committed, naming each with what it holds; where it is a child repository's worktree elsewhere, to build the task there; where that worktree's folder is gone, the `git worktree remove` that removes it, as the landing's remedy does; and, where it is the repository's own checkout, the `git -C {path} switch --detach` that switches it off the branch; its branch made already where the worktree switching to it has changes not committed, naming them, and made where the task is built on where it has, naming them (`task worktree: {branch} is made at {commit}, where the task is built on, and {path} has changes not committed: …`); the work below the task no one stack in a repository, as the phase's stack refuses it; a child repository's folder there that is a worktree of it whose folder is gone, the same way; and a child repository's folder there holding anything but a worktree of that repository. As `done` or `close` ends a task, before the tracker moves, every worktree of a child repository the task has is taken apart: each with the task's branch out — never a repository's own checkout — and each in the worktree recorded for the task in the calling conversation, so one a `/clear` lost the record of is found by its branch. Each is checked first: one holding changes not committed refuses the verb, naming them (`task done: {branch} is checked out at {path}, with changes not committed: …, so nothing moved — commit them, or move them out of the way, then run it again`) — its own files, and a nested submodule's pointer moved, staged or not, never what that submodule holds, checked as its own, nor a nested clone's folder, nor a link the worktree's preparation made — an untracked symbolic link at a folder the project's `worktree.symlinkDirectories` names (**`verify`**'s `mutants`), in any repository of the project, the root among them, which a take-over's check, `code status`, `code diff`, `verify run` and the commit door's `left_dirty` pass over alike. Then each is removed, deepest first, its prepared links first, a registration whose folder is gone among them, a submodule's folder left empty as a checkout of its holder leaves it, so no child repository holds the task's branch out; a removal failing leaves the task in flight, and the verb run again finishes it. The worktree itself stays for Claude Code or plain `git worktree remove`, and the branches stay, the landing's. Every argument the engine can judge is checked before anything is written, and the verbs refuse in the engine's words: a unit the project lacks, a phase not written, a task the tracker lacks or already holds, a task already ended — and a verb the driver fails, never one the engine refuses, for a tool the driver needs that is missing or failing, naming it. Each runs under the writer lock. A verb that writes commits the tracker's store in one state commit, pushed (`task({unit}): {what it did}`), with whatever of setting the tracker up the state branch does not yet hold — the store's files, the driver setting, the ignore lines, and nothing else of the manifest or the ignore file; a read commits only that setting up, where there is any (`task({unit}): set up the tracker`); where the tracker refuses after the verb wrote, what it wrote is committed under a message saying the verb was refused. Responses: `phase` `{phase}`; `create`, `add`, `start`, `done` and `close` `{task, id}`, `done` and `close` adding `removed`, each child worktree removed, where any was, `close` adding `unparked`, the questions it was taken off, where any was, `type` adding `type`; `link` `{task, after}`; `ready` `{tasks: [{task, id, title}], back?}`; a quick-fix's `start` adding `put_back`, the paths its set-aside work put back, where it held any, a rename's both, as the raise's `set_aside` names them; `list` `{phase, tasks: [{task, id, title, status}]}`, a status `open`, `in-progress`, `done` or `closed`; `show` `{task, id, title, status, type, text, additions: [{by, text}], after, reason}`; `worktree` `{task, id, branch, worktree, repos: [{repo, path, already?}], taken_over?: {worktree, removed}, recorded}` — `worktree` the worktree's top, `repos` the project's own (`.`) first, then each child repository by its folder, `already: true` where it was laid out already, `taken_over` the earlier worktree the branch was taken over from and each child repository's worktree removed there, where it was, and `recorded` whether the position took the worktree — each with `committed` and `warnings` where it committed, and `note: held for the migration's commit` where the migration holds it. `stopped <unit> <task>` (`domain/position.cjs`) is the orchestrator's, as a task's builder stops and reports it could not build past a problem (13.11.9): it holds the time against the task in flight on the calling conversation's position, a stop held already kept, and writes nothing to the state. The task stands failed on the board (20.4.9) until the next **`brief`** `builder` or `fix` for it sends its builder back in, or a **`raise`** parks it, each taking the stop off. It refuses no conversation, and a task not in flight in it. `stopped` `{task, id, stopped}` — `stopped` the time held, ISO.

```bash
engine task phase <work-unit> <N> --title <title>                     # the phase written into the tracker as it starts
engine task create <work-unit> <N> --file <path>                      # a task from the template, numbered after the phase's highest
engine task add <work-unit> <task> --file <path> --by <orchestrator|person>
engine task type <work-unit> <task> <type>
engine task link <work-unit> <task> --after <task>
engine task ready <work-unit> [<N>]                                   # every task waiting with nothing before it, no question parking it; in step, the freed task whose turn has come
engine task start <work-unit> <task>                                  # in progress, and in flight on this conversation's position beside any others
engine task done <work-unit> <task>                                   # refused while a question waiting parks it
engine task close <work-unit> <task> --reason <reason>                # taken off every question parking it
engine task list <work-unit> <N>
engine task show <work-unit> <task>
engine task worktree <work-unit> <task>                               # the task's branch made in the builder's worktree where the task is built on, each child repository laid out on it
engine task stopped <work-unit> <task>                                # its builder's stop held against it in flight on this conversation's position
```

**The build's records** — `account` and `settle` (`domain/task-account.cjs`, a phase's settlement `domain/phase-settlement.cjs`, the reports read by `domain/round-report.cjs`) and `note` (`domain/notes.cjs`) — each run under the writer lock and commit what they took in one state commit, pushed; a commit git refuses is a warning, the files written. Each list in a record the engine reads is read as a careful writer writes it, and never loses or misreads what it holds (`domain/list-items.cjs`): an item opens on a line at the margin, outside a fenced block, its marker `-`, `*`, `+`, `N.` or `N)`, and holds every line to the next item's, a fence or a heading below level two among them; lines before the first item are a lead-in no reader reads, and a thematic break is neither; a line shaped like an item that does not read is refused, naming it; and a list says there is nothing where its one line, an item or not, begins with the word `None`, emphasis allowed. `account <unit> <task>` is the route by which a task's account and its review rounds' reports reach the state branch (12.14): the builder writes the account at `build/tasks/{task}.md` as the task lands, and each reviewer of the count (`kernel/reviewers.cjs`, two) its report of each round at `build/tasks/{task}/round-{N}-{letter}.md`, letters from `a`. The count may change between rounds as the model pass re-measures it, so a round before the last is read by the letters it holds, and the last, the round under review, by the count's: it is complete, and the next round is reviewed, once every reviewer of the count has reported it. **A reviewer's report** gives its notes for later under `## Notes for later`, before its findings, which the engine never reads in a task's or a phase's review — a review round's are its items, read by `domain/review-report.cjs` (**`review`**) — and ends in its last heading at level one or two, `## Findings`, holding `None` as all it holds, or a list — each finding an item at the margin opening on its call, `serious`, `bug` or `nit`, in any case, emphasis aside, then `:` or a dash, and somewhere in it, outside a fenced block, `Scenario: {the user scenario}`, emphasised or bulleted or not, on a line of its own or after the finding's text on its opening line — then in its last line, `Verdict: pass` or `Verdict: fail`, in any case, bold or not, a period after it or none, which says what its findings do: a report passes with nothing serious and no bug, and a round where every reviewer's report passes. Under `## Findings`, outside a fenced block, the report refuses, naming the line: a line at the margin beginning with a call that opens no finding, a heading among them; one below the margin opening as a finding would; an item opening `{word}:` on a word that is no call; and an item before the first finding. **The account** is five level-two headings in order — `## What was done`, `## Where in the code`, `## What the tests prove`, `## Differs from the task`, a list, an item for each way the work differs from the task, an item beginning `None` never one, or `None` as all it holds, and `## Reviews`, a `### Round N` summary of each round, in any case, from 1, one for every round the reports hold, and after each round the orchestrator settled, wherever it sits, `### Settled`, and nothing else — with nothing before the first, no other heading at level one or two, and none empty; the builder's brief carries it. An account is taken once the task's last round passed, or once the orchestrator settled it: `settle <unit> <task> --file <why>` writes the file's text, why the task was settled and what was done about it (13.4.6), at `build/tasks/{task}/round-{N}-settled.md` beside the last round's reports, the engine's alone, and commits it (`task({unit}): settle {task}, round {N}`); it refuses, opening `task settle: `, where the task's last round passed or is settled already. The account verb computes the task's figures and writes them, the engine's alone, at `build/tasks/{task}/figures.json`: `{"files", "added", "removed", "criteria", "additions", "started", "finished"}` — the files changed and lines added and removed by the task's own work across every repository holding its branch — a quick-fix's task's the net change of its own commits alone, each stamped with it — each applied in turn onto what the one before made from the first one's parent, so a line changed twice counts once and the other fixes' commits between them stay out, renames found; a commit that does not apply there, a line of it another fix changed in between, leaves each commit's lines counted as it was made, each file once, under the path it ends at — else from where the work starts there to the branch's tip — the later of the commit `task worktree` laid it out at and the tip of the work below it the branch holds since, merged back onto its chain, else the branch the repository lands on — so nothing it merged back from its chain counts, a submodule's pointer never a file of them, as `verify mutants` leaves it out; the acceptance criteria the task holds, each item at the margin of its `Acceptance scenarios` list, or one where it holds none; `additions`, how many additions the task holds (`task add`), whoever made them (13.11.3); `started`, when the task first moved into progress, as the tracker holds it (the task driver's `started`), which a second clone's merge of the tracker's store carries, null where it never moved, and `finished`, now, each ISO. It commits the account, every round's reports and settlement and the figures (`task({unit}): account {task}`). Each refuses, opening `task account: ` or `task settle: ` and writing nothing: a unit or a task the project lacks; a task done or closed; a task with no round's reports, a round holding none, the last round missing a report of the count's, or a report that does not read, every fault named. The account refuses besides a task a question waiting parks, naming each question's `raise draw <unit> Q{n}` (13.4.6); a last round neither passed nor settled; a task changed after its last round passed — something added to it since (`add`), or one of its calls decided against its lean (**`raise`**'s `answer`) — until its pair passes it in the round after (`task account: task {N}.{n} of {unit} changed after round {r} passed — something was added to it, or a call of it decided against its lean — so its pair reads it again first: engine brief reviewer {unit} {N}.{n} --letter <letter>, each reviewer of round {r+1}`, 12.1.4); no account, or one that does not read, every fault named; a task with no type; and no repository of the project holding work on the task's branch, as the reviewer's brief does. Settling refuses besides no such file, or one holding nothing. `settle <unit> --phase <N> --file <why> [--fixes <task>,…]` settles a phase review that failed (13.10.1): its fixes are tasks of the phase, each passed by its pair, so the orchestrator writes them, then settles the review naming them, none where it judged the findings need none. The engine writes, its alone, at `build/phases/phase-{N}/settlement.json`, `{"why", "fixes", "reports"}` — the file's text, the fix tasks by their ids, each once, and each report of the review by its file's name and the fingerprint of its text (`kernel/fingerprint.cjs`) — and commits it with the reports it answers (`task({unit}): settle phase {N}'s review`), so a second clone reads the review as this one does; settling again, at any time, replaces it — a fix closed and another written in its place, or a fix found owed after a settlement naming none. A settlement answers the reports as they stand where each was given to it, its text the same, so a report written since leaves the review unsettled; it is judged by what the files hold, never by the order of their commits, which a second clone's merge does not keep. It refuses, opening `task settle: ` and writing nothing: a unit the project lacks; a review not complete — none reported, a report of the count missing, or one that does not read; one every report passes; each fix named that is no task of the phase, one the tracker does not hold there, or one it has closed, every fault named; and no such file of the why, or one holding nothing. A task named with `--phase`, neither, or `--fixes` without `--phase` is a usage error. `note <unit> --file <note> [--phase <N>]` deposits a note for later (12.10) into `build/notes.md`, the engine's alone: `## Note {k}`, numbered after the last, over the file's text trimmed — `## Note {k}, for phase {N}` where `--phase` holds it for a phase of the cut not yet written, whose task writer takes it — and commits it (`task({unit}): note {k}[, for phase {N}]`). It refuses, opening `task note: `: a unit the project lacks; no such file, a file holding nothing, or a heading at level one or two; and for `--phase`, a unit with no cut, a phase the cut lacks, and one the tracker has written. A notes file holding anything but notes numbered from 1 refuses the note and every read of it — the briefs' notes, the consolidation's check — naming the fault and how the person puts it right by hand: each note under its heading numbered from 1 in the order they stand, the next note committing the file. A second clone's merge keeps them so, placing each machine's notes by note and renaming this machine's reports with them (**`state-merge`**). **A consolidation report**, a phase's at `build/phases/phase-{N}/consolidation.md`, ends in its last heading at level one or two, `## The notes`, holding `None` as all it holds, or a list, each item `- Note {k}[, Note {k}…]: dropped — {why}` or `- Note {k}[, Note {k}…]: a task — {title}`; an item opening on `Note` or `Notes` that does not read so is refused, naming it. A phase's notes to take are those held for no phase that no other phase's report took, whatever their numbers, as phases close in either order; a note banked after its consolidation is one more, the report gaining its line. A phase's notes are consolidated where it has none to take, report or not; or where its report reads, takes every one of them, takes none another phase's report took or held for a phase, and takes each once. Responses: `account` `{task, id, rounds, figures}`, adding `settled`, the rounds the orchestrator settled, where it settled any; `settle` `{task, id, round}`, with `--phase` `{phase, fixes}`; `note` `{number}`, adding `phase` where held — each with `committed`, and `warnings` and `note` as a state commit answers them.

```bash
engine task account <work-unit> <task>                                # the task's account and rounds' reports checked, its figures computed, all committed
engine task settle <work-unit> <task> --file <why>                    # the orchestrator's settlement of the task's failed last round
engine task settle <work-unit> --phase <N> --file <why> [--fixes <task>,…]  # the orchestrator's settlement of the phase's failed review, naming its fix tasks
engine task note <work-unit> --file <note> [--phase <N>]              # a note for later, held for an unwritten phase with --phase
```

**`land`** — a task's change landed through the project's landing driver (`domain/landing.cjs`): `open` the task's pull request, `chain` it into its phase's stack, merge a `phase` whole, and `finish`, which merges every phase still standing — the review phase's fix phases after every other. **The driver** is a folder the workflows ship, `scripts/drivers/landing/{name}/driver.cjs`, named by the project manifest's `drivers.landing`; where that is absent the first verb settles it — the driver serving the project's remote, else the local driver — and commits the setting on the state branch (`chore: the landing driver is {name}`), whatever the driver then does. A verb that fails where a tool the driver needs is missing or fails to run is refused for that. **The names** (`domain/landing-names.cjs`), one form for every driver: a task's branch is `{type}/{unit}-{phase}.{n}-{slug}` — the slug the title lowercased, its accents folded (`ß` as `ss`, `æ` as `ae` and the like), its runs of letters and digits joined by `-`, as many whole words as fit in 48 characters (a first word longer cut at 48), and `task` where no letter or digit is left — the name the task's branch must already carry, made by whoever makes the branch; a phase's branch `feat/{unit}-phase-{phase}`; a task's pull request title, and the subject of the commit it squashes to, `{type}({unit}): {title}`, the message closed by a `Task: {internal-id}` trailer — how a landed task is read back off git. **Where it lands**: the default branch of each repository of the project the task touches — its own, and each nested in its folder, a clone or a submodule — never the state branch, under the same names, children first and the project's own last, so a submodule's landing merges before the repository holding it records its pointer. `open` reads the task's type and title from the tracker as `task show` does — a task it lacks, or holds with no type, refused — and refuses, before it opens anything: no repository of the project holding a branch by that name; the branch changing nothing in the repositories holding it; its change carrying anything under `.leeway/` at the top of the project's repository; the branch not descending from its chain's tip; a task of one of the review's fix phases whose branch, in a repository holding the unit's work not landed below its phase, does not descend from that work's tip (`land: task {N}.{n}'s branch {branch} does not descend from {tip}, where {unit}'s work not landed below phase {N} ends, which a fix phase is built on (7.2.6) — merge it back onto {tip} first, then open it again`); a branch pointing a submodule at a commit the submodule here does not hold; and, where no repository holds anything past its default branch or the chain's tip and none has the task opened already, nothing past the tip anywhere, naming the project's own where it holds the branch. Past those, a repository with nothing past its default branch or the chain's tip is passed over while another holds the task's work. A merge refuses, before anything merges, where a default branch points a submodule the walk lands in at a commit the submodule's own default branch does not descend from (`land: {into} points {folder} at {commit}, a commit {folder}'s {into} does not hold — bring {folder}'s {into} up to it, as \`git -C {folder} pull\` does where its remote holds it, and land again`) — but where that branch holds the commit's change, as the submodule's pull request merged by hand after its holder's leaves it, for every work type, the merge goes on: a stack moving that submodule lands its pointer at the submodule's landing of the moving commit's change, as every merge does, and no re-point is made for it; one the repository's merge leaves pointing where it did is then pointed at the submodule's landing of the change, found as a merge finds a submodule's landing — by the `Task:` line of a task the commit that last moved the pointer names, else the oldest commit there holding the change — by one commit of its own onto its default branch, `chore({unit}): point {folders} at its landing` (`their` for more than one), naming no task, landed as the driver lands a change: locally, the default branch moved to it; on GitHub, pushed to `chore/{unit}-point-submodules` and merged through a pull request of its own from it, squashed, once GitHub has worked out that it can — the one open from that branch, where a landing before opened one — the default branch here then brought to it. The repository's answer then carries `repointed` `{pointers, commit?, pull_request?}` — `pointers` each submodule pointed, by its path, at the commit it points at now — beside `already` where its merge merged nothing; the verb answers it among the repositories where it did something, never `already` for it, and a refusal of the re-point names what its repository merged before it. A driver landing through a host refuses a repository whose remote it does not serve. Every verb first stops on a rebase stopped in a worktree a remedy made, until it is settled or ended there. A refusal in a nested repository names it, one after a repository moved names what moved where and carries the warnings those landings gave, and the verb run again picks up where it stopped; `open` and `phase` run again where their work is done answer `already: true`. `chain` refuses a task not open, `phase` a phase with no task opened; `chain` refuses a quick-fix's task first of all, its tasks landing in its one pull request with no stack to chain into (`land: {unit} is a quick-fix, whose tasks land in its one pull request — land open {unit} {N}.{n} takes each, with no stack to chain it into`); `chain` refuses too, first, before the landing driver is settled, a task its pair has not passed, read off its rounds' reports alone (12.1.4): none reported (`land: task {N}.{n} has no review round reported — a task chains into its phase's stack once its pair has passed it`), a report that does not read, refused as the round's read refuses it, a last round failed and not settled (`land: round {r} of task {N}.{n} did not pass — … once the orchestrator settles it: task settle {unit} {N}.{n} --file <why>`), and a task changed after its last round passed, as `task account` refuses it (`land: task {N}.{n} of {unit} changed after round {r} passed — …`); `open` and `chain` both refuse a task a question parks, and one with no account taken of its review as it stands — never taken, or taken over fewer rounds than its review holds (`land: task {N}.{n} of {unit} has no account taken of its review as it stands — once its builder writes it from its brief, engine brief builder {unit} {task}, take it: engine task account {unit} {task}`), `open` taking a quick-fix's task before its pair has read it, its one pull request opening at its first commit and each later one pushed to it (19.5.20) — `task done` holds a quick-fix's task to its account, as any task's — `open` before anything else it judges; `open` a bugfix's reproducing task, 1.1, while the build's series of its test on the unfixed code does not prove the bug at its tip, as `task done` refuses it (`land: {unit} is a bugfix, so task 1.1 of {unit}, its reproducing task, lands once the test it wrote proves the bug, failing five times on the unfixed code at its tip (15.5.3): {why}`), so a test that passed is reworked before it lands; `open` a quick-fix's task closed, `open` the one verb that puts a task in the quick-fix's pull request (`domain/fix-end.cjs`; `land: task 1.{n} of {unit} is closed — a fix closed lands nothing`), refused first; and one landed, its landing read as `task done` reads it, naming its done (`land: task 1.{n} of {unit} has landed — a fix is done once it lands: task done {unit} 1.{n}`; one done already `… has landed, and is done — nothing of it is left to open`), each before any other refusal of the task. **A quick-fix lands as one pull request in each repository it touches** (`quickFixLand`, `domain/landing.cjs`, 7.2.24): its one branch, `fix/{unit}`, every task of its build committed there through the door, onto the branch that repository lands on — the project's own, and each nested one it touches: one its record names, `fixes.repos` on the unit's manifest — each nested repository the door committed a share of a fix in, recorded on the state branch before the commit there (**`commit`**), so every clone carries it — or one whose branch there holds work; no host of any other is asked anything — its branch made at the first commit of a task's there (**`commit`**), landed children first, as a task's are (7.2.21), each walked as a task's verbs walk theirs, whatever its branches here hold, so a repository whose branch is gone here, a second clone of it, or one merged by hand is read by its content and landed. The host's copy of the branch is the truth for what the pull request holds: every read fetches it and the open pull request's head. A commit past the branch it lands on is the quick-fix's work where it was committed since the day before the unit was made — a fix's through the door, which refuses one git would date before then (**`commit`**), or a person's own made during the quick-fix — its stamp naming the task it is. What a host holds is the quick-fix's only where it is the unit's: a pull request opened from the repository itself, never a fork, since that day; and the host's branch where it holds the quick-fix's work past the branch it lands on, or a commit stamped with one of its tasks since then, as a branch merged by hand and kept there holds — one holding neither, made before the unit was, is another's, which every readying refuses on before anything moves, naming it and its repository (`land: {the project's host|{repo}'s host} holds a branch fix/{unit} that is not {unit}'s — none of its commits is a fix of {unit}'s or was made since {unit} was — and nothing moved: delete it there, or rename it, then try again`), so it is never taken, brought in, retitled or landed. A repository's work is the quick-fix's to open and land where its branch holds the quick-fix's work — the person's own commits made during it, alone on it or not, landing with the squash — so all that is pushed is the unit's. `open` of any of its tasks pushes the branch and opens the pull request, once the branch holds a commit, or pushes each later commit to the one open, in each repository whose branch holds the quick-fix's work past the branch it lands on, its title the quick-fix's landing subject, `{type}({unit}): {description}` — `feat` where any task in it is, else `fix` where any is, else the first's type, `fix` where it holds none, the person's own commits alone — and its body each task in it, in the order the fixes were done, as their records were written, then the rest by their commits' order, each as `{type}({unit}): {title}` in its own type then its `Task:` line, kept so as tasks join it — a task with no type refused, as a task's opening refuses it (`land: task 1.{n} has no type in the tracker, … give it one with \`task type {unit} 1.{n} <type>\``) — the branches readied first as `task start` readies them (**`task`**), a pull request merged opening a new one for what is left, each push leased against the host's copy as the read found it, so one moved since is refused (`land: fix/{unit} could not be pushed to {remote} ({git's words}), so {unit}'s pull request was not opened — clear what git names, and open it again`); refused before anything opens where a branch points a submodule at a commit the submodule here lacks, as a task's opening refuses it, and where no repository's branch holds the quick-fix's work past the branch it lands on (`land: task 1.{n}'s branch fix/{unit} changes nothing past {into} — commit its change there first`). `phase` of it and `finish` merge each pull request, children first — the branches readied first, so each squash names what is left alone, each pushed to its pull request as it then stands, a repository holding a submodule the walk lands in pointing it at its landing first, as a task's merge points it — the submodule's landing named by a task's `Task:` line, else, where its default branch lacks the pointer, the oldest commit there holding the pointer's change, as a merge by hand under the person's own message lands it — each repository with nothing past brought up to the host's — every commit squashed into one under its title — on GitHub, `{title} (#{k})` — and body, so the commit names each task it lands by its `Task:` line, a commit no task's stamped, the person's own, landing with it — and, where no branch holds the quick-fix's work past the branch it lands on, a merge by hand having landed it all, bring each branch here up to the host's, answering `already`; refused, before anything merges, while a task done holds work neither landed nor on the quick-fix's branch — as a branch cut again leaves a commit that did not apply there — or none at all, naming its change made again through the door, which takes it though the task is done (`land: {unit} lands once each task done is landed or in its pull request — task 1.{n} of {unit} holds {commit}, which is neither landed nor on fix/{unit} — make its change again there through the door, then land again: {the remake}`), never answering `already` so; `phase` of a quick-fix refused for a phase the tracker holds none of (`land: {unit} has no phase {N} — a quick-fix lands as one pull request, every phase it holds with it: land finish {unit}`); a conflict with that branch at the merge names the merge of it into the quick-fix's branch (`land: {branch} conflicts with {into} at {paths}, so nothing moved — merge {into} into {branch}, settling the conflict, and land again`; on GitHub, `land: {unit}'s pull request #{k} conflicts with {into} on GitHub, so {unit}'s pull request was not merged — merge origin/{into} into {branch}, settling the conflict, and land again`). Elsewhere, on a host, a pull request merged by hand stays in its phase's stack, landed once its change is on the default branch as the host has it — merged into it, or into the branch of the one below once that one has landed and its own change has reached it — never by its merged state alone, as the site's button merges a pull request into its own base; `phase` and `finish` finish a stack merged in part by hand, for every work type, each pull request not landed merged alone, bottom first, the default branch read afresh before each: one open on another's branch retargeted onto the default branch first, and one merged into a branch that never reached it opened again onto the default branch from its branch as the host holds it — what was merged into that branch since landing with it — or, the host holding it no more, pushed from here (`land: task {N}.{n}'s pull request #{k} was merged into {base}, whose change never reached {into}, and its branch {branch} is gone here and on GitHub — make it again at {commit} with \`git branch {branch} {commit}\`, and land again`); `phase` answers `already` once each has landed. `phase`, for a phase the unit's cut holds, is refused until its phase review has passed and its fixes are done, as the phase close's `phase-review-passed` reads it (**`checklist`**; `land: phase {N} of {unit} merges once its phase review has passed and its fixes are done — {what stands in the way; …}`, a failed review no settlement answers naming `task settle {unit} --phase {N} --file <why> [--fixes <task>,…]`, 7.2.4) — a unit with no manifest asking none; one of the review's fix phases, past the cut, is refused while a task of it is neither done nor closed, in flight among them, the first named in its way on (`land: phase {N} of {unit} merges once each of its tasks is done or closed — task {N}.{n} is neither: task done {unit} {N}.{n}, or task close {unit} {N}.{n} --reason <why>`, `tasks {N}.{n}, … are` for more than one); `finish`, for every work type, and `phase` of a quick-fix, which lands its one pull request whole, are refused before anything moves exactly as finish's yes refuses before it lands anything, through the one read (`finishLandRefusal`, `domain/finish.cjs`), each in finish's words after `land: `: a unit the project lacks or holds not in progress; the build's own refusal — a unit of a retired type, one built under the old plan, a build not begun (`land: {unit}'s build has not begun — ready to build's yes begins it: render ready-to-build {unit}`); where the unit has a review phase, until it is over, as finish's `review-passed` reads it — a task of the build unended, a phase not landed in step, a round not passed or its fix tasks not done, a quick-fix's review asked for among them (`land: {unit} finishes once its review phase is over — {the way on}`), a quick-fix with no review asked asking none; finish's checklist not closed, or an item of it the engine checks failing since its close — a question waiting, a record not indexed among them; a quick-fix's fix neither done nor closed, or done with no record; a verdict still to ask (`land: a verdict is still to ask — render finish asks each before the yes`); and a quick-fix's project checkout holding changes not committed (`land: {unit}'s tasks were built in the project's own checkout, which holds changes not committed — {paths} — so nothing was landed: commit them or set them aside, then close it again`); `finish` makes each repository's next merge until none is left, a refusal saying which phases merged before it. Nothing is recorded but the driver setting, the tracker's setting up that `open`'s read commits where the state branch lacks it, and what a driver keeps in a repository's own refs: the landing's state is read off git and the host each time. Response: `open` `{task, branch, already?, repos}`, `chain` `{phase, repos, next?}` — `next`, where the task is the last of its phase of the cut to end, its phase's notes consolidated (**`checklist`**'s `notes-consolidated`), its phase review not begun and the verify setup naming a mutation tool — named once for the phase, by whatever ends its last task once its notes are consolidated, or else by `brief phase-review`'s refusal of a phase the tool has not run over at its tips — `{kind: "engine", argv: ["verify", "mutants", unit, N]}`, the phase's mutation run, run once the task ends, from wherever the conversation is, `verify mutants` laying the phase out at its tips (15.6.3) — `phase` `{phase, already?, repos}`, `finish` `{repos}` — `repos` children first, each repository where the landing did something, `repo` naming it (`.` the project's own) beside what the driver answered, `finish`'s `{into, phases}` — each with `driver`, and `committed` and `warnings` where it has them. A quick-fix's: `open` `{task, branch, already?, repos, cut_again?}`, each repository's `pull_request` on GitHub; `phase` `{phase, already?, repos, cut_again?}` and `finish` `{already?, repos, cut_again?}`, `repos` children first, each repository it landed in, `{into, merged, commit}` — `merged` the tasks the squash landed, `commit` it here, `pull_requests` in its place on GitHub — `cut_again` as `task start` answers it.

```bash
engine land open <work-unit> <phase>.<task>     # the task's pull request, in every repository its change touches
engine land chain <work-unit> <phase>.<task>    # the task chained into its phase's stack; its phase's last naming the mutation run
engine land phase <work-unit> <phase>           # the phase merged whole
engine land finish <work-unit>                  # every phase still standing merged
```

**The build's screens** (`domain/build-screens.cjs`, drawn by `domain/projections/build.cjs`) — what the person sees of the build in the conversation, the same whatever order Claude works in (12.12.2): render surfaces, each refusing, opening `render {surface}: `, a unit the project lacks or whose build has not begun, a quick-fix — but for `task-account`, `build-stop` and `build-progress`, which serve its fixes once begun (`{wu} is a quick-fix, whose fixes have no cut to build by — they are entered at enter implementation {wu}`) — and a task the tracker lacks. Where the build stands they read from `buildStanding` alone, for the calling conversation; a task from the tracker, a phase past the cut named `the review's fixes, round {k}` (P14); a task's account, rounds and figures as `task account` took them; the questions waiting from `raise waiting`'s projection (12.13.7); a task's calls, the current decisions of Claude's at the build's places carrying it (12.12.12). Nothing on them counts a measure (17.2.2). **`build-headline <wu> --file <payload>`**, as work starts (12.12.4): the payload `{"tasks": [{"task", "changes", "watch"}, …]}`, what each task started together is about to change and what to watch when it lands; `DISPLAY: build marker`, the step marker `□ Build (phase {N} of {M}) · {position}`, the phase the first of the cut not closed — once the cut is through, the review's, `□ Review[ (the review's fixes, round {k})] · {position}`, the first of its fix phases not closed named while one is open (3.3.10) — then `DISPLAY: build headline`, one block naming each task by its number and title with its phase — refused for a task not in progress, one named twice, none, or a payload of another shape. **`task-landed <wu> <task>`**, as a task lands, once its account is taken — a bugfix's reproducing task's once its bug is proven at its tip too, refused till then as `land open` refuses it (**`land`**) (12.12.5): `DISPLAY: task landed` — the task; `✓ Landed`, the round its pair passed it in and the rounds of fixes before it, or the round the orchestrator settled its review in, and how many calls went to the ledger; WHAT CHANGED, the account's What was done; ITS TESTS PROVE, each acceptance criterion in its own words, the scenario id it opens with taken off; where the task holds scenarios of the cut, RUNNING {PRODUCT} PROVED {k} OF {n}, `runsOf` at the tips of the task's branch, each scenario not proven by its criterion's words, and where a run there counts, one skipped never, that the proof is on the task's page on the board; then, every time, the questions waiting, counted, a line each with what it parks. No review report is printed (12.12.6), and no gate. **`task-account <wu> <task>`**, any task's account printed when asked (12.14.1, 12.14.2): `DISPLAY: task account` — the task, its verdict — passed in round {N}, after the rounds of fixes before it, or settled in round {N} — its phase and place among the phase's tasks, WHAT IT DOES (its Outcome), WHAT WAS DONE (the account's What was done, then a `·` line for each item of Where in the code and What the tests prove, a fenced diagram kept as drawn, then the engine's figures), DIFFERS FROM THE TASK, and REVIEWS, each round's summary under its heading; a task whose account is not taken drawn with its description alone, saying none is written yet, or, where it closed, that it closed without one. **`step-gate <wu> <task>`**, in step alone, once the task's account is taken and before it is done, a bugfix's reproducing task once its bug is proven at its tip, refused till then as `task-landed` is — a task waits at its gate in progress, `task done` following the person's go (12.7.9, 12.14.3): `DISPLAY: step gate`, the account as `task-account` draws it without its reviews, then `MENU: step gate — {task}`, `Approve this task?`: `y/yes` `continue`; `t/technical` and `s/show` each with `next` `render step-gate {wu} {task}`, the gate again once the retelling is shown; `a/auto`, `workunit position {wu} auto` (3.3.6); `Ask` and `Comment` typed — refused in auto (12.12.7), before the account is taken, once the task has ended, and for a task waiting behind another in flight, until that one is done, as **`task`**'s `start` reads the task in flight (`{wu} builds in step, one task at a time — task {N}.{n} is in flight, and task {N}.{n} goes on once it is done`, 12.7.9). **`phase-merge-gate <wu> <N>`**, in step alone, before `land phase` (7.2.4): `MENU: phase merge — {N}`, `y/yes` running `land phase {wu} {N}`, and `Comment` — refused in auto, for a phase the tracker lacks, one merged already, one of the cut where `land phase` would refuse the phase review, and one of the review's fixes with a task not done. **`build-stop <wu>`**, when nothing can go on without the person's answer (12.12.9, 12.13.22): the step marker — a quick-fix's `□ Quick-fix`, once its review is asked for `□ Quick-fix review[ (the review's fixes, round {k})]`, no position — then `DISPLAY: build stop`, the red stop, `The build has stopped: nothing more can be built until you answer`, a quick-fix's `The fixes have stopped: nothing more can be built until you answer`, once the cut is through — a quick-fix's review asked for — `The review has stopped: nothing more can go on until you answer`, then `DISPLAY: build stop report` — the questions waiting first, WHAT WAS BUILT as `build-progress` draws it, and WHAT RUNS ONCE YOU ANSWER, each question's parked tasks and the tasks linked after them — refused while no question waits, and while the build can go on: a task in progress in any phase written, or one ready, that no question parks, of a phase whose tasks can start: its start checklist closed, the phase past the cut, or every item the engine checks of its start holding, so only Claude's own items stand before it (**`checklist`**); and, once the cut is through, while the review's round goes on without the person, `{wu}'s review goes on: round {N}'s reads are named next: review reads {wu} <area> …` or `{wu}'s review goes on: round {N}'s readers are still to brief or reading: {report}, …`, each report not yet in named — once every report of the round is in, the stop is drawn, Claude's call. **`build-progress <wu>`**, the build's phases and tasks when asked (12.12.11, 7.4.2): the step marker, then `DISPLAY: build progress` — the tasks done of those not closed and how many run, unparked, a task behind the task in flight or at its gate running no more;
