then each phase of the cut, `Phase {N} of {M} · {name}` — a quick-fix's fixes `Phase 1 of 1 · the fixes` — and each of the review's fix phases written, `Phase {N} · the review's fixes, round {k}`, its state, a written phase relying on one not merged `written · after phase {N}`, on another unit not finished `written · after {unit}`, `⚑ fixes owed: …` beneath it in the standing's words, and its tasks, each `waits on Q{n}` while a question parks it, `after {task}` while it is linked after one not done, else where it stands, `behind the task in flight` and `at its gate` among them; a phase not yet written drawn from the cut.

**`review`** — the review phase's verbs (`domain/review-verbs.cjs`; the round recorded `domain/review-round.cjs`; the round's state in `domain/review-phase.cjs`). Each refuses, opening `review {verb}: ` and writing nothing, a unit whose review phase `enter review` refuses, and where no round is under review: one ended the phase (`{unit}'s review phase {passed|was settled} in round {N} — finish is next`, or `— its fix tasks are built, then finish: task {N}.{n}, …` while that round's fix tasks are neither done nor closed, or `— the notes its fix tasks' builders left are answered, then finish: notes#{k}, … — review notes {unit} --file <notes.json>` once they are and a builder's note stands), or the round before's fix tasks wait (`round {N} is read once every fix task of round {N-1} is done — task {N}.{n} {not started|in progress|not in the tracker}, …`), or, in step, once they are done, their phase's merge at its gate (`round {N} is read once round {N-1}'s fix phase has merged — phase {M} waits at its gate: render phase-merge-gate {unit} {M}`, 14.6.2), {M} the lowest phase up to theirs whose stack waits there, a fix phase built on those below it merging after them (7.2.6); a closed fix task waits on nothing.

`review reads <unit> <area|tests> …` names the reads a later round takes, as Claude names them once the round before's fix tasks are done, from what they changed (14.3.3): each area by its name in the layout, the test surface as `tests`, the test surface read only where named. The reads whose findings made fix tasks are each such finding's area and each read whose report holds an item it answers in `from`. It writes `review/round-{N}-reads.json`, `{"reads": [names…]}`, in the spec's order, the test surface last, and commits it (`review({unit}): round {N} reads {names}`), before the round's briefs are filled; the round opens there, its snapshot of the working tree taken where none is held, so naming more reads keeps the one held. Refused besides: round 1, which reads every area and the test surface (`round 1 reads every area and the test surface (tests) — a later round's are named`); a name that is no area of the spec (`{name} is no area of {unit}'s spec — name its areas by {names}`); a set leaving out a read whose findings made fix tasks in the round before (`round {N} reads every area whose findings made fix tasks in round {N-1}, and leaves out {reads}`) or one named for the round before (`round {N} already reads {reads} — areas are added, never dropped`). Response: `{"ok": true, "round": N, "reads": [names…], "committed": "<short-sha>"}`.

`review accept-tree <unit>` takes the working tree as it is, on the person's word, after `review round` refused a tree changed since its round's snapshot or found none held: it takes the round's snapshot again, over any held, and writes nothing else — the one retake (12.7.8, 14.S13). Response: `{"ok": true, "round": N, "snapshot": "taken"}`.

`review notes <unit> --file <notes.json>` — answering, where it ends a quick-fix's review, `next`, its close, as `review round` does; so does `task done` or `task close` of the last fix task of the round that ended it, where no note stands — answers the builders' notes standing once the round that ended the review has its fix tasks done — the notes those tasks' builders banked, which no round is left to answer — from Claude's account, a payload file `{findings}`, each finding as `review round` takes one, of the area `notes`, every note standing answered by exactly one, out of scope, filed or dropped by Claude's call (14.3.5). Their findings join that round's record, `review/round-{N}.json`, its report `review/round-{N}.md` written again with them, each filed listed as the round's are, and both are committed (`review({unit}): round {N}'s builders' notes`). The review is not over while a builder's note stands, so finish's `review-passed` stays open, naming each. Refused besides: a round under review, naming why it cannot be read yet where it cannot, as `review round` refuses it, else (`round {N} is under review, and answers the builders' notes standing among its items — review round {unit} --file <round.json>`); that round's fix tasks waiting (`{unit}'s review phase {passed|was settled} in round {N} — its fix tasks are built first, then the notes their builders left are answered: task {N}.{n} {standing}, …`); no note standing (`no builder's note stands unanswered — {unit}'s review phase {passed|was settled} in round {N}, and finish is next`); and a payload of another shape, every fault named, as `review round` names them. Response: `{"ok": true, "round": N, "answered": ["notes#{k}", …], "committed": "<short-sha>"}`.

`review restore-tree <unit> --listed <print>` puts back what changed under the round that its snapshot proves, the row `put` of the gate over it (`render review-tree`), and names the rest: each listed path clean when the round began restored, index and working tree, to its repository's HEAD as the snapshot holds it — a file that HEAD lacks, added since, removed with it — and each untracked one removed where the snapshot proves it absent, untracked and not ignored then; a path changed already when the round began, one holding now what such a path held then, one ignored then or in a folder ignored whole then, a folder git lists whole and one in a repository the snapshot did not see are not put back, and a HEAD moved since puts nothing back. Before it touches any path it copies what each holds now — a link as a link, to a folder or to nothing — and, where a path's index holds a version neither its HEAD's nor its working tree's, that version beside it as `{path}.staged`, into a folder of its own, numbered after the round's last: `.cache/.set-aside/{unit}/round-{N}/put-{k}/{path}` in the state, beside the units' caches and in none, so no lifecycle verb that takes a unit's cache — finish's yes, a cancel, an absorb — takes it, and nothing in the engine removes it: the person does. Every copy is made before any path is touched, the folder named only once all are. Nothing else is written and the snapshot stands, so the round is recorded again, refusing again while anything listed remains. `--listed` is the fingerprint of the changes as the gate listed them. Refused besides, putting nothing back: no snapshot of the round's held (`no snapshot of round {N}'s working tree is held — {its cache is gone|the one held is round {K}'s}, so nothing can be put back: render review-tree {unit} asks the person whether the tree stands as it is`); a tree as the snapshot holds it (`the working tree outside .leeway/ is as round {N}'s snapshot holds it — record the round: review round {unit} --file <round.json>`); a tree no longer as the gate listed it (`the working tree changed since the gate listed it — render review-tree {unit} lists it as it stands`); nothing listed the engine puts back (`{change} — {so nothing is put back here|not put back here: {why}}; … — the person puts it back by hand, or keeps the tree as it is: review accept-tree {unit}`); and a copy that cannot be set aside, the copies made for it gone with its folder (`{path} could not be set aside in {folder} — {error}; nothing is put back — keep the tree as it is, review accept-tree {unit}, or put it back by hand`). Response: `{"ok": true, "round": N, "restored": [paths…], "kept": [changes…], "set_aside": {"folder": "{absolute path of put-{k}}", "paths": [paths…]}}` — `paths` each copy as it landed in the folder, each restored path that held anything and each `{path}.staged`; `set_aside` null where nothing was copied.

**The gate over the tree**, `render review-tree <unit>` (`domain/review-tree.cjs`, the screen `domain/projections/review-phase.cjs`), stands where `review round` refused a tree changed since the round's snapshot, or found none held. It draws `{Title} — the working tree changed under round {N}` and `CHANGED OUTSIDE .leeway/ ({n})`, each repository whose HEAD moved (`{repository}'s HEAD moved, from {commit} to {commit}`, `{repository} is a repository new since the round began`, or `{repository} holds no commit now, at {commit} when the round began`, marked `— so nothing is put back here`), then each path whose status or what it holds changed, a rename as its two paths, one the engine does not put back marked `— not put back here: {changed already when the round began|it holds what {path} held when the round began|ignored when the round began|in a folder ignored whole when the round began|a folder git lists whole|in a repository the round did not see}`; while the unit's set-aside holds anything, `SET ASIDE BY PUT BACK ({n}) — kept there until you remove them`, its folder, `.cache/.set-aside/{unit}`, absolute, then each copy from it, `round-{N}/put-{k}/{path}`, its lines never wrapped; then `MENU: review tree`, `Put the working tree back?`: `p/put` (`Put it back as the round found it — what it changes is set aside under .leeway/, nothing deleted`), where no HEAD moved and at least one change can be put back, runs `review restore-tree {unit} --listed {print}`; `k/keep` runs `review accept-tree {unit}`; and `Comment`. Where no snapshot of the round's is held it draws `{Title} — round {N}'s working tree` and why, `no snapshot of round {N}'s working tree is held — {its cache is gone|the one held is round {K}'s}: nothing can be put back here.`, and `Nothing can be put back here: keep the tree as it is, or tell me what to do with it.` over `Keep the working tree as it is?`, with `k/keep` and `Comment` alone. It refuses, opening `render review-tree: `, a round it cannot read, as `review round` refuses it, and a tree as the snapshot holds it.

`review round <unit> --file <round.json>` records the round under review from Claude's account of it, a payload file `{can_now, findings, settled?}`: `can_now` each line of what the person can now do; each finding its `area`, by name, one the round reads, `tests` for the test surface, its `scope`, `in` or `out` of the change, its `call`, `serious`, `bug` or `nit`, its `text`, `from`, the items of the round's reports it answers, each `{report}#{n}` — the report's file name without `.md`, then the finding's place among its findings, from 1 (`1-cards-a#2`) — or, for a note for later, each item of a notes section — headed `Notes for later` or `Note for later` at level two or three, numbered or not (`## 2. Notes for later`), or that label on a line alone, bold or plain, any case, emphasised, a parenthesis after it or none, a colon after it or none, anywhere in the report outside a fenced block, after its findings too, and ending the finding above it — `{report}#n{k}`, its place among them, from 1 (`1-cards-a#n1`), a note lying beyond the change so the finding answering it `out` of scope, filed or dropped, never a fix — or, for a builder's note standing, `notes#{k}`, note {k} of `build/notes.md`, a note for later held for no phase that no phase's consolidation report took and no round answered, as a fix task's builder banks one (`task note`), answered as a report's note is, a finding answering those alone of the area `notes` — or, in a bugfix's round 1 where its reproducing test passed on the unfixed code again since the review began (**`verify`**'s `run … --unfixed`), `unfixed#1`, a serious item — and exactly one of where it landed: `fix`, a task of the round's fix phase, numbered past the cut, phase M+k for round k (P14), the tracker holding it and not closed; `inbox`, out of scope only, a live inbox item (`inbox file`); `drop`, why it was dropped; with `ledger`, the id of Claude's call filing or dropping it, a current decision of Claude's at `{unit}.review` (14.3.7), beside each finding out of scope and each in scope dropped as `serious` or `bug`, and no other; and `settled`, how Claude settled a failing round that makes no fix task — its failing findings fixed in earlier rounds or dropped so (14.6.3). It reads each report the round asks for, at `review/round-{N}/{area}-{a|b}.md` for each area it reads and `review/round-{N}/tests-a.md` where it reads the test surface, by the reviewers' grammar: every item of every report, nits and notes for later included, is answered by exactly one finding, at no lesser call than the gravest finding it answers; the round passes where every report does and, in a bugfix's round 1, no run of its reproducing test on the unfixed code since the review began passed. It records each scenario of the cut — a quick-fix's, each its fixes' acceptance names, `{n}.S{k}` — at the change's tips, then writes `review/round-{N}.json`, the round's record, `review/round-{N}.md`, its report — the closing report, every round's, where the round ends the review — what each round decided, its fix tasks as filed under `FIX TASKS, PHASE {M+k}`, no standing claimed, and the calls decided for the person counted when the round was recorded — what put back set aside, a folder of this machine's, left to the screens that draw it live — and `review/round-{N}-checks.md`, every report whole, drops the round's snapshot, and commits them with the reports (`review({unit}): round {N}`). A round that passed, or that was settled, ends the review, which is over once that round's fix tasks — a passing round's nits — are done, no area read again for them; else the next round reads, once every fix task is done, the reads `review reads` names. Refused besides: a bugfix's round 1 before its reproducing test has run on the unfixed code again since the review began (`{unit} is a bugfix, and round 1 is recorded once the test that reproduces its bug has run on the unfixed code again since the review began, where it must fail (15.5.6): engine verify run {unit} {scenario} --unfixed --test {command}`); a later round whose reads are not named (`round {N}'s areas are not yet named — review reads {unit} <area> …, from what round {N-1}'s fix tasks changed, among them {reads}`); no snapshot of the round's held, its cache lost or an earlier round's held (`no snapshot of round {N}'s working tree is held — {its cache is gone|the one held is round {K}'s}, and nothing is taken of the tree unasked: render review-tree {unit} asks the person whether it stands as it is`); the working tree outside `.leeway/` changed since the snapshot (`the working tree outside .leeway/ changed since the snapshot the round is held to: {repository}'s HEAD moved, from {commit} to {commit} (or `{repository} is a repository new since the round began`), …, {path}, … — nothing is put back unasked: render review-tree {unit} asks the person whether it is put back or kept`) — entering again never retakes it; a report missing (`round {N} has no report at {path}`) or not reading as a reviewer's — among them a notes section holding prose and no item, its lead-in above its items and a sole `None` line allowed (`"{line}" under {heading} is no note — each note is an item, …`), and a line naming the notes for later that is no heading of them — the label with a note after it, or worded or marked otherwise, `## Notes (for later)`, `*Notes for later*`, `> **Notes for later**` (`"{line}" names the notes for later but is no heading of them — the notes go under a heading of their own, …`); a payload of another shape, every fault named — among them an item no finding answers (`{report}#{n} is answered by no finding — every item of every report lands in the round's record (14.3.1)`), one two findings name (`{report}#{n} is named by findings {i} and {j}, and is one finding's`), an item no report of the round holds, a note for later answered in scope (`finding {i} answers a note for later, which lies beyond the change — its scope is out, and it is filed or dropped, never fixed`), a call lesser than the items' (`finding {i} is a nit, and the report items it answers call it a bug`), an area the round does not read (`finding {i} is of "{area}", which the round does not read — it reads {reads}`, adding `; a finding answering builders' notes alone is of notes` where it answers one), and a missing or stray `ledger`; a passing round settled (`round passed, and only a failing round is settled`); a settled round that makes fix tasks (`round {N} is settled, and makes fix tasks — a round settled makes none, its failing findings fixed in earlier rounds or dropped`); and a failing round with no fix and no settlement (`round {N} failed — write its fixes as tasks of {unit}-{M+k} and name each on its finding, or say how it is settled`). Response: `{"ok": true, "round": N, "passed": bool, "fixes": [tasks…], "ends": bool, "fix_phase": M+k where it made fix tasks, "reads_at_least": [reads…] where the review goes on, "areas": [areas…], "committed": "<short-sha>", "next"?}` — `next`, for a quick-fix whose round ends its review and leaves it over, no fix task or builder's note holding it open, its close in the same conversation, `{"kind": "engine", "argv": ["render", "finish", "<unit>"]}`, whose checklist then carries `review-passed` done.

`review ask <unit>` asks for a quick-fix's review at its close (19.5.13), the close's `r/review` row: it records the request, `fixes.review_asked`, the instant it was asked, and commits it (`review({unit}): asked for at its close`), answering `next` the review phase's skill, invoked in the same conversation with no clear, which enters it, `{"kind": "invoke", "skill": "leeway-review-process", "args": ["quick-fix", "<unit>"]}` — once, a request recorded answering `already` with the same `next`. Until it is asked, `enter review` refuses a quick-fix (`{unit} is a quick-fix, whose review runs only once the person asks for one at its close — render finish {unit} draws it`); once asked, its one area is the change (`1-the-change`, `--area 1`), its record and its change-set from the base, held against its fixes' acceptance in their own words where a build's is held against its spec, never spec.md (19.5.24, **`brief`**); the change-set read through the landing's one read by content (7.2.22), so a merge of its pull request by hand by any method — a fast-forward, a merge commit, a squash under the host's message or the person's own, a rebase — still gives it, never refused for having landed: in each repository it touches, each landing of a task of it, the oldest commit on the first-parent line of the branch it lands on, as the host has it, holding the task's change (or, holding no commit of it here, naming it), then `fix/{unit}` where it holds a commit not landed, as the landing cuts it again; its fix tasks are tasks of the quick-fix, phase 2 for round 1 (P14), each committed through the door on `fix/{unit}`, pushed to its one pull request by `land open`, done once committed, in it and passed by its pair, and landed with the close's squash — `land phase {unit} {N}` of a phase the tracker holds landing that pull request (**`land`**). Refused, writing nothing: a unit that is no quick-fix built as one (`{unit} is no quick-fix built as one — a review is asked for at a quick-fix's close; any other unit's review phase follows its build`), and a close that does not stand, as `render finish` refuses it — a fix neither done nor closed among them. Response: `{"ok": true, "unit": "<unit>", "review_asked": "<instant>", "next", "committed"?, "already"?}`.

```bash
engine review ask <work-unit>                          # a quick-fix's review asked for at its close, recorded and committed
engine review round <work-unit> --file <round.json>   # the round under review recorded, its report and checks written and committed
engine review notes <work-unit> --file <notes.json>   # the builders' notes standing answered once the round that ended the review has its fix tasks done
engine review reads <work-unit> <area|tests> …        # a later round's reads named and committed
engine review accept-tree <work-unit>                  # the working tree taken as it is, its snapshot taken again
engine review restore-tree <work-unit> --listed <print> # the working tree put back as the round's snapshot found it
```

**`finish`** — the authority gate after the review phase's report (14.5), and a quick-fix's close, its finish gate (19.5.8; `domain/finish.cjs`, the screen `domain/projections/finish.cjs`). **The screen**, `render finish <wu>`, stands on finish's checklist closed, every engine item holding still — the review phase over among them, where the unit has one: a quick-fix's only once the person asked for its review at its close (**`checklist`**). It draws the title, `{Title} — finish`; every inbox item the unit closed, where it closed any (18.6.7), `CLOSED IN THE INBOX ({n})` over a tree of each one's title and `[{kind}]`, then `↳ Each is archived as closed by {Title}. One closed wrongly comes back from the archived items in triage, or ask me to bring it back — its Resolved: line taken out of the build's log with it.` (`↳ It is archived … Closed wrongly, it comes back …` for one), read from **`inbox`**'s record; each item skipped on the way, with where and its reason — every phase start's and close's, in the cut's order, then finish's own (`Skipped at phase {N}'s start: {text} — {reason}`, `Skipped at finish: …`, 6.4.5) — read from the unit's manifest, beneath any warning its reading gave; what the review's put backs set aside, while the unit's set-aside holds anything, as the gate over the tree draws it, kept until the person removes it; and no measure (17.2.2). Each departure the person made in the build and the review phase — their answer at `{wu}.implementation` or `{wu}.review` standing over Claude's lean — that this machine logged is asked its verdict first, one at a time, its rows `verdict give <entry> … --at finish` (16.10.3), as ready to build asks its own; then the gate, `finish`: questions about what was built invited above it (14.5.1), `y/yes`, running the yes, and `Comment`. **A quick-fix's close** (19.5.13) is drawn the same, but for its title, `{Title} — ready to close`; beneath it each fix done with its pair's verdict, `REVIEWED BY THE PAIR` over `✓ {N.n} {title} · passed in round {r}`, or `settled in round {r}` where the orchestrator settled it, a line each, or `No fix was done.`; then what running the product proved of each fix done, `PROVEN BY RUNNING {PRODUCT}` over each scenario the fix names, a line each in the fixes' order, `✓ {criterion}` where its runs at the quick-fix's branch prove it and `△ Not proven: {criterion}` or `△ Named, not run: {criterion}` where they do not, or, with no verify setup recorded, the one line `Nothing was proven by running {Product} — the project has no way to run it` (P9); then, for each fix's work a question's park set aside and none put back — a fix closed keeps it — a line for each repository holding it, `⚑ Task {N}.{n}'s work, set aside when a question parked it, is kept in {the project's repository|{repo}} at {ref} — see it with {see}; bring it into the checkout with {recover}, settling any conflict` (`domain/fix-aside.cjs`, `keptAside`, R26); then the inbox items it closed, as finish draws them; and its gate, `finish` still, `◆ Close it?`: `y/yes` (`Close it — merge its pull request and mark it complete`, where a fix done is still to land; `Close it — its fix has landed, so mark it complete`, or `its fixes have`, where a merge of its pull request by hand landed every task done of its build already, a review's fix tasks among them; or `Close it — mark it complete` where no fix is done), running the yes; `r/review` (`Have the whole change reviewed before it closes`), drawn only while no review has been asked, running `review ask {unit}`, its `next` the review phase's entry (**`review`**); and `Ask` (`Ask about the change before it closes`). The verdicts asked first are those of its departures at `{wu}.implementation`, their rows `verdict give <entry> … --at close` (16.10.4). **It refuses**, opening `render finish: `: a unit the project lacks or not in progress; a unit built under the old plan and one whose build has not begun, as **`enter`** `implementation` refuses each; where the unit has a review phase — a quick-fix's once one is asked for — until it is over (`{wu} finishes once its review phase is over — {the way on}`), the close drawn once it is; a quick-fix with a fix not ended (`{wu} closes once each of its fixes has ended — {N}.{n} is {status}, …: each is done once it is committed and in {wu}'s pull request, or closed with its reason`) or a fix done with no record (`{wu} closes once each fix done has its record — {N}.{n}, … has none: quick-fix record {wu} {N}.{n} --file <record.md>`), as **`quick-fix`** `record` writes it; a quick-fix while a question asked at its places, its implementation or its review, stands open in its ledger with no park holding it — one Claude raised in its conversation, whether or not the conversation still holds its raise, as after a `/clear` (19.5.25) — naming each and the raise that asks it again (`{wu} closes once each question asked in it is answered — {id} {question}; …: raise it again for the person to answer, raise {wu}.{phase} --answers {id}, …`), as the build never concludes over a question waiting (12.13.22); a quick-fix whose review was not asked for while a note for later stands unanswered (`{wu} closes once each note for later is filed to the inbox or dropped — notes#{n}, …: quick-fix notes {wu} --file <notes.json>`); the checklist open (`finish is open — its items are worked, then it is closed: checklist close finish {wu}`); and an engine item failing since the close (`finish has items open since its close: {items} — they are worked, and it is closed again`). A quick-fix's yes refuses first, before anything moves, a project's checkout holding changes not committed outside the state, naming each (`{wu}'s tasks were built in the project's own checkout, which holds changes not committed — {paths} — so nothing was landed: commit them or set them aside, then close it again`); it lands the quick-fix's one pull request as **`land`** `finish` lands it — every task's commit squashed into one naming each by its `Task:` line, or, where a merge of it by hand landed every task, the branch it lands on here brought up to the host's — then puts the checkout back on that branch.

**`yes <wu>`** is the person's yes: it stands on the screen as drawn, refusing as the screen refuses and while a verdict is still to ask (`a verdict is still to ask — render finish asks each before the yes`). A quick-fix's refuses first, landing nothing, while the project's checkout, where its tasks were built, holds changes not committed outside the state (`{wu}'s tasks were built in the project's own checkout, which holds changes not committed — {paths} — so nothing was landed: commit them or set them aside, then close it again`). It lands every phase still standing as **`land`** `finish` does — the review phase's fix phases, numbered past the cut, after every other (7.2.6); a quick-fix's pull request in each repository it touches, children first, every task's commit squashed into one (19.5.21) — a refusal there its own, carrying the warnings the reading gave, the unit left in progress with whatever merged before it, so the yes run again picks up where it stopped; a quick-fix's then puts each checkout that has its branch out back on the branch its repository lands on, which **`task`** `start` and the door took it off; then completes the unit, **`workunit`**'s completion transaction (`finish({wu}): completed`). Refusals open `finish yes: `; arguments of another shape answer the usage. Response `{"ok": true, "unit", "landed": [repos…], "checkout"?, "completed_at", "committed", "next"}`, `landed` as `land finish` answers its `repos`, `checkout` a quick-fix's, the branch its checkout was put back on, with `warnings` where the reading, the landing or the commit gave any; `next` a handoff each (24.1.2): the epic's menu, its review completed (`leeway-continue-epic` with `[wu, review, completed]`), and the start menu for every other type (`leeway` with `[]`).

```bash
engine finish yes <work-unit>   # every phase still standing landed, the review's fixes last, and the unit completed
```

**`quick-fix`** — a quick-fix's own verbs (`domain/quick-fix-record.cjs`). **`record <wu> <N>.<n> --file <record.md>`** writes a fix's short record after the work (19.5.6, 19.5.17) into the unit's `summary.md`, a file only the engine writes (**`layout`**): the file holds three level-two headings, in this order, and no other heading at level one or two — `## What changed`, `## Why`, `## Leaves out` — none empty, each closing every fenced block it opens; written at its fix's place in the set's order — after the record of every fix numbered before it, as the fixes land bottom to top — under the fix's title as the tracker holds it, `## {title}`, each section an item (`- What changed: …`, `- Why: …`, `- Leaves out: …`), its lines after the first beneath it, indented to its text — all of them beneath it where the section opens on a fenced block, so the fence opens and closes as written. It takes a fix holding a commit of its own, as `task done` reads it — one landed by hand among them — in flight or done (**`task`**). A record is its fix's, by task: the fixes recorded are kept on the unit's manifest, `fixes.recorded`, their task ids in the set's order, written under the unit's lock with `summary.md`. It commits the two together (`quick-fix({wu}): record {N}.{n}`), pushed, then indexes `summary.md` where the checkout's knowledge base is set up, each fix's record a chunk of its own under its heading, at the unit's own identity (`{wu}/summary/.unit`), so a query for one fix finds its record (22.6.9) — an index that fails a warning; finish's `records-indexed` holds it too. **It refuses**, writing and committing nothing, opening `quick-fix record: `: a unit the project lacks or not in progress; one that is no quick-fix (`{wu} is a {type}, and only a quick-fix keeps a short record of each fix`), or a quick-fix built under the old plan, as **`enter`** `implementation` refuses it; a task none of its fixes (`task {N}.{n} of {wu} is none of its fixes — a quick-fix's fixes are its phase 1, 1.n`), or one the tracker lacks; a fix closed (`task 1.{n} of {wu} is closed — a fix closed lands nothing, so it keeps no record`); a fix recorded already (`task 1.{n} of {wu}'s record is written already — {wu}/summary.md holds it, under \`## {title}\``); a fix holding no commit of its own (`task 1.{n} of {wu} holds no commit of its own — a fix's record is written once its change is committed through the door`); and a file missing, empty, or not reading as a record (`{file} does not read as a fix's record — {faults}`), every fault named. Arguments of another shape answer the usage. Response `{"ok": true, "unit", "task", "id", "summary", "committed"}`, `summary` the file's absolute path, with `warnings` where the tracker's read, the commit or the index gave any. A fix closed has its record taken out, as **`task`** `close` says. **`notes <wu> --file <notes.json>`** answers a quick-fix's notes for later at its close, where no review was asked for (R24) — the notes its builders and pairs banked with **`task`**'s `note`, which a review's rounds answer once one is asked for: Claude's account, a payload file `{findings}`, each finding as **`review`** `round` takes one, of the area `notes`, every note standing answered by exactly one, out of scope, filed to the inbox (**`inbox`** `file`, its call at `{wu}.implementation`) or dropped, by Claude's file-or-drop call, a current decision of Claude's own at `{wu}.implementation`. They are kept at `{wu}/build/notes-answered.json`, which only the engine writes, a note banked later answered by a call of its own after them, and committed (`quick-fix({wu}): notes for later answered`); finish's `notes-answered` reads them (**`checklist`**). It refuses, writing nothing, opening `quick-fix notes: `: a unit that is no quick-fix built as one; one whose review was asked for (`{wu}'s review was asked for at its close, and answers its notes for later — review notes {wu} --file <notes.json>`); no note standing (`no note for later stands unanswered in {wu}`); and the payload's faults as `review notes` names them, every one at once. Response `{"ok": true, "answered": ["notes#{n}", …], "committed"}`.

```bash
engine quick-fix record <work-unit> <task> --file <record.md>   # a fix's short record, into summary.md, committed and indexed
engine quick-fix notes <work-unit> --file <notes.json>   # a quick-fix's notes for later answered at its close, where no review was asked for
```

**`measures`** — the measures read over the projects named (`domain/measures.cjs`), never drawn on a gate the person reads (17.2.2). **`findings [--project <path>]…`**, review findings per phase (17.1.5, 17.2.4), the query this repo's authors run over their own projects (17.2.1), over each project named, the calling one where none is: for each phase of the pipeline in its order, what its reviews found — `discussion`, discussion's reviews and closing pass, and `specification`, the spec's review, each every question a review asked on the ledger (`"by": "review"`) tagged with the phase — a replacement a review prompts is the person's or Claude's on the ledger and a rewrite never reaches it, so neither is counted; `task-writer`, every question Claude asked at `{wu}.implementation` on no task, a gap the task writer met reaching the ledger before any task holds it — the consolidation agent's and the orchestrator's own questions on no task among them, nothing on the ledger telling them apart; `build`, the build's pairs, every reviewer's report of a task's round (`{wu}/build/tasks/{task}/round-{N}-{letter}.md`) and of a phase review (`{wu}/build/phases/phase-{N}/{set}-{letter}.md`), a phase's review one round however many areas it is split into; `review`, the review phase, every round recorded (`{wu}/review/round-{N}.json`) — the last two with their findings by call, their rounds and those failed. A report or record that does not read is passed over and named. Response `{"ok": true, "projects": [paths…], "phases": [{"phase", "findings", "calls"?: {"serious", "bug", "nit"}, "rounds"?, "failed"?}], "unread"?: [lines…]}`, with `warnings` where a read of the ledger gave any. **`bugs [--project <path>]…`**, bugs found after review (17.1.6), the product's measure (17.2.1), over the same projects: every unit typed bugfix whose investigation's conclude recorded the unit whose code it fixes with that unit's review phase already ended (**`investigation`**, `fixes` and `after_review` on its item), grouped by the area the conclude named, each project's own — areas known alike folded for case and spacing, each by its first spelling, the earliest concluded — then by the unit fixed. A cancelled bugfix counts; one moved to another type does not; a reopened investigation's record stands until it concludes again. Left out: a bug in code no unit built, since no review ran on it; and one concluded before its unit's review ended, since no review had ended on it, whatever that review did later. It answers beside review findings per phase in one call, so the two read together: findings falling while bugs after review rise is the reviews weakening, not the code improving (17.3.1). Response as `findings`', with `"bugs": [{"project", "area", "count", "fixed": [{"unit", "bugfixes": [names…]}]}]` beside `phases` — areas, units and bugfixes each in name order, `null`, no area named, last. Each reads alone and takes no lock. Each refuses, opening `measures {findings|bugs}: `, a project with no workflows state; arguments of another shape answer the usage, `Usage: engine measures findings|bugs [--project <path>]…`.

```bash
engine measures findings [--project <path>]…   # review findings per phase over the projects named
engine measures bugs [--project <path>]…       # bugs found after review by area, beside review findings per phase
```

**`board`** — the workflows board's one read (§20; `domain/board.cjs`, the model `domain/board-model.cjs`, each view its own projection under `domain/projections/board-*.cjs`), which the gate mod calls for the conversation it draws after every engine call and every press. It answers the board's view model, versioned, for the mod to draw: kinds, states, counts, times and the records' words, never a colour, a mark or a button. **The tabs**: `this`, the default, follows the conversation's position (20.3.3) — the build, the review phase, a quick-fix's among them, a discussion, the specification, an epic's discovery, an experiment, a bug's investigation or a quick-fix's one session by its own view; the unit's page at its menu and in a phase whose view comes later; All work at the start menu or a project-level place, and at a unit's review once the unit is complete — any type but an epic — or a quick-fix's one session once it closes, its banner ✓ the unit's name, its type, complete, at the start menu, while the position names that place and at the start menu boot hands it to, until the conversation records a position again. **Where a unit stands** is one derivation (`domain/projections/board-standing.cjs`) the unit's tab, the epic's page and All work read, from the records each phase's banner reads: its phases — a quick-fix's its discovery and its implementation, and its review once asked for at its close — each complete, under way or later, and the day each complete one closed; the phase it is in — the furthest under way, an epic past its discovery by its stages — since the one before closed; how that phase stands — stopped where nothing of its build, the review's round and fix tasks among it, a quick-fix's fixes alike, can go on without an answer, as `render build-stop` draws the stop — the one reading of it This session, the banner, the tab and All work share — waiting on the person where something asked in the conversation waits, else under way; and what waits on the person, in order, each with its words and the page a press opens: approve the map while a map is staged, the sweep's candidates among it; the spec's gaps, each opening its finding, and then ready to build's yes while it is written and the build not begun; the questions its build and the review wait on, each opening the first task it parks, one parking none its question's entry — a replacement's, the decision it would replace — a quick-fix's those its fixes' builders parked them on among them; each question asked at a quick-fix's places still open in its ledger with no park holding it, whether or not this conversation holds its raise, so it outlives a `/clear`, and each replacement this conversation raised there still waiting, parking nothing, which holds nothing (19.5.25); and finish once a round ended the review, its fix tasks and builders' notes are done, and no question waits, a quick-fix's its close once its screen draws, saying whether a verdict is owed on a call the person changed — each drawn by its gate's name, `finish` or `close`; an investigation's conclude while its yes waits, as `render conclude` stands on it, its recap staged against the root cause and the fix as they stand; and each raise its investigation asks in this conversation, at its topic or at its own level, unanswered with its question open or the decision it would replace current, with what raised it — the validation one of whose findings was surfaced as it, asked as its question or raised replacing its decision, whatever that validation's row stands at (**`agent`** `surface`), else the fix direction where it is the fix direction's — and for the fix direction's how many options its screen draws and whether Claude's lean is the first, opening no page. `unit` shows the unit `--unit` names, else the session's — an epic's page once its map is approved (`phases.discovery.approved_at`, which approve the map's yes, the pivot and the reshape's move write), what waits on the person under its head, its specification held by the first wait of the list that holds it — the map the sweep staged, a gap or ready to build's yes — its build and review stopped or finish waiting; else the unit's page, its phases with what each one's records hold — an epic's discovery the topics on its map, the staged map's while approve the map waits, the review its round and, once recorded, where it stands as the banner says it, a bug's investigation its hypotheses, validating while either of its validations runs, a quick-fix's implementation its fixes done of all, or that none is written yet, building, and opening once concluded on its view — the phase it is in stopped or waiting on the person as it stands, what waits on the person, whether it is the unit the session is in, and what happened lately — refused with no unit to show. `all` shows All work: every unit in progress by its phase, how far it has come — an epic in discovery its topics on the map, its discussion its topics decided, the specification where it stands as its banner says it, a bug's investigation how far its hunt has come — finding the cause, the cause found or confirmed, the fix picked or confirmed — the build its tasks done, a quick-fix its fixes done or that none is written yet, and the review its round, each stopped where nothing can go on, finish waiting on the person, a quick-fix's close, an investigation's conclude — and what waits on the person in it, counted, as its tab lists it, their total, the roadmap by horizon, each item by its summary, a topic postponed out of an epic by its title there, with the epic's title, the inbox, the units complete. **A page**, `--page <ref>`, is read against the unit it was opened in, `--page-unit <work-unit>`, whatever tab shows it and wherever the session has gone since, the start menu or another unit; with none named, against the tab's unit — on the unit's tab the unit `--unit` names, else the session's, on This session the session's (20.3.5). `--unit` names the unit's tab's unit alone, never a page's. The pages: `task:{N.n}`, a task's page, its line, start and finish, outcome, account, rounds — with no account, each round reported whole by how it ended — calls and runs, each question parking it whole, so the page holds them wherever the session goes, and for a fix task the findings it fixes; `task:{N.n}:written`, the task as written; `task:{N.n}:round:{k}`, a round of its review's reports; `task:{N.n}:scenario:{id}`, a scenario of the task's, what the run that decides its standing proved and when — read at the tips of the task's branch, a quick-fix's its one branch, a task with none yet not proven — what that run drives and checks by the scenario's words, and the evidence kept with the run; `task:{N.n}:scenario:{id}:evidence:{k}`, one evidence kept with that run, whole; `entry:{id}`, a ledger entry; `finding:{round}:{k}`, a round's finding — by its number once the round is recorded; while it is read by its report item, `{report}#{n}`, as its reviewer's report holds it, then as the round's finding answering that item once recorded; `check:{round}:{scenario}`, a check a recorded round ran, or round 1's while it is read as it stands at the change's tips, with its scenario's words; `section:{n}`, a section of the spec, with the passes that changed it, its words its statements' and screens' blocks, each number and bracket of cites taken off; `pass:{k}`, a reported pass of the spec's review — what it read and changed, and each finding by its label as it stands with the person: waiting on them while the question it was asked as, through every question it grew into, is open, theirs once they answered it, Claude's once Claude did, and Claude's for any other finding (`agent surface --question`); `pass:{k}:{finding}`, a finding of a reported pass, its text in the report; `topic:{topic}`, a topic's discussion once it is under way or decided, or was cancelled after it started — its discussion's item past unstarted when the cancel set it aside — else — not started, postponed, or cancelled before it started — its brief by its sections, with the messages waiting for it, while approve the map waits the staged map's, read from its draft; `message:{topic}:{file}`, a message waiting in a topic's mailbox; `review:{topic}:{id}`, a round of a discussion's review, its report whole; `spike:{folder}`, a spike of the unit; `report:{topic}:{id}`, an experiment's report; `phase:{phase}`, a linear unit's finished discovery, on the note it was started from or else its brief, or its finished discussion, on its decisions, or its finished investigation, on its view, concluded, nothing waiting in it; `hunt:{item}`, a part of a bug's investigation read in full — a hypothesis or an experiment by its name, `root-cause`, or `stepped-back`, what the step back found — each with where it stands and the lines beneath its entry; `option:{key}`, an option of the fix direction as its raise's screen draws it in this conversation, the keep row among them, its words and what the person would notice, Claude's lean or the person's pick; and from All work, wherever it shows, `unit:{unit}`, a unit's page, which names its own unit and is read on any tab, refused with a `--page-unit` naming another; All work's tab opens any other page only with `--page-unit`. Under a page the answer carries `beneath`, the view the tab shows under it, which its way back returns to; the banner, the tabs and `unit` stand as they would with no page open. **Each tab is answered on its own**: a view or page that cannot be drawn — a page its unit does not hold among them — answers `{"kind": "unavailable", "reason"}`, and the banner and the questions stand beside it; This session that cannot be drawn leaves the banner at the session's place, no question queued, and says why among the warnings. **The banner** is the session's place's whichever tab is shown, a fact `needs` only where the banner flags it — in a phase, how many of the unit's list wait on the person, `the map waits on you` where the map alone does: the map, the spec's gaps, ready to build's yes, the questions in the build and the review phase, finish, an investigation's raises and its conclude, and a quick-fix's raises and its close — a fact `waits` where it says, unflagged, what waits beside them: at the unit's menu that same count (D40), and the messages waiting in a topic; every other plain, `stopped` among them (20.2.4). **The clocks** are a brief's issue in this conversation (P15): a task in progress building or in review since the last builder's, fix round's or reviewer's brief issued for it, its clock ended by `task account`; an area of the review phase being read since its area review's brief in that round (each `review-area` brief's record names its `area` and `round`, a `test-surface` brief's its `round`); a pass of the spec's review, and a round of a discussion's review, out since it was dispatched, each by where the agent store stands it — running, its report in and not yet taken, or lost with the conversation that sent it; and an investigation's validation, running from its dispatch since what it validates was made until it is taken in, its findings still being acted on among it — and, taken in, while a finding of it surfaced as a question or a replacement still waits on the person: the question open, or the decision it would replace current and kept by no answer since. **The records it reads besides**: a task in progress whose builder stopped is failed, by the stop **`task stopped`** holds against it in flight in this conversation, until a builder's or fix round's brief or a park takes it off (20.4.9); an experiment's runs by condition, each its runs done of its runs and their median, from the tally **`experiment runs`** recorded, and the day it was approved, from the record's `approved`, the banner counting them while it runs (20.13.1, 20.2.4); in an epic's discovery, the directions not taken, the person's views of the product and each spike's tool, live link and what it showed, from the lists **`discovery-log`** wrote under the fixed headings of its discovery logs, read across every log in order — every line under a fixed heading that is an entry, at the margin outside a fenced block, one entry wherever it stands, an indented line and prose passed over, a later spike's line standing over an earlier — an entry that does not read as its heading's shape, or a spike's naming none of the epic's spikes, passed over and said among the warnings; and the day its session started, the first its log names (20.11.1, 20.11.3); and the day a unit started, its manifest's `created`, and the day each of its phases closed — discovery the day the unit was made, an epic's its map last approved (`phases.discovery.approved_at`), the discussion its last topic's decided close, the investigation its item's `concluded_at`, the specification ready to build's close, the build its last phase's close, a quick-fix's implementation its review asked for (`fixes.review_asked`), else the unit's `completed_at`, the review the unit's `completed_at` (20.15.1); a discussion's closing pass running, where its agent's row stands in flight and running; in a bugfix's investigation alone, never another unit's, the ledger folded once a call for every unit it draws, the hunt from the lists **`investigation-log`** wrote under the fixed headings of its log — the symptoms, each hypothesis where it stands, each experiment, the root cause and the fix direction — each decision read where its chain stands now: the root cause the current entry the logged one leads to, the fix the current answer of the fix direction's question; its validations' rows, as **`investigation`**'s conclude reads them, with what each finding was surfaced as, the rows' `asked` and `raised`, and the effort each validation's agent runs at; the person's decisions at its place and Claude's calls since, but the root cause and the fix; and the raises this conversation sealed at its place (**`raise`**), the fix direction's those down its question's chain or carrying a step back, the latest the person did not answer by keeping standing; in a quick-fix's one session, each fix as the build reads a task, with its pair's rounds reported whole from its reports — those sent back, the one this conversation briefed and not yet reported whole, the round its review ended in and whether it was settled — every current call of Claude's made in it, for each decision made there the decision of another unit its replacements go back to, past those made in the quick-fix, with that decision's words, whose and its unit's title, the questions open at its places and the replacements this conversation raised there, its close as finish stands on it, and once every fix has ended, one done, each fix's record from `summary.md`. Claude's calls since the person last looked are read as `ledger since` reads them, never moving the mark (5.8.14), and the call never marks the conversation that ran it. What happened lately, and everything a topic sent since the day before its unit was made, are read from the state branch's history, one bounded `git log` each where a view draws them — each message sent with its sender, read no further back than the oldest message lately shows was sent, and each run of messages one topic took in one moment with their count; the spec landed with its sections and phases cut; and each reported pass of the spec's review, when it was dispatched, with what it found as its findings stand; a bugfix's lately takes in its hunt's moments besides, each when its record dates it — each experiment and the root cause written when the ledger entry each rests on was made, the fix direction picked when the fix's was, and the investigation concluded at its `concluded_at`; a hypothesis confirmed or ruled out, which nothing dates, is none — and what a topic's sent mail was taken in by, by its own `git log` bounded by the unit's life. It takes no writer lock and writes nothing, reading the tracker from a copy of its store, with one exception every read of the ledger shares: a landing rewrites the ledger before it moves the state branch, so a read that finds the ledger departing from the branch takes the writer lock — waiting while a catch-up landing runs, and finishing a landing a kill cut short, which writes. Each unit is read once a call, its tracker in one read once its build or a quick-fix's fixes have begun, of a copy of its store's files, each read whole once — the phases it has written, the review's fix phases past the cut among them, the tasks ready, and the task a page names — All work among them: it reads each unit's tracker whose build runs by its cut, one copy of its store per unit per call. The ledger is checked once a call, at its first read. While round 1 is read, its checks not proven, and a check's page, are read at the change's tips as `review round` judges them, the tasks of the change's phases all ended read from another copy of the store only there — a quick-fix's change read by its landing's one read by content off git alone, the branch each repository lands on as last fetched from its host, nothing fetched and no host asked — and a quick-fix's checks worded, and its tests' reader titled, by its fixes' acceptance. Response `{"ok": true, "version", "place": {"unit", "type", "phase", "topic"}, "banner": {"name", "state", "facts": [{"text", "needs", "waits"?}]}, "unit": {"unit", "title"}|null, "tab", "view", "beneath": view|null, "questions": [{"question", "asks", "parks", "after", "since"}], "warnings": [lines…]}`, `view` one of the kinds `board-model.cjs` types and `questions` the build's and the review phase's waiting, oldest first, those parking no task among them. It refuses: no session id or more than one, a tab none of the three, the unit's tab or a page at no unit, a unit the project lacks — `--unit`'s or `--page-unit`'s — a page its ref does not name, a page's task that is no task's number, `--page-unit` with no page, and a unit's own page with `--page-unit` naming another.

```bash
engine board <session-id>                                  # This session, as the conversation's position puts it
engine board <session-id> --tab unit [--unit <work-unit>]  # the unit's page, the session's unit where none is named
engine board <session-id> --tab all [--page unit:<unit>]   # All work, or a unit's page from it
engine board <session-id> --page <ref> [--page-unit <u>]   # a page of the unit it was opened in, else the tab's
```

**`inbox`** — archive / restore / delete one or more inbox items as a single transaction. Paths are validated strictly against the inbox layout (`.leeway/.inbox/{ideas|bugs|quickfixes}/…`, archived items under `.inbox/.archived/…`) before anything moves; one commit covers the whole set (`workflow(inbox): archive {slug}` for one item, `… archive {N} items` for several — same forms for restore/delete). Response: `{"ok": true, "archived": [paths…], "committed": "<short-sha>"}` (key matches the verb: `archived` / `restored` / `deleted`). `inbox close <work-unit|none> <path> …` archives live items as `archive` does and records who closed each on the project manifest, `inbox_closed: {"{folder}/{file}": {"unit", "at"}}` — the unit whose work resolved it, finish's (18.6.4), or `null` for `none`, an item the person had fixed straight away (18.6.6) — the moves and the record one commit (`workflow(inbox): close {slug}[, resolved by {wu}]`, `… close {N} items…` for several), every file put back where the record cannot be written; a unit the project lacks is refused, writing nothing (`there is no work unit "{wu}" — name the unit whose work resolved the items, or none`). Response: `{"ok": true, "closed": [archived paths…], "unit": "<wu>"|null, "committed": "<short-sha>"}`. `restore` and `delete` drop each item's record, in the same commit; finish's screen lists the items the record says the unit closed (**`render`** `finish`, CLOSED IN THE INBOX, 18.6.7). `inbox capture <idea|bug|quick-fix> [--project <folder>]` is the capture skills' one line (18.6.1–18.6.3, 18.6.8): the item's text on stdin, its title its first `# Title`, written at `.inbox/{ideas|bugs|quickfixes}/{date}--{slug}.md`, the slug its title's, a name the inbox already holds today, the archive included, taking the next free `-{n}`; committed and pushed (`workflow(inbox): capture {slug}`), a failed push a warning, and a failed commit noted with its retry, `engine commit --inbox`, to be run from the named project's folder where it is another's (`…, run from {folder}`). The state is found from any checkout or worktree of the project; `--project` captures into the project whose folder it names, read from the caller's, and is refused where that folder holds no Leeway state — so are a kind it does not know and a text with no title, writing nothing. It takes the writer lock of the state it captures into itself, and marks no conversation as running the workflows. Response: `{"ok": true, "captured": "<path>", "committed": "<short-sha>"}`, then a confirmation DISPLAY of one line, `Logged {kind}: "{title}"` — `Logged {kind} in {Project}: "{title}"` where the project is not the caller's. `inbox file` files a review finding out of scope (14.3.5) — or a quick-fix's note for later its close answers, where no review was asked for (**`quick-fix`** `notes`) — a payload file `{kind, text, call}`: its kind, `idea`, `bug` or `quick-fix`, its text holding its title, its first `# Title`, and its call, the id of Claude's file call, a current decision of Claude's at `{unit}.review` — at `{unit}.implementation` for the close's; it writes `.inbox/{ideas|bugs|quickfixes}/{date}--{slug}.md`, a name only the archive holds today taking the next free `-{n}`, and commits it (`workflow(inbox): file {slug} from {unit}'s review|close`). Refused for a unit whose review phase `enter review` refuses, but a quick-fix with no review asked for, a payload of another shape, every fault named, and an item filed already at that path (`{path} is filed already — group this finding with it, or title it apart`). Response: `{"ok": true, "filed": "<path>", "committed": "<short-sha>"}`.

```bash
engine inbox archive <path> [<path> …]   # live → .archived/{folder}/
engine inbox restore <path> [<path> …]   # .archived/{folder}/ → live
engine inbox delete <path> [<path> …]    # git rm archived items
engine inbox file <work-unit> --file <item.json>   # a review finding out of scope, or a quick-fix's note at its close, filed
engine inbox close <work-unit|none> <path> [<path> …]   # live → .archived/{folder}/, the closer recorded
engine inbox capture <idea|bug|quick-fix> [--project <folder>]   # the text on stdin; written, committed, pushed
```

**`brownfield`** — the brownfield offer's one verb (`domain/brownfield.cjs`): `record <native|skipped|accepted|completed>` writes the offer's answer to the project manifest (`brownfield_offer`) and commits it confined to that file in one transaction — a peer session's dirty files never ride. `native` (the project grew up on the workflows), `skipped` (the offer declined) and `accepted` (its research owed, which the product's conversation opens on — **`enter`**'s `research owed: yes`) answer the offer once, while nothing is recorded; `completed` follows `accepted` alone, once the product researcher's findings have landed. Refuses any other value and any answer out of that order. Response `{"ok": true, "answer": "native"|"skipped"|"accepted"|"completed", "committed": "<sha>"|null}` (`note: "nothing to commit"` when the manifest was already committed as written).

**`walkthrough`** — the walkthrough's one verb: `record <walked|skipped>` writes the answer to leeway's one-time offer to the project manifest (`walkthrough.status`) and commits it confined to that file (`chore(help): walkthrough walked` / `chore(help): walkthrough skipped`) in one transaction — a peer session's dirty files never ride. Refuses any other value, and refuses once an answer is recorded: the offer is answered once and the answer is the record. Response `{"ok": true, "status": "walked"|"skipped", "committed": "<sha>"|null}` (`note: "nothing to commit"` when the manifest was already committed as written). Nothing else is stored — no seen-set, no screen position — and the recorded status drives the offer alone: the `h/help` row on both start menus is unconditional, and no render surface reads it.

**`roadmap`** — the product-roadmap layer: the project manifest's `roadmap` node — an ordered `horizons` list (user-named release labels; position carries the semantics) and an `items` record — ideas at capability grain, a bug or a quick-fix one fix each — every one `{horizon, summary, kind, origin[, sources][, pulled_to][, postponed_from]}`, never a status field, or, archived, `{summary, kind, origin[, sources], archived: {at, horizon}}`. Item lifecycle is computed at read time by joining `pulled_to` against the named work unit (`waiting` = no join and no archive mark; `in-flight` = unit in-progress; `shipped` = completed; `orphaned` = the join names a missing or cancelled unit — the honest fallback for a revert that never ran). Every mutation is one transaction under the project-manifest lock **plus its own pathspec commit of `.leeway/manifest.json`** (engine-owned message, e.g. `roadmap: add {name} ({horizon})`) — no work-unit commit cadence covers the project manifest, and a park fired mid-session must be durable immediately; a git failure degrades to the standard commit-pending note. The node and any named horizon are created just-in-time by `add`/`add-batch`/`move` (`horizon_created: true` rides the response) — the roadmap has no genesis ceremony. `origin` is `harvest` | `park:{work_unit}` (the mid-flow valve — the suffix names the parking session's containing work unit, or the literal `park:discovery` from pre-commit shaping, where no unit exists yet) | `inbox:{file stem}` (moved off the inbox with its note) | `postpone:{work_unit}` (a topic that left an epic — see `topic postpone`) | `topic:{work_unit}/{topic}` (a candidate parked from a one-topic unit at the yes that takes its close, ready to build's for a feature — see `decided`, its one source the topic's log); `kind` is `idea` | `bug` | `quick-fix`, required on every item — `add --kind` (default `idea`), an `add-batch` entry's `kind` (default `idea`), `idea` at the postpone's birth while a re-wait keeps the item's own, carried by `rename` — and an unknown kind is refused; `--source` entries are provenance pointers into session logs (relative paths under `.leeway/`, validated non-absolute, non-traversing, and never carrying a leading `.leeway/`), and every one must already exist on disk — `add` and `add-batch` refuse the whole call naming each missing path, so a session log's open comes before the op that records it. Item and horizon names follow the dot/slash-free rule, and `roadmap` and `product` are reserved work-unit names (the project's identities).

**Notes.** An item moved off the inbox keeps its note in the product's notes, `.leeway/.product/notes/{file}` (the inbox's file name kept, 4.11.16), never KB-indexed while it waits, and there for good: neither an archive nor a pull moves it. An `add-batch` entry's **`note`** names a live inbox path in the form `inbox archive` takes; with it the entry's kind is the note's folder's (`ideas` → `idea`, `bugs` → `bug`, `quickfixes` → `quick-fix` — a contradicting `kind` refused), its origin `inbox:{file stem}` (a note whose file name has a space is refused, naming the file), and its one source the note's new home, `.product/notes/{file}` — an `origin` or `sources` beside a note is refused. Every entry is validated first — legal names, no name twice or taken on the roadmap, an archived item's among them, each note a live inbox file named once with its home free and no other note of the batch bound for it (two kinds' notes of one name refused, naming both) — so a refusal moves and persists nothing (`render inbox-roadmap-gate` runs the same validation over the same file); then every item lands and every note moves under one commit naming the inbox paths, the notes and the project manifest (`notes_moved: [{from, to}]` on the response, absolute). A note is never in neither place: it moves after the manifest names it, and a move that throws puts back what moved and leaves the manifest as it was. A pulled item's note stays where it is: the pull reads it as a source.

**`archive <name>`** (18.8.13) takes a waiting item off its horizon and keeps it on the project manifest, marked `archived: {at, horizon}` — when, and the horizon it left — in place of its `horizon`, its sources and its note kept, and commits (`roadmap: archive {name}`). Response `{"ok": true, "op": "archive", "name", "horizon", "state": "archived", "committed"}`. Nothing else reads an archived item as waiting: `state` lists it apart, the board's All work and `horizon remove`'s count leave it out, and start this work's `roadmap`, `pull-forward`, a topic's take-in and approve the map's bind refuse it, naming the way back. Its name stays taken (18.8.21): `add`, `add-batch`, `rename`, approve the map's parks and the decided screen's candidates refuse it (`"{name}" is archived on the roadmap, and its name stays taken — bring it back onto a horizon (engine roadmap move {name} --horizon <h>), or name the new one apart`), and so does a postpone whose topic goes by it. `move <name> --horizon <h>` brings it back, the mark gone, its sources and note as they were (`from_archive: {at, horizon}` on the response; commit `roadmap: move {name} to {h}, back from the archive`). It refuses an item archived already (`"{name}" is archived already`) and a pulled one, as `move` does.

Start this work joins the items a unit starts from (`pulled_to: {work_unit}`, **`start-this-work`**); the unit's seed set is derivable from these joins, nothing mirrored onto the work unit (one home per fact); approve the map binds an item to the topic it crystallised as (`pulled_to.topic`, **`approve-map`**). **`pull-forward`** is the mid-epic expansion move, one composed transaction: it creates the map topic on the epic (topic = item name, source `roadmap`, summary carried; the map's own gates apply — `--force-dismissed` passes a user-confirmed re-add through), writes the join with the topic, and lands one commit staging both manifests; refused for a non-epic, an epic past its spec, whose map is closed — the return branch below too, before anything is written (`{epic} has its spec, so its map is closed — the item stays on the roadmap: pull it into an epic still before its spec, or start it as work of its own`) — a joined item, an occupied topic name, or an item whose kind is not `idea` — an epic grows by topics, so a bug or a quick-fix starts from the roadmap menu's pull, which hands it into discovery as work of its own, and the refusal says so. A postponed item — one carrying `postponed_from` — moves three ways the others do not. **`archive`** cancels the epic's row in the same transaction ("actually never" has one door), at any stage of the epic, the spec started or not (18.8.17): the map marker flips from `postponed` to `cancelled` and every stashed item with it, each stash and the stashed map order kept for a later reactivate, the item is archived with its `postponed_from` dropped, the hold it named gone, and one commit stages both manifests (`roadmap: archive {name} — {topic} cancelled in {wu}`; `epic_row_cancelled: {work_unit, topic}` on the response). An item whose epic no longer exists refuses rather than orphaning the row, and so does one whose named topic holds no postpone (`the join is stale`) — the archive never reports a cancel it did not make. **`pull-forward` back into the epic that postponed it** takes the **return** branch instead of creating: `restored` names every item the marker returns, the map order comes back unless a live row took the number, the join is re-recorded and `postponed_from` dropped (the item is in flight again; a later postpone re-sets it). The epic menu's own `f/forward` row is that branch's door, over a sub-view of every topic the epic postponed whose item still waits. **A pull that lands anywhere else** carries the prior record's address onto the receiving epic's map row as `prior: {work_unit, topic}` — `pull-forward` at creation, staging the epic's manifest alongside the project's, and approve the map's bind (**`approve-map`**); the topic's discussion reads that record at its first opening (**`enter`**), and the prior epic's files stay where they are, never moved or drained. `move` re-buckets a postponed item freely — it is waiting. **The cancel-revert hop is the un-pull**: `topic cancel` whose cancellation leaves the topic's map lifecycle cancelled — and `workunit cancel`, whole-unit, and `topic remove` of a topic not started — delete the affected joins (`sources`/`origin` intact, items back to waiting on the horizon they came from, as renamed or merged since), report `roadmap_reverted: [names]` (`roadmap_returned` for `topic remove`), and stage the project manifest in their own commits; reactivation never silently re-joins (a re-pull re-binds deliberately). An item whose horizon was removed while it was in flight comes back on none, `roadmap_horizon_gone: [{name, horizon}]` naming it with the horizon it came from, and Claude asks the person which horizon it goes on (18.8.2, 18.8.20), then `move`s it there.

The product's conversation's sessions are `discovery-session … product`'s, and its shared files `asset import product`'s. `state` also reports the conversation's `session_logs` (number + path, ascending, from `.leeway/.product/sessions/`), read whether or not the node exists, a log alone giving a roadmap that `exists` with nothing in it.

Authority splits at the pull: un-pulled items take every edit (`edit`, `rename`, `move`, `archive`); a **pulled** item refuses `move` and `archive` — re-bucketing or archiving in-flight delivery is the work unit's decision, and the refusal names the join and the item's way back to waiting: for a topic join, cancelling or postponing that topic; for any other join (a bug or a quick-fix riding with an epic, an idea not yet bound, a join into a feature, bugfix or quick-fix), only the work unit's cancel; a shipped item stays as its release's record, and an orphaned join is named as such — while `edit` (cosmetic; the row is a window) and `rename` (every field carried, the join included) stay open. Horizon ops run at any join state — relabeling a release never un-commits work: `rename` cascades to every member's `horizon` field, `reorder` demands a complete permutation of the current list, `merge` moves every member and drops the source label, `split` creates the new horizon (default: right after the source; `--position` overrides, 1-based) and moves the named members (each must belong to the source), `remove` refuses a horizon with waiting members, naming them — an item from it in flight has left the roadmap (18.8.20), so it holds nothing, and comes back on no horizon. `add-batch` validates every entry before applying any (a failing entry means nothing persisted) and lands under one commit; `added` names each item with its horizon and kind. `state` is the derived read every consumer shares: `{"ok": true, "exists": bool, "horizons": […], "items": [{name, horizon, summary, kind, origin, sources, state[, work_unit][, topic][, work_type][, earlier]}…], "archived": [{name, summary, kind, origin, sources, archived: {at, horizon}}…], "totals": {items, waiting, in_flight, shipped, orphaned}}` — items horizon-ordered, an item on no horizon of the list last, archived ones apart and outside the totals, absent/malformed state reading `exists: false`; an item carrying `postponed_from` names the record its topic left there as `earlier`, `{brief?, record, log?, mail}` — the brief and log by absolute path where they exist, the `ledger record` call, each message in the mailbox — read as input by a conversation it seeds, never changed. Mutation responses are decision-ready: `add`/`move` echo the placement plus `horizons` and `item_total` (`add` its `kind` too, `move` its `moved_from`, null for an item that stood on none); `rename` carries `renamed_from`/`preserved_fields`; horizon ops echo the list after (`horizons`) plus op-specifics (`items_updated`, `items_moved`).

```bash
engine roadmap state
engine roadmap add <name> --horizon <h> --summary <text> [--kind <idea|bug|quick-fix>] [--origin <tag>] [--source <path> …]
engine roadmap add-batch --file <items.json>       # entries {name, horizon, summary, kind?, origin?, sources?, note?} — atomic, one commit; a note moves off the inbox to .product/notes/
engine roadmap edit <name> --summary <text>        # any join state (cosmetic on a pulled item)
engine roadmap rename <old> <new>                  # any join state; every field carried, join included
engine roadmap move <name> --horizon <h>           # a waiting item re-bucketed, or an archived one brought back; JIT-creates the horizon
engine roadmap archive <name>                      # waiting items only; kept on the manifest off its horizon, its note where it is, its name taken; a postponed item cancels the epic's row with it
engine roadmap pull-forward <name> --into <epic> [--force-dismissed]   # ideas only; map topic + join, one commit; back into the epic that postponed the item it restores that row instead
engine roadmap horizon add <name> [--position <n>]
engine roadmap horizon rename <old> <new>          # cascades to member items, joins included
engine roadmap horizon reorder <name> [<name> …]   # the complete order — a permutation of the current list
engine roadmap horizon merge <from> --into <to>    # moves every member, drops the source label
engine roadmap horizon split <name> --new <name> --items <a,b,…> [--position <n>]
engine roadmap horizon remove <name>               # a horizon no waiting item is on
```

**`cache`** — analysis-cache stamping: record that the gap analysis ran over the current completed inputs. Collects and checksums the input files exactly as the read side (`computeAnalysisCacheStatus` in domain/derivations) does — a fresh stamp is `valid` by construction. Writes `checksum`, `generated` (current ISO timestamp), and the input file names to `phases.discovery.gap_analysis_cache` (`input_files`) — then indexes the `.state/discovery-gap-analysis.md` cache file into the knowledge base (warn-don't-block). Errors when no qualifying inputs exist — the analysis's preconditions skip the stamp in that case. Response: `{"ok": true, "kind": "…", "checksum": "…", "files": N, "warnings": []}`. No git commit — the calling flow's commit cadence picks the manifest change up.

```bash
engine cache stamp <work-unit> gap-analysis
```

**`commit`** — the scoped commit helper. **Every engine commit is confined to a pathspec** — this helper and every transaction tail alike: each commits exactly its paths, from HEAD plus the working tree's content of them — in the code tree `git add -- <paths>` then `git commit -m … -- <paths>` (the code side of `--migrations` alone commits its paths as the index records them — below). Every path is taken literally: one holding `[`, `*` or `?`, or opening on `:`, names that file and never a pattern (`app/[id]/page.tsx` commits that file, never `app/i/page.tsx` beside it). Nothing outside the declared scope can ride but a state commit's logs (below), so a concurrent session's dirty or staged files (and the user's own staged code) are ignored and left exactly as they were. Pathspecs matching neither disk nor index are dropped before the add — a scope naming a path its action never created is normal. A clean scope is fine: `{"ok": true, "committed": null, "note": "nothing to commit"}`, exit 0 — `"committed": []` for `--paths`, whose commits are listed per repo (below).

**Two doors, one per side** (`domain/commit.cjs`). **The state commit** takes paths under `.leeway/`, named from the state root, and is made in the state worktree on `leeway-state` (`domain/state-worktree.cjs`) under the writer lock: built in a private index from HEAD and the working tree, never through the worktree's own index, so nothing a killed verb or a person left staged there can break it; a merge's landing a kill cut short is finished first, so the commit moves the branch on from where that landing put it. A commit whose ledger does not start, byte for byte, with its parent's is refused, but the one a migration's conversion lets through. Then the branch is pushed wherever the project has a remote, never forced: a push that fails, runs out of time, or finds no remote chosen among several is a warning in `warnings`, and the commit stands. Every form but `--paths` makes one, and so does every verb's own commit. **The code commit** — `--paths`, and the code side of `--plan` and `--migrations` — is made in the code root's own tree on the branch checked out, never on the state branch: no code commit lands in a tree that has `leeway-state` checked out.

The scope by form, named from the state root: `{wu}` bare, `.inbox` with `--inbox`, `.product/sessions` plus the project manifest with `--product` (the product's conversation's cadence commit — its marker and the roadmap node live on the project manifest), the whole state with `--workflows` (a flow writing across work units — a quick-fix promotion), the whole state and the files the workflows own in the code tree with `--migrations`. No form stages the knowledge directory or the cache: the state's own `.gitignore` ignores them whole, beside the writer lock.

Every state commit — this helper's state forms and every verb's own, a transaction tail's among them — is held to the layout (`domain/layout.cjs`) before its tree is built, reading the state worktree's changes once (`git status`: untracked files among them, ignored ones not). A file on disk the layout has no place for — one a shell wrote past the layout hook — refuses the commit, naming each with the way on, and nothing is committed: `the state at {root} holds {unit}/tmp/x.md, outside the workflows' layout — nothing committed; remove it, or move it into the layout`. An agent's staging beside a file place, a `.txt` waiting on its rename, is neither refused nor committed — the layout hook's own rule — so an agent mid-write holds up no commit. A transaction tail answers it as it answers any commit git refuses: the verb `ok`, the refusal in `warnings`, and the `commit pending` note. `--workflows` and `--migrations` refuse it too, `--migrations` committing nothing on the code side either. A file gone refuses nothing, whether deleted from the working tree or with its deletion staged. Every changed log of the layout's — a topic's `log.md`, discovery's `session-NNN.md`, the product's `sessions/session-NNN.md` and the build's `log.md` — rides along with the scope under the same message, and is pushed with it, and with each log every file below a spike or an `assets/` folder that the log links (`log-links.cjs`: a markdown link read from the log's folder, or else from the state root, its `#` or `?` part dropped, a link naming a scheme or leaving the state passed over) — a file no log or entry links stays untracked.
