The files the person's tools leave beside the state's are neither refused nor committed, wherever they are — matched by base name, `*` any run of characters: Finder's, Explorer's and AppleDouble's (`.DS_Store`, `Thumbs.db`, `desktop.ini`, `._*`), Vim's (`.*.swp`, `.*.swo`, `.*.swx`), Emacs's (`*~`, `#*#`, `.#*`) and JetBrains' (`*___jb_tmp___`, `*___jb_old___`). A merge's landing at the catch-up is no verb's commit, and is never held to the layout.

`--migrations` is the reviewed migration commit — leeway's, on the user's yes at the migration gate — one commit on each side that changed, under the one message. The state side is the whole state, a state commit like any other. The code side is the files the workflows own in the code tree, a list held once in the commit door (`OWNED_PATHS`, `domain/commit.cjs`): `.claude/settings.json`, `.worktreeinclude` and the code root's `.gitignore`. It stages every change under them — edits, new files, deletions — and commits them as the index then records them, from a scratch index holding HEAD with those paths' entries taken from the real one: a removal a migration staged (`git rm --cached`, the file left on disk) lands, where a partial commit would read the file straight back from the working tree, and whatever else is staged stays staged and out of the commit. An owned path git refuses to stage — one the project ignores, one beyond a symbolic link — is left as it is, never forced in, and one that neither exists nor is tracked is dropped. The code side refuses while a merge, cherry-pick, revert, rebase or bisect is in progress, staging nothing. The response carries the state commit as `committed` and the code commit as `code_committed` (null where that side had nothing), `nothing to commit` noted only where neither committed.

`--topic <phase>/<topic>` is the session-cadence form: the topic's artifact (`topics/{topic}/log.md` for discussion and investigation, `topics/{topic}/experiments/` for experiment) plus the topic's mailbox, `topics/{topic}/mail/` (the mail phases — discussion, investigation) plus the work-unit manifest. `--sweep` marks a commit made on a topic the session is not working — the conclude sweep's leavings, the retry a foreign-topic delivery's pending note prescribes, a resolution landed in another phase's document. Its presence semantics are in the beat table under `presence`, where `--sweep` outranks everything and the item's own status decides the rest. Mutually exclusive with `--discovery` and `--state`.

`--discovery` is the discovery session's cadence form: the unit's `discovery/` (its logs), each topic's `topics/{topic}/brief.md`, and the work-unit manifest (the map lives there). Discovery runs beside live discussion sessions on the same work unit, so it slices its own paths and never theirs — a topic's mail and a peer session's documents stay out.

`--state` is the analysis form, and names two scopes by whether a work unit rides with it. `{wu} --state` is the work unit's analysis dir plus its manifest — the scope the epic's gap analysis writes, and the retry a start's pending note names. It runs beside live topic sessions whose half-written documents sit in the same work unit. Bare `--state` is the state's `.state` alone — the global bookkeeping dir (migrations, environment setup), written from inside whatever session happened to need it. Neither beats: an analysis is not a session sitting in a topic.

`--paths <file> …` is the code commit — the one scope no layout derives (code has none), so the session declares it and the engine checks it: every path must resolve inside the project and never in the state, `.leeway/`, whose artifacts have derived scopes of their own. Paths are the project's wherever the code lives. A project can keep code in repos of its own inside its folder — a nested clone — untracked, or its gitlink staged with no `.gitmodules` entry, as `git add` stages one — a submodule, a gitlink `.gitmodules` maps, read from what defines one and never from the index alone, or an ignored folder holding `.git` itself, each searched for its own in turn, and never a linked worktree of the repository holding it, the state worktree among them — found on every call, never configured (`kernel/repos.cjs`); a path belongs to the deepest of them whose folder holds it, else to the root. In that repo the path must be on disk or in the index and stageable there (an ignore rule or a symbolic link refuses it), have its deletion staged, or have been deleted by the repo's HEAD commit (one an earlier run of the call landed, with nothing left to commit). Every repo the call would commit in, a reached submodule's parent among them, must have no merge, cherry-pick, revert, rebase or bisect in progress — the commit would take on its state — must be on a branch: a commit on a detached HEAD (a submodule after `git submodule update`) would be orphaned by the next checkout — and must not have the state branch checked out. Each refuses the call, naming the repo; a revert in progress names its end by `git revert --quit`, which keeps its change, then the commit again. Every path and repo is checked before any repo commits. Each repo then commits its own paths with the one message, deepest first so a nested repo commits before its parent, holding out the untracked repos and submodules inside it: a parent never commits a nested repo's folder, and commits a submodule's pointer only where the call reached into that submodule — its parent then takes the submodule's folder too, never holding it out of a folder the call also names, so the pointer lands whenever it differs from the parent's HEAD. The call names its target, `--task <work-unit> <N.M>`, the task in flight (below). `committed` lists the commits made in that order, `[{"repo": "api", "sha": "…"}, {"repo": ".", "sha": "…"}]` — `.` naming the root, a repo with nothing to commit left out — and is `[]` beside the `nothing to commit` note when no repo had anything. A repo whose commit fails fails the call, the error naming that repo and the commits already made; re-running the same call is the retry, since those repos have nothing left in the named paths and a moved pointer lands whatever moved it. The response adds `left_dirty` — every tracked modification and untracked file left in the code, in every repo, each untracked file on its own (never its folder, so a file the call left beside one it named is named itself), named from the project (a project inside a larger repository reads only its own folder of it); a parent's view of a nested repo (its untracked folder, a submodule's moved pointer) is never dirt, nor a link the worktree's preparation made — an untracked symbolic link at a folder the project's `worktree.symlinkDirectories` names (**`verify`**'s `mutants`), in any repository of the project, the root among them — so the session can close the loop on a path it forgot to name. Such a link is never staged, whatever a folder named holds, by either route; an index holding one staged already, a nested clone's gitlink, or a file in the folder of a repository nested in that repo, by whatever way git staged it, refuses the call in that repo before anything commits, naming each and how to unstage them (`commit --paths: the index in {repo} holds {path}, {what}; and …, which the door never commits — unstage them, \`git -C {repo} restore --staged -- {path} …\`, then run the commit again`); and one named outright refuses the call (`commit --paths: "{name}" in {repo} is a link the worktree's preparation made, as worktree.symlinkDirectories names, never the work's — leave it out`). The state is never code dirt: document sessions run concurrently with the code session by design, and their dirt is theirs.

`--task <work-unit> <N.M>` commits for a task, which must be in flight in the calling conversation (`domain/commit-task.cjs`) — the one `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID` names, as its position records it once `task start` puts the task there (read through `conversation.cjs`'s `tasksInFlight`, as the hook before Bash reads it). Before any path is read it refuses, each with the way on: no conversation; a unit the project lacks; a task id that does not read `{phase}.{n}`; a task the tracker holds ended, done or closed, which takes no more work and no start — naming a task of its own made (`commit --task: task {N.M} of {unit} is closed, and a task ended takes no more work — make it a task of its own with \`engine task create {unit} {N} --file <task.md>\``), and, for a quick-fix's fix, first the start of the next fix not ended, where one is, the same kind of work (`… — commit it under the fix it is for — start the next with \`engine task start {unit} 1.{n}\` — or make it a task of its own …`); no task in flight — start it with `engine task start <unit> <N.M>`, again after a `/clear`, which begins another conversation; and only other tasks in flight, each named; from a worktree `task worktree` laid out for another task in flight, any task but that one (`commit --task: the worktree at {path} is task {N.M} of {unit}'s — …`); a quick-fix's task in flight that does not hold its one checkout, so no commit carries another fix's work under its stamp (19.5.23) — one freed and waiting behind the fix that holds it (`commit --task: task {N}.{n} holds {unit}'s one checkout, and task {N}.{n} of {unit} waits behind it, so a commit now would carry task {N}.{n}'s work under task {N}.{n} of {unit}'s stamp — task {N}.{n} of {unit}'s change is committed once it holds the checkout again, started again with `engine task start {unit} {N}.{n}`, once task {N}.{n} is done or closed, or a question parks it`), and one a question parks (`commit --task: a question parks task {N}.{n} of {unit}, so it holds no part of {unit}'s one checkout — task {N}.{n} holds it, and a commit now would carry its work under task {N}.{n} of {unit}'s stamp|no fix holds it now; … once the answer frees it`); and a task with a worktree recorded, from anywhere but that worktree (`commit --task: task {N.M} of {unit} is built in its worktree at {path}, … — commit its work from there`); and any task from a checkout on a branch other than the task's, so no task's commit lands on another branch — naming the worktree git's worktree list has the task's branch checked out in (`commit --task: this checkout has {branch} out, not {task}'s branch, so its commit would land on another branch — commit it from {path}, where its branch is checked out`), or, where none has, `task worktree` (`… — no worktree has it out — \`engine task worktree {unit} {N.M}\` lays one out, from a worktree of Claude Code's isolation`) — a quick-fix's task, built in the project's own checkout, naming the quick-fix's one branch and its start (`commit --task: this checkout has {branch} out, not {unit}'s branch, so its commit would land on another branch — a quick-fix's task is built in the project's own checkout, on fix/{unit} — \`engine task start {unit} {N.M}\` puts the checkout on it`); a quick-fix's commit git would date before the day before its unit was made, as a clock behind dates it, so no landing would read it as the unit's work, refused before anything moves, naming the way on (`commit --task: git would date this commit {YYYY-MM-DD}, before {unit} was made on {created}, so no landing would read it as {unit}'s work, and nothing moved — set this machine's clock right, then commit again`); a detached HEAD and the state branch are the paths' check's to refuse, below. A quick-fix's task done whose work is neither landed nor on its branch — as a branch cut again leaves a commit that did not apply there — or gone, takes its change made again though it has ended and is in flight nowhere, from the project's own checkout on the branch alone (`domain/fix-end.cjs`). Each repository nested in the project that a quick-fix's task commits in is put on the quick-fix's branch there first, made where it lacks one from the tip of the branch it lands on as the host has it — one the quick-fix touched has it already, taken from the host's copy at the task's start (**`task`**) — its changes not committed carried there — refused where git cannot carry them (`commit --task: {repo} takes {unit}'s work on fix/{unit}, and git could not put it there with its changes ({git's words}) — commit or set aside what stands in the way there, then commit again`) — so its share of the fix lands with the quick-fix's pull request there (7.2.24); once the checks pass, and before it commits, each is recorded in the unit's state where it is not already, `fixes.repos` on the manifest — each nested repository by its folder, in the order first committed in — committed to the state branch, so a commit there never stands without it and every clone reads the repository as one the quick-fix touches (**`land`**). A task with none recorded commits from where its branch is checked out. A builder's commands carry the conversation's session id, so a commit from its worktree finds its task among those in flight. The paths and repos are then checked as above, and the project's checks run: its tests, `environment.run.tests` on the project manifest, then each of `environment.linters`' commands (`domain/environment.cjs`) — a part the setup does not hold is no check, and one of another shape refuses. Each runs by `sh -c` in the code root of the calling checkout — a code worktree the call is made from is its own — before any lock is taken, its output and errors captured, with no timeout of the engine's own. The first that fails refuses the commit, nothing committed, naming the check, its exit code (a signal's as the shell gives it, 128 and its number) and its output's last 40 lines. The checks run inside the Bash tool's call, which stops at two minutes unless given a longer timeout, so a commit whose checks run long is called with one; the refusal says so. A bugfix's task is held to its bug-proven check (`domain/bug-proven.cjs`, 6.3.6, 15.5), read for a bugfix alone, any other unit read no further than its manifest. The reproducing task's own commits — task 1.1's, phase 1's lowest-numbered — run the tests allowed to fail, since its test reproduces the bug before any fix lands, the linters held as ever. Any other task's commit is refused before any check runs until the build's series of that test on the unfixed code (**`verify`**'s `run … --unfixed`) proves the bug at task 1.1's tip, the commit being made built on task 1.1's work — in each repository holding its branch, HEAD, and while a merge is in progress there the commit it takes in (`MERGE_HEAD`), descends from that branch's tip or holds task 1.1's landing, read by its `Task:` line as the landing reads one (**`land`**), since every landing squashes: `commit --task: {unit} is a bugfix, so {task}'s code lands once its bug is proven by the test its reproducing task wrote — task 1.1, phase 1's lowest-numbered (15.5.3): {why}`, the why naming the way on — no series run, naming the call that runs one; task 1.1's branch moved past the commits its series ran at, a new series; a pass in the series, the test reworked as a new commit on task 1.1, then a new series; or the commit holding neither task 1.1's tip nor its landing, naming the repository behind — where task 1.1 has landed, a merge of the branch it landed on; for a task of another phase, phase 1's merge, then that merge; for one of phase 1, a merge of task 1.1's branch — each made as the hook before Bash lets a task in flight make one, `git merge --no-ff --no-commit {branch}` — in a nested repository run in its folder, `git -C {dir} merge …` — then committed through the door, which finishes it. Once it is proven, the series' command runs after the tests as a check of its own, `reproducing test`, and must pass, its refusal saying the test passes once the bug is fixed — for a task the fix is not in, once the fix's task is done, its branch merged in through the door — and that a command failing for another reason, such as a wrong path, is run in a new series with the right one. Then each repo commits as above, under the message given with the trailer `Leeway-Task: {id}`, the task's internal id (`domain/task-id.cjs`) — added to the trailers the message ends in, as `git interpret-trailers` adds one, where its last paragraph past the subject is all `Token: value` lines, else a paragraph of its own — never landing's `Task:` line, which the landing drivers read as the task landed. A repo with a merge in progress — the chain's tip merged back onto the task's branch (**`brief`**'s `chain`) — is the one exception to the rule against an operation in progress: the call finishes the merge there, its paths there the resolution, staged as any commit of the door's stages its paths — every nested repository's folder and every link the worktree's preparation made held out, a submodule's pointer named staged as its folder stands — then the merge committed whole, as a merge commit is, under the same message and trailer; it refuses, before the checks run, while a path there stays unmerged that its paths do not name (`commit --paths: the merge in progress in {repo} still conflicts in {path}, … — resolve each and name it, then run the commit again`). A child's merge committed first, the repository holding it records its pointer as the call reaches it. It beats no topic. The response is `{committed, left_dirty, task, checks}`, `note` beside them as above: `task` the id, `checks` each check run, `{name, exit}`, in order — the tests named `tests` — and `[]` where the project sets none; the reproducing task's tests failing, `allowed`, why they may, and `tail`, their output's last 40 lines, beside them; and `warnings`, where the state commit recording a quick-fix's nested repositories warned.

Every code commit runs under a process-wide commit lock in the git dir of the repo it commits in (`.git/leeway-commit.lock` — in the `.git` dir so it can never be staged; a holder stale only after five minutes, a contender waiting up to one), and every state commit under the writer lock; index-mutating git operations retry briefly through `index.lock` contention before surfacing git's error. Transaction-tail commits degrade rather than abort: the state write has landed, so a git failure leaves the verb `ok` with a `note` prescribing the retry and the git error appended to `warnings`, beside any push warning. A note is contract surface — a session runs the command it names verbatim — so a tail whose commit was narrower than the work unit spells its own scope (`retry with: engine commit {wu} --discovery -m "<message>"`); only a genuinely work-unit-wide tail keeps the bare `retry with engine commit`.

While the migration runs — `leeway-state` checked out with no commit yet — every state commit is held: a file outside the layout refuses it as ever, and otherwise nothing is committed or pushed, the writes staying on disk for the migration's commit to take, and a verb answers `"committed": null` with the note `held for the migration's commit`.

```bash
engine commit <work-unit> -m "<message>" [--discovery | --state | --topic <phase>/<topic> [--sweep]]
engine commit --paths <file> … -m "<message>" --task <work-unit> <N.M>    # the task in flight in this conversation; the project's checks first
engine commit --state -m "<message>"                # the state's own .state dir
engine commit --inbox -m "<message>"
engine commit --product -m "<message>"
engine commit --workflows -m "<message>"
engine commit --migrations -m "<message>"            # the whole state, and the owned code files as the index records them
```

**`code`** — the code change, read across every repo in the project (`domain/code.cjs`): the root and the repos the code commit finds inside it (see `--paths` under `commit`). Paths in and out are the project's, whichever repo holds the code; a project inside a larger repository reads only its own folder of it, a commit that touched nothing there none of its. In a project of one repo each form answers what git answers there. Nothing is written.

`log` and the selecting `diff` take the commits `--subject <prefix>` selects: those whose subject opens with the prefix, taken as a fixed string. A mention in a body, or the prefix later in a subject, never matches.

The forms:

- `log` answers `{"ok": true, "commits": [{"repo", "sha", "subject"}], "files": […], "repos": [{"repo", "base"}]}` — the selected commits grouped in repo order (the root, `.`, first, then the nested repos in folder order), oldest first within each; the project paths they touched, sorted, each once; and one entry per repo holding any, its `base` the full sha of the parent of its earliest selected commit, or git's empty tree where that commit is the repo's first. A move names both its paths. A submodule's pointer is the submodule's change, never its parent's: the commit that records a moved pointer under the task's subject drops out, and one carrying more keeps the rest. A repo with no commits yet adds nothing; a selection nothing matches answers three empty lists.
- `diff` with a selection is the change those commits made: each repo's diff from its base to its `HEAD`, confined to the files its selected commits touched — a later commit on one of those files is part of it, a file only an unselected commit touched is not.
- `diff` alone is the change not yet committed: each repo's change against its `HEAD` (the empty tree before the repo's first commit), staged and unstaged, with its untracked files read as additions in the same patch — taken in as intent-to-add on a scratch copy of the index, so the real one never changes. `.leeway/` is held out, and so is a parent's view of a nested repo — its untracked folder, a submodule's moved pointer — and a link the worktree's preparation made — an untracked symbolic link at a folder the project's `worktree.symlinkDirectories` names (**`verify`**'s `mutants`), in any repository of the project, the root among them.
- `status` answers `{"ok": true, "files": [{"path", "status"}]}` — every uncommitted file across the repos with its two-letter porcelain status (`??` untracked, whatever the user's `status.showUntrackedFiles`; each untracked file named on its own, never its folder, so a new folder lists every file in it), the same dirt `commit --paths` answers as `left_dirty`, with the same hold-outs as `diff`.

The two `diff` forms answer the unified diff itself on stdout, never JSON — read as `git diff` output is — and nothing at all when there is no change. One patch per repo, in repo order, its paths named from the project: a nested repo's folder rides the `a/` and `b/` prefixes (`diff --git a/api/src/billing.ts b/api/src/billing.ts`), and a move reads as a deletion and an addition, so both its paths carry it. The patch is plain whatever the user's git config — no colour, no external diff driver. Bad usage refuses (`{"ok": false, "error": …}` on stderr, exit 1): a missing or empty prefix, a second one, an unknown flag, a stray positional.

```bash
engine code log --subject "<prefix>"
engine code diff [--subject "<prefix>"]
engine code status
```

**Transaction receipts.** The lifecycle verbs answer with their one-line JSON only. The user-facing receipt — `DISPLAY: confirmation`, the `DISPLAY: kb warning` advisory, the absorption summary, the `MENU: pivot continuation` — is fetched by the calling flow right after the verb via the `render` receipt surfaces (`workunit-receipt`, `topic-receipt`, `absorb-receipt`, `absorb-continuation`, `pivot-continuation`). Each surface validates that the state it renders from matches the verb it receipts and refuses loudly out of place; `--warn` prepends the knowledge advisory, set by the caller when the transaction's JSON carried `warnings` (the failure detail itself stays in the JSON line). The calling flow emits each section verbatim per its marker, in the step that shows it, and never parses it for decisions.

**`handoff`** — every move into work, named by one call (`domain/handoff.cjs`), the skill bare or as its slash command — a menu's stored route passes as it stands. One table holds each skill a handoff lands on and the arguments it takes: `leeway-discovery <work-type|start|none> <none|epic> [<seeds|none>]` (how the conversation was come to: a work type, the person starting a piece of work of that kind; `start`, a piece of work of no kind yet, and only where no epic is named; or `none`, the product's conversation or an epic's itself (8.2.9); `none`, the product's conversation, where every start lands, or an epic the project holds, its own discovery, and an epic only beside the work type `epic` or `none`; `none` or comma-joined seeds, only where no epic is named — live inbox items on disk and waiting roadmap items, `roadmap:{item}`, which a pull hands in (8.1.8), each named once — absent reads `none`), `leeway`, the start menu, taking nothing (where finish hands every unit but an epic once it is done, 24.1.2), `leeway-roadmap open`, `leeway-continue-epic <epic> [<completed-phase> <outcome>]` (the phase `discovery` or one the epic's pipeline holds, the outcome `completed`, `cancelled` or `postponed`, the two given together; an epic in progress, or one finish completed handed `review completed`, its one move onto the menu once done, 24.1.2), and each phase's own skill, `leeway-{phase}-process` — but a quick-fix's implementation, whose skill is `leeway-quick-fix-process <quick-fix> <work-unit>`, the one that runs it from its opener to its close (19.5.1), so the build's `leeway-implementation-process` serves an epic, a feature and a bugfix — `<work-type> <work-unit> [<topic>]` (a work type whose pipeline holds the phase, a work unit the project holds of that type, and a legal topic name, which need not exist yet — an epic names it), and the spec's, entered at the unit, `<work-type> <work-unit>` alone. Implementation's and review's take every unit at the unit alone, a topic refused by the usage; what a unit's build cannot yet enter — one built under the old plan (P12), one whose build has not begun, a quick-fix's review — the phase's own `enter` refuses. One more form names no skill: `topic <work-unit> <topic>`, the start skill's direct open (`/leeway {unit} {topic}`, the line a send report ends with), hands off into the phase the unit's topics are talked through — a bugfix's investigation, any other's discussion — its work type read from the unit and the rest checked as that skill's handoff is. It refuses, before anything travels, what that phase's `enter` would refuse, by its rule (`domain/topic-standing.cjs`) and in its words: a unit the project lacks or not in progress, one past its `spec.md`, a topic the unit does not hold, and one cancelled, postponed, promoted or, in discussion, decided — a bugfix's investigation once its menu leads past it (`computeNextPhase`); a quick-fix, which has no topics to open by name; and a migrated unit of a type the reshape retires, in its line, as `enter` gives it. Anything else refuses (`{"ok": false, "error": …}` on stderr, exit 1): a skill outside the table, the wrong number of arguments (the error is the target's usage), an argument of the wrong shape or in a combination its target cannot take, a work unit no longer in progress (a handoff moves into work in progress) but an epic finish completed handed to its menu, a manifest that does not parse (refused as such, never as a unit not found), and an argument the invocation could not carry — a backtick in any (it would end the continuation's code span), whitespace or a quote in any but the quoted seeds, a double quote in those. `none` is a reserved work-unit name, so the placeholder never names a unit. The engine composes the continuation, ``Invoke `/<skill> <args>`.`` (``Invoke `/leeway`.`` for the start) — the inbox seeds quoted, as discovery reads them — and the line naming where the work goes, the place and then its most specific name (`→ Discussion · note-window`, `→ Discovery`, `→ Epic · fumi`, `→ Start`); the session writes neither, and nothing travels but the skill and its arguments. The answer is sections, as a render surface's is, and nothing is written: a `DATA` section reading `handoff: mod` where the gate mod announced it carries handoffs (`LEEWAY_HANDOFF=1` in the environment, set at session start beside and independent of `LEEWAY_GATE_SURFACE`) and `handoff: inline` where it did not, then `skill:` and `args:`, always present, as the skill is invoked with them; a `DISPLAY: handoff` section carrying the line; and, announced alone, a `=== HANDOFF … ===` section — one line of JSON, `{"skill", "args", "text", "line"}` with `text` the continuation — for the mod, which cuts it from the conversation's Bash result, clears the conversation as the turn ends and sends `text` into the new one. Under `inline` the session invokes the skill in place, as it does under `mod` where the section is still in the result — the mod did not take it.

```bash
engine handoff <skill|/skill> [args …]
engine handoff topic <work-unit> <topic>
```

**`render`** — the surface catalogue (`domain/render.cjs`): named runtime surfaces returning demarcated `=== DISPLAY: … ===` / `=== MENU: … ===` sections the calling flow emits verbatim per their markers, in the step that shows them. Address-backed values come from the manifest (JSON state only — the engine never parses markdown artifacts); judgment content arrives as a JSON payload file written to the phase cache with the Write tool and validated loudly per-field. Gate-mode branching happens inside the surface: the response carries the menu when the mode is `gated` and the auto-proceed line otherwise — a payload may force the gate over an `auto` mode (the `finding` surface's `choice` move), so the calling flow follows the response's sections, never a section re-derived from gate-mode state. No git commit; nothing is written, with one exception — `phase-note` beats the addressed topic, because announcing a phase's start is the session taking it. While `LEEWAY_GATE_SURFACE=1` is in the environment a `=== GATE … ===` section sits directly above the response's first `MENU` — one line of JSON stating the menu's question, its statement (every other line of prose it draws), its pressable rows (each part of a row's label a field of its own) and its typed rows, each row with its detail (the lines drawn directly beneath it, `null` when none), every value as text with its markup removed and its escapes honoured. The collector is taken once per response, so a later `MENU` in the same response carries none, and neither does a `MENU` that draws nothing; the section is for a surface that draws the gate, never displayed, and absent without the flag.

```bash
engine render backlog-gate <wu>.<phase>.<topic> --file <payload>  # the backlogging door's ambiguity stop; payload {title} — what is set aside, an idea, a bug or a fix, named in the statement; the words left the home open, so the two places it can wait are the rows: r/roadmap (decided, waiting under a horizon) and i/inbox (not decided yet, triaged later); the address is validated like every per-topic surface
engine render candidate-gate <wu> --variant announce             # the gap-analysis approval gate's opening line, counted from the staging subtree — "Gap analysis surfaced K candidate topic(s)." over the candidates marked `pending`, "Nothing new to review." where none are or nothing was staged
engine render candidate-gate <wu> --file <payload>  # the gap-analysis approval gate's per-candidate stop; payload {name, summary} — the candidate display plus the y/a/s/Comment menu when the staging subtree's gate_mode is gated, the approval line when it is auto; a name the manifest does not mark `pending` refuses
engine render send-closed-target <wu>.discovery.<target>  # the send's stop over a cancelled target: reactivate the unit and send there, elsewhere, or drop — a target that is not cancelled refuses
engine render map <wu>.<topic>  # the topic's map as the conversation shows it, one DISPLAY section as a text code block: the person's questions and Claude's calls, a line each, cut to fit — a summary (the person's decided, open and moved on, Claude's calls, and the rows carried over where there are any, counted apart — a carried entry never passes as the person's decision), then Claude's calls since the person last looked, each answer beneath its question; OPEN, an open question noted where it came as a message from another topic or an experiment measures it (still running, or reported with its result to write); DECIDED, each tagged yours, your call, don't care, carried over, or Claude's call; MOVED ON, moved to another topic, later on the roadmap, or on another topic; every tag in one column across the sections. Drawn in the same read as `ledger shown`, so drawing it moves the last-looked mark; refuses an address that is not <wu>.<topic> of a unit's topic, moving no mark
engine render decided <wu>.<topic> [--file <payload.json>] [--calls]  # the decided screen and its gate — see **`decided`**
engine render decided-strike <wu>.<topic> [--page]  # the pick of the staged candidate to strike, where more than one stands — see **`decided`**
engine render build-headline <wu> --file <payload.json>  # the tasks started together, one block — see **The build's screens**
engine render task-landed <wu> <task>  # a task's landing summary, the questions waiting beneath it — see **The build's screens**
engine render task-account <wu> <task>  # a task's account, printed when asked — see **The build's screens**
engine render step-gate <wu> <task>  # in step, the gate after a task, its account above it — see **The build's screens**
engine render phase-merge-gate <wu> <N>  # in step, the gate before a phase's stack merges — see **The build's screens**
engine render build-stop <wu>  # the stop report, when nothing can be built without an answer — see **The build's screens**
engine render build-progress <wu>  # the build's phases and tasks, each with its state and what holds it — see **The build's screens**
engine render start-this-work [--file <payload.json>]  # the start this work screen and its gate — see **`start-this-work`**
engine render approve-map <wu> [--file <payload.json> | --sweep]  # the approve the map screen and its gate, after the sweep with --sweep — see **`approve-map`**
engine render approve-map-strike <wu>  # the pick of the sweep's candidate to strike, where more than one stands — see **`approve-map`**
engine render ready-to-build <wu> [--page] [--calls]  # the spec's gate: the outline, the phase cut and the build's position — see **`ready-to-build`**
engine render discovery-map <wu>  # an epic's map as discovery reads it: a DATA section — the counts, each topic by its order, name, lifecycle, the name a rename gave it, an unstarted topic's brief (absolute) and summary, and the names struck from it — then the map's DISPLAY; refuses a unit that is no epic
engine render wait-agents <wu>.<topic>  # the process gate over the topic's agents still running as it closes: the statement counts them by kind (`1 deep dive, 1 review`), then w/wait (take in what they find first), whose `next` draws `render wait-agents` again — run once the running agents' reports land, it is empty when none still runs, else the gate over those left — and g/go (their reports wait for the next opening), whose `next` continues to the close; empty where none runs — an agent in flight with no report and its owner alive
engine render experiment-register <wu>.experiment.<topic>  # the series register — the topic's full measurement history off the manifest, never a hand-maintained file: one row per experiment, sub-experiments nested under their parent, a live row tagged with its status, a terminal row spelling `Concluded — {verdict}` / `Abandoned — {reason}` on its ↳ line; an empty series renders the none-yet line
engine render experiment-approval-gate <wu>.experiment.<topic> --id <E{n}>  # the briefing gate — the user-confirmed freeze between a written design and the first measurement (yes / abandon / Amend); refuses any record not `designed`
engine render experiment-pick <wu>.experiment.<topic>  # the record picker — the several-live-records path, rendered directly beneath the register it picks from: one row per live top-level record keyed by its id, then `b/back` (resolves no record and returns to the caller); refuses a series with no live record
engine render experiment-next-gate <wu>.experiment.<topic>  # the return leg's gate — a record just closed and the series still holds live experiments: the statement names the live ids, `y/yes` works the next experiment in this session, `m/menu` goes back to the menu; refuses when no live top-level record exists (the bridge exit follows a finished series)
engine render experiment-spawn-gate <wu>.discussion.<topic> --id <E{n}>  # the now-or-later choice right after a spawn is recorded, addressed to the spawning item (yes = the conversation pauses and the laboratory opens for the topic, its `next` the handoff `leeway-experiment-process {work-type} {wu} {topic}`; later = the conversation carries on, its `next` `continue`, the question the experiment measures waiting for its result unless the person answers it first); refuses an id the item holds no evidence wait on — the gate follows the recorded spawn
engine render summary-backfill-gate <wu> --variant batch|proposed|unsourced [--file <payload>]  # the epic's provenance recovery over the map's rows missing a summary or description, read in map order (refused where none are): batch = the proposed summaries (as a text code block — each row numbered by its title, beneath it its drafted line, `(source file missing — please provide)` where the draft is null, or the map's own summary `(already populated)`) above the accept/edit/skip gate, payload {summaries: {topic: line|null}} keyed by exactly the rows missing a summary; proposed = the same display alone, the edit loop's re-render; unsourced = the provide/dismiss/leave gate over the topics with no source file, payload {names: [topic, …]} listing them
engine render phase-completed <wu> --phase <phase>      # one-line phase-completed display
engine render cancel-gate <wu>.discovery.<name> | <wu>   # the cancel confirm over one unit, the epic menu's and a conversation's own — the statement names exactly what the cancel takes (a never-started topic: only the map row is marked; a started one: its items by phase with their `[status]`, the open experiments that end abandoned — counted top-level, a split named with its parent; a single-topic unit, addressed by its bare name: the whole unit) + y/n, yes's `next` running the cancel (`topic cancel {wu} discovery {name}`, or `workunit cancel {wu}`), no's `continue`; a locked, already-cancelled, or nothing-to-cancel unit refuses — the menu never offers it — and so do an epic by its bare name and a single-topic unit not in progress
engine render postpone-gate <wu>.discovery.<topic> --horizon "<horizon>"   # the postpone confirm over one Discovery unit — the statement in two halves: what goes (a never-started topic: the row alone; a started one: its items by phase with their `[status]`) and where it lands (the item waiting under the horizon, flagged `(new)` over an existing map, `the roadmap is created with it` over none, or the topic's own item re-waiting) + y/n/Comment, yes's `next` running `topic postpone {wu} {topic} --horizon "{horizon}"`, no's and Comment's `continue`; a locked unit refuses with the plan's first lock, so it is met at the confirm
engine render phase-note <wu>.<phase>.<topic> --verb <Word> [--noun <word>]   # a phase start's one-liner: "{Word} {noun|phase}: {Topic}"
engine render workunit-receipt <wu> --verb cancel|reactivate|pivot [--inbox <n>] [--warn]  # lifecycle confirmation from manifest state (pivot: advisory-only; --inbox, cancel only: how many waiting messages the cancel moved to the inbox, from its response's `inbox`; a quick-fix's cancel names each fix's work its park set aside, which the cancel keeps, with the calls that show it and bring it into the checkout); refuses when the state doesn't match the verb
engine render topic-receipt <wu>.discovery.<name> --verb cancel|reactivate [--warn] | <wu>.discovery.<topic> --verb cancel [--inbox <n>] [--warn] | <wu>.discovery.<topic> --verb postpone|restore [--warn]  # topic lifecycle confirmation (--inbox: how many waiting messages a discovery cancel moved to the inbox, from its response's `inbox`) (each addressed by topic — the reactivate and the pull forward's restore name the statuses their items returned to, the postpone the horizon its roadmap item waits under)
engine render absorb-summary <feature> --into <epic> --topic <name>   # the pre-confirm summary — what the absorb will move and do, every fact read from the feature's manifest and its folder by the surface: its log, brief, waiting mail, experiments, seeds, assets and spikes; experiments count top-level records only
engine render absorb-receipt <epic> --topic <name> [--moved seeds,assets,spikes] [--experiments <N>] [--renamed <from>:<to>[,<from>:<to>…]] [--warn]  # the post-absorption summary; --moved lists what the absorb's JSON reported moved, --experiments its top-level record count when a series moved (ids without a dot — a split is worked inside its parent), --renamed its `renamed_assets` and `renamed_spikes` pairs, one line each
engine render import-reprompt --file <payload.json>               # the re-prompt after a landing refused; payload {"missing": ["path", …]} — the refused paths listed as a text code block (nothing there, a folder, or unreadable), corrected paths or `s/skip`; addressless because the work unit may not exist yet
engine render pivot-continuation <wu>                             # the manage flow's post-pivot continue/back menu; refuses unless the unit is an epic
engine render absorb-target <feature>                             # the absorb flow's target-epic selection menu; refuses when the absorb guard doesn't hold
engine render absorb-confirm-gate <feature>                       # the absorb flow's proceed consent, rendered beneath the summary the calling prose displays; refuses when the guard doesn't hold
engine render absorb-continuation <epic> --feature <name>         # the post-absorption continue-as-epic/back menu, the pivot continuation's sibling
engine render archived-actions --path <archived path>             # the archived sub-view's action menu over the selected item (view/unarchive/delete/back) — the title on the label is the file's own; refuses without --path, a live inbox path, or one the store does not hold
engine render archived-delete-gate --path <archived path>         # the archived item's delete consent — the consequence stated, then "Delete it?" (y/n); same refusals
engine render inbox-content --path <inbox path>[,<inbox path>…]   # inbox items in full, live or archived, in the order given — one DISPLAY section, as markdown: each item's italic `[type] — date` line, then its file content as written (frontmatter skipped), its own heading its visible title; refuses without --path, a path outside both stores, or one nothing sits at
engine render inbox-restore-receipt --path <live path>              # the receipt after `inbox restore`, over the live path it answered — 'Restored "{title}" to the inbox.', the title the file's own; refuses without --path, an archived path, or a live path nothing sits at
engine render inbox-delete-receipt --path <archived path>           # the receipt after `inbox delete`, over the archived path it removed — 'Deleted "{title}".', the title read from git's last copy of the file (HEAD's while the deletion is only staged, else the deleting commit's parent); refuses without --path, a live path, a path the archive still holds, or one git holds no copy of
engine render completed-actions <wu>                              # the completed & cancelled view's action menu over the selected unit (reactivate/back/Ask), its status read from the unit's own manifest; refuses a unit that is neither completed nor cancelled
engine render revisit-phases <wu>                                 # the revisit-phase selection menu over the revisitable earlier phases — completed, never the experiment or a decided discussion; linear work types only, refuses when nothing is revisitable
engine render roadmap-view                                        # the Roadmap display — horizon groups, item rows with join notes (a bug's or a quick-fix's kind ahead of its state; an idea's unmarked), the breakdown header; refuses a never-born roadmap
engine render roadmap-add-gate --horizon <name>                   # the add-to-joined-horizon routed confirm. Delivery is a new topic, so only an epic in flight a member was pulled into counts — a bugfix, quick-fix or feature takes no topic. Every member in such epics → strict two-way (d/delivery into the work underway / h/horizon); any other member, waiting or pulled elsewhere → three-way (w/waiting on the roadmap in the horizon); the DATA's `work_units` names the epics the delivery row pulls into. Prints nothing where the add passes — no member of the horizon in an epic underway, an unknown horizon and a never-born roadmap among them; refuses only a missing `--horizon`
engine render horizon-pick [<wu>.discovery.<topic> | --park <name>]  # the horizon pick, shared by the park, the postpone, the inbox working set's move onto the roadmap, and a decided screen's candidate parked from a one-topic unit (--park <name>: "Putting "{Name}" on the roadmap.", every row's `next` `continue`, the candidate's horizon the person's pick; takes no address) — the map's horizons as numbered rows in `roadmap state`'s `horizons` order, each with what waits in it, then n/new; addressed to a topic leaving for the roadmap, each horizon's `next` renders its postpone gate (`render postpone-gate {address} --horizon "{horizon}"`) and n/new's is `continue`, unaddressed no row declares one; refuses a never-born roadmap and one with no horizons (the caller asks for a name in prose instead), and an address at any phase but discovery
engine render park-gate --name <kebab> --horizon <h> --summary <text> [--kind <idea|bug|quick-fix>] [--source <path>]  # the park confirm before `roadmap add` — the item and its summary (a bug or a quick-fix named by its kind; `--kind` defaults to idea), the horizon flagged `(new)` when the map does not hold it, the roadmap's own birth when there is no map, the source when given; y/n + Comment. Refuses a name already on the roadmap in the add verb's words, an unknown kind, and any missing flag
engine render inbox-roadmap-gate --file <entries.json>            # the confirm before inbox items move onto the roadmap, over the very file `roadmap add-batch` then takes — entries {name, horizon, summary, note}, every one with a note and all under one horizon; add-batch's own validation runs as a dry run (nothing moves), so it refuses exactly where the verb would, naming what failed. States each item (the kind its note's folder gives it, where not an idea) and the horizon, flagged `(new)` when the map does not hold it, the roadmap's own birth when there is no map; y/n + Comment
engine render roadmap-harvest-gate                                # the roadmap harvest's sort confirm (y/e/Adjust)
engine render roadmap-parks-gate                                  # the epic synthesis' parks-only confirm (y/e/Adjust, park register)
engine render query-failure-gate --file <payload.json>           # the knowledge query-failure gate: payload {error} — the failed query's error output, verbatim — rendered beneath a `⚑ Knowledge query failed` callout, each line keeping its own indentation, with the likely causes and the `knowledge status` command that diagnoses them, above the r/retry, s/skip menu
engine render brownfield-offer-gate                               # leeway's Step 0.6 offer to research a codebase that predates the workflows; refuses once any answer is recorded — a native verdict included
engine render walkthrough-offer [--menu-only]                    # leeway's one-time offer, standing before the walk and outside it (no position, never counted toward its length): TITLE, the content file's prose as markdown (not a code block), then the menu — y/yes, s/skip, a question row; --menu-only emits the menu alone, the return from a question
engine render walkthrough-screen --screen <1..8> --from <first-run|help> [--menu-only]
                                                                  # one screen of the walk: TITLE, the content file's prose as markdown (not a code block) and its diagrams as text code blocks, in file order, then the menu — whose rows follow the screen's position and the origin the walk was entered from; --menu-only emits the menu alone, the return from a question
engine render walkthrough-home                                    # the help home — the walk, the reference cards, a question, and the way back to the start menu
engine render walkthrough-topics                                  # the reference cards' index: one numbered row per card in file order, labelled with its title, then b/back and a question row. The DATA table (`CARDS (key  name):`, `  N  <slug>` per row) carries each number's slug — route on it, never on the label: a card's title does not determine its slug ("The kinds of work" is `kinds-of-work`). The set is the content directory's, so a card is added by writing one
engine render walkthrough-topic --name <slug> [--menu-only]       # one reference card: TITLE, then the content file's prose as markdown (not a code block) and its diagrams as text code blocks, in file order — no menu, so a card shown mid-conversation carries no gate. `--name` is the slug the topics DATA table gave for the number the reader pressed; an unknown or missing one refuses and names every slug. --menu-only emits the card menu alone (t/topics, b/back, a question row — the same at every card), fetched by the help flow beneath the card and again after a question
engine render send-report <wu>.<topic> --sent <path>               # where a message `topic send` delivered landed, --sent the path it answered: its title under the target, waiting in the mailbox — postponed, for its return — then `/leeway {unit} {topic}`; a cancelled or removed topic's inbox idea in one line; refuses a path no send to that topic answers
engine render migrations-applied --file <payload.json>           # leeway's Step 0.1 summary of what the migrations did — `DISPLAY: migrations applied` under a **Migrations Applied** label: payload {summary, notices?, migrations?, files?} — the session's own summary, verification fixes included; the notices boot's `migrations.notices` carried, verbatim, each its own paragraph beneath the summary; and the run's counts from `migrations.output`, given together or not at all (left out where the run updated no file)
engine render migration-gate                                      # leeway's Step 0.1 confirm gate after the migration summary — static yes/ask menu
engine render label-gate                                          # leeway's Step 0.4 tmux session-label opt-in — static yes/no menu
engine render git-floor-stop                                      # boot's `git` stop — `DISPLAY: git floor stop` (the red ⚑ fact: older than the floor, or no git) + `DISPLAY: git floor account` (the floor, the version found, where to get a newer one); reads the version again, and refuses where this git meets the floor
engine render repo-stop                                           # boot's `repo` stop — `DISPLAY: repo stop` (the red ⚑ fact: this folder is in no git repository) + `DISPLAY: repo account` (Leeway runs inside a git repository, naming the folder; make it one, or open Leeway in the repository the work lives in, then `/leeway` again); offers nothing to run, and refuses where the folder is in a repository
engine render state-lost-gate                                     # boot's `lost` stop — `DISPLAY: state lost stop` + `DISPLAY: state lost account` (the state branch gone here and on the remote; another clone can push it back) + `MENU: state lost gate` (y/yes start again with a fresh state, its next `boot --start-again`; n/no not now, nothing written, its next null)
engine render state-merge-gate                                    # boot's `merge` stop — DATA `choice:` (the identity `state-merge pick` names) + `DISPLAY: state merge stop` + `DISPLAY: state merge account` (the field, where, and each side's value) + `MENU: state merge gate` (1 this machine's value, 2 the remote's; at names made twice or a report set aside, 1 carry on), each row's next `state-merge pick <key> <choice>`; refuses where no merge waits on a pick
engine render github-rule-gate                                    # setup's offer of the rule on leeway-state against deletion and force-pushes, where boot's `github_rule_offer` is true — `MENU: github rule gate` (y/yes set it, n/no leave it unset), each row's next `github-rule yes|no`
engine render github-squash-gate                                  # setup's offer of the squash default, where boot's `github_squash_offer` is true — `MENU: github squash gate` naming each nested repository it covers (y/yes set it, n/no leave it), each row's next `github-squash yes|no`
engine render ledger-stop                                         # the screen of the engine's last stop on the ledger, as the stop found it — a line not as committed, naming it and the id it shows, or a ledger a newer release wrote to; refuses where no stop is kept
engine render directive-stop --directive <id>                     # boot's `directive` stop — `DISPLAY: directive stop` (the ⚑ fact: the step the migration stopped at, named from the directive's slug) + `DISPLAY: directive account` (no session works in the project until it is done; where any piece is done, how many of the project's pieces have what its check says a piece done has, each checked, then the piece in hand redone from the start, or, every piece done, the step ready to close; this session carries it on) — nothing said of what is done where the check fails; refuses without `--directive`, and a directive the migrations lack
engine render knowledge-gate --variant reuse|deviate|mode|retry|wizard [--provider <name> --model <name>]  # leeway's Step 0.5 knowledge gate menus, variant-keyed: reuse = adopt the system configuration (yes/different/terminal — `--provider` names it on the yes row, `--model` beside it when the configuration carries one, both omitted for keyword-only; a lone `--model` refused), retry = the re-run once the key is stored (yes/keyword) beneath `DISPLAY: knowledge retry`, the terminal commands that store the key for `--provider openai|openai-compatible` (required; `--model` refused) as a text code block — the session's own guidance against pasting the key goes between it and the menu; neither flag on any other variant, deviate = the per-project alternatives (keyword/terminal), mode = the search-mode pick (openai/compatible/keyword/terminal), wizard = the wait while the terminal wizard runs (a `DISPLAY: knowledge wizard` naming the command above the yes gate)
engine render review-report <work-unit> [--round <N>]              # a review round's report, the last by default — `DISPLAY: review report`, drawn from the records to it, each round with the fix tasks it made, each standing as the tracker holds it now: FIXED where done, NOT BUILT where closed, else TO FIX in its phase, what the unit's put backs set aside while it holds anything, as the gate over the tree draws it, and the calls decided for the person counted as they stand now, as `render review-calls` lists them — the round that ended the review its closing report, every round's
engine render review-calls <work-unit>                            # every call decided for the person across the unit, by kind, the unlabelled last — `DISPLAY: review calls`
engine render review-tree <work-unit>                             # the gate over the working tree changed under a round — `DISPLAY: review tree` (each change outside .leeway/ since the round's snapshot, one the engine cannot put back marked) + `MENU: review tree` (p/put `review restore-tree`, where no HEAD moved and something can be put back; k/keep `review accept-tree`; Comment — keep alone where no snapshot is held) — see **`review`**
engine render finish <work-unit>                                  # finish's gate: the skips on the way, each verdict owed in the build, questions invited, then the yes — see **`finish`**
engine render investigation-plan <bugfix> --file <plan.json> [--revised]  # the investigation's plan gate (19.4.19): payload {"order": [{"hypothesis": "H{n}", "check"}, …]}, every hypothesis the log holds open, each once, in the order Claude checks them — `DISPLAY: investigation plan` (each with how it is checked, then those settled so far) and `MENU: plan gate`, y/yes going on and Comment; --revised brings the revised plan back to the same gate, its payload holding besides "changed", what the finding changed, drawn first (19.4.24); refuses a place whose investigation is not open, a hypothesis the log does not hold, named twice or settled, one held open left out, none open, and "changed" given without --revised or missing with it; a line of the log the read passed over is said ahead of the gate, a DATA section of `warning:` lines
engine render investigation-check-in <bugfix> --file <check-in.json>  # the investigation's check-in gate: payload {"resolved": ["H{n}", …], "next"}, the hypotheses resolved since, each one the log holds settled — `DISPLAY: investigation check-in` (the counts, every hypothesis, those resolved marked just now, the experiments, what comes next) and `MENU: check-in gate`, y/yes carrying on and Comment; refuses as the plan does, a hypothesis still open, and none resolved; says the lines passed over as the plan does
engine render conclude <bugfix> [--file <recap.json>]  # the investigation's conclude gate (19.4.23), a learned process gate: it stands as `investigation conclude` does but for what its yes alone checks, refusing likewise; payload {"hunt": "<one paragraph>", "fixes"?: "<unit>", "area"?: "<area>"}, Claude's recap and what the bug is in, refused as `investigation conclude` refuses them, staged in the topic's cache against the root cause and the fix as they stand, read back without --file — a `DATA` section first, where this project's bugfixes have recorded any area, an `area: {area}` line for each, in name order and as first spelt, so Claude names an area alike; then `DISPLAY: investigation conclude` (THE HUNT, the symptoms in the person's words from the log, the ROOT CAUSE and the FIX DIRECTION), then each departure the person made in the investigation that this machine logged asked its verdict first, one at a time, at the conclusion — its verdict block in the display and `MENU: verdict` (`verdict give … --at conclusion`; 16.10.5, 16.10.11) — then `MENU: conclude gate`: y/yes runs `investigation conclude {wu}`, k/keep going carries on, and Comment takes what does not match what the person saw
engine render knowledge-ready                                     # the knowledge gate's closing line — `DISPLAY: knowledge ready`, the configuration this clone's store was built with (provider · model, or keyword-only), read from its metadata; refuses when the checkout has none
engine render help <menu> [<arg>] [--<name> <value> …]           # a menu's help, the screen its h/help row opens (18.9.5, 23.2.1), the menu's arguments as it was drawn: `TITLE` "Help · {Menu}", `DISPLAY: help` — what the menu is for, a bullet a row, "Ask about anything else." — from its help file beside the projection that draws it, `{menu}.help.md` (paragraphs; a line a row, `{key} → {what it does}`, `#` for the numbered rows; `Ask → {label}`), and `MENU: help` — b/back, its next the menu drawn again with every argument it was drawn with, and Ask, its next `continue`; a menu whose registry entry declares its help's heading, closing line or rows of its own draws them — its rows before b/back, each with its next (23.2.3); leaves the conversation's position as it was; refuses a menu the registry does not name and one with no help file yet
```

The pipeline continuation surfaces — the bridge's gates and the epic menu's banners — take a bare `<work_unit>` address (work-unit-level, type read from the manifest).

The `render` group also keeps its dev/debug primitives (`signpost`, `wrap`, `tree`) — authoring aids only, never called from skill flows.

**Static chrome stays literal in skill prose; everything parameterised or state-branching renders in code** — adapter-side via projections, shared runtime surfaces (gates, receipts, menus) via the `render` catalogue above. Transactions answer with one JSON line and render nothing. Only the `render` group's dev primitives (`signpost`, `wrap`, `tree`) are authoring aids never called from flows.

**The menus** (`domain/menus.cjs`, E1) are surfaces of this catalogue, each named once in the registry with its gate's name — `start menu`, `epic menu`, `triage`, `archived items`, `archived item`, `roadmap`, `manage`, `manage unit`, `closed units`, `verdicts`, and `help`, keys of the trust log for good — and drawn in one frame: its `TITLE` where it has one, what it shows above its question as a text block, and its `MENU`, a statement under the rule where it makes one, over its question. Every row carries its `next`, recorded with the gate shown as drawn and returned by `gate "<menu>"` as recorded, never worked out again, so a pick after the state moved meets the refusal of the verb its next names. The frame adds `h/help` to every menu, its next `render help <menu>` with the menu's arguments as drawn — the unit it is for positional, any other as `--<name> <value>` — then the menu's back row and its prompt row; a gate or a raise never draws `h/help` (18.9.4), and an answer typed at a menu or a gate goes to `gate --typed`, which sets it aside, leading nowhere. A menu's render leaves the conversation where the registry says, with no writer lock, as `session label` takes none: the start menu and the project's menus reached from it drop the position as `session repair` does, so the board shows All work (20.3.3); the epic menu arrives at its unit and the roadmap at `roadmap`, as `session label` arrives.
